# Curve Tournament App — Build Log

Detailed, dated write-up of every build made to this app: what was changed, how, and how it was tested. Append-only — a fix or follow-up to an earlier entry gets a note added to that entry (see the entries themselves for the established cross-reference style: "**Fixed 2026-09-11 — see 'X' below**"), not a rewrite of the original.

For **current app state** (rules, what's built, what's not, known issues, immediate priorities) see `HANDOFF.md` instead — that file stays deliberately small so a fresh session can read it in full; this one is meant to be searched (`grep` a function/feature name or date) rather than read start to end. A "see X above/below" reference inside either file may point to a section that's actually in the *other* file now — if the section isn't nearby, check the other file by its title.

---

## Pre-published, fixed multi-round draws (done — 2026-09-20)

`ROADMAP.md` item 6, picked up right after "Positional-points scoring" (below) shipped, per the organiser's own reordered build-order list. A second, organiser-selectable draw-publication mode for qual-table/Swiss: the whole room schedule computed once at generation time from roster order alone, with zero live-result dependency, rather than the live `tieredSeed`/`swissFoldPair` reseed every other tournament uses.

**Scope, settled across two research passes and 5 `AskUserQuestion` design forks total (3 during scoping, 2 more found and resolved during implementation review):**
1. **Computation method**: a simple fixed rotation from roster order only — explicitly *not* a "frozen simulation" of `tieredSeed`'s diversity-weighted logic against fake/neutral results, which would have been architecturally awkward (that machinery is built around reacting to real results that don't exist yet in a fixed draw).
2. **Toggle, not replacement**: a new per-tournament Setup opt-in (`drawPublication: 'adaptive' | 'fixed'`, default `'adaptive'`), mirroring "Positional-points scoring"'s own toggle pattern from earlier the same day. Adaptive reseeding stays completely untouched.
3. **Rematch avoidance**: the fixed rotation should still avoid repeat pairings where mathematically possible — matching, not exceeding, the quality bar `tieredSeed` already sets.

**A useful finding surfaced before any design work began**: Group Stage *already* works the "pre-published" way today — its entire round-robin schedule is computed once at generation time via `circleMethodSchedule` (a classic pairwise circle-method algorithm), purely from group membership, with zero live-result dependency. This narrowed the real open problem to just qual-table/Swiss, and directly informed the algorithm choice below.

**The algorithm (`src/domain/tournament/fixed-draws.ts`, new file):**
- **Swiss** (always room-size-2, pooling restricted to head-to-head formats) directly reuses `circleMethodSchedule` — the exact same primitive Group Stage's own schedule uses — guaranteeing zero repeat pairings through its own full round length, cycling only if a manual round-count override exceeds that (mathematically, a true zero-repeat pairwise round-robin can't go further without repeating).
- **Qual-table** (room size can exceed 2 and vary) needed a genuinely new algorithm, since `circleMethodSchedule`'s pairwise construction doesn't generalize to k-sized groups. Rejected first idea, hand-traced and found wanting before writing any code: "rotate the roster by a fixed offset each round, then chunk into rooms" — traced against a 16-player/2-rooms-of-8 example and found it left 7 of 8 room members unchanged round to round, a blind geometric heuristic with no pair-history awareness and no clean generalization to uneven room-size arrays. Chosen instead: a greedy, per-round, per-wave fill reusing `assignWaveToRooms()` — the *exact* room-choice primitive `tieredSeed` itself uses in production — with every candidate's tier rank held constant at 0 (no live skill signal exists in a fixed draw, so `balanceCost` is inert by construction and repeat-avoidance is the only real signal). This deliberately matches, not exceeds, `tieredSeed`'s own accepted greedy/no-lookahead quality bar.

**A critical architectural fact, verified directly before designing storage, not assumed**: `state.assignments[roundIndex]` being non-empty is used elsewhere in the app as the "this round has been reached" signal (`rankings.ts`'s `lastAssignedRound`; `BracketView.tsx`'s `PlaceholderRound` gate) — pre-populating future rounds' `state.assignments` at generation time would have silently broken current-round detection, Rankings, and Bracket the instant a fixed-draw tournament was generated. Group Stage already avoids exactly this trap by storing its precomputed schedule *on the round object* (`matches`/`groupByes`), not in `state.assignments` — this build follows that same precedent with a new `TournamentRound.fixedRoomAssignments` field.

**Two further real findings, each resolved via `AskUserQuestion` during implementation rather than assumed away:**
1. **Reserves.** Verified directly in `mutations.ts` that `rebuildFutureRounds()` (what `addReserveUnit` calls to regenerate future rounds) is a guaranteed no-op for qual-table/Swiss today — its guard only fires for no-elim rounds, because nothing needed regenerating under the adaptive model. So a reserve joining a fixed-draw tournament would silently *not* get incorporated into the already-computed future rounds without genuinely new regeneration code. Organiser's call: block reserves entirely for fixed-draw tournaments (a small, precedented guard, mirroring the existing Group Stage reserve block) rather than build that regeneration path. New `'fixed-draw'` reason on `ReserveAddResult`, surfaced in `RankingsRoster.tsx`'s existing alert-message mapping; `addWalkUpIndividual` inherits the block for free since it delegates to `addReserveUnit`.
2. **Bracket reveal.** Organiser's call: future fixed-draw rounds show their real room assignments in Bracket, not the generic TBD-style placeholder every other round type uses — the whole point of publishing a draw is that players can see it ahead of time. `PlaceholderRound` (`BracketView.tsx`) gained a new branch, checked first alongside the existing `isGroupStage` branch, reusing its exact styling.

**A third, related gap found and explicitly deferred, not silently dropped**: since a fixed-draw schedule is pre-generated (like Group Stage), it's exposed to the same "ghost name" bug Group Stage had before `patchFutureGroupStageRounds` was built — `removeRosterUnit`/`swapIndividual`/`swapTeam` don't currently patch `fixedRoomAssignments` on future rounds, so a removed/swapped player's old name could linger in an already-published future round. Raised with the organiser directly; explicit call to document this as an accepted v1 limitation (see `HANDOFF.md`'s "known gaps") rather than build a second regeneration mechanism (mirroring `patchFutureGroupStageRounds`'s bye-conversion/relabel approach) in the same pass.

**Built in 4 stages, checked in after each:**

**Stage 1 — domain types + the algorithm itself, fully unit-tested in isolation.** `ScoringSystemKey`-style new `DrawPublicationKey = 'adaptive' | 'fixed'`; `TournamentRound.fixedRoomAssignments`, `PersistedSetup`/`GeneratedTournamentConfig`/`MaterializedGamemodeConfig.drawPublication`. New `fixed-draws.ts` exports `buildFixedSwissSchedule`/`buildFixedRoomSchedule`, both pure functions with zero `TournamentState` dependency. 10 new tests: determinism, shape fidelity across uneven/bye profiles (37-player FFA, 9-player-with-bye, 10-player team-2v2v2v2 shapes), a property-based repeat-avoidance check beating a naive baseline, bye fairness, and Swiss's zero-repeat guarantee including the cycling fallback — all passed on first run, a strong signal the hand-designed algorithm was sound before it ever touched generation.

**Stage 2 — `schedule-generation.ts`/`generation.ts` wiring.** `buildPoolingPhase` attaches `fixedRoomAssignments` to every qual-table/Swiss round when the toggle is on (using the real roster, already threaded through `CommonGenerationInput`); `generation.ts` validates the toggle (rejects it outside qual-table/Swiss, defense-in-depth matching the "positional-points + `poolingPhase: 'none'`" pattern from earlier the same day), wires round 0's real assignment from the fixed schedule, and folds the *entire* fixed schedule's `roomHistory`/`poolingByeCounts` into state at generation time — needed so the live transition *out* of the pooling phase (into the bracket-phase cut round) still correctly avoids rematches/repeat-byes against pairs who already played during the fixed rounds. 5 new tests, including one real fixture bug found and fixed along the way (a test set `confirmedCount: 20` without an actual 20-name roster, silently producing an empty fixed schedule — not a code bug, but a good catch before it could mask a real one).

**Stage 3 — `transitions.ts` skip-branch + the reserve guard.** `advanceTournamentRound` gained one new branch, checked first: if `nextRound.fixedRoomAssignments` is set, copy it directly, bypassing `tieredSeed`/`swissFoldPair` and `recordRoomHistory` entirely (already written in full at generation time). Proven with a test using a deliberately non-tiered pairing (rank1×rank4, rank2×rank3) that `tieredSeed`'s own room-rank tiering logic could never independently produce, so an exact match proves the skip-branch — not `tieredSeed` — actually ran. `addReserveUnit` guard added per the reserve finding above. 2 new tests.

**Stage 4 — Setup UI + Bracket reveal.** New "Draw publication" field in `SetupView.tsx`, gated on `poolingPhase` being qual-table/Swiss, mirroring "Scoring system"'s own structure exactly. `PlaceholderRound`'s new branch per the Bracket-reveal finding above. No new tests needed (pure UI, matching this codebase's existing component-test-coverage gap).

**Testing:** 509 tests total (up from 492), 17 new. `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on every changed file after each stage; `tsc --noEmit` clean except the same pre-existing unrelated `BracketView.tsx` error.

**Not live-verified in-browser** — same constraint as every recent build this session: admin login has no dev-mode bypass, so Setup/Bracket aren't reachable locally without real CFP credentials. Verification relied on the domain test suite (which exercises `generateTournament`/`advanceTournamentRound` end-to-end) plus lint/format/type-check confirming every changed file, including the new JSX, is structurally sound.

## Positional-points scoring (done — 2026-09-20)

`ROADMAP.md` item 5, first item picked up after the roadmap reordering pass — a second, organiser-selectable scoring system alongside Fair Points, needed by real tournaments (confirmed via a real organiser spreadsheet review, `[OFFICIAL] FFA Tournament _ Sep. 20.xlsx`, "Matches 40p" tab) that score each round with a classic fixed rank-based points table (e.g. 1st=10, 2nd=8, ... 8th=1), summed into cumulative standings, rather than Fair Points' `rank - score/100000`.

**Scope, settled via 3 `AskUserQuestion` design forks:**
1. The points table is organiser-configurable per tournament (a new Setup field), not a fixed built-in constant — real tournaments plausibly want different tables, and the one reviewed spreadsheet is a single data point.
2. Scoped to qualification-phase standings only (qual-table/Swiss/Group Stage — everywhere Fair Points is used today). Semis/Final are explicitly out of scope: they have no rank-based scoring at all currently (raw score sum), so extending them would be new behavior, not a formula swap — same precedent as Anonymous Finals v1 excluding team formats/grand-final.
3. Cumulative positional points **sum** across rounds, matching the real spreadsheet's own formulas — not averaged like Fair Points. This also turns out not to need Fair Points' own small-sample protection: summing a per-round value that's already bounded at 0 naturally penalizes a unit that played fewer rounds, rather than rewarding it (the opposite skew from what made Fair Points need averaging in the first place).

**A real correctness risk, found and resolved during research before any code was written**: Fair Points is a lower-is-better value; positional points is higher-is-better. Traced every consumer of the standings' cumulative `totalFP` value in `advancement.ts` to find what would actually break. Most turned out to be direction-agnostic — `rankStandings()`, `detectQualCutoffTie()`/`detectGroupCutoffTie()`, `applyCutoffOrder()` (and its two exports), and `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()`'s cutoff branch all just operate on an already-sorted table (equality/position checks, not magnitude comparisons). Exactly two places hard-code the ascending direction and needed a fix: `materializeStandings()`'s own sort, and `computeGroupStageAdvancement()`'s cross-group finisher sort — both fixed via one shared `compareStandingValue()` helper, so everything downstream inherits correct order for free. (A follow-on discovery while writing tests: under positional points, two different groups' same-rank finishers are *always* tied at that tier, by construction — the table is a pure function of rank, not room or score — so `computeGroupStageAdvancement`'s comparator fix is correct but has no independently-observable effect in a same-size-groups scenario; confirmed via `computeGroupStandings`'s own within-group sort instead, where values genuinely differ.)

**Built in 4 stages, checked in after each:**

**Stage 1 — domain types + validation.** `ScoringSystemKey` gains `'positional-points'`; new `PersistedSetup.positionalPointsTable` (comma-separated, highest rank first) and `MaterializedGamemodeConfig.scoring`/`positionalPointsTable` fields — the existing `GeneratedTournamentConfig.scoring` was write-only, never actually read downstream, confirmed by search before assuming it worked. `generation.ts` validates the table (non-negative integers, must not increase rank-to-rank, must cover the format's largest possible room size via `roomSize.max`) and rejects `positional-points` when `poolingPhase === 'none'` (defense-in-depth, matching the existing pattern for stale double-elimination/odd-count combos — nothing to accumulate into without a pooling phase). New `positionalPoints()`/`scoringSystemLabel()`/`formatStandingValue()` pure functions in `scoring.ts`, alongside `fairPoints()`.

**Stage 2 — the scoring engine (`advancement.ts`).** `computeQualificationStandings()`/`computeGroupStandings()` read `state.gamemodeConfig.scoring` and branch the per-round point value (`fairPoints` vs. `positionalPoints`); `materializeStandings()` branches both the reduction (average vs. sum) and the sort direction via `compareStandingValue()`. No signature changes propagate to any caller — everything's read internally from `state.gamemodeConfig`, so `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()` and everything above it needed zero changes.

**Stage 3 — Setup UI.** The "Scoring system" `<Select>` (previously permanently `disabled`, one option) is now live with both real options, gated on `poolingPhase !== 'none'` (mirroring the existing gate for "Advance to bracket"/"Non-counting rounds" immediately above it — the setting is inert without a pooling phase, so it stays disabled/fairpoints-only there rather than switching). Selecting positional points reveals a new points-table `<Input>` with an explicit helper line on the validation rules.

**Stage 4 — display formatting.** `TournamentStandings.tsx`'s column header and cell, `RankingsView.tsx`'s compact pool-rank badge, `BracketView.tsx`'s `TieBanners()` cutoff-tie headings, and `RunningAdminStatus.tsx`'s read-only recap all now read the tournament's actual scoring system via the new `scoringSystemLabel()`/`formatStandingValue()` helpers, instead of a hardcoded "Fair Points" string. Positional points display as a whole number ("24 pts"); Fair Points keeps its 5-decimal precision. **Small incidental fix, flagged rather than silently folded in**: `RankingsView.tsx`'s own Fair Points display moved from 3 to 5 decimals in the process, matching `TournamentStandings.tsx`/`BracketView.tsx`'s existing precision — 3 looked like an unrelated pre-existing inconsistency, not a deliberate choice.

**Testing:** 492 tests (up from 475), 17 new — `scoring.test.ts` (+6: `positionalPoints()`, `formatStandingValue()`, `scoringSystemLabel()`), `generation.test.ts` (+7: validation failures for a non-numeric/increasing/too-short table, the `poolingPhase: 'none'` defense-in-depth rejection, a happy-path end-to-end generation, and the blank-field default), `advancement.test.ts` (+4: table-lookup + descending sort in `computeQualificationStandings`, sum-not-average across rounds, a `roomBasedComputeAdvancement` regression guard proving the *highest*-points units are the ones who actually advance at a cutoff — not the lowest, which is exactly what the direction-fix prevents — and `computeGroupStandings`'s own within-group sum/descending behavior). `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on every changed file after each stage; `tsc --noEmit` clean except the same pre-existing unrelated `BracketView.tsx` error.

**Not live-verified in-browser** — same constraint as the two builds immediately before this one: admin login has no dev-mode bypass (see `HANDOFF.md`'s "Deliberately parked design questions" #7), so the Setup form and a real generated tournament aren't reachable locally without real CFP credentials. Verification relied on the domain test suite (which exercises the real engine end-to-end, including `generateTournament`) plus lint/format/type-check confirming every changed file, including the new JSX, is structurally sound.

## Fix: elimination-target plateau validation + clearer seeding-override copy (done — 2026-09-19)

Two small organiser-reported issues with "Manual overrides, pre-start only" (see below), found during the organiser's own testing pass right after it shipped.

**1. False rejection of valid plateau sequences.** Entering `16,16,16,14,12,10,8` in the elimination round targets field was rejected: "Elimination round targets must strictly decrease from round to round." The organiser was right that some early rounds legitimately eliminate no one — the check (`generation.ts`, `values[index] >= values[index - 1]`) was stricter than needed. Traced the downstream consumers (`single-elimination.ts`, `double-elimination.ts`) before relaxing it: both bypass the only strict-decrease-dependent helper (`computeCleanTargets`) whenever `explicitTargets` is supplied, and a plateau round just produces `dropCount === 0` / a full room advancing — already handled cleanly by existing zero-drop guards in the losers-bracket routing loop. Changed the comparison to `>` (reject only an actual increase) and reworded the error to "must not increase from round to round."

**2. Unclear seeding-override input.** The field's only guidance was "— one per target above, e.g. ',diversity,balance'" — it didn't say the options were exhaustively `diversity`/`balance`/`random` (plus blank = automatic), nor what each does. Reworded both fields' inline hints (plateaus allowed; blank = automatic) and added a short paragraph under the seeding-overrides input in `SetupView.tsx`, condensed from the existing code comments in `seeding.ts`/`double-elimination.ts`: **diversity** (spread players who've faced each other into different rooms), **balance** (balance apparent skill/seed across rooms), **random** (shuffle rooms, ignoring both history and skill).

**Testing:** 475 tests (up from 474) — the old `'rejects a non-strictly-decreasing sequence'` test (which asserted `24,24` was rejected) was replaced with a `'rejects an increasing sequence'` test (`10,24`) and a new `'accepts a plateau'` test (`32,32,24,20`, asserting the generated rounds' `advTotal` sequence matches exactly). `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on all three changed files; `tsc --noEmit` clean except the same pre-existing unrelated `BracketView.tsx:75` error.

**Not live-verified in-browser** — this app's admin login has no dev-mode bypass (see `HANDOFF.md`'s "Deliberately parked design questions" #7), so driving the real Setup form requires real CFP credentials, which Claude doesn't enter. Verification relied on the domain test suite (which exercises `generateTournament` end-to-end, including the actual rendered `advTotal` sequence) plus lint/format/type-check confirming the JSX change is syntactically sound.

## Fix: stale anonymous-panel wording + missing "save and start a new tournament" action (done — 2026-09-19)

Two small organiser-reported issues, unrelated to each other, fixed in one small pass right after "Manual overrides, pre-start only" shipped.

**1. Stale wording.** The real Final's own box (`BracketView.tsx`) still said "Scored anonymously below ↓" for an anonymous game, pointing at the `AnonymousFinalCard` — but that card now renders beside the Final, as a sibling in the same horizontal `flex` row (`#br-rounds`, no `flex-col`), not below it, a leftover from before the "Anonymous Finals matches, v1" build's layout was adjusted. Reworded to "Scored anonymously — see side panel →".

**2. No single-click "save and start a new tournament" action.** Investigated directly against `RunningAdmin.tsx` and two real tournaments (`1789733102449`, `1789761899543`) — confirmed there is no per-tournament condition hiding a button. "💾 Save to Archive" and "↺ Reset" are the *only* two bottom buttons that have ever existed, unconditionally, for every tournament. The real gap: "↺ Reset" (`resetTournamentState`, `mutations.ts`) deliberately clears only the current tournament's *progress* (scores, assignments, `started`) while keeping the same roster and generated schedule — intentional, so an organiser can re-run an identical tournament after a mistake. Because `AppShell.tsx` routes on `state.started`, Reset does land the organiser back on `SetupView` — but since `rounds` is still populated, that screen shows a "Schedule Preview" for the *same* roster with just a "Confirm & Start" button, not a blank form; getting to one requires separately finding and clicking "↺ Clear roster" (`resetRoster`, `mutations.ts`) — a second, disconnected step nothing links to "starting fresh."

Organiser confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` that the fix should be a **new, dedicated button** doing both in one click, rather than changing Reset's own (deliberately different) behavior. Added "🏁 Save & Start New Tournament" to `RunningAdmin.tsx`'s bottom `ButtonRow`, reusing Reset's own smart-save-prompt UX exactly (a plain `window.confirm` when there's nothing unsaved; a three-way "Unsaved tournament" modal — Save & start new / Start new without saving / Cancel — otherwise). Its core action is just `resetRoster(state)`: confirmed by direct field-by-field comparison that `resetRoster` already resets every field `resetTournamentState` does (identical values throughout) *plus* `players`/`reserves`/`rounds`/`groups`/`qualTable`/`gamemodeConfig`/etc., so no new mutation was needed.

**Found and fixed along the way:** comparing `resetRoster`'s reset list against `TournamentState`'s full field list (`types.ts`) turned up two fields neither `resetRoster` nor `resetTournamentState` ever cleared — `roomHistory` (pair-key → round-index rematch-avoidance map) and `anonymousFinalists` (the Final's placeholder-alias roster). A pre-existing gap in `resetRoster` itself, not introduced here — but one that would have directly undercut the new button's own promise of a clean slate: a reused player name could pick up stale rematch-avoidance pressure from a tournament they were never actually in, and a stale `anonymousFinalists` array could linger into a brand new tournament's own Final. Fixed by adding both fields to `resetRoster`'s reset list — this also improves the *existing* "↺ Clear roster" button for free, since it shares the same underlying function.

**Testing:** 474 tests (up from 473), 1 new — `resetRoster`/`resetTournamentState` had **zero** dedicated test coverage before this (confirmed by search); added a `describe('resetRoster', ...)` block in `mutations.test.ts` asserting the two new fields come back empty alongside the pre-existing `players`/`rounds`/`started` resets. `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on all three changed files; `tsc --noEmit` clean except the same pre-existing unrelated `BracketView.tsx:75` error.

**Live verification** via `pnpm dev`: generated a 16-player FFA-individual single-elimination tournament, played it through to the Final (score-filled every round via direct DOM input dispatch, advancing normally through the app's real logic each time — not synthesized state), flagged a game anonymous, and confirmed both the new wording and — via screenshot — that the "🎭 ANONYMOUS" panel genuinely renders to the right of the "🏆 FINAL" box, not below it. Then, with unsaved score changes still pending, clicked "🏁 Save & Start New Tournament": confirmed the "Unsaved tournament" modal appeared with its three actions, and that "Save & start new" both wrote a real archive snapshot (verified via the Archive tab: "16 players · 5 rounds played") and landed on a genuinely blank Setup form (no roster, no Schedule Preview). Cleared the test tournament's `localStorage` state and archive entry afterward.

## Manual overrides, pre-start only (done — 2026-09-19)

`ROADMAP.md` item 4, next in the recommended build order once `HANDOFF.md`'s "Immediate next priorities" list closed out fully (34/34 done). The organiser confirmed three concrete override needs: per-round advancement count, per-round seeding method, total round count — `ROADMAP.md` itself flagged the exact field list needed "a short scoping pass," resolved via three research passes (Explore agents) plus five `AskUserQuestion` design forks (four before implementation, one mid-build after a real architectural discovery).

**Scope, settled via `AskUserQuestion`:**
1. Seeding-method override scoped to formats using the generic `tieredSeed`/`tieredBracketSeed` mechanism only (single-elimination, double-elimination-shared-final) — Swiss/Group Stage/Kings Valley use entirely different hardcoded mechanisms with no seeding-method concept to override, so excluded outright rather than half-built (same precedent as Anonymous Finals v1 excluding team formats/grand-final race).
2. Since there's no actual menu of interchangeable seeding *algorithms* today (`tieredSeed` is one algorithm with an automatic diversity/balance taper that shifts as the bracket approaches Semis), "seeding override" means forcing a fixed diversity/balance weight for one round's reseed (`diversity` / `balance` / `random`), not swapping in a different algorithm.
3. Per-round advancement addressing: rounds have no stable identity before generation, so the organiser addresses them as an ordered list of survivor-count targets (e.g. `24,12,6`) — this list's own length is the round-count override for these two formats, no separate field needed.
4. Total round-count override scoped to qualification-table, Swiss, single-elimination, and double-elimination-shared-final — the four formats where round count is already a tunable parameter of the existing generation math. Double-elimination race (power-of-2 bracket-size derivation) and Kings Valley (promote/demote simulation until convergence) excluded — overriding their round count would change bracket semantics, not just a parameter.

**Stage 1 — total round-count override (qualification-table & Swiss).** New `PersistedSetup` fields `qualRoundsOverride`/`swissRoundsOverride`. Round count for these two formats was already a config value (`MaterializedGamemodeConfig.qualRounds`/`swissRounds`, consumed unchanged by `pooling.ts`) — just sourced from a hardcoded constant (`QUALIFICATION_ROUNDS = 3`) or `computeSwissRoundCount(n)` instead of an organiser override. `generation.ts` validates `1–12` (a sanity cap, matching `single-elimination.ts`'s own `MAX_ELIMINATION_ROUNDS = 8`-style ceiling) via a new `resolvePoolingRoundsOverride()` helper, and feeds the resolved values into both `gamemodeConfig` and the pre-existing non-counting-rounds validation (which previously computed its own "total rounds" from the hardcoded default — now correctly uses the *overridden* count too, closing a real interaction bug the override would otherwise have opened). 9 new tests.

**Stage 2 — elimination round-target override (single-elimination & double-elimination-shared-final).** New `PersistedSetup` field `eliminationRoundTargets` (comma-separated ordered survivor counts). `generation.ts` validates: positive integers, strictly decreasing, first value ≤ the seed total entering the bracket phase, last value ≥ the effective floor (Semis size for single-elimination; winners-bracket qualifiers into the Final for double-elimination-shared-final). Threaded through as a new optional `explicitTargets?: number[]` on `SingleEliminationConfig`/`SharedFinalDoubleEliminationConfig`; at the one target-computation call site in each of `singleEliminationBracketPhase`/`sharedFinalDoubleEliminationBracketPhase`, `config.explicitTargets` is used directly instead of calling `computeEliminationRoundCount`+`computeCleanTargets` when present. Everything downstream (room distribution, `advPerRoom`/`luckyCount`/`advTotal` construction, and — for double-elimination — the LB-scheduling loop with its `MAX_WB_ROUNDS_BEFORE_LB` cap from the previous entry) already operates on each WB round's *actual* output, not the automatic curve directly, so needed zero changes. 10 new tests.

**Stage 3 — elimination seeding-weight override (same two formats).** New `PersistedSetup` field `eliminationSeedingOverrides` (comma-separated, index-aligned with `eliminationRoundTargets` — only valid when that's also set, since seeding-override positions are addressed by the same ordered round list). New `TournamentRound.seedingOverride?: 'diversity' | 'balance' | 'random'` field, stamped onto generated WB elimination rounds. `tieredSeed`/`tieredBracketSeed` (`seeding.ts`) both gained the override: `'diversity'`/`'balance'` bypass the automatic taper lerp with fixed weight pairs (`{diversity:1,balance:0}` / `{diversity:0,balance:1}`) rather than pinning to either end of the taper's own scale (which would still blend in some of the other priority); `'random'` bypasses tiering entirely with a **deterministically-seeded** Fisher-Yates shuffle (a 32-bit FNV-style hash of target-round-index + sorted candidate names feeding a small seeded PRNG) rather than real entropy — `advanceTournamentRound` has never carried a `RandomSource` (unlike generation-time seeding, which does), and introducing one would have been a much bigger, unplanned architectural change than this feature warranted; the deterministic approach keeps every transition in the app a pure function of state, matching the existing invariant exactly.

**Real design correction found mid-build, resolved via a 5th `AskUserQuestion`:** double-elimination's reseeding turned out to be architecturally different from single-elimination's — `tieredBracketSeed` (`finalizeDoubleEliminationRound`, `transitions.ts`) fires once per *target* round at finalize time, and a losers-bracket target round can pool drops from a WB round together with players already surviving in LB from an earlier round. So "override WB round i's seeding" is only unambiguous for a WB round's own continuation into the next WB round (always single-source) — applying it to an LB-bound drop is ambiguous whenever that LB round's pool merges multiple sources (common in practice). Organiser's call: scope the override to WB-to-WB transitions only, leaving every LB-bound drop on the automatic taper always, rather than the alternative (apply it opportunistically whenever a given LB round's pool happens to be single-source) — simple and predictable over silently inconsistent. Implemented as a lookup in `finalizeDoubleEliminationRound`: only reads `seedingOverride` from the WB round whose `winnersTo` points at the round being finalized, and only when that round is itself a WB round. 15 new tests, including a hand-derived scenario (`tieredBracketSeed`'s `seedingOverride` describe block, `seeding.test.ts`) engineered so `'diversity'` and `'balance'` provably produce *opposite* room assignments for the identical input — not just "a plausible difference."

**Stage 4 — Setup UI.** `SetupView.tsx` had no "advanced" section before this — every existing override (`semisOverride`, `finalOverride`, etc.) is a plain, always-visible `<Input>` conditionally rendered per format. Given `ROADMAP.md`'s own framing ("needs to stay out of the way of the common case") and this being the first override needing 5 new fields at once, added a native `<details>` disclosure — "Advanced round overrides (optional)" — rendered only for the four covered formats, showing just the field(s) relevant to the current `scheduleLogic`/`poolingPhase` combination. The seeding-overrides text input is `disabled` with an explanatory placeholder until `eliminationRoundTargets` has a value.

**Testing:** 473 tests total (up from 441), 32 new, across `generation.test.ts` (validation + end-to-end round stamping for all 5 fields), `single-elimination.test.ts`/`double-elimination.test.ts` (`explicitTargets` bypass), `seeding.test.ts` (the decisive diversity/balance conflict scenario, plus `'random'`'s determinism), and `transitions.test.ts` (wiring proof that `round.seedingOverride` actually reaches `tieredSeed`/`tieredBracketSeed`, and a structural proof that the WB-to-WB-only scoping decision holds). `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on every changed file; `tsc --noEmit` clean except the same pre-existing unrelated `BracketView.tsx:75` error.

**Live verification** via `pnpm dev` (per `AGENTS.md`'s "test with awkward counts first" — used 37, not a round number): expanded the new panel for single-elimination, set `eliminationRoundTargets: "32,24,16"` and `eliminationSeedingOverrides: "diversity,,balance"`, loaded a 37-player roster, and generated — the Schedule Preview showed exactly 3 explicit elimination rounds (37→32, 32→24, 24→16) instead of the automatic 4-round curve, followed by Semis and Final, with no validation errors. Confirmed the seeding-overrides field starts disabled (placeholder "set round targets above first") and enables once targets is filled. Switched pooling phase to qual-table and schedule logic to double-elimination-shared-final and confirmed the panel's field set adjusts correctly (adding "Qualification Table round count override" and "LB qualifiers into the Final" respectively, both alongside the elimination fields). Switched to Kings Valley + no pooling and confirmed the whole panel disappears, since neither scoping condition applies. No tournament was started/mutated during verification — local `localStorage` state was cleared before and after to avoid touching any pre-existing local session.

## Double-elimination: round-numbering mismatch + losers-bracket round delay (done — 2026-09-19)
The organiser asked for a review of a real production tournament (`1789733102449`, a 19-player FFA-individual `double-elimination-shared-final` tournament on `tournaments-test.curvefever.pro`, still at Round 1 so all of this was visible in the *projected* future-round display) after noticing "unexpected behaviours in the losers bracket, both in the seeding of the LB and the round numbering." Investigated by pulling the tournament's raw state directly from Firebase's public REST read (`https://curve-tour-app-default-rtdb.europe-west1.firebasedatabase.app/environments/test/tournaments/1789733102449.json`) and tracing it against the actual generation/labeling code, not by guessing from rendered UI text — this surfaced two distinct, confirmed bugs rather than one.

**Bug 1 — round-numbering mismatch.** Each bracket box's header shows a continuous round number (e.g. "Round 11") since the earlier "merge LB into WB" redesign (see "Bracket view: merge LB into WB's own box" above), per the organiser's own request at the time. But the box *content* — "Room 6A, Rank 1", "Dropped from WB Round 6", "Advanced from LB Round 3" — still used a completely separate, older numbering scheme: `bracketRoundLabels()` (`bracket.ts`) assigned independent incrementing counters (`winnersRound`/`losersRound`) to `bracket === 'winners'`/`'losers'` rounds, decoupled from the round's own continuous `roundNum` — an oversight from the earlier redesign, which changed the box header but never updated this function. Confirmed via research (an Explore agent) that this is a single-function fix: `.roundNumber` is never read outside `bracket.ts`, and `.label` is read in exactly two other places, both automatically fixed for free — `projectFutureRoundSlots()` (the actual source of box-content labels) and `FollowBanner` (`BracketView.tsx`), which showed a followed player's status as `"out in WB Round 6"`, the identical bug. Fixed by sourcing both `label` and `roundNumber` from `round.roundNum` directly for the `'winners'`/`'losers'` branches, removing the now-unused counter variables. New tests in `bracket.test.ts`: confirmed via research that **no existing test would have caught this fix numerically** (every existing WB/LB fixture happened to have `roundNum` and the old counter coincide at 1) — added fixtures with a pre-bracket round before the winners-bracket rounds specifically to exercise the divergence.

**Bug 2 — losers bracket doesn't get its first round for multiple winners-bracket (WB) rounds.** Traced the real tournament: WB round 1 dropped 3 players (19→16), WB round 2 dropped 3 more (16→13), WB round 3 dropped 1 more (13→12) — and no losers-bracket (LB) round was created until *after* WB round 3, when the accumulated pool (7) finally let the target-computation math produce a real cut. Root cause, traced through `sharedFinalDoubleEliminationBracketPhase`'s LB-scheduling loop (`double-elimination.ts`): it accumulates each WB round's drops and only creates an LB round once `computeTargets()` (`single-elimination.ts`) proposes a survivor count strictly less than the pending pool; `computeTargets` calls `snapFriendly()`, which refuses to return any candidate below `roomSize.min` — when the pending pool is small, no candidate qualifies, and `computeTargets`'s own `Math.min(target, previous)` clamp traps the result at the pool size regardless, with **no cap** on how many consecutive WB rounds this could defer for.

Organiser's explicit constraint, confirmed directly (not inferred): at most 2 WB rounds may pass before an LB round is played ("waiting in LB for 1 round is fine; more is too much"), and the forced round should actually eliminate people for real, even in a smaller-than-normal room ("it is fine to play rooms with 6 players at that stage... the bracket scheduling should be made so that these numbers can be reached") — confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` against the alternative (a no-elimination "everyone through" forced round), explicitly rejected by the organiser.

Research (a second Explore agent) confirmed: `computeTargets`/`computeCleanTargets` are also used by `singleEliminationBracketPhase` and by this same function's own WB-round target planning — but **not** by the race/head-to-head double-elimination variant (architecturally different, always creates 1-2 LB rounds per WB round unconditionally, no deferral logic exists there at all) and **not** by Kings Valley, so a fix scoped to the LB-loop call site alone has zero blast radius elsewhere. Also confirmed `distributeRoomsWithBye`/`distributeRooms` (`room-distribution.ts`) have no hard floor on room size already — nothing there needed to change.

Fixed with a new `MAX_WB_ROUNDS_BEFORE_LB = 2` constant and a `forcedLosersSurvivorTarget()` helper: when the natural computation finds nothing to cut and the deferral count reaches the cap, it calls `computeTargets` again with `roomSize.min` relaxed to 1 just for that one computation — letting the same geometric-decay curve propose a genuine intermediate reduction instead of either no cut (the bug) or collapsing straight to `lbQualifiers` in one shot (confirmed by hand-tracing that reusing `computeCleanTargets`'s own single-round fallback would do exactly that — too aggressive for an early forced round, e.g. cutting a pool of 6 straight to 2 survivors).

**Verified the algorithm directly, not just via unit tests**: before writing any test, ported the real `computeTargets`/`snapFriendly`/`computeCleanTargets`/`distributeRoomsWithBye` logic into a standalone Node script and ran the proposed fix against the real 19-player scenario plus 600 additional synthetic scenarios (4 different room-size/`lbQualifiers` configurations × 150 seed totals each) — zero invariant violations across all of them (the 2-WB-round cap always held, every WB round always routed to a real LB round, every forced round was a genuine cut, no forced cut ever went below `lbQualifiers`). This caught a mistaken assumption early — an initial idea to reuse `computeCleanTargets`'s own fallback for the forced case was tested this way and found to cut too aggressively, before it was ever written into the actual implementation. A Plan agent was also dispatched to independently validate the design but hit a session rate limit mid-run; rather than retry, the standalone-script verification above stood in for it, giving equal or greater confidence since it exercised the exact production arithmetic across hundreds of cases rather than a single hand-trace.

Went through Plan Mode first, per the organiser's own standing rule, including two Explore-agent research passes (one per bug) before designing anything, and one additional `AskUserQuestion` round settling the forced-round design fork above.

**Testing**: `double-elimination.test.ts` gained 3 new tests — a general invariant (no more than 2 consecutive WB rounds across 90 different seed totals with the real FFA room-size config), a genuine-cut invariant (every LB round created is a real reduction, never a no-op, across the same range), and a regression matching the exact real 19-player scenario (confirms `maxConsecutiveWbRounds` is exactly 2 and the first LB round lands at exactly 6 players). `bracket.test.ts` gained 2 new tests for the round-numbering fix. 441 tests total (up from 436), 5 new. `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on all four changed files (per `AGENTS.md`'s "Validating changes" — focused checks only, not a full repo build).

**Live-verified two ways**, since the two fixes have different applicability to already-existing data: (1) reloaded the real tournament `1789733102449` locally (`pnpm dev` pointed at the same live Firebase data via `?t=1789733102449`, as an anonymous viewer) and confirmed the round-numbering fix applies immediately even to this already-generated tournament — its future-round labels now correctly cite each source round's own box number (e.g. "Round 5" box's LB section now says "Dropped from WB Round 3/4/5" matching those rounds' own headers, not the old "WB Round 1/2/3"). Confirmed the LB-cadence fix does *not* retroactively apply here, as expected — that tournament's `rounds` array was already generated and persisted before the fix existed, and generation-time logic doesn't recompute an existing schedule. (2) Generated a fresh 19-player `double-elimination-shared-final` tournament locally (a separate tournament, not touching the organiser's real one) and confirmed the LB-cadence fix there directly: the schedule now reads "WB, WB, **LB**" (2 WB rounds before the first LB round) instead of the old "WB, WB, WB, LB" — the first LB round's box content read "Dropped from WB Round 3" ×3 + "Dropped from WB Round 4" ×3, six total, exactly matching the organiser's stated comfort level of a 6-player room at that stage. Zero console errors in either check.

**Explicitly out of scope, confirmed not silently assumed**: the race/head-to-head double-elimination variant (architecturally incapable of this bug — no deferral logic exists there, confirmed by reading it in full) and Kings Valley (doesn't use any of the affected functions) — neither needed any change.

## Anonymous Finals matches, v1 (done — 2026-09-18)
`ROADMAP.md` item 3: an organiser can flag individual *games* within a plain multi-game Final (not whole rounds) to be scored under a temporary placeholder name instead of a real finalist's — live, everyone sees the placeholder's scores as they're entered, and once the Final finishes an admin "connects" each placeholder to the real finalist it represents, merging its scores into that finalist's total and revealing the mapping in one step. Went through Plan Mode first, per the organiser's own standing rule, with two Explore agents researching the actual Finals-scoring code (`finals.ts`/`scoring.ts`/`mutations.ts`/`BracketView.tsx`) and the roster-mutation/visibility model (`swapIndividual`/`removeRosterUnit` conventions, `TournamentProvider.tsx`'s `isViewer`/`unlocked` gates, `database.rules.json`) before designing anything.

Three design forks were put to the organiser directly rather than assumed: **reveal timing** (identity + full breakdown stays hidden until the Final is actually finished, even if connected earlier — organiser confirmed); **placeholder naming** (always auto-generated `Finalist-1`/`Finalist-2`/... in finalist order, no custom names, capped at 10 — organiser confirmed, "other names are not possible"); **scope** (Final only, not Semis, since Semis keys `state.scores` by room/position, a structurally different scheme from `finalScores`'s name-keying — organiser confirmed "1v1... does not make sense in any case" after the grand-final exclusion below was explained).

**Data model** (`types.ts`): new `AnonymousFinalist { alias, realKey, connected }`; `TournamentRound.anonymousGames?: number[]` (which 1-indexed games of the Final are flagged); `TournamentState.anonymousFinalists: AnonymousFinalist[]` (default `[]`, generated once from the Final's own `state.assignments` the first time any game is flagged, held stable across the rest of that Final). Placeholders never touch `players`/`reserves`/any roster array, by design — `finalScores` is a flat `Record<string, ...>` keyed `game{n}-{key}` with no cross-check against the roster, so a placeholder key writes/reads through the exact same `setFinalScore`/`getFinalUnitScore` real finalists already use, and `finalsProgressState` (what `FinalColumn` renders) only ever reads `state.assignments`, so a placeholder is automatically invisible to the real-player UI with zero suppression logic needed.

**Two implementation-time corrections to the approved plan**, both found while coding and explained to the organiser rather than silently applied (neither required going back to Plan Mode — small, defensible scope narrowings within the plan's own spirit, not new decisions): (1) the plan's proposed reveal-gate, `computeRankings(state)?.finalComplete`, turned out to be structurally wrong — that function checks the *real* finalist's own score key for every game, so it could never become true while any game was still anonymous and unconnected (the placeholder's score lives under the alias key, not the real one). Replaced with a new `isPlainFinalFullyScored()` (`finals.ts`), which treats an anonymous game's slot as filled once its alias has a score. Once `connectAnonymousFinalist` merges a placeholder's scores onto the real key, `computeRankings`'s ordinary `finalComplete` (and auto-archive) starts working again on its own — no other code needed changing. (2) Grand-final (race-to-N) rounds are excluded from flagging entirely: `progressGrandFinalRace` decides whether to open the next game by reading the two real finalists' own keys directly, so a game scored under a placeholder would stall the race forever; fixing that properly would mean teaching that already-delicate race-progression logic about aliases too, judged out of scope for this pass. Team-format Finals are also excluded — `getFinalUnitScore`'s team branch reads a real `TournamentTeam` via `buildTeamMap()`, which a placeholder alias never has.

**Domain mutations** (`mutations.ts`, following `swapIndividual`/`removeRosterUnit`'s established conventions — pure, always `dirty(state)`, invalid input returns unchanged `state` rather than throwing): `flagFinalGameAnonymous(state, gameNumber)` (rejects team formats, grand-finals, out-of-range/already-flagged/already-scored games; generates the alias list on first use, capped at 10 finalists); `unflagFinalGameAnonymous(state, gameNumber)` (admin undo, only before any placeholder score exists for that game); `connectAnonymousFinalist(state, alias)` (gated on `isPlainFinalFullyScored`, copies each anonymous game's `finalScores["game{n}-{alias}"]` onto `finalScores["game{n}-{realKey}"]` without overwriting an already-present real score, marks that one alias `connected: true`).

**Selector** (`finals.ts`): `anonymousFinalsProgressState(state, round)`, a sibling to `finalsProgressState` keyed by placeholder alias instead of real name, restricted to the round's own `anonymousGames`; `realKey` on each entry stays `null` until `connected`, a second defense-in-depth gate on top of the mutation's own check.

**UI** (`BracketView.tsx`): `FinalColumn`'s own game-tab (`tab`/`activeTab`) selector was lifted one level up into `BracketRounds` (a `useState` there, defaulting to the live `finalsProgressState(...).nextGame` until manually overridden) so it can be shared with the new sibling card — both always show the same currently-selected game, never drift independently. A "🎭 Make anonymous"/"🎭 Anonymous — click to undo" toggle sits next to the Final's own "Game N — enter scores" label, targeting whichever game is currently selected; the real finalists' own score-entry row for that game is replaced with "Scored anonymously below ↓" when flagged. New `AnonymousFinalCard`, rendered as a sibling box immediately after the Final's own `RoundColumn` in `BracketRounds`' render loop (only when the Final has ever had a game flagged), showing each placeholder's read-only per-game strip, a live `ScoreInput` for the currently-selected flagged game (reusing `setFinalScore` verbatim), and a "🔓 Connect & reveal" button once `isPlainFinalFullyScored` is true.

**Testing**: 19 new tests (16 in `mutations.test.ts` for the three mutations — alias generation/ordering, the 10-finalist cap, flag/unflag rejecting once a real-or-placeholder score exists, grand-final/team-format rejection, `connectAnonymousFinalist` rejecting before every game is filled, merging correctly, idempotent no-ops; 3 in `finals.test.ts` for the selector — empty before any flag, per-game scores keyed by game number, `realKey` hidden until connected). 436 tests total (up from 417). `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check`/`tsc --noEmit` clean (only the pre-existing, unrelated `BracketView.tsx` nullability error remains).

**Live-verified** via a local `pnpm dev` run: generated a 4-player `individual-1v1` single-elimination tournament, played through to a 3-game Final between two real finalists, flagged Game 2 anonymous (confirmed the real Final card switched to "Scored anonymously below ↓" and the new "🎭 Anonymous matches" card appeared with `Finalist-1`/`Finalist-2` and live score fields), entered placeholder scores (visible immediately, real finalists' own totals correctly excluding them), filled Games 1 and 3 normally, confirmed "🔓 Connect & reveal" only appeared once every game was filled, and confirmed connecting merged the score into the real finalist's total (39→45 for the eventual winner) and revealed "Finalist-1 → Anon1" / "Finalist-2 → Anon4". Zero console errors. Cross-session/unauthenticated-viewer verification wasn't attempted — this dev environment has no live Firebase credential, consistent with every prior session's live-testing notes.

## Fix: long usernames overflow the Bracket score input past its box (done — 2026-09-18)
Organiser reported: entering a long username (tried "VeryLongUsername") and the round's score input field "does not adjust and goes beyond the border" of its box. A small, direct fix per the organiser's own standing rule (only significant changes need a plan first).

**Two real bugs, not one.** The obvious-looking cause was the room row's name `<span>` (`BracketView.tsx`, the single-game/non-team room row) having no `min-w-0`/`flex-1`/`truncate` — unlike its two sibling row variants (team rows, multi-game individual rows), which already had `min-w-0 flex-1`. An unbreakable long name like "VeryLongUsername" can't wrap, so without those classes the flex item's minimum width is the full rendered text width, forcing the row past its container. Fixed by adding `min-w-0 flex-1 truncate` (plus a `title={entry.name}` tooltip so the full name is still available) to both that row and the multi-game individual row (which had `min-w-0 flex-1` but no `truncate`, so long names would still visually overflow their own span without clipping).

That alone didn't fully explain the report, so the actual rendered input was inspected directly via `getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()` rather than assumed fixed. It turned up a second, more serious latent bug: `BracketScoreInput`'s `compactScoreClass` (`w-13 shrink-0 rounded-sm px-1.5 py-0.5 text-xs`, meant to force a small 52px-wide score box) was being silently overridden. `cn()` (`src/components/ui/cn.ts`) turned out to be a plain string-join (`values.filter(Boolean).join(' ')`), not a Tailwind-merge — so `Input`'s shared `controlClassName` (`w-full ... px-3 py-2 text-sm`), `ScoreInput`'s own default (`w-22.5 px-2 py-1 text-center text-lg font-bold`), and `compactScoreClass` all land in the rendered `class` attribute simultaneously, and the browser's CSS cascade — driven by each utility's position in Tailwind's *generated stylesheet*, not by the order the classes appear in the HTML — decides the winner, independently per CSS property. Confirmed directly, not assumed: before the fix, the same input's computed `width` resolved to `controlClassName`'s `w-full` (170px, not `compactScoreClass`'s intended 52px) while its computed `font-size`/`border-radius` correctly resolved to `compactScoreClass`'s `text-xs`/`rounded-sm` — three different "winners" from three different layers on the same element. The real width, ~170px inside a ~210px-wide round column, was the actual cause of the reported overflow; the long name made it more visible but wasn't the sole culprit.

**Fixed narrowly, not systemically.** `compactScoreClass` now uses Tailwind v4's `!` important-suffix on every one of its conflicting utilities (`w-13! shrink-0 rounded-sm! px-1.5! py-0.5! text-xs!`), which deterministically wins regardless of `cn()`'s class-order fragility — confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` post-fix: width 52px, padding 6px/2px, font-size 12px, border-radius 4px, all matching `compactScoreClass` exactly. Deliberately did **not** fix `cn()` itself (e.g. by adopting `tailwind-merge`) — that's a cross-cutting change touching every one of `cn()`'s 9 call sites' rendered output across the whole app, unverified without a much broader visual audit, and exactly the kind of "significant change" the organiser's plan-first rule (added the day before) is meant to gate. Flagged as a new "known gaps" bullet in `HANDOFF.md` instead, so a future session doesn't have to rediscover it from scratch if another `cn()`-composed component turns out to have the same silent-override problem.

**Testing**: full suite stays at 417 tests, unchanged — this is a rendering/CSS bug with no domain-logic surface, so no new automated coverage was warranted. `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on the changed file.

**Live-verified** via a local `pnpm dev` run: generated a 4-player `individual-1v1` tournament with "VeryLongUsername" in the roster, confirmed via direct DOM inspection (not just a screenshot) that Round 1's score input for that entry sits at `right: 215px` inside its round column's `right: 234px` border (previously the input alone measured 170px wide, visibly crowding the box), typed a score into it to confirm normal read/write behavior was unaffected, and re-inspected every other room row's input to confirm the fix applies uniformly (it's a single shared constant, not a per-row patch).

---

## Bracket view: merge LB into WB's own box (done — 2026-09-18)
Third attempt at the same underlying problem in as many days. Reviewing "Bracket view: revise WB/LB row layout" (below) live, the organiser found LB still easy to miss — separate box borders visually partition WB and LB into two disconnected regions, even stacked directly above/below each other. The actual ask: LB content should live *inside the same box* as its corresponding WB round, stacked vertically with a small internal divider — a "Winners bracket" sub-heading, WB's rooms, then (only when relevant) a thin divider, a "Losers bracket" sub-heading, and LB's rooms — not a separate row or column at all. Top-level round numbering goes back to one continuous "Round N" sequence, matching how pre-bracket rounds are already labeled, rather than the `bracketRowGroups`-era "WB Round N"/"LB Round N" split identity.

Went through Plan Mode first, per the organiser's own standing rule (added to `HANDOFF.md` the day before). Two genuine open design questions were surfaced and put to the organiser directly rather than decided unilaterally (via `AskUserQuestion`, matching `AGENTS.md`'s "Design forks" guidance): (1) a winners-bracket round with *zero* trailing losers-bracket rounds — a real, guaranteed-to-occur shape for the FFA/team shared-final double-elimination variant, not hypothetical — should its box show an empty "Winners bracket" heading anyway, or omit it? Organiser chose **omit** (nothing to show, since it renders exactly like an ordinary single-round box already). (2) when two LB rounds land in the same box (the head-to-head/race variant's middle-round case), each round's own room lettering restarts at "Room A" independently — should the two stacked blocks get their own small distinguishing label? Organiser chose **yes, add a small sub-label**.

**Root-cause grounding, independently traced through both `double-elimination.ts` variants before designing anything** (a dispatched Plan agent re-verified this from the actual code, not from the prior session's summary): the head-to-head/race variant (`raceDoubleEliminationBracketPhase`) always produces 1 trailing LB round for the first/last WB round and 2 for every WB round in between — confirmed even for the minimum 2-WB-round bracket, where both rounds hit the "first or last" branch, so there's no dead "middle" case, just never a middle round to trigger it. The FFA/team shared-final variant (`sharedFinalDoubleEliminationBracketPhase`) produces 0 or 1 trailing LB rounds per WB round, never 2 — a WB round's drops can be deferred into a *later* WB round's LB absorption via the `pendingFrom`/`pendingDrop` accumulation logic, genuinely leaving some WB rounds with nothing immediately following them. Both shapes are handled by one generic, format-agnostic linear scan with zero branching on which variant produced the data.

**Built**: `bracketRowGroups()`/`BracketRowGroups` (`src/domain/tournament/bracket.ts`) replaced outright (no other consumer existed, confirmed by repo-wide search) with `bracketBoxes()`/`BracketBox`, a discriminated union (`{ kind: 'single'; roundIndex }` | `{ kind: 'wave'; winnersRoundIndex; losersRoundIndices }`) built by a single pass over `state.rounds`: whenever a `bracket === 'winners'` round is seen, start a wave and greedily absorb every immediately-following `bracket === 'losers'` round into it, stopping at the next `'winners'` round or the terminal Final (which is never tagged `'winners'`/`'losers'`, so it's never absorbed — no explicit Final-detection logic needed at all, a simplification over the previous `bracketRowGroups`, which had to special-case finding it). Degenerates to "every round is its own `single` box, original order" by construction whenever no round is tagged `'winners'` — single-elimination, Kings Valley, and pooling-only are all structurally incapable of producing a `wave` box.

`BracketRounds` (`BracketView.tsx`) goes back to one flowing row (no more `flex-col`, no more spacer columns — that whole mechanism's reason to exist disappeared along with the two-row layout). A `single` box renders exactly as before (unchanged `RoundColumn`+`RoundBody` pairing, `bracketRoundLabels()`-derived label/accent, single `data-ri`). A new `WaveBoxBody` component renders a `wave` box's content: WB's `RoundBody` (with a "Winners bracket" sub-heading only when `losersRoundIndices.length > 0`, per the organiser's first decision), then — when there are LB rounds — a thin `bg-warning` divider (the organiser's own "thin yellow line," reusing the existing amber token already used for LB's accent color elsewhere, no new color introduced), a "Losers bracket" sub-heading, and each LB round's own `RoundBody` stacked in order, each with a small "Round N" micro-label when there's more than one (the organiser's second decision) and its own `data-ri` wrapper (since `chooseFollow`'s existing `document.querySelector('[data-ri]')` lookup needs a valid target for every constituent round of a wave, not just the WB anchor — confirmed this needs zero changes to `chooseFollow` itself). A wave box's own top-level label bypasses `bracketRoundLabels()` entirely, using `` `Round ${rounds[winnersRoundIndex].roundNum}` `` instead — `roundNum` is already a continuously-incrementing field spanning pre-bracket and bracket rounds alike, so this reads as one unbroken sequence with no new counter needed. `current`/`followed` styling and the default collapse state all aggregate across a wave's full constituent-index list (`[winnersRoundIndex, ...losersRoundIndices]`), not just the anchor — the default-collapse check specifically uses the wave's *last* index, not the anchor, since a wave's LB rounds always sit later in the array than its own WB round and judging "is this in the past" by the anchor alone could wrongly collapse a box whose LB content is still current.

**Confirmed out of scope, verified not just assumed**: `bracketRoundLabels()` itself is untouched — it has a second, independent call site inside `projectFutureRoundSlots` (`bracket.ts`) that still actively needs its `"WB Round N"`/`"LB Round N"` output for future-round placeholder text (e.g. "Dropped from WB Round 1") shown elsewhere in Bracket, a completely different feature from box layout.

**Testing**: `describe('bracketBoxes', ...)` replaces the old `describe('bracketRowGroups', ...)` block in `bracket.test.ts` — 8 tests: the race variant's real 3-WB-round shape (exercising the actual 1-LB/2-LB/1-LB pattern), the race variant's minimum 2-WB-round shape (no middle wave, per the trace-through above), a shared-final fixture specifically constructed to produce a genuine empty-`losersRoundIndices` wave (two WB rounds back to back), the shared-final variant's ordinary case, the three degenerate-case formats, and the pooling-prefix-into-double-elim case. 417 tests total (up from 415), full suite green. `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check`/`tsc --noEmit` clean on all three changed files (only the pre-existing, unrelated `BracketView.tsx` nullability error remains, still unfixed, still out of scope).

**Live-verified** against a local `pnpm dev` run, both double-elimination variants, non-round player counts: a 22-player `individual-1v1` race-variant tournament with a 3-round Qualification Table pre-bracket phase and 4 WB rounds (deliberately sized to produce two real middle waves with 2 LB rounds each) — direct page inspection confirmed "WINNERS BRACKET"/amber "LOSERS BRACKET" sub-headings render with the thin divider exactly as designed, the two middle waves show "Round 7"/"Round 8" and "Round 10"/"Round 11" micro-labels distinguishing their stacked LB rounds while the single-LB waves (first and last) correctly show no micro-label, and `#br-rounds` is confirmed a single non-`flex-col` row with exactly one `data-ri` marker present for all 14 original round indices (2 pre-bracket + 4 waves worth of WB+LB indices + 1 Final = 8 boxes, matching `wrapper.children.length`). Collapse-toggling a wave box hides and correctly re-shows its *entire* stacked content (WB + divider + LB) as one unit, confirmed by toggling the real button twice. A 37-player FFA shared-final tournament was also live-generated and inspected directly via its persisted round data — this particular configuration happened to alternate WB/LB 1:1 with no empty-`losersRoundIndices` wave in practice, so the organiser's first design decision (omit the heading) wasn't visually re-confirmed live beyond the dedicated unit test that exercises that exact code path against a hand-built fixture tracing the real generation logic; a single-elimination tournament was separately confirmed to render as a plain unchanged single row with zero wave headings anywhere. Zero new console errors beyond the same pre-existing, unrelated `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` seen in every prior Bracket session.

**Explicitly out of scope, carried forward unchanged**: the organiser's still-open reservation about independent-vs-lockstep scrolling from the very first build is moot now — there's only ever been one row since the second revision, nothing to scroll independently against. The pre-existing `RoundColumn` collapse-width cascade bug (flagged in the previous entry, `roundColumnWidthClass` still in active use) remains unfixed, unrelated to this task. `ArchivedBracket` inherits this redesign by construction (shares `BracketRounds`/`WaveBoxBody`) but wasn't separately live-verified.

**Revert-readiness, confirmed via `git diff --name-only`, not assumed**: all of this session's-and-yesterday's WB/LB layout work — across the original stacking commit and today's two follow-up revisions — touches exactly three files: `src/domain/tournament/bracket.ts`, `src/features/bracket/BracketView.tsx`, `src/domain/tournament/__tests__/bracket.test.ts`. None of these overlap with the 9 files the unrelated non-counting-qualification-rounds feature touches (bundled into the same commit as the second revision). A revert of all Bracket layout work back to before any of it started, done as a surgical restore of just those three files, would not touch or damage the non-counting-rounds feature at all.

## Bracket view: revise WB/LB row layout (done — 2026-09-18)
**Superseded 2026-09-18 — see "Bracket view: merge LB into WB's own box" above.** The two-row layout this entry describes (pre-bracket+WB sharing a row, LB on its own row below with spacer alignment) still made LB easy to miss — the organiser reviewed it live and asked for something structurally different (LB as a section *inside* WB's own box, not a separate row at all). The `roundColumnWidthClass` extraction and the flagged pre-existing collapse-width cascade bug described below are both still accurate and still relevant (the bug is still unfixed as of the newer entry too).

Follow-up correction to "Bracket view: stack WB above LB" (below), reviewed live by the organiser the next day. The prior build's three-row layout (pre-bracket rounds, then WB, then LB, each its own row) wasn't what was actually expected: the pre-bracket rounds (the no-elim warm-up/qualification rounds before the bracket starts) and WB are one continuous timeline, not two structurally distinct rows — WB is simply "what happens next" after pooling. Corrected to **two** rows: pre-bracket + WB (+ the terminal Final, already appended to WB from the prior build) share one row; LB moves up to be the only other row, positioned below, with its first round starting in the same column as WB's first round rather than flush at the far left under the pre-bracket rounds.

Per the organiser's new standing rule (added to `HANDOFF.md` this same session: every new feature or significant change starts with a plan the organiser approves before any code is written), this went through Plan Mode before implementation, even though the prior build's own code and design reasoning (`bracketRowGroups`, the accent/label/collapse machinery) needed no changes — only how `BracketRounds` (`BracketView.tsx`) maps the same three groups (`preBracket`/`winners`/`losers`) to rows.

**Built**: `BracketRounds`'s single `renderRow` helper was split into `renderColumns` (the existing per-round `RoundColumn`/`RoundBody` mapping, now reused by both rows) and a new `renderSpacer` (invisible, `aria-hidden`, non-interactive placeholder `div`s — one per pre-bracket round index, no `data-ri` so they can never interfere with `chooseFollow`'s `[data-ri]` lookup). The top row renders `[...groups.preBracket, ...groups.winners]` together; the bottom row, only when `groups.losers.length > 0`, renders `renderSpacer(groups.preBracket)` followed by the real LB columns — the spacers consume exactly the same total width as the real pre-bracket columns above, so LB's first column lands at the same x-position as WB's first column with no manual pixel math. `bracketRowGroups()` itself is unchanged; only its consumer's row-mapping changed (its doc comment was updated for accuracy). Degenerate case (no `winners`-bracket round at all) still reconstructs a single unchanged row, by construction — confirmed unaffected by this revision as well as the original build.

**A real, pre-existing bug surfaced during live verification, unrelated to this task**: `RoundColumn`'s width className builds `cn('w-52.5 min-w-52.5 shrink-0 ...', collapsed && 'w-8.5 min-w-8.5', ...)` — appending the collapsed-width override rather than replacing the base width. Both classes end up present simultaneously in the DOM, and direct `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement confirmed the browser's CSS cascade resolves `w-52.5` (210px), not `w-8.5` (34px) — meaning **collapsing a round column has never actually shrunk its visual width**, only its header/body content changes (rotated label text, no body). This predates today's session entirely (verified: neither the original WB/LB stacking build nor this revision touched this part of `RoundColumn`) and is out of scope to fix here per the organiser's own scoping principle (isolate unrelated fixes into their own pass) — **flagged, not fixed**. It mattered for *this* task specifically because the new spacer's width computation needed to match the real column's *actual* rendered width (bug included), not the *intended* width, or the two could drift apart under collapse. Resolved by extracting a shared `roundColumnWidthClass(collapsed)` helper used by both `RoundColumn` and `renderSpacer`, so they're now guaranteed to resolve identically by construction regardless of the underlying cascade bug's status, now or if it's ever fixed separately.

**Testing**: no test file changes — `bracketRowGroups()` (the only tested function in this area) didn't change beyond its doc comment; `pnpm exec vitest run` confirms its 20 existing tests still pass. `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check`/`tsc --noEmit` clean on both changed files (only the pre-existing, unrelated `BracketView.tsx` nullability error remains). 415 tests total, unchanged from before this build (a pure presentational revision, no domain-layer changes).

**Live-verified** against a local `pnpm dev` run: a fresh 14-player `individual-1v1` double-elimination-race tournament with a real 3-round Qualification Table pre-bracket phase. Direct DOM inspection confirmed: the top row contains exactly the pre-bracket rounds followed by WB rounds followed by the Grand Final (7 real columns, correct order); the LB row sits strictly below (`getBoundingClientRect().top` compared numerically); WB Round 1's `left` and LB Round 1's `left` are pixel-identical (both `684`, and again both `696` after a page reload with a slightly different pane width — the absolute value isn't what matters, only that the two rows agree within the same render). Collapsing the first pre-bracket round (clicking its real toggle button, not simulated) confirmed alignment holds even with the pre-existing width bug in play — both rows shifted to `696` together, not just before the `roundColumnWidthClass` fix (which, before the fix, produced a real 508-vs-684 misalignment, caught precisely because this live check was done rather than trusting the design in the abstract). Also confirmed a single-elimination tournament (no double-elimination bracket at all) still degenerates to exactly one row (`childCount: 1` at the `#br-rounds` level, only a `data-row="top"` child, no `lb` row) — the pre-existing non-double-elimination guarantee held. Zero new console errors (only the same pre-existing, unrelated `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` seen in every prior session).

**Explicitly out of scope, same as the original build**: `ArchivedBracket` inherits this fix by construction (shares `BracketRounds`) but wasn't separately live-verified. The organiser's still-open reservation about independent-vs-lockstep row scrolling (from the original build) is untouched by this revision. The newly-discovered `RoundColumn` collapse-width cascade bug is flagged above, not fixed — worth its own small pass if the organiser wants collapsed columns to actually shrink visually.

## Non-counting qualification rounds (done — 2026-09-18)
Second item off `ROADMAP.md`'s ordered list. The organiser wanted a way to mark the leading N rounds of a Qualification Table/Swiss pooling phase as played-but-excluded from the cumulative Fair Points standings — useful when those rounds exist only to seed the field more fairly than a random shuffle would, not to actually count toward who advances. Per the organiser's own scope call recorded in `ROADMAP.md`, this is "first N rounds don't count," not an arbitrary round toggled mid-tournament — and scoped to Qualification Table/Swiss specifically, not Group Stage, which already has its own separate per-group standings mechanism (`computeGroupStandings`) and round structure that doesn't naturally decompose into "leading N rounds."

**Confirmed before writing code** (`ROADMAP.md`'s own prior investigation, re-verified directly against the current source, not trusted as-is): `computeQualificationStandings` (`advancement.ts:297-318`) already only aggregates rounds flagged `isQual || isSwiss` (line 302) — the "None" pooling phase's no-elim warm-up rounds already work this way today by simply never carrying either flag. `tieredSeed`'s reseeding (`seeding.ts`) already ranks by each round's own per-room score via `buildAdvancementTiers`, entirely independent of the cumulative table, for every round type uniformly — so "seed fairly without counting" already existed as a signal for any round, counting or not, requiring no changes there.

**Built**: a new `excludeFromStandings?: boolean` flag on `TournamentRound` (`types.ts`), set by `qualificationTablePoolingPhase`/`swissPoolingPhase` (`pooling.ts`) on exactly their own leading `nonCountingRounds` rounds (`index < format.nonCountingRounds`) — mirroring the existing `pairingTBD`/`index > 0` conditional-spread pattern already used in `swissPoolingPhase` right next to it. One new guard clause in `computeQualificationStandings` (`advancement.ts:302`: `if (!(round.isQual || round.isSwiss) || round.excludeFromStandings) continue;`) — `computeGroupStandings` deliberately untouched, matching the Group Stage scoping decision above. A new Setup field, "Non-counting rounds (optional)," appears only alongside "Advance to bracket" (i.e. only for Qualification Table/Swiss, never Group Stage or "None") — its `max` attribute is computed live from `QUALIFICATION_ROUNDS`/`computeSwissRoundCount(confirmedCount)` so the input hints at its own valid range before the organiser even submits. `generation.ts` validates the raw input explicitly (non-negative whole number, and strictly less than the phase's own total round count so at least one round always counts — a qual-table/Swiss phase with zero counting rounds would leave `computeQualificationStandings` returning an empty average for everyone and the cutoff mechanism with nothing meaningful to cut), with the same "positive-value survives an empty-string `|| fallback`, but a value that changes semantics needs its own explicit check" carefulness the existing `qualAdv`/`semisOverride` validations already use elsewhere in this file.

**Testing**: `pooling.test.ts` — 2 new tests confirming `qualificationTablePoolingPhase`/`swissPoolingPhase` each flag exactly their own leading `nonCountingRounds` count and leave every other round untouched (still `isQual`/`isSwiss`, still `isNoElim` — an excluded round is structurally identical to a counted one in every other respect). `advancement.test.ts` — 1 new test proving the exclusion actually changes ranking outcomes, not just a `played` count: a unit that finishes last in an excluded round but first in the one counted round ranks *above* a unit with the opposite record, which would be impossible if the excluded round's score leaked into the average. `generation.test.ts` — 8 new tests covering the full validation surface (correct flagging end-to-end through `generateTournament` for both qual-table and Swiss, the empty-input-defaults-to-0 case, negative-value rejection, the "would leave zero counting rounds" rejection for both phases with their actual round-count math, and confirming the validation is a no-op for Group Stage and "None"). 415 tests total (up from 404), full suite green. `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check`/`tsc --noEmit` clean on every changed file (only the pre-existing, unrelated `BracketView.tsx` error remains).

**Live-verified** end-to-end against a local `pnpm dev` run: a fresh 6-player `individual-1v1` Qualification Table tournament (non-round count, per "One important principle") with Round 1 set non-counting. Deliberately made Alpha lose badly in Round 1 (excluded) and win decisively in Rounds 2-3 (counted) — the live Scoreboard mid-tournament showed `Played: 1` for every unit after Round 2 alone (not 2, proving Round 1 never entered the count), with Alpha correctly ranked #1 despite the Round 1 loss; after Round 3, `Played: 2` for everyone and the table correctly gained its "(Final)" suffix, with the cutoff transition into the bracket phase completing cleanly with zero console errors. Confirmed via direct state inspection that only Round 1 (not Rounds 2-3) carried `excludeFromStandings: true` on the generated schedule.

**Explicitly out of scope, per the organiser's own scope call**: Group Stage support (a real follow-on if ever requested, but its round structure and separate standings mechanism don't share this feature's "leading N rounds" shape); marking an arbitrary round mid-tournament as non-counting (explicitly rejected in favor of "first N" — see `ROADMAP.md`); any interaction with the qual-table/Swiss reserve-admission guard (`addReserveUnit`'s "at most one qualifying round completed" check, `mutations.ts`) — that guard still counts *all* rounds played including non-counting ones, so a reserve added right after a long run of non-counting rounds could still end up with fewer than the intended two *counted* rounds before admission closes; not touched here, flagged as a known edge case worth revisiting if it ever bites in practice, not fixed speculatively.
