## Bracket view: stack WB above LB (done — 2026-09-17)
**Layout revised twice since, 2026-09-18 — see "Bracket view: merge LB into WB's own box" and "Bracket view: revise WB/LB row layout" above.** The three-row layout this entry describes (pre-bracket, WB, LB each their own row) was not what the organiser actually wanted on review — first corrected to two rows (pre-bracket+WB merged, LB below), then corrected again to LB living *inside* WB's own box rather than any separate row at all. `bracketRowGroups()` itself no longer exists — replaced by `bracketBoxes()` in the second revision. The follow-scroll fix and the two design-fork decisions below are still accurate history, just no longer reflecting the current row/box structure.

First item off `ROADMAP.md`'s ordered list of future projects. The organiser found Bracket's single horizontal row — which interleaves a double-elimination tournament's winners-bracket (WB) and losers-bracket (LB) rounds in raw array order (`wb1, lb1, wb2, lb2, ..., wbLast, final`) — hard to navigate, and asked for WB stacked above LB as two separate rows instead. Scoped strictly to when a genuine double-elimination bracket is present; every other format (single-elimination, Kings Valley, pooling-only) had to keep rendering exactly as before.

Two design forks were surfaced and settled with the organiser before writing any code (via `AskUserQuestion`, matching `AGENTS.md`'s "Design forks" guidance): where the terminal Final/Grand-Final round renders (fed by both brackets, doesn't obviously belong to either) — chosen: appended to the end of the WB row, not its own row, not the LB row; and whether WB/LB rows scroll independently or in lockstep — chosen: independent, "with reservations," explicitly flagged by the organiser for a live human check before considering it settled.

**Root-cause grounding, all independently verified by reading the code (not assumed from any prior summary)**: `state.rounds` is always `[...poolingRounds, ...bracketPhaseRounds]`, a plain concatenation (`schedule-generation.ts:86`). `round.bracket` (`'winners' | 'losers' | 'grand-final' | undefined`) is set only by `double-elimination.ts` — confirmed by grep that `single-elimination.ts`/`kings-valley.ts` never set it. Both double-elimination variants push their terminal round *last* (`sequence.push({ type: 'gf' })` at `double-elimination.ts:136`, `sequence.push({ type: 'final' })` at line 300, both immediately before `.map()`, both computing their position as `sequence.length - 1`) — confirmed directly by reading the loop bodies, not just the final push call. The shared-final variant's terminal round carries `isFinal: true` but **no** `bracket` field at all (unlike the race variant's `bracket: 'grand-final'`), so identifying it needs `isFinal` + last-index, not `bracket === 'grand-final'` alone.

**Built**: a new pure `bracketRowGroups()` (`bracket.ts`), partitioning a tournament's rounds into `{ preBracket, winners, losers }` index arrays — `winners` includes the terminal Final/Grand-Final index appended last, per the organiser's placement choice. Degenerates to `{ preBracket: every index in original order, winners: [], losers: [] }` whenever there's no `winners`-bracket round anywhere, which — combined with `BracketView`'s new row-rendering skipping any empty group — reconstructs the exact original single-row rendering for every non-double-elimination format, by construction rather than by a format-specific special case (matching the "avoid structure-specific special-casing" principle). `BracketView.tsx`'s single inline round-map was extracted into a new shared `BracketRounds` component (used by both the live, editable `BracketView` and the read-only `ArchivedBracket`, via optional props — `RoundColumn`'s `onToggle` prop was already optional, confirmed directly, so `ArchivedBracket`'s no-collapse case needed no new branching), rendering one row per non-empty group in a `flex flex-col` wrapper. Rendering identity (`roundIndex`, `data-ri`, `collapse[roundIndex]`, `follow?.rounds[roundIndex]`) is completely untouched — only which physical row container a given index renders into changed. `bracketRoundLabels(state)` is still called exactly once over the full, unmodified `state.rounds` array (its `winnersRound`/`losersRound` running counters would misnumber if ever re-run over a filtered subset) — verified this stayed true after the refactor, not just assumed.

`chooseFollow`'s hand-rolled scroll-centering math (`scroll.current.querySelector(...)` + manual `scrollTo({ left: ... })`, which only ever worked for a single scrollable row) was replaced with native `column.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'smooth', block: 'nearest', inline: 'center' })`, found via `document.querySelector('#br-rounds [data-ri="..."]')` — this walks up through every scrollable ancestor automatically (each row's own horizontal scroll, and the page's vertical scroll to bring a lower row into view), replacing bespoke math with browser-native behavior that generalizes to any number of stacked rows. The `scroll` ref and its `useRef` import became entirely unused as a result and were removed (confirmed via grep no other consumer existed).

**Testing**: new `describe('bracketRowGroups', ...)` block in `bracket.test.ts` — 6 tests: double-elimination race (WB/LB alternating + trailing `grand-final`), double-elimination-shared-final (the untagged `isFinal`-no-`bracket` terminal round, the case specifically needing the `isFinal` + last-index branch), and three degenerate-case tests (single-elimination, Kings Valley, pooling-only — each asserting the exact original index order is reconstructed), plus a pooling-prefix-into-double-elim fixture confirming `preBracket` captures only the leading pooling indices. 404 tests total (up from 398), full suite green. `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on all three changed files; `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` shows only the pre-existing, unrelated `BracketView.tsx` error (a pre-existing `roomLetter(follow.room)` nullability gap, present before this session and confirmed unrelated by grep — not introduced by this change).

**Live-verified** against a local `pnpm dev` run, both double-elimination variants (a 14-player `individual-1v1` race-variant tournament with a Bye odd-count strategy, and a 34-player FFA shared-final tournament — both intentionally non-round player counts per "One important principle"): confirmed via direct DOM inspection (not just visual screenshot, since the Browser pane's screenshot capture was unreliable this session) that WB rows render strictly above LB rows in both variants (`getBoundingClientRect().top` compared numerically), that the terminal round is correctly appended to the WB row with each variant's own correct label ("🏆 Grand Final" for race, "🏆 Final" for shared-final, confirming the `isFinal`-no-`bracket` detection path), and that non-double-elimination formats are covered by the degenerate-case unit tests rather than re-verified live (already exhaustively proven by construction). The follow-scroll fix specifically — the piece flagged by the organiser for a human check — was exercised three ways: a genuine end-to-end test through the real UI (typing a name still in the pre-bracket row, confirming the page scrolled correctly and the "Following X — Round 1 · Room A" banner + highlighted column appeared, screenshot-confirmed), plus two direct `scrollIntoView` calls against a WB-row middle column and an LB-row last column (simulating what `chooseFollow` does, since no scores were entered yet to naturally place a followed unit inside the bracket phase) — both resolved sensibly, including the LB-row last-column case correctly hitting the row's true scroll boundary (can't center something with nothing to its right) and the WB-row case landing within half a pixel of the viewport edge. Zero console errors beyond one pre-existing, unrelated `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` (confirmed present regardless of this change). **The organiser's own reservation about independent-row scrolling is not considered resolved by this automated check alone** — a live human pass through the real UI is still recommended before treating it as settled.

Before testing, discovered this session's browser had the real live tournament `1789648099343`'s data cached in `localStorage` from the previous session's seeding-fix verification — confirmed via direct inspection (`tournamentId` matched exactly) before touching anything, and cleared local storage only (never touched the "Reset" button, which would have pushed a reset to the real synced Firebase tournament) to get a clean slate for creating fresh test tournaments instead.

**Explicitly out of scope**: Archive tab's `ArchivedBracket` was updated to share the same `BracketRounds` component and inherits identical behavior by construction, but wasn't separately live-verified (would have required archiving a completed double-elimination tournament first) — the shared-component argument was judged sufficient given `onToggle`'s optionality was independently confirmed in code.

## Fix: future-round Bracket placeholders clustered by rank instead of spreading (done — 2026-09-17)
The organiser reported, from a real live tournament (`1789648099343` on `tournaments-test.curvefever.pro`), that Bracket's Round 3 Room A placeholder showed only Rank 1 and Rank 2 finishers from every source room — never Rank 3 through 8 — making it look like Room A would only ever hold the tournament's best players. Verified live before touching any code: Round 3 Room A read `Room 2A, Rank 1 / Room 2B, Rank 1 / Room 2C, Rank 1 / Room 2D, Rank 1 / Room 2A, Rank 2 / Room 2B, Rank 2 / Room 2C, Rank 2 / Room 2D, Rank 2` — nothing past Rank 2.

**Root cause**: `projectFutureRoundSlots` (`src/domain/tournament/bracket.ts`) builds a flat "tier-major" pool of advancing candidates — every source room's Rank 1, then every source room's Rank 2, and so on — a design from the 2026-09-15 fix ("Fix: future-round placeholders show same-room carryover instead of the real diversified path," below) that solved same-room carryover. That pool was then handed to `sequentialSeed` (`seeding.ts`), a dumb sequential chop: target Room A got `pool[0..size-1]`, Room B the next chunk, and so on. Whenever a target room's size is large relative to the source-room count (this tournament: 4 source rooms, target room size 8), the first target room swallows two entire rank-tiers before the chunk boundary ever reaches Rank 3 — a case the 2026-09-15 fix's own test coverage (a single target room, unable to expose any cross-room chunking behavior) never exercised.

**Scope confirmed before fixing**: purely a display bug. The real room assignment once a round is actually played goes through `tieredSeed`/`tieredBracketSeed` (`seeding.ts`), an unrelated, genuinely correct, balance-aware mechanism — no tournament result was ever affected, only the predictive placeholder shown ahead of time.

**Fix**: added `distributeTierMajorPool` (`bracket.ts`), replacing the `sequentialSeed` call at this one call site (`sequentialSeed` itself is untouched — still correctly used by Kings Valley's real room-ladder movement in `transitions.ts`). Walks the existing tier-major pool in order, round-robin-assigning each tier's own entries across target rooms that still have remaining capacity, and rotates the round-robin's starting room by one every time the tier changes (not per entry) — so every target room receives one entry from (almost) every tier its size allows (a real spread of ranks) while the rotation keeps source-room provenance mixed too, instead of a room correlating with the same source room tier after tier. Collapses to today's exact behavior when there's only one target room (most existing test cases), since there's nothing to rotate against. Both pool-building branches (no-elim, ordinary cutting-round) are unchanged — only the final chunking step changed.

**Testing**: two existing `bracket.test.ts` cases (the two multi-room no-elim projections) had their expected arrays updated — hand-verified against the new algorithm by tracing it tier-by-tier before writing the new expectations, not just accepting whatever the code produced. Added two new regression tests: one reproducing the live tournament's exact shape (4 source rooms of size 8/7/7/7 feeding a same-shaped target round, no-elim branch) asserting Room A now spans more than 2 distinct ranks (max rank ≥ 5) and more than one source room, not exact-array equality; one covering the ordinary cutting-round branch with uneven target room sizes ([4, 3, 2] from 3 source rooms of size 6). 398 domain tests (up from 396), 2 new, full suite green. `pnpm exec eslint`/`prettier --check` on both changed files clean. **Live-verified**: reloaded a local `pnpm dev` against the real tournament `1789648099343`'s live Firebase data (the deployed test site itself still runs the old code until this is pushed) — Round 3 Room A now shows the full Rank 1–8 spread across a mix of source rooms 2A/2B/2C/2D, and Rooms B/C/D plus Rounds 4 and 5 show the same genuine mixing with no new homogeneous blocks; every room's slot count still exactly matches its declared size.

**Out of scope, explicit**: deploying this fix (push to `test`/`main` needs separate authorization per the standing branching rule); attempting to make the projection predict `tieredSeed`'s exact eventual output — that tradeoff (the projection is an honest approximation, not a prediction) is unchanged, only the approximation's quality improved.

---

## Extend Playwright e2e to run against the real test site (done — 2026-09-17)
Closed the remaining half of `HANDOFF.md`'s design-question #5, same day as the local-only version below — the organiser's own stated plan was "local first, extend to the real test site once that's proven," and the local step passed cleanly with no issues, so this followed immediately.

`playwright.config.ts` now derives its target from an optional `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL` env var, defaulting to `http://localhost:3000`; the `webServer` block (which auto-starts `pnpm dev`) is conditional on the resolved hostname actually being `localhost` — pointed at a real deployed URL, there's nothing local to spin up, and trying to would be wrong anyway. No changes needed to `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` itself: the same three tests (shell renders, tab switching, login wall exists) are equally valid against any real deployment, local or not, since they only ever exercise the public, unauthenticated read paths.

Ran `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL=https://tournaments-test.curvefever.pro pnpm run test:e2e` — **all 3 tests passed on the first try**, no Cloudflare/bot-protection challenge blocking Playwright's browser, no flakiness, no config surprises. This had been a real open question going in (the production site's own network log shows a `cdn-cgi/challenge-platform` script, confirming Cloudflare is in front of it) — worth stating plainly that it just worked, since that wasn't guaranteed.

**Testing**: the run above, plus a full re-run of the local-target path (`pnpm run test:e2e` with no env var) to confirm the conditional `webServer` logic didn't regress the default case — still 3/3 passing. `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`prettier --check` on `playwright.config.ts` — clean, only the pre-existing unrelated `BracketView.tsx` error present. `pnpm vitest run` — 396 tests, unaffected.

**Out of scope, explicit**: this only proves *read-only, unauthenticated* pages work against the real site — it says nothing about whether authenticated admin actions would work there, since that's still item #7's unrelated, unresolved question (real CFP test credentials vs. a Firebase emulator). No CI wiring to actually run this automatically against the test site on a schedule or on push — that's a separate decision if the organiser wants it.

## Playwright e2e — first test, running locally only (done — 2026-09-17)
Closed the last parked design question from Stage 1 test-coverage work (`HANDOFF.md`'s "Deliberately parked design questions" #5), following the organiser's own recommendation: start with a Playwright test running against a local copy of the app, extend to the real test site only once this step proves solid — that extension is explicitly not done here.

Research surfaced a real complication before any code was written: this app's admin login is real per-CFP-account auth, re-validated server-side against the actual CFP API on every request (`getInitialAuth`/`getCachedAuthSnapshot`), with no dev-mode bypass — and that server-side re-validation happens in the Node process running the dev server, which Playwright's `page.route()` interception can't reach (it only intercepts requests the browser page itself makes). A Firebase emulator was considered and rejected as real scope creep for a first test: `firebase-client.ts` hardcodes the real production Firebase project with no emulator switch, `firebase.json` has no `emulators` block, and `firebase-tools` isn't even a dependency yet. Rather than solve authenticated-admin-testing as a side effect of "does Playwright work at all," scoped test #1 to what's provably true without it: `database.rules.json` makes tournament reads fully public, and the UI only gates the `admin` tab specifically (`AppShell.tsx`'s `chooseTab()`) — every other tab, and the base shell itself, renders a complete real screen for an anonymous visitor using only `localStorage`/defaults, no Firebase round-trip needed for first paint.

**Built**: `playwright.config.ts` (chromium only for now; `webServer` auto-starts `pnpm dev` on port 3000, matching `.claude/launch.json`'s existing `tour-hub-dev` config, or reuses one already running) and `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`, three tests — renders the app shell for an anonymous visitor (header, all 5 nav tabs, Bracket active by default); switching between viewer tabs (Scoreboard/Rankings/Archive/Bracket, each section visible/hidden correctly); clicking Admin opens the `CfpLoginModal` (verified via `getByRole('dialog', { name: 'Sign in with CFP' })` — the `Modal` component's existing `aria-labelledby`/`role="dialog"` wiring — with `Email`/`Password` fields visible via real `<label htmlFor>` associations) and Cancel closes it without ever reaching an authenticated state. All three share a `pageerror`/console-error collector that fails the test on any console error, matching this codebase's own "zero console errors" bar from its manual verification passes. Added `"test:e2e": "playwright test"` to `package.json`.

**Two real gotchas found and fixed during implementation**, both genuine, not obvious in advance:
1. **Hydration timing.** The first version of the tab-switching and login-modal tests failed — clicks landed on real, visible buttons but nothing happened. `AppShell.tsx` already exposes a `data-hydrated` attribute on `<main>` for exactly this reason; the app's SSR markup renders before React attaches its click handlers, so a click that lands before hydration completes is a silent no-op. Fixed with a small `gotoHydrated()` helper that waits for `main[data-hydrated="true"]` before any interaction.
2. **Vitest/Playwright file-glob collision.** `pnpm vitest run` started failing once `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` existed — Vitest's own default test-file glob also matches `*.spec.ts`, so it tried to execute the Playwright spec directly (`test.describe() called here` error, since Playwright's own `test` object refuses to run outside its runner). Fixed by adding a `test.exclude` block to `vite.config.ts` (`[...configDefaults.exclude, 'e2e/**']`). This surfaced its own smaller issue: typing that block hit a genuine version mismatch — vitest 3.2.4 vendors its own older `vite` typings as a separate pnpm-nested install from this project's `vite@8.0.8`, so `defineConfig` from `'vite'` doesn't structurally recognize the `test` key (tried the documented `/// <reference types="vitest/config" />` workaround first; it didn't resolve the augmentation against the *project's* `vite` module identity specifically, for the same nested-dependency reason). Resolved with a narrowly-scoped, clearly-commented `@ts-expect-error` on that one property — a real tooling-version gap, not an app bug, confirmed the value is still read correctly at runtime by both `vitest` and `playwright` regardless.

**Also found and fixed**: `e2e/**/*.ts` wasn't covered by either `eslint.config.mjs`'s typed-lint `files` glob or `tsconfig.eslint.json`'s `include` (TS `include` replaces rather than merges with the base config's on `extends`, so the main `tsconfig.json`'s `"e2e"` entry didn't carry over) — meant `eslint e2e/smoke.spec.ts` would have silently skipped linting it entirely rather than actually checking it. Added `'e2e/**/*.ts'` to both.

**Testing**: `pnpm exec playwright install chromium` (confirmed with the organiser first, since it's a real ~90MB download — landed in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ms-playwright\`), then `pnpm run test:e2e` — all 3 tests pass. `pnpm vitest run` — 396 tests, no regressions, and confirmed `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` is now correctly excluded from Vitest's own run (21 test files, down from the erroring 22). `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` — only the pre-existing, unrelated `BracketView.tsx` error remains. Lint/Prettier clean on every new/changed file, including a whole-repo `pnpm run lint` pass to confirm the `e2e/` wiring works end-to-end, not just on the files touched directly.

**Explicitly out of scope**: extending this to run against the real test site (the organiser's own stated next step, once this lands cleanly — a separate decision, not assumed here); testing any authenticated admin action at all (new parked design question, `HANDOFF.md` #7 — blocked on deciding real CFP test credentials vs. a Firebase emulator, not solved as a side effect of this test); Firefox/WebKit projects, CI wiring, visual/screenshot testing — no current need.

## Resolve parked design question: `auth-session-cache.server.ts` testability seam (done — 2026-09-17)
Closed one of the two design questions parked at the end of the previous Stage 1 test-coverage entry (`HANDOFF.md`'s "Deliberately parked design questions" #6). Explained the tradeoff to the organiser in plain terms (a "reset" function touching production code vs. tests reaching into `globalThis` directly) and they chose the seam, matching the recommendation: it mirrors the injected-`BrowserStorage` pattern already used in `lib/persistence/storage.ts`/`archive.ts`, and avoids coupling any future test to an internal implementation detail that could silently break if the file is ever restructured.

Added a new exported `resetAuthSessionCache()` in `auth-session-cache.server.ts` — clears the module-level cache Map via the existing private `getCache()` helper, with a comment explaining it's test-only (the cache otherwise lives for the whole server process's lifetime, which would leak entries between test runs). No behavior change to the real `getCachedAuthSnapshot()` path.

**Testing**: `pnpm vitest run` — 396 tests, no regressions (this file has no direct tests yet — that's Stage 2 work, not yet planned). Lint/Prettier clean; `tsc --noEmit` shows only the pre-existing, unrelated `BracketView.tsx` error (confirmed via `git stash` against a clean commit in the previous entry). No live/browser verification — additive-only server-side export, not reachable from any UI path.

**Explicitly out of scope**: writing the actual direct tests for `auth-session-cache.server.ts` that this seam enables — that's part of Stage 2 (server functions), still only sketched, not planned in detail.

## Test coverage outside the domain layer — Stage 1 (pure/near-pure logic) (done — 2026-09-17)
Followed up on `AGENTS.md`'s "Testing" section, which flagged "everything outside the domain layer has zero coverage" — item #21 in `HANDOFF.md`'s deferred list. Two research agents found the real starting state was better than that: `@testing-library/react`, `@testing-library/jest-dom`, `jsdom`, and `@playwright/test` were all already installed as devDependencies but completely unconfigured (`vite.config.ts` has no `test` block at all — Vitest runs on plain node-environment defaults; no `playwright.config.ts`; `tsconfig.json`'s `include` already lists an `e2e/` directory that doesn't exist), and `auth.shared.ts` already had a companion `auth.shared.test.ts` with 2 tests (out of ~8 pure functions in that file), split out of the auth feature originally for client/server code-sharing, not for testability per se.

The rest of the non-domain code splits into three genuinely different testability tiers, which is why this was staged rather than done as one pass: pure/near-pure logic (testable today, zero new config, zero mocking beyond trivial injected fakes) is **Stage 1**, covered here in full. Server functions (`*.server-fns.ts`/`*.server.ts`, need `getRequest()`/Firebase Admin/`fetch` mocked) are Stage 2, and React components (need a new jsdom Vitest environment + a render-with-providers helper, neither of which exist yet) are Stage 3 — both sketched in the plan but not yet designed in detail, no code written for either.

Two scope questions came up while planning and were explicitly parked by the organiser rather than decided now (added to `HANDOFF.md`'s "Deliberately parked design questions" #5/#6): whether Playwright/e2e is in scope at all (real infra decisions — test target? a Firebase emulator? — not confident enough to call without the organiser), and whether `auth-session-cache.server.ts`'s module-level `globalThis` cache (a real test-isolation gotcha for TTL-cached state) needs a small reset seam or should be worked around from the test side. Neither blocks Stage 1; the second doesn't even block Stage 2's testing of that file's *callers*, since those can mock the cache module's boundary instead of touching its internals.

**Stage 1 scope, five sub-items done in sequence, each checked in on before the next:**
1. `live-sync.ts` → new `live-sync.test.ts` (26 tests): the pure `marshalNullsForFirebase`/`unmarshalNullsFromFirebase`/`mergeRemoteWriterState` exports, plus `SyncCoordinator` using `vi.useFakeTimers()` + `vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync()` (the sync `advanceTimersByTime` doesn't flush `push()`'s internal `await` — a real gotcha caught while writing these) — debounce timing, coalescing multiple rapid updates into one write timed from the *last* call, the in-flight-push guard (a deferred-promise fake `write` proving a second scheduled push coalesces rather than firing concurrently), and a genuine race worth pinning as intentional: a key edited again while its earlier push is still in flight stays dirty afterward, proven by confirming a stale remote echo of the old value doesn't silently overwrite the newer local one.
2. `lib/persistence/live-state.ts` → new `live-state.test.ts` (17 tests): default-merging, team-format member-array padding, the legacy `qual === 'yes'` → `qual-table` migration fallback chain, and `parseLiveEnvelope`'s two distinct failure modes (a malformed-JSON `SyntaxError` propagates uncaught here; a valid-but-non-object top level throws a documented `TypeError`).
3. New shared `lib/persistence/test-fixtures.ts` (`createMemoryStorage`, a plain in-memory `BrowserStorage` fake), then `storage.ts` → `storage.test.ts` (11 tests) and `archive.ts` → `archive.test.ts` (39 tests, the largest file — `runArchiveImport`'s collision-mode logic is the centerpiece: `skip`/`new`/`overwrite` all covered, including two same-original-id collisions in one batch each minting a distinct new id since `mintId` is called against a live-mutating index; a mid-batch `saveArchiveEntry` failure stops the loop immediately rather than just being counted, confirmed by checking later entries were never attempted; a failure saving the final index is silently swallowed, matching the existing "mirrors legacy best effort" comment).
4. `lib/api.ts` → new `api.test.ts` (9 tests): `getApiBaseURL`'s env-var-vs-hostname fallback chain (had to explicitly `vi.stubEnv('VITE_API_ENDPOINT', '')` in several tests, since the repo's real `.env` sets a real value that Vitest loads by default) and a minimal `postJSON` happy/error pair.
5. Extended the existing `auth.shared.test.ts` (22 new tests, 24 total): `extractAccountRoles` exercised through its own sole caller chain (same "test through the caller" precedent as the domain layer's unexported `circleMethodSchedule`), including a real asymmetry worth pinning — a decimal-looking string like `"8.5"` truncates to `8` via `Number.parseInt`, while a real number `8.5` is rejected via `Number.isInteger` — plus `getUsernameFromPayload`, `getUserIdFromPayload`'s precedence chain, `getAnonymousAuthSnapshot`, and `formatAccountRole` (including role `0`/ADMIN's classic falsy-value trap, which the existing `if (!label)` check actually handles correctly since it checks the label string, not the numeric id).

**One production-code change, done last after an explicit go-ahead:** `validateWriteInput` in `tournament-write.server-fns.ts` couldn't be tested through its caller the way item 5's `circleMethodSchedule`-style precedent worked, because its only caller lives in the same module as top-level imports of `requireTourAdminPermission` and `writeTournamentToFirebase` — which pull in the Firebase Admin SDK and `@tanstack/react-start/server`. Importing *anything* from that module at all (ES modules don't tree-shake at import time) would drag that whole graph into a plain-node Vitest run. Extracted `validateWriteInput` (+ its `TournamentWriteInput` type) into a new co-located `tournament-write.shared.ts`, mirroring `auth.shared.ts`'s own naming/purpose — `tournament-write.server-fns.ts` now just imports it and passes it to `.inputValidator()`, a one-function, behavior-preserving move. New `tournament-write.shared.test.ts` (6 tests): valid-input passthrough with `adminProof` stripped, whitespace trimming, empty/overlong/disallowed-character `tournamentId` rejection, and non-object payload/input rejection.

**Testing**: every file mutation-tested per the domain layer's own established discipline (break 1-3 representative lines, confirm the intended test — and only that test — fails, revert, confirm `git diff --stat` clean) before moving to the next file; every mutation attempted was caught cleanly by exactly the intended test. `pnpm vitest run` — **396 tests total** (up from 264), 132 new, zero regressions. Confirmed the extraction is genuinely behavior-preserving: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` shows one pre-existing, unrelated error in `BracketView.tsx` (confirmed via `git stash` to exist identically on a clean `9552ef4`, untouched by this work); lint/Prettier clean on every new/changed file. No live/browser verification — Stage 1 is pure-logic unit tests only, no UI or server surface changed.

**Explicitly out of scope**: Stage 2 (server functions) and Stage 3 (React components) — sketched in the plan file for future reference, not designed in detail, no code written. Playwright/e2e and the `auth-session-cache.server.ts` testability seam — both explicitly parked, see `HANDOFF.md`'s "Deliberately parked design questions" #5/#6.

## Fix: group-stage's pre-generated future rounds aren't updated on removal/swap (done — 2026-09-17)
Closed the last open item from the DNF/no-show work (`project_scoreboard_audit_followups.md`, item 7). Group Stage's entire round-robin schedule is built once, upfront, at generation time (`groupStagePoolingPhase`/`buildRoundRobinRounds`/`circleMethodSchedule`, `pooling.ts`) and baked into every round's `matches`/`groupByes`; `removeRosterUnit`/`swapIndividual`/`swapTeam` (`mutations.ts`) never touched `state.rounds` at all, only `state.assignments[state.curRound]` and `state.groups[].members`. So a withdrawn unit's old name sat untouched in every future group-stage round, and the moment `curRound` actually advanced into that round, `seedFromGroupStageRound` flattened the stale `matches`/`groupByes` straight into a real, live room assignment — a ghost.

Investigated with two parallel research agents before touching any code, which upgraded this from "cosmetic ghost" to a **confirmed real correctness bug**: the ghost occupies a sort position in `orderRoomByScore`/`scoreRoom` before `computeGroupStandings` drops its own line (seeded from *current* `state.groups[].members`), but dropping the ghost's own line doesn't undo the rank shift it caused for the real unit(s) sharing that room. For team formats this happens automatically with zero admin action (a missing team's score silently resolves to `0`, never `null`, via `getUnitScoreForGame`, so it's never excluded as incomplete); for individual formats it only corrupts things if an admin unknowingly types a score into the indistinguishable-looking still-live ghost input.

The reason this was originally parked as "a bigger question than a contained bugfix": Group Stage's own round *count* is a live function of group size (`circleMethodSchedule`'s `numRounds = positionCount - 1`, with a parity discontinuity — size 4 → 3 rounds, size 5 → 5 rounds), and that count is baked into every downstream bracket round's absolute index (`winnersTo`/`losersTo`). A full schedule regeneration would need to re-derive/shift every bracket round's indices and re-run generation-time bracket-shape validations — real risk. Presented this trade-off to the organiser directly (bye-conversion vs. full regeneration) rather than picking silently; **bye-conversion** was chosen: on removal, a future match involving the removed unit becomes a bye for its surviving opponent (the exact same fallback `buildRoundRobinRounds` already uses when a group's own schedule runs out early); on swap, simpler still — just relabel old→new wherever it appears, since group size is unchanged. Neither touches round count, so bracket-round indices are never at risk. The residual cost (a removed unit's would-be opponents lose that specific matchup rather than getting a freshly rebalanced one) is no longer a standings problem now that Fair Points is averaged, not summed (this session's earlier fix).

New private helper `patchFutureGroupStageRounds` (`mutations.ts`), called eagerly from all three mutation functions (mirroring `addReserveUnit`'s existing `rebuildFutureRounds` eager-call pattern), patches `matches`/`groupByes`/`roomGroups`/`rooms`/`byeCount`/`players` for every round strictly after `curRound`, a no-op outside group-stage pooling. Confirmed via grep audit that every consumer of these fields (`computeGroupStandings`, `BracketView.tsx`'s both the not-yet-seeded preview and the seeded round body) derives room numbers/group labels from array position at read time, never anything baked in separately — so filtering `matches`/`roomGroups` in lockstep keeps everything aligned, and fixing the underlying data automatically fixed Bracket's future-round *preview* too, with zero UI-layer changes.

**Testing**: 264 domain tests (up from 257), 7 new — removal converts a future match to a bye with rooms/byeCount/players recomputed and roomGroups shifted correctly; removal drops the unit from an existing future groupByes entry; swap relabels old→new in both matches and groupByes for both `swapIndividual`/`swapTeam` with zero room/bye/player-count change; past/current rounds are never touched; non-group-stage phases are completely unaffected; two sequential removals compose correctly. Lint/Prettier clean. **Live-verified** on local dev with both an individual (`individual-1v1`, 5 players, one group) and a team (`team-3v3`, 5 teams) group-stage tournament: removed a unit scheduled for a future round, confirmed the Bracket preview immediately showed the correct bye-conversion for every affected future round (not just the next one) with zero ghosts and correct room/bye counts; repeated for a swap, confirming clean old→new relabeling everywhere. All live results matched hand-derived predictions exactly, including the team-format case (the more dangerous silent-0-score path) showing no auto-resolving ghost anywhere.

**Explicitly out of scope**: full schedule regeneration (a freshly rebalanced round-robin for the shrunk group) — rejected in favor of bye-conversion per the organiser's explicit choice; revisit only if the lost-matchup trade-off turns out to matter in practice. `addReserveUnit`'s existing group-stage block is unrelated and untouched.

## Fair Points: sum → average, plus a reserve-admission guard for qualification-table/Swiss (done — 2026-09-17)
Followed up on the last open item from the 2026-09-16 Scoreboard follow-up audit (`project_scoreboard_audit_followups.md`): cumulative Fair Points was a raw sum of each played round's `fairPoints(rank, score)`, so a unit with fewer counted rounds (a bye, or a reserve joining late) got an unearned edge in the ascending sort, independent of how well they actually performed. Worked through two hand-computed simulations with the organiser first (a Swiss bye case, and a qualification-cutoff case) before touching any code — both confirmed the effect was real, and the cutoff case showed it could hand the one qualifying spot to a one-round reserve over a player with a far stronger multi-round record.

**Fix 1 — averaging.** `materializeStandings` (`advancement.ts`, the single shared reducer behind both `computeQualificationStandings` and `computeGroupStandings`) now divides by `entry.rounds.length` instead of returning the raw sum — a one-line change that fixes qual-table, Swiss, *and* Group Stage identically, since all three funnel through the same function (confirmed via a research agent: no phase-specific branching exists anywhere in this path). `totalFP`'s field name was deliberately kept as-is (not renamed to something like `avgFP`) despite now holding an average — renaming would ripple through every consumer and, since `qualTable`/`groupStandings` are part of the persisted `TournamentState` shape, risk a blank standings table for any in-flight tournament until its next round recomputes the field; a one-line comment on `materializeStandings` explains the semantics instead. `totalScore` stays an unchanged raw sum (purely informational, never used for ranking). Confirmed via full-repo test grep that every existing multi-round-adjacent fixture only ever used a single scored round, so `sum / 1 === sum` — none of the 251 pre-existing tests needed updating; added 2 new tests instead, directly encoding this session's Swiss and cutoff-flip simulations as regression coverage (one for `computeQualificationStandings`, one for `computeGroupStandings`).

**Fix 2 — reserve-admission guard.** Averaging alone isn't enough for the qualification-table/Swiss standings mechanism specifically: a reserve who only ever gets to play a single qualifying round could still win a real spot outright from one lucky round, since averaging has no volume discount at all. Rather than a Bayesian-shrinkage/smoothing formula (rejected in discussion — opaque, needs an arbitrary tuning constant with no obviously-correct value), the organiser chose a hard, non-configurable rule: a reserve can only be added while **at most one qualifying round has already been completed**. `addReserveUnit` (`mutations.ts`) now counts `state.rounds.slice(0, state.curRound).filter(round => round.isQual || round.isSwiss).length` and blocks (`reason: 'qualification-in-progress'`) once that's over 1 — reusing the exact same `isQual || isSwiss` predicate already used elsewhere in this codebase for this distinction (`isLastStandingsRound`, `reserveWindowAtCurrentRound`), so it naturally covers **both** qual-table and Swiss with zero explicit `poolingPhase` string-matching (confirmed with the organiser this should apply to both, since they share the identical standings engine — special-casing one but not the other would have contradicted this codebase's own avoid-special-casing convention). `addWalkUpIndividual` needed no separate change, since it already delegates straight into `addReserveUnit`. Group Stage reserves were already blocked outright for an unrelated, pre-existing reason (fixed group membership/round-robin schedule once started) — confirmed via research this rule doesn't need to extend there at all. New `messages` entry in `RankingsRoster.tsx`'s existing `handleReserveResult` pattern surfaces the block with no new UI mechanism. 6 new tests in `mutations.test.ts` (allowed at 0 and exactly 1 completed round; blocked at 2+ for both qual-table and Swiss; Group Stage's own block still fires regardless of round count; `poolingPhase: 'none'` unaffected regardless of round count).

**Testing**: `pnpm vitest run src/domain/tournament` — 257 tests pass (up from 249, 8 new), no regressions. Lint/Prettier clean. Live-verified on local dev: added a reserve at Round 1 (0 completed, succeeded), added another at Round 2 (1 completed, succeeded), then confirmed a third attempt at Round 3 (2 completed) was blocked with the exact new message and the reserve was not added to the roster; confirmed Scoreboard's Fair Points column showed values in a stable, comparable range regardless of a unit's "Played" count (2 vs. 1) instead of growing unboundedly with more rounds played.

**Out of scope, explicit**: a configurable reserve-cutoff number instead of the fixed ≤1-round rule — organiser's explicit preference was a fixed rule "to begin with"; any change to `totalScore`'s cumulative-sum semantics (stays informational only, never used for ranking).

---

## Setup UI's "Advance to bracket" silent-fallback bug + non-negative-integer score validation (done — 2026-09-16)
Followed up on item 4 from the second Scoreboard follow-up audit (`project_scoreboard_audit_followups.md` in Claude's memory) — the "Advance to bracket" (`qualAdv`) field hit twice in past sessions, worked around each time by patching `localStorage` directly because it "looked like the typed value didn't stick." Investigated with two parallel research agents before touching anything: `setup.qualAdv` turned out to be a plain, fully-controlled string with no debounce and no stale closure, and `PersistedSetup` is confirmed local-only (never touched by the Firebase `SyncCoordinator`), ruling out a remote-write race. The real bug was in `generation.ts`'s `parsed()` helper (`Number.parseInt(value, 10) || fallback`), which treats both an empty string and the literal `"0"` as invalid and silently substitutes `24` with no error shown, before clamping to `[floorMin, n]` — so a field that's empty or momentarily "0" at the instant "Generate Schedule" is clicked silently generates with a number that has nothing to do with what the admin typed. This exact class of bug was already fixed for the sibling `semisOverride`/`finalOverride` fields (explicit rejection + `generationError`, with a comment documenting the `|| fallback` pitfall) — that fix had just never been extended to `qualAdv`. Fixed by replacing the blind clamp with the same explicit-rejection pattern: `poolingPhase !== 'none'` now parses `form.qualAdv` directly and returns a `generationError` naming the bad value for anything that isn't a finite integer >= 1, before clamping the valid case. `SetupView.tsx` already had the `result.status === 'invalid' → setError(...)` plumbing from the sibling fields, so this needed zero UI code changes to surface as a visible error banner. 4 new tests in `generation.test.ts` (empty/`"0"`/negative all rejected with a message naming "Advance to bracket"; `poolingPhase: 'none'` unaffected, confirmed via grep that no existing test relied on the old silent-fallback-to-24 behavior).

Bundled in the same session (unrelated files/subsystems, raised fresh by the organiser): room/game score inputs (`BracketScoreInput` and the two inline Finals inputs in `BracketView.tsx`) had a `min='0'` that was purely cosmetic — nothing actually blocked a negative value or a decimal from reaching `setRoundScore`/`setFinalScore` (`mutations.ts`), which already treated non-numeric garbage as "unset" via `Number.isNaN` but did nothing for `-5` (a valid, non-NaN parse) or `5.7` (silently truncated to `5` by `parseInt`, no indication given). Confirmed with the organiser: `0` stays a valid score (already treated as "complete" everywhere downstream — sort, Fair Points, round-completion check), and a decimal must be *rejected*, not silently truncated. Added a small `isValidNonNegativeInteger()` helper in `mutations.ts` (checks `Number.isInteger(Number(value)) && numeric >= 0` — `Number()`, not `parseInt`, so a fractional part is actually detected instead of silently dropped) and routed both `setRoundScore`/`setFinalScore` through it, keeping each function's existing unset-sentinel (`null`/`''`). Added `step={1}` alongside the existing `min='0'` on all three score `ScoreInput` usages for native spinner/`:invalid` affordance only — the domain layer is the real enforcement point. 12 new tests in `mutations.test.ts` covering both functions (negative/decimal rejected, `0`/positive accepted, empty/garbage unset as before).

**Testing**: `pnpm vitest run src/domain/tournament` — 249 tests pass (up from 235, 14 new), no regressions. Lint/Prettier clean on all changed files. Live-verified on local dev (`localhost:3000`, since these fixes hadn't been deployed yet): cleared/zeroed/negated "Advance to bracket" each correctly blocked generation with a visible error banner naming the bad value; a valid value generated correctly with the typed qualAdv reflected in the schedule preview. In Bracket, typing `-5` and `5.7` into a score cell both correctly cleared the input instead of storing a value; `0` and a normal positive integer were both accepted normally.

**Explicitly out of scope**: the other two items from the same deferred list — Fair Points summed-vs-averaged (a design question for the organiser) and group-stage's pre-generated future rounds not being regenerated on a removal/swap — were not touched.

---

## DNF / no-show tracking for removed and swapped-out roster units (done — 2026-09-16)
Followed up on the "mid-tournament swap silently drops historical Fair Points/standings" item parked at the end of the previous Scoreboard audit (see `project_scoreboard_audit_followups.md` in Claude's memory). Investigating further reframed the actual requirement, confirmed directly with the organiser: standings must never show a removed/swapped-out unit at all (their scores don't matter and shouldn't distort the table); Rankings' final standings must reflect only real competitive results — a withdrawn unit that played at least one match should still appear, but appended last, no rank number, labeled "DNF"; a unit removed/swapped out before playing anything (a no-show) shouldn't appear in public Rankings at all, only to Admins.

**Root findings, confirmed against the actual code, not assumed**: `replaceAssignedUnit` (the shared helper behind swap) only ever rewrites the *current round's* assignment — a deliberate, tested design choice from the original 2026-09-12 "One-click player swap" build (a swap is a genuine substitution, "a different competitor going forward," not a rename — that's `renameIndividual`'s job, which *does* rewrite every round). `swapTeam` already remaps `state.groups[].members` (old teamId → new); `swapIndividual` never did, so an individual swapped in mid-group-stage never got an accumulator bucket in `computeGroupStandings` and could never actually qualify — the one real *standings* bug in scope, since it affects an active player, not a withdrawn one. No existing state tracked "this unit was withdrawn," and a team's display name doesn't survive removal (the live `TournamentTeam` object is gone), so DNF/no-show tracking needed a real snapshot captured at the moment of departure, not something derivable after the fact.

**New `WithdrawnUnit` type + `state.withdrawnUnits` field** (`types.ts`): `{ name, label, members, playedAnyMatch, reason: 'removed' | 'swapped' }`, snapshotted via `unitDisplay()` *before* the unit is stripped from `state.players`. Added to `state-defaults.ts`'s default state and cleared in `resetRoster`/`resetTournamentState` (stale once a roster/run restarts); `generation.ts` needed no change since its state literal spreads `...current`, inheriting the field automatically — unlike schedule-run-scoped fields, withdrawal history has no reason to reset on regeneration.

**`mutations.ts`**: new `hasPlayedAnyMatch()` (room-based `getUnitScore` for ordinary rounds, `getFinalUnitScore` for Final-round slots, which always carry `room: null`) and `recordWithdrawal()`, called from `removeRosterUnit`/`swapIndividual`/`swapTeam` against the *pre-mutation* state. `swapIndividual` gained the same `state.groups[].members` remap `swapTeam` already had (the active-player fix above). **Found during live verification, not in the original plan, and fixed the same way**: `removeRosterUnit` had the identical `state.groups` staleness gap `swapIndividual` did — added the same strip-from-groups step there too, reusing the function's own existing `strip()` helper (already used for `luckyLosers`/`byes`).

**`rankings.ts`**: `computeRankings` gained `dnfList`/`noShows`, filtered from `state.withdrawnUnits` by `playedAnyMatch` — fully additive, no change to `stillActive`/`finalists`/`eliminatedList`'s own computation (they already exclude a withdrawn unit via their existing `rosterKeys(state.players)` walk). **A second gap found during live verification**: group-stage's whole schedule is pre-generated upfront, so a removed/swapped unit's old name can linger in a *later* round's own pre-built assignments — `computeRankings`'s `active`/`stillActive` derivation read that raw assignment list directly, so a removed player could show as "Still in tournament" to viewers, the worst version of the very thing this build was meant to prevent. Fixed with a one-line roster-membership filter on `active` right before `activeNames` is derived — general, not group-stage-specific, and a no-op whenever nobody's been withdrawn.

**`RankingsView.tsx`**: DNF rows appended after `eliminatedList` in the existing "Final standings" block (`<Position>DNF</Position>`, needs no component change since `children` is already `ReactNode`); new `NoShowsPanel` reading `state.withdrawnUnits` directly (not `data.noShows`, so it still works pre-generation, mirroring `ManageRoster`'s own `data`-independent rendering), rendered in the two spots already gated on `editable`.

**Testing**: 235 domain tests (up from 221), 14 new — `rankStandings`-adjacent tests untouched; new coverage in `rankings.test.ts` (DNF vs. no-show list membership, a withdrawn unit staying absent from `eliminatedList` even with historical assignments under its old name, and — the live-verification-driven addition — a ghost name in a pre-generated round correctly excluded from `stillActive`) and a new `mutations.test.ts` (zero coverage existed before this build): withdrawal recording for both `removeRosterUnit`/`swapIndividual`/`swapTeam`, a team's label/members surviving removal, the Final-round `playedAnyMatch` edge case, the `swapIndividual`/`swapTeam` groups remap, and `removeRosterUnit`'s new groups-strip. Live-verified end to end on a real 4-player `individual-1v1` + Group Stage tournament: removed a never-scored player (correctly "No-show," admin-only, absent from public Rankings and Scoreboard); swapped out a player who'd played a scored match (correctly "DNF" in Final standings, no rank number); confirmed the swapped-in replacement's own subsequent group-stage round correctly accumulated real Fair Points in Scoreboard (`Played: 1`, not silently dropped); confirmed the removed player never resurfaced in Scoreboard's group table nor as "Still in tournament" in Rankings, even in the round pre-built before their removal.

### Explicitly out of scope
Group-stage's pre-generated future rounds are **not** regenerated on a removal/swap the way `addReserveUnit`'s `rebuildFutureRounds` does for a reserve addition — a ghost name can still occupy a room slot (with an editable score input) in Bracket's *future* rounds, even though standings/Rankings now correctly ignore it everywhere. Noted as a real, separate gap for a later session (see `project_scoreboard_audit_followups.md`), not fixed here — closing it properly means deciding whether remove/swap should trigger the same future-round rebuild reserve-adding already does, a bigger question than this build's scope. Swiss pairing order / room-history rematch-avoidance "forgetting" a swapped-in unit's pre-swap opponents — confirmed with the organiser as correct, not a bug (the incoming competitor genuinely hasn't played those opponents); `seeding.ts` untouched. Fair Points summed vs. averaged, and the Setup UI's `qualAdv` field not reliably committing before schedule generation — both separately parked already.

## Scoreboard follow-up audit: frozen-standings label + tie-aware ranking (done — 2026-09-16)
A second audit pass on Scoreboard (now a pure standings view since the 2026-09-15 trim below), asked for explicitly: "find any remaining bug that would have gone past your recent audit" plus a redundancy/organization check. Two agents — one re-auditing `ScoreboardView.tsx`/`TournamentStandings.tsx` fresh, one auditing the underlying `computeQualificationStandings`/`computeGroupStandings` for edge cases (ties, byes, mid-tournament reserves/swaps, team aggregation, uneven group sizes) — found two real bugs in scope for this pass, plus two deeper domain-layer issues explicitly parked for a dedicated session (see "Explicitly out of scope" below and `project_scoreboard_audit_followups.md` in Claude's memory).

**Bug 1 — standings freeze with no indication once pooling ends.** `ScoreboardStandings`'s `hasGroups`/`hasStandings` check scans every round up to `curRound`; since `state.rounds` is fully pre-generated, once any qual-table/Swiss/group-stage round has happened the table stays visible for the rest of the tournament — including a Kings Valley tail that can run 10+ more rounds — with nothing indicating the data is frozen, not live.

**Bug 2 — the "Pos" column ignored ties, and display order ignored resolved tie-breaks.** `TournamentStandings.tsx` numbered rows by plain array index, so two entrants tied on Fair Points showed as consecutive ranks (e.g. 3 and 4) as if one clearly beat the other — the same naive-index bug also existed in `rankings.ts`'s `poolRanks` (Rankings' compact `#rank · FP` badge), same root cause, fixed together per this repo's "fix the recurring pattern once" rule. Separately, `roomBasedComputeAdvancement`/`computeGroupStageAdvancement` already reorder the real advancement decision via `applyQualCutoffOrder`/`applyGroupCutoffOrder` once an admin resolves a cutoff-boundary tie, but those functions weren't exported and Scoreboard never called them — the table could show a resolved tie's pair in the wrong order.

### Fix 1
`ScoreboardView.tsx`'s `ScoreboardStandings`: rather than hiding the table once pooling ends (real reference value would be lost — an admin may still want "who topped the qual table" mid-Kings-Valley), it now appends " (Final)" to the table/group titles once `state.rounds[state.curRound]` is no longer a qual/Swiss/group-stage round itself, while an earlier round was.

### Fix 2
`advancement.ts`: exported the previously-private `applyQualCutoffOrder`/`applyGroupCutoffOrder` (no logic change); added a new exported `rankStandings()` — dense/competition ranking by `totalFP` (a tie shares one rank, the next distinct value's rank correctly skips ahead by the tie's size; `totalFP === null` gets `rank: null` instead of a misleading sequential number), mirroring the dense-ranking pattern `rankings.ts` already used for the eliminated/finalist lists. `TournamentStandings.tsx` now calls `rankStandings` for its Pos column (its only consumer, confirmed via repo-wide search). `ScoreboardView.tsx` now wraps each computed table in `applyQualCutoffOrder`/`applyGroupCutoffOrder` before rendering, so a resolved cutoff tie reorders the display to match what actually happens on advancement. `rankings.ts`'s `poolRanks` construction also switched from naive `index + 1` to `rankStandings`, fixing the identical bug there (an entrant with zero rounds played now shows no pool-rank badge at all, rather than a bogus number).

**Testing**: 3 new tests for `rankStandings` (no-tie sequential ranks; a tie sharing one rank with the next distinct rank correctly skipping ahead; `totalFP: null` → `rank: null`) — 221 domain tests total (up from 218), all passing. `eslint`/`prettier` clean on all five changed files. Live-verified: built a 16-player qual-table→Kings-Valley tournament, engineered a full round of symmetric ties across both rooms for 3 rounds (every rank-tier tied between the two rooms) — Scoreboard's Qualification Table showed dense ranks `1,1,3,3,5,5,7,7,...` exactly, correctly skipping ranks for each tie. Set `qualAdv` to land exactly on a tied pair (P10/P16 both rank 5); Bracket's tie-break card correctly offered to resolve it; resolving it ("P16 ranks next") immediately reordered Scoreboard's table to show P16 before P10 while both kept sharing rank 5. Advancing past Round 3 into the first Kings Valley round flipped the title to "Qualification Table (Final)" instantly. Rankings' compact pool-rank badges independently confirmed the same tie (P1/P15 both `#1`, P3/P5 both `#3`). Zero console errors throughout.

**A real, separate bug hit twice while testing (2026-09-15 and again this session), not part of this fix**: Setup's "Advance to bracket" (`qualAdv`) number field visually accepts a typed value but does not reliably commit to `state.cfg.qualAdv` before "Generate Schedule"/"Confirm & Start" is clicked — both times, the tournament generated with the field's *previous* value (the all-advance default) instead of what was typed, requiring a direct `localStorage` state patch to test the real cutoff behavior. Not investigated further this session (out of scope), but worth a dedicated look — a real admin hitting this in the Setup UI would get a silently wrong qualification cutoff with no error shown.

### Explicitly out of scope
Two deeper domain-layer issues found during the audit, deliberately not fixed here (see `project_scoreboard_audit_followups.md` in Claude's memory for the fuller writeup, kept there rather than only here since the user asked to track them for a later session specifically):
- **Mid-tournament swap silently drops historical Fair Points/standings** (`replaceAssignedUnit` in `mutations.ts` only rewrites the current round's assignment; the standings-accumulator functions seed from the *current* roster, so a swapped-out unit's past rounds become orphaned — worse for Group Stage, where `swapIndividual` never updates `state.groups[].members`, so the swapped-in player's contribution vanishes going forward too). Can affect the real advancement decision, not just display.
- **Fair Points is summed, not averaged**, so anyone with fewer counted rounds (a bye, a late reserve, or a victim of the swap bug above) gets an unearned edge in the ascending sort — a genuine design question (whether to normalize by rounds played), not a contained bug, since "cumulative Fair Points" is `HANDOFF.md`'s documented, intentional description of qual-table scoring today.
- The `qualAdv`-field-doesn't-persist bug noted above under Testing.

## Scoreboard audit: trim to standings-only, fix a shared pooling-cutoff bug (done — 2026-09-15)
Asked to audit Scoreboard's usage across gamemodes/pooling phases/schedule logics. Found four display bugs plus a structural redundancy problem, then traced one bug further than expected before deciding what to actually fix.

**The four bugs found**: (1) Kings Valley rounds showed every player "Eliminated" — Scoreboard never read `isKingsValley`/`kvPromoteCounts`/`kvDemoteCounts`/`kvEliminateCount`, so its generic `advPerRoom ?? 0` cutoff became 0 for every KV round. (2) The real pooling-phase cutoff round (the last qual-table/Swiss/group-stage round, where a cross-round standings cutoff actually eliminates most units) showed "everyone advances," because that round is *also* flagged `isNoElim: true` and Scoreboard's shortcut couldn't distinguish the two. (3) The double-elimination grand-final race showed blank "—" scores for its entire live duration, since Scoreboard required every game 1..`numGames` scored before showing a total, but the race's newest game is always open by construction. (4) Finals never distinguished champion from runner-up — both got a green "Advances" badge.

**The deeper finding**: tracing bug (2) further showed `BracketView.tsx`'s `RoundBody` has the *exact same flaw* — both views independently hand-roll `advances = round.isNoElim || index < direct` instead of consulting `roomBasedComputeAdvancement`'s already-correct `advancing` list (discarded by both, which only ever read `.luckyNames`). Bugs (1), (3), (4) don't exist in Bracket — its `RoundBody`/`FinalColumn` already handle Kings Valley, the grand-final race (`finalsProgressState`), and winner labeling correctly. They existed *only* in Scoreboard's own per-round/per-Final table — the exact code that duplicates Bracket, now with bugs Bracket doesn't have.

**Redundancy**: per the organiser's direction (fix bugs, trim Scoreboard to what's genuinely unique), and since the one thing Scoreboard shows that exists nowhere else in full is the qualification/Swiss/group-stage standings table (Rankings only shows a compact one-line rank badge) — the efficient path was to **delete Scoreboard's per-round table first**. That deletion eliminates bugs (1), (3), (4) as a byproduct (the buggy code is simply gone), leaving only bug (2) to actually fix, and only in Bracket, since that becomes the sole remaining live per-round view. No throwaway bugfix work landed in code that was about to be deleted.

### Part 1 — Trim Scoreboard to a standings-only view
Deleted the entire per-room table (`ScoreboardView.tsx`) and the bye-card loop, trimmed `StatStrip` to just Round + entrant count (dropped Rooms/Advancing, meaningless without a room breakdown), and removed now-unused imports. `ScoreboardStandings` now returns a short `<Alert>` ("No standings table for this tournament format — check Bracket for live results.") instead of silently rendering blank for a pure single/double-elimination or Kings Valley tournament.

### Part 2 — Root-cause fix: real standings cutoff for the last pooling round
`src/domain/tournament/advancement.ts`: exported `isLastStandingsRound` (already correct internally, just made public); added `computeStandingsCutoffAdvancing(state, roundIndex): Set<string> | null` — returns the real advancing-name set (via `roomBasedComputeAdvancement(state, roundIndex).advancing`) for the last qual/Swiss round or the last group-stage round, `null` for every other round type (ordinary no-elim, ordinary elimination, Kings Valley, double-elimination, Final) so those callers' existing logic is untouched.

`BracketView.tsx`'s `RoundBody`: calls the new helper once per round (wrapped in the same try/catch-then-`console.error` pattern this codebase already uses for `roomBasedComputeAdvancement`), guarded to skip bracket/Kings-Valley rounds. `advances` now checks `cutoffAdvancing?.has(entry.name)` first when non-null, falling back to the original `round.isNoElim || index < direct` expression otherwise.

**Testing**: 7 new tests in `advancement.test.ts` (`computeStandingsCutoffAdvancing` — correct set for the last qual-table round and the last group-stage round; `null` for an ordinary no-elim round, an ordinary elimination round, a Kings Valley round, and a Final), 218 domain tests total, all passing; `eslint`/`prettier` clean. Live-verified: built a 16-player qual-table tournament (`qualAdv: 6`), scored all 3 qual rounds with distinct per-player totals, and confirmed the last qual round's per-row DOM classes showed exactly 6 "advance" / 10 "eliminate" — matching `qualAdv` precisely, not "everyone advances." Confirmed an ordinary (non-last) qual round still showed all 16 as "advance" (no regression). Confirmed Scoreboard shows the standings table for the qual-table case and the new fallback alert for a "None"-pooling single-elimination tournament, with zero console errors in both live sessions.

### Explicitly out of scope
Double-elimination WB/LB rounds' own advancement (`doubleEliminationComputeAdvancement`) — not mis-flagged `isNoElim`, not part of this bug, untouched. A "presentation mode" for Bracket (hiding score inputs/follow-search for a shared-screen view) — raised as a possible future alternative to a Scoreboard room table during planning, not requested, not built. Rankings' compact per-player rank badge — unchanged, still a legitimate lighter-weight view distinct from Scoreboard's full standings table.

Continuing the "Reorganize Admin" thread below: asked to review everything Admin shows once a tournament has started, against an explicit keep-list (admin access status, tournament name, tournament settings, viewer link/live sync, round summary "round number + players remaining", tournament progress, bottom buttons) and flag what else could move or go.

## Comfort: letter room names, round-aware future-round placeholders (done — 2026-09-15)
Two small clarity requests for Bracket (and the room labels it shares app-wide): count rooms by letters, not numbers ("Room A" not "Room 1"); and make future-round placeholders name their source round too, since a lettered room reference alone is ambiguous once every round's rooms share the same letters (e.g. distinguish Round 1's Room A from Round 4's Room A).

**Letters.** New `roomLetter()` helper in `bracket.ts` — bijective base-26 (Excel-column style: 1→A, 26→Z, 27→AA...) so it degrades gracefully past 26 rooms instead of breaking, though that's far beyond any real room count here. Applied at every room-number display site app-wide, confirmed complete via a repo-wide grep for the literal `Room ${`/`Room {` pattern: Bracket's round headers, follow banner, lucky-loser standings, Kings Valley disclaimer, and placeholder headers; Scoreboard's room headers; Admin's tie-break banner heading (`RunningAdminScores.tsx` at the time, later moved — see "Admin cleanup" above); Rankings' still-active room line. The underlying numeric `room`/`roomIndex` values are untouched everywhere they're used for logic (filtering, score-key construction, array indexing, React keys) — only the final display interpolation changed.

**Round-aware placeholders.** `BracketRoundLabel` (`bracketRoundLabels()`) gained a `roundNumber` field — the WB/LB-specific display counter for bracket rounds (the same number shown in "WB Round 2"), `roundNum` otherwise — so `projectFutureRoundSlots`'s `room-rank` tokens could carry their source round's own display number alongside the room. `projectedSlotLabelText` combines them into one tag: "Room 2A, Rank 1", "Winner of Room 1A".

Implemented and verified as two explicitly checked-in steps (letters, then round-numbering) per the organiser's standing "check in per item on large tasks" preference, rather than one combined pass.

**Testing**: updated all `bracket.test.ts` room-rank assertions for both changes (12 tests, all passing); full domain suite 212/212 green; `eslint`/`prettier` clean. Verified live end-to-end on a qual-table tournament through to the Final: every round's room headers show letters, and placeholder chains correctly track their real source round — Round 3's placeholders tagged "2X" (from Round 2), Round 5's "4X" (from Round 4), Semis "5X", Final "6X" — matching the actual round each was sourced from.

## Fix: future-round placeholders show same-room carryover instead of the real diversified path (done — 2026-09-15)
After the qual-table `roomHistory` fix below made the *real* transition correctly diversify room membership round to round, the organiser confirmed on the test server that Round 1→2 now visibly reshuffles — but reported the same-looking bug a second time against Bracket's *future-round placeholders* specifically: "before starting the tournament... round 2 still shows, in Room 1: Room 1, Rank 1 / Room 1, Rank 2 / ... Room 1, Rank 7." First diagnosis found the fix already prepared but not yet pushed to `test` — a deployment-lag false alarm, not a fresh bug — but investigating it surfaced a second, real defect in the same area.

**Root cause**: `projectFutureRoundSlots` (`bracket.ts`) built its placeholder pool room-major (all of source Room 1's ranks, then Room 2's, ...) before chunking into the target round's rooms via `sequentialSeed`. Whenever source and target share the same room shape — the normal case for qual-table and no-elim warm-up rounds — room-major chunking mathematically collapses to exact same-room carryover: the worst-case form of an already-disclosed, accepted tradeoff (this projection can't reproduce the real transition's score-dependent `tieredSeed` output ahead of time, and was never meant to).

**Fix**: build the pool tier-major instead (every room's own rank-1 finisher, then every room's own rank-2, and so on) in both the no-elim branch (covers qual-table + warm-up) and the ordinary cutting-round branch — the one shared step both feed into, not a qual-table-specific carve-out, per the standing "avoid structure-specific special-casing" feedback. This still can't reproduce `tieredSeed`'s exact output, but it stops asserting the one thing that's flatly false — that rooms never mix.

**Testing**: updated 3 existing `bracket.test.ts` assertions that encoded the old room-major order; all 212 domain tests pass. Verified live on a fresh 22-player qual-table tournament: Round 2's placeholder now interleaves all three Round 1 rooms instead of showing one room's own ranks 1-N; Round 3's placeholder correctly re-derives from Round 2's real (already-diversified) shape after Round 1 is scored and advanced. Zero console errors.

### Explicitly out of scope
Still an approximation, not an exact reproduction of the real transition's `tieredSeed` output — an accepted, disclosed tradeoff, unchanged by this fix (see "Room reseeding" in `HANDOFF.md`).

---

## Reorganize Admin: score entry to Bracket, roster to Rankings, add settings recap (done — 2026-09-15)
Admin had accumulated everything added since the rework — score entry, roster management, standings, a room-assignment preview, tie resolution, sync status, archive controls — and had become hard to navigate. Narrowed to three things: the tournament's configured settings, ties that need resolving, and other admin-only info (sync link, archive actions). The organiser corrected an early misreading mid-planning: roster/team management belongs on the *existing* Rankings tab, not a new one — Rankings is meant to evolve from a simple pre-tournament player list into a true live ranking over the tournament's lifecycle, visible to all and editable once unlocked.

**Bracket (score entry)**: closed three real gaps before removing Admin's score UI, so no capability was lost — (1) multi-game non-Final rounds (e.g. a multi-game Semis) previously had no entry UI at all in Bracket; ported Admin's per-game/Total tab pattern. (2) Finals/Semis editing was restricted to "the next game only," with a literal "Earlier games are edited in Admin" message; replaced with the same per-game-tab + Total-tab navigation Admin had, so every game is editable regardless of order. (3) Kings Valley promote/stay/demote/eliminate only had border-color styling; added the same text badges Admin showed. Also moved "Next Round"/"Previous" and the pending-ties gate onto Bracket's own sticky action bar, since advancing now belongs next to where scores are entered (tie *resolution* itself stayed in Admin at this point — see "Admin cleanup" above for where it moved next).

**Rankings (roster/team management)**: moved `ManageRoster`/`ReservePanel` (`RunningAdminRoster.tsx` → `RankingsRoster.tsx`) into Rankings. Threaded an `editable` prop through `RankingsContent`/`RankingsView`, mirroring Bracket's existing live-vs-archived-snapshot pattern — `ArchiveView`'s call site is untouched and defaults to non-editable, so a saved tournament snapshot never gains a live-mutation code path.

**Admin**: added a new read-only "Tournament Settings" recap (game format, schedule logic, pooling phase + specifics, odd-count strategy, team scoring, scoring system, finals/semis format) using two new label maps in `formats.ts`. Removed the now-fully-redundant Standings panel and "Room Assignments" preview (both had equivalents elsewhere — Scoreboard and Bracket, respectively), plus the dead `RoomScores`/`FinalsScores` code once nothing referenced them.

Done in explicitly checked-in stages (settings recap → Kings Valley check → score entry → roster move) per the organiser's standing "check in per item on large tasks" preference, each part verified live before the next began, landing as one commit at the end.

**Testing**: verified live across a Kings Valley tournament, a 3-game Final, a multi-game Semis round, and a Group Stage tournament — score entry, round advancement, tie resolution, roster editing (both pre-schedule and mid-tournament), and the archive-safety check (zero edit controls on an archived snapshot) all confirmed working with zero console errors. Domain test suite (212 tests) untouched and still green — feature-layer only, no domain logic changed.
