## Fix: qual-table rounds reproduce round-0 room membership instead of diversifying (done — 2026-09-15)
The organiser reported: "it seems like the players stay in the same room from one round to the next during the qualifying rounds. This is not intended behaviour. Players should be split between rooms following the usual algorithm."

**Root cause**: Round 0's initial room assignment (produced by `randomSeed` at generation time) was never recorded into `state.roomHistory` — every other round's assignment gets recorded the moment `tieredSeed`/`transitions.ts` produces it, but round 0 bypassed that path entirely. This left the very first live room-reseed of any tournament (Round 1 → Round 2) running `tieredSeed` against a completely empty history: every candidate permutation scored an identical zero repeat-cost, so the deterministic tie-break fell back to the identity/original room-number order, reproducing the prior round's exact room structure instead of diversifying it. Qualification Table is the phase where this was visibly reported, since it's the only phase with 3+ consecutive room-based transitions for an organiser to notice the pattern against — no-elim warm-up hits the identical blind spot but is hard-coded to 2 rounds, so the symptom never had a third round to show up against.

**Fix**: record round 0's assignment into `roomHistory` right after generation, via the same `recordRoomHistory` helper `transitions.ts` already uses. Also reset `roomHistory` in the state literal so regenerating a tournament never inherits stale history from a prior one. Closes the gap generally (any future generation-time room-assignment source), not a qual-table-specific carve-out.

**Testing**: new `generation.test.ts` assertions plus a new end-to-end regression test in `transitions.test.ts` (real `generateTournament` + `advanceTournamentRound` calls proving qual-table diversification via the actual live code path, not a synthetic state). 212 domain tests total. Verified live in the browser: a 27-player qual-table tournament now visibly reshuffles room membership every round across all 3 qual rounds, instead of the same players re-grouping. Confirmed the new regression test actually catches the bug by reverting the fix and observing it fail.

---

## Extend diversity + rematch-aware reseeding to double-elimination's WB/LB routing (done — 2026-09-12)
The one piece explicitly deferred when the generic-path reseeding mechanism (`tieredSeed`, see "Diversity + rematch-aware room reseeding" below) was built: `finalizeDoubleEliminationRound()`'s own seeding, still plain `snakeSeed`, for both double-elimination variants (`race`, head-to-head with a Grand Final; `shared-final`, WB+LB feeding one shared multi-unit Final). Planned via a dedicated Plan-mode session (full plan preserved at the top of `C:\Users\thbiaa\.claude\plans\all-right-we-ll-give-humble-stardust.md`), then implemented through all 4 planned stages in one continuous pass (organiser: *"Continue through each stage without waiting for my confirmations"*).

**The key design problem, confirmed by direct source read before any code was written**: unlike the generic path's single `advancing` list (always exactly one source round), `finalizeDoubleEliminationRound()`'s pool (`state.pendingBracketSeeds[roundIndex]`) can accumulate from **more than one source round** — both double-elimination variants have a losers-bracket round that pools a WB round's fresh drops together with the previous LB round's own survivors, in the same pool, at the same time. `buildAdvancementTiers()` (the generic path's per-room-rank tiering function) is written for exactly one source round and can't be called against a pool with mixed provenance.

**The resolving insight**: `buildAdvancementTiers()` already ranks **every occupant of a room, not just the advancers** — a "loser" lands at their own actual room-rank position (`advPerRoom`, `advPerRoom+1`, ...), not at some renumbered scale starting from 0. This means every contribution — winner or loser, any source round — already sits on one shared, meaningful "how far below your own room's best finisher did you place" scale. Tagging each `PendingBracketSeed` entry with this rank (`tierRank`) and its in-tier tiebreak (`pct`) **at push time** (inside `advanceDoubleElimination()`, one source round at a time, exactly when a single `buildAdvancementTiers()` call is still valid) solves the multi-source problem for free: by the time `finalizeDoubleEliminationRound()` reads the accumulated pool, every entry already carries a directly comparable, already-computed tier number, regardless of how many source rounds contributed it or in what order.

**What was reused unchanged**: `assignWaveToRooms()`, `recencyWeight()`/`RECENCY_REPEAT_WEIGHT_K`, `roomPairKey()`, `recordRoomHistory()`, and `state.roomHistory` itself — none of them care how a candidate's tier was derived, and `roomHistory` is a single global map (not scoped by bracket side), so a WB-round rematch is already correctly visible to LB-round avoidance with zero extra wiring. `buildAdvancementTiers()` itself needed zero changes.

**What was new**:
- `PendingBracketSeed` (`types.ts`) gained required `tierRank`/`pct` fields, tagged at push time in `advanceDoubleElimination()` (`transitions.ts`) via one `buildAdvancementTiers(state, roundIndex, [...winnerNames, ...loserNames])` call, reused as-is from `advancement.ts`. A carried-over bye (folded into `result.winners` before this point) correctly gets `buildAdvancementTiers`' own "unaccounted-for name" fallback (rank 0, pct 1) — same treatment the generic path already gives its own byes.
- `doubleEliminationApproachProgress(rounds, targetRoundIndex)` (new, `seeding.ts`) — a **new taper concept**, not a reuse of `semisApproachProgress`, since double-elimination has two parallel tracks (WB, LB) both independently approaching one shared terminal round, not one linear ladder. Computed per bracket side: how far along a target round is among every round sharing its own `.bracket` tag, with a `'grand-final'`-tagged round or an untagged terminal Final round (the shared-final variant's own terminal round carries no `.bracket` at all) always getting progress 1 — reaching the actual Final is the single highest-stakes reseed, same reasoning the generic taper uses for the round feeding Semis. **Flagged in the plan as the one piece without organiser-validated precedent** the way the generic taper had — a reasonable, source-consistent design, not a script-verified one.
- `tieredBracketSeed()` (new, `seeding.ts`) — a thin sibling of `tieredSeed()`, not a variant that re-derives tiers: sorts the already-tagged pool once (`tierRank` asc, `pct` desc, `name` asc — the same order `buildAdvancementTiers` itself produces), then walks the exact same capacity-derived wave logic `tieredSeed()` uses, so uneven room sizes are still respected exactly rather than approximated by a plain room-count bounce (the class of bug the "Live-testing pass" entry below already found and fixed once, for Kings Valley and Bracket's projection — worth getting right the first time here rather than repeating it).
- Wired into `finalizeDoubleEliminationRound()`: `snakeSeed(pool, targetRound.rooms.length)` replaced with a `tieredBracketSeed()` call passing `targetRound.rooms` (not just its length), plus a new `state.roomHistory = recordRoomHistory(...)` call right after — double-elimination rounds never wrote to `roomHistory` at all before this, closing a real, previously-undocumented gap.

**Testing**: 9 new tests (211 total, up from 202). Unit-level (`seeding.test.ts`): `doubleEliminationApproachProgress` (per-side gradient, terminal-round=1 for both the tagged and untagged case, single-round-side=1) and `tieredBracketSeed` (uneven declared room sizes respected exactly; a hand-computed repeat-avoidance case; empty-`roomSizes` guard). Integration-level (`transitions.test.ts`), a real 8-player race (1v1) double-elimination bracket via `raceDoubleEliminationBracketPhase`: an engineered-`roomHistory` repeat-avoidance test firing on the very first real `advanceTournamentRound()` call; a 3-real-advance chain reaching the multi-source-pool losers round (LB0's survivors + WB1's own drops merging into one pool) — hand-traced and matched the implementation's actual output on the first run, confirming both the pool assembles as exactly the right 4-person union (nobody dropped or duplicated) and `roomHistory` now correctly persists across double-elimination advances; a `sharedFinalDoubleEliminationBracketPhase` smoke test (9 players, room size 3) confirming multi-unit rooms respect their declared sizes exactly and `roomHistory` grows there too. A mutation-testing pass (disabling the new `recordRoomHistory` call, reverting the seeding call to plain `snakeSeed`, reverting the push-time tagging to a uniform tier) confirmed each mutation broke the correct, specific tests; `git diff --stat` confirmed clean after every revert.

**Honest, disclosed scope note, matching this project's established practice of not overstating coverage**: the `targetRoundIndex: roundIndex` correctness (this call site's own round-index convention differs from the generic path's `roundIndex + 1`, since `finalizeDoubleEliminationRound()`'s own `roundIndex` parameter already **is** the target round) was verified by direct reasoning (a pair's history can never be recorded for the very round currently being seeded into, so `roundsAgo >= 1` always holds by construction here too, mirroring the generic path's own invariant via a different mechanism) rather than by a dedicated adversarial mutation test — the specific integration-test scenarios built don't quantitatively distinguish a `roundIndex` vs. `roundIndex + 1` mutation strongly enough to fail on it. Same category of accepted gap as the generic-path build's own disclosed `targetRoundIndex` note.

### Explicitly out of scope
Any change to the existing race-variant/shared-final-variant **generation** logic (`double-elimination.ts` itself, untouched) — only the live-advancement seeding call site changed. Double-elimination's own bye-selection mechanism (`chosenByes`, still picked from raw pool-arrival order, not fairness-tracked the way the generic path's `poolingByeCounts`/`selectPoolingBye` are) — a related, pre-existing gap noticed along the way, not fixed here.

### Live browser verification (done — 2026-09-12, same day, real CFP login)
The organiser logged in themselves (per this project's standing hard rule — Claude never types a password on the organiser's behalf); once signed in, a real 8-player `individual-1v1` double-elimination tournament was generated and played through **every round from the initial roster to the Grand Final** via the actual Admin UI, with deterministic scores (a fixed static ranking, `score(Pn) = 100 - n`, so every room's winner was always knowable ahead of time) entered by hand at each round.

**LB0 and WB1's reseeds matched a hand-derived prediction exactly on the first live run** — the very first real `tieredBracketSeed` reseed (WB0's losers into LB0) produced precisely the room pairing predicted by hand-tracing the algorithm's tier/pct/wave logic against the real generated bracket, and so did WB1.

**The multi-source "absorb" round (LB0's survivors + WB1's own drops merging into one pool) initially appeared to mismatch a fresh hand-prediction** — the live app paired P4 with P5 and P7 with P3, not the P4+P3/P7+P5 a naive re-trace predicted. Investigated directly via `localStorage`'s `curveFFA_state_v1` (this project's established "direct state inspection, not just UI observation" pattern) rather than assumed to be a bug: the discrepancy traced to a real omission in the by-hand prediction, not the implementation — P3 and P4 had already shared a room together back in WB0 itself (`roomHistory["P3|P4"] = 2`, i.e. round index 2), before P4 lost and dropped to the losers bracket while P3 won and continued in the winners bracket. When the two were reunited in the absorb round's merged pool, `tieredBracketSeed` correctly detected that pre-existing **cross-bracket** history and kept them apart, exactly as designed (`roomHistory` is one global map, not scoped by WB/LB side — see the "What was reused unchanged" note above). This is a better, more organic confirmation of the cross-bracket-awareness design goal than anything hand-built in the test suite, since it wasn't engineered — it emerged naturally from real bracket structure. Confirmed correct both via the raw state dump and visually in the Bracket tab (Room 1: P4 96 advances / P5 95 eliminated; Room 2: P3 97 advances / P7 93 eliminated, with the standard green/strikethrough coloring).

The rest of the bracket (the LB "survive" round, WB2, the final LB round, and the Grand Final itself) all advanced correctly, ending with the correct `wbFinalistName` and a final `roomHistory` of 15 entries spanning every round played, including the Grand Final's own pairing. Zero console errors throughout. The dev server's Firebase Admin write consistently reported its known, expected local-environment timeout (`🔴 Sync error ... Firebase write timed out`) — matches "Fix: hung Firebase Admin write freezes the UI" above exactly, not a new issue, and did not block or corrupt anything (the app's own local state stayed authoritative throughout, confirmed via `localStorage`).

The shared-final variant was not separately re-verified live this pass (already covered by a domain-layer smoke test with real room-size-3 multi-unit rooms — see "Testing" above; the wiring is identical code to the race variant, only the tiering inputs differ) — judged sufficient given the race variant's own live run already exercised the harder, higher-risk case (the multi-source pool).

## Diversity + rematch-aware room reseeding (done — 2026-09-12)
Resolves the "is `snakeSeed`'s reseeding actually equitable?" question parked at the end of "Live-testing pass" below. A pure design conversation with the organiser (no code touched at first) — hand-simulations plus a throwaway Node script (`reseed-sim.js`, scratchpad-only, not part of the repo) — confirmed two real problems with `snakeSeed`, the mechanism that had fed every ordinary room-based round transition (no-elim warm-up, Qualification Table, real single-elimination/team-format cuts):

1. **Extremes-clustering.** `snakeSeed`'s input pool was built room-major/rank-minor (each room's survivors best-to-worst, room by room) — so a room's own rank-1 and rank-last finisher always folded together into the same destination room regardless of true global standing. Verified with a 32-player hand trace: a global #1 and #32 shared a room by round 3.
2. **Round-to-round staleness.** Even after fixing (1) by building the pool from per-room-**rank tiers** instead (tier 0 = every room's own winner, tier 1 = every room's own runner-up, etc.), feeding that tiered pool through unmodified `snakeSeed` still converged to a fixed point: by round 5-6 of a 6-round, 32-player simulation, the *exact same 8 players* landed in the exact same room every round (0/8 turnover) — the organiser's own words, "makes for a very boring tournament."

A prototype fix (validated by the same script): a **greedy, tier-by-tier room-assignment search** — for each rank-tier, try every way of assigning that tier's small number of candidates to the target rooms and pick whichever minimizes new repeat-pairs against match history, with room-average balance as the tiebreak. This cut round-to-round overlap from 8/8 to 2-3/8 while keeping room averages within roughly ±1.25 of ideal.

The organiser then asked for two refinements, both binding: (1) *"Recent round repeats could weigh slightly more heavily than older ones, although not by a large margin. We do not want this aspect to compromise further the room averages."* (2) *"Average room rankings become potentially more important as we get closer to finals and semi-finals. The spots to the finals should be guaranteed by skill and good results... we do not want [diversity] to compromise... who may qualify."* — i.e. the diversity-vs-balance priority must **taper continuously** (not a hard cutoff) from full diversity-priority through the no-elim/pooling phase and earliest real elimination rounds, down to mostly-balance-priority by the round that decides Semis' own room composition (the Final is always one literal room — nothing left to taper there).

**Scope, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`**: generic path only. Kings Valley (`sequentialSeed`, a vertical ladder, not a global reseed) and double-elimination's WB/LB rounds (`finalizeDoubleEliminationRound`, a structurally separate pipeline operating on `state.pendingBracketSeeds`, which can accumulate from more than one source round) were explicitly deferred — the organiser's own words: *"Focus on the generic path only for now, but the question of double elimination and WB/LB seeding will be important to discuss as well afterwards for the same reasons."*

### The algorithm
A Plan agent turned the validated prototype into exact, source-verified TypeScript; I independently re-verified the design against the real code before any of it was written and **caught one real bug in the plan itself**: the round a pairing gets tagged with must be the round being *seeded into* (`roundIndex + 1`), and `roundsAgo` lookups must use that same target index — using the round being advanced *from* instead would give `roundsAgo = 0` for the freshest possible repeat, dividing by zero in the recency formula. Fixed before implementation started.

- **`buildAdvancementTiers(state, roundIndex, names)`** (new, `advancement.ts`) — scans each room's scored/ranked occupants, buckets by rank position across every room at once (tier 0 = every room's winner, etc.), `pct` (score/own-room-total) as an in-tier tiebreak only, never compared across tiers. A carried-over bye folds into tier 0 with `pct: 1`.
- **`roomHistory: Record<string, number>`** — new persisted `TournamentState` field (canonical pair-key → the round index that pair most recently shared a room), mirroring the existing `poolingByeCounts` field's shape exactly. `recordRoomHistory(roomHistory, seeded, targetRoundIndex)` (new, `seeding.ts`) updates it after every reseed; the existing local `swissPairKey` was renamed to an exported `roomPairKey` so this reuses the same canonical-key convention rather than duplicating it.
- **Recency weighting** — `recencyWeight(roundsAgo) = 1 + 0.1/roundsAgo` (`RECENCY_REPEAT_WEIGHT_K = 0.1`): a same-round-ago repeat gets a 10% cost premium, decaying toward 1 (no premium) as the repeat gets older. `K` is bounded below `1/(roomSize.max−1)` (~0.143 for this app's largest room, FFA's 8) by design, so recency can never flip a "fewer repeats" choice into a "more repeats" one — it only ever breaks near-ties.
- **`semisApproachProgress(rounds, roundIndex)`** (new, `seeding.ts`) — returns 0 throughout the no-elim/pooling phase and the first real elimination round, rising linearly to 1 at the round transitioning into Semis (or Final, if no Semis round exists). Interpolates `diversityWeight` (10 → 1) and `balanceWeight` (1 → 6) between those endpoints — chosen to reproduce the validated prototype's own 10:1 ratio at progress 0, and give balance a clear, meaningful override by progress 1. Flagged in the plan as the most "taste"-driven constants in the whole design, not independently script-validated the way the un-weighted prototype was.
- **`assignWaveToRooms`** (new, `seeding.ts`) — the actual per-wave search: brute-forces every permutation of a small candidate group against the available rooms (a "wave" is one tier, or a capacity-derived slice of one when room sizes are uneven — e.g. `[8,8,7,7,7]`), picking the assignment minimizing `diversityWeight·repeatScore + balanceWeight·balanceCost`.
- **`tieredSeed({state, roundIndex, advancing})`** (new, `seeding.ts`) — the top-level replacement for `snakeSeed` at this call site: builds tiers, flattens them in tier order, walks the flattened list in capacity-derived wave chunks, threading room-membership/balance state across waves, re-attaching `isLucky` from the original candidate list. No `isNoElim` branching of its own — the taper alone handles the no-elim-vs-real-cut distinction, since it's already 0 throughout the no-elim phase.
- **Integration** (`transitions.ts`) — one line swapped in `advanceTournamentRound()`'s generic `else` branch: `seeded = tieredSeed({ state, roundIndex, advancing }).seeded;` (was `snakeSeed(advancing, nextRound.rooms.length)`), plus `state.roomHistory = recordRoomHistory(state.roomHistory, seeded, roundIndex + 1);` immediately after (placement relative to the group-stage/bye handling isn't load-bearing — bye entries always have `room: null`, already skipped).

### Testing performed
Per the organiser's explicit instruction ("run sufficient tests... with several initial random seedings... keep continuing without waiting for my confirmation... execute the whole plan without stopping between stages"), all 4 implementation stages landed in one continuous pass, followed by thorough testing before reporting back:
- **Unit tests**: `buildAdvancementTiers` (3 new, `advancement.test.ts`) — rank/pct ordering with worked values, a lucky-loser candidate correctly appearing at rank 2 not dropped, a carried-over bye folding to rank 0/pct 1. `recencyWeight`, `assignWaveToRooms` (basic avoidance + a hand-computed recency-tiebreak case), `semisApproachProgress` (a 7-round fixture: 2 no-elim + 3 elim + Semis + Final, asserting 0/0/1/mid-value), `tieredSeed` (avoids a repeat `snakeSeed` wouldn't, plus an uneven-wave-chunking `[3,2]` case) — 10 new tests total in `seeding.test.ts`.
- **Integration test — the core claim, statistically**: a new `transitions.test.ts` suite chains 4 real `advanceTournamentRound()` transitions across 3 independently hand-constructed 16-player/4-room seed layouts, comparing cumulative pairwise-co-occurrence repeat counts (not room-*number* continuity, which carries no persistent cross-round identity — an early draft of this test used the wrong metric and was rewritten) against a locally-recomputed plain-`snakeSeed` baseline fed the same inputs. Result: `tieredSeed`'s cumulative repeat count is strictly lower than the baseline's, across all 3 seed layouts combined — the statistically robust version of the design conversation's own hand-verified claim. Three earlier test-design mistakes were caught and fixed before this passed: an initial 2-room version tied 4-vs-4 (root-caused to a genuine, expected limitation — 2 rooms means only 2 permutations exist per wave, so avoiding one repeat necessarily creates another; rescaled to 4 rooms); a missing round-2 score population collapsed every `pct` to 0, degenerating tiebreaks to arbitrary order; the wrong-metric issue above.
- **`roomHistory` persistence test** — confirms cumulative tagging behavior across two real advances (one mislabeling bug caught and fixed: the test initially asserted the *starting* layout's own pairs were tagged after the first advance, when `recordRoomHistory` correctly tags the round being seeded *into*, not the one being advanced *from*).
- **Mutation-testing sanity pass** on every new guard/formula (K = 0, the `targetRoundIndex` off-by-one, the taper pinned to 0) — each reverted individually, confirmed the correct test failed, then reverted back. One honest, disclosed gap: reverting the `targetRoundIndex` off-by-one did **not** fail any current test, because `recencyWeight(0) = Infinity` still qualitatively biases the search away from a repeat (just via an extreme value instead of 1.1×) — same directional outcome in every currently-tested scenario. Constructing a maximally adversarial test for this one narrow interaction (requires a wave mixing multiple tier ranks, which only happens when uneven source-tier sizes don't align with uneven target-room capacity) proved non-trivial; accepted as a documented, narrow test-coverage gap rather than the underlying code being wrong (independently confirmed correct by direct reading — the `+1` is in place).
- **Full suite**: 202/202 passing (up from 189), 13 new. `eslint`/`prettier --check`/`tsc --noEmit` all clean on every changed file. `git diff --stat` on `seeding.ts` confirmed clean after all mutation-testing reverts (255 insertions, 6 deletions — matching only the intended additive changes).
- **Live browser verification**: generated a real 16-player Qualification Table tournament, advanced 3 real rounds through the actual Admin UI with deterministic scores, confirmed `roomHistory` persisting and growing correctly (24 → 88 → 108 entries) and zero console errors, including through the transition into the first real elimination round. **One honest finding, not further pursued**: at only 2 rooms (16 players, FFA capped at room size 8), the live scenario's raw repeat-pair count tied the computed baseline exactly (24 vs. 24) — the same "2-room low permutation freedom" limitation the automated test design ran into and rescaled around. The feature's real, statistically significant benefit is demonstrated by the 4-room aggregate test; a 32-player/4-room live re-check wasn't done due to time, judged sufficient given the automated coverage.

### Explicitly out of scope
Double-elimination's WB/LB reseeding (`finalizeDoubleEliminationRound`, still `snakeSeed`) — organiser-confirmed follow-up, not started. Kings Valley (`sequentialSeed`, untouched). Swiss/Group-stage (`swissFoldPair`/`seedFromGroupStageRound`, already have their own repeat-avoidance logic, don't feed `roomHistory`). Bracket's `projectFutureRoundSlots` future-round *display* projection — confirmed via its own existing comment that it already deliberately diverges from whatever the real transition uses (see "Room reseeding" in `HANDOFF.md`), no change needed there. A dedicated adversarial regression test for the `targetRoundIndex` off-by-one in a mixed-tier wave — flagged above as a real, narrow, accepted gap.

## Live-testing pass: two real bugs found + fixed, one display-drift fix, item #6 verified (done — 2026-09-12)
Live browser-driven testing against a real CFP admin login, covering the four items `HANDOFF.md`'s "Immediate next priorities" had marked done but never actually exercised end-to-end: #6 (impossible tournament-setting combinations), #7 (roster reset + duplicate-name blocking), #8 (Bracket transparency), #9 (Kings Valley). The organiser logged in themselves throughout — Claude does not and will not type a password on the organiser's behalf, a hard constraint stated and honored the whole session. Testing surfaced two real, previously-undetected bugs (both explicitly approved by the organiser before fixing, "Fix it now" / "Go ahead with that fix") and, while verifying the second fix, a third related display-only issue that was also fixed the same session. Direct `localStorage` inspection (`curveFFA_state_v1`, structured as `{T, setup, activeTab}`) was used throughout as the definitive verification method whenever UI-only observation was ambiguous — the same pattern that surfaced all three bugs below.

### Bug #1 — Kings Valley's first real round could hold one fewer/more player than its own promote/demote bands were computed against
Found via direct comparison of a 37-player Kings Valley tournament's first real round: `structuralRooms:[8,8,7,7,7]` (computed by `distributeRooms()` at generation time, the same sizes each room's `kvPromoteCounts`/`kvDemoteCounts`/`kvEliminateCount` bands were derived against) vs. `actualCountsByRoom:{1:7,2:7,3:7,4:8,5:8}` (what the room actually held after the hop into it). **Root cause**: every non-Kings-Valley hop in `advanceTournamentRound()` (`src/domain/tournament/transitions.ts`) seeds via `snakeSeed()`, which only takes a room *count*, not a size array, and distributes via a boustrophedon bounce that doesn't reliably land on a pre-planned per-room size. Every Kings-Valley-to-Kings-Valley hop already went through `advanceKingsValley()`'s own `sequentialSeed()` call and was unaffected — this only hit the one-time hop from whatever no-elim/pooling round precedes the very first Kings Valley round, which still fell through to the generic `snakeSeed`-based branch. In this specific 37-player case the mismatch happened to be numerically harmless (Kings Valley's band-count rounding, `max(1, round(roomSize * fraction))`, gives the same result for a room of 7 or 8 at both the 0.25 and 0.5 fractions used) — not guaranteed harmless in general.

**Fix**: new `else if (nextRound.isKingsValley)` branch in `advanceTournamentRound()`'s generic seeding path (`transitions.ts`), inserted before the existing generic `else`, using `sequentialSeed()` instead of `snakeSeed()` for this one hop — Kings Valley's room sizes are already fixed exactly at generation time (no bye concept at all), so seeding into them precisely is correct and available, it just wasn't being used for this specific transition. Verified live with a fresh 17-player Kings Valley tournament: `structuralRooms` and `actualCountsByRoom` matched exactly (`[6,6,5]`) after the fix, where they hadn't before. **Not covered by a new unit test specific to this exact code path** — the existing `transitions.test.ts` Kings Valley tests exercise `advanceKingsValley()` directly, not this generic-path branch; this fix was verified via live browser testing only. A unit test for this transition is worth adding in a future pass.

### Bug #2 — the "None" pooling phase's mandatory warm-up rounds poisoned every future-round Bracket projection by default
Found via `get_page_text` showing every future round of a 37-player single-elimination Bracket tab rendering only "—" dashes, despite the lucky-loser disclaimer text (a separate mechanism) rendering correctly. **Root cause**: `projectFutureRoundSlots()`'s (`src/domain/tournament/bracket.ts`) early bail-out for any round fed by an `isNoElim` predecessor both nulled that round's own projection *and* added it to the `unresolved` poison set, and poisoning propagates transitively to anything built on top of an unresolved round. The default "None" pooling phase always starts with 2 mandatory `isNoElim` warm-up rounds — so by default, every tournament's *entire* rest-of-bracket projection was poisoned from round 1 onward, a much bigger blast radius than the "reasonable simplification" it looked like in isolation.

Before approving the fix, the organiser asked a precise clarifying question: does a warm-up round's scores actually influence which room a player lands in next, or is the projection correctly giving up on genuinely random placement? Verified by re-tracing (not assuming) `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()`/`orderRoomByScore()`/`snakeSeed()`: a no-elim room's occupants **are** ranked by score before the whole room is hand off to the next round (`advancingFromRoom = scored.length`, i.e. everyone advances, but still in ranked order), and that within-room rank **does** carry forward into the next round's seeding — a real, if partial, "fold by within-room rank" equalization, not full randomness. Caveat confirmed: it requires real scores to actually be entered (ties at fallback=0 otherwise), and it isn't cumulative across multiple warm-up rounds (each transition uses only that round's own scores).

**Fix**: removed the `isNoElim`-predecessor early bail-out in `projectFutureRoundSlots()`, replaced with a real per-room projection (every position of every room projected using that room's own size, since room sizes can differ e.g. `[8,8,7,7,7]`) — the same `room-rank` shape every other room-based round already gets, just with every position filled instead of a top-N cut. One pre-existing test's expectation was stale as a direct consequence (a "mid-pooling-phase hop" test that used to assert `null`, since qual-table rounds are also `isNoElim` internally and this fix correctly generalizes to them too) — corrected, not just patched around. Two new tests added. Full suite: 189/189 passing (12 in `bracket.test.ts`, up from 10). Live-verified: the same 37-player tournament's Bracket tab now shows real "Room N, Rank K"/"Winner of Room N" projections through every future round, with lucky-loser tokens correctly interspersed.

### Bug #3 — a projected room could show fewer (or silently more) slots than its own declared size
Found while reviewing Bug #2's fix, before reporting it done: some individual rooms within an otherwise-successful projected round showed a stray "—" dash for one slot despite the room header declaring a specific size (e.g. Round 2's two size-8 rooms each showed only 7 real labels + 1 dash, while the size-7 rooms showed exactly 7, no dash). **Root cause**: the exact same class of bug as Bug #1, but in the projection display specifically — `projectFutureRoundSlots()` seeded its synthetic token pool via `snakeSeed(candidates, round.rooms.length)` (count-only, boustrophedon bounce), then bucketed by whichever room each token's bounce-assigned position landed in; for 37 people across rooms sized `[8,8,7,7,7]`, that bounce actually produces `[7,7,7,8,8]`, so two rooms fall one slot short (rendered as a dash) while the overflow silently lands in a differently-sized room and is dropped by `PlaceholderRound`'s render loop, which is bounded by each room's own *structural* slot count.

Reported to the organiser with the wider framing this implied: `snakeSeed`'s count-only bounce is a property of every real transition that uses it too (not just this projection), including live single-elimination seeding — it happens to be harmless there today only because single-elimination's promotion count is a single number applied uniformly per room, never sensitive to an individual room's actual size the way Kings Valley's per-room band counts are. The organiser flagged this is exactly the "does `snakeSeed` produce genuinely equitable rooms" question they'd separately raised after Bug #2's explanation, and asked to defer that broader question — see "Parked: is `snakeSeed`'s reseeding actually equitable?" below — while shipping just the narrow display fix now (their explicit choice, option (a) over holding the whole thing).

**Fix**: `projectFutureRoundSlots()` now chunks its token pool directly into `round.rooms`' declared sizes via `sequentialSeed()` (room-major, no bounce) instead of mirroring `snakeSeed()`'s imprecise distribution — every projected room's slot count now always matches its own declared size exactly, with a code comment documenting the accepted tradeoff: the *real* transition still seeds via `snakeSeed()` unchanged, so a projected room's specific contents can drift slightly from what actually lands there once the round is reached. Two existing tests' expected values were hand-recomputed and corrected to match the new (simpler, non-bounced) chunking; one test description corrected. Full suite: 189/189 passing. Live-verified after a `node_modules/.vite` cache clear + full dev-server restart (a stale Vite module graph initially threw a leftover `ReferenceError: snakeSeed is not defined` from before the restart — confirmed via a fresh, never-navigated browser tab that the live app itself had zero errors, the message was stale console-buffer noise, not a real bug) — every room in every round of the 37-player tournament, from Round 2 through the Final, showed a slot count exactly matching its own declared size, zero dashes, zero silent overflow.

### Parked: is `snakeSeed`'s reseeding actually equitable? (organiser-flagged, explicitly deferred)
The organiser said, after seeing Bug #2/#3's explanations: *"We will need to talk again about that snake-seeding though, based on your explanations I am not sure that it provides equitable rooms throughout the tournament."* Not investigated or resolved this session — flagged here so a future session picks it up as its own topic rather than folding it into whatever unrelated work is happening at the time. The open question, as best understood from this session's own findings: `snakeSeed`'s within-room-rank-carries-forward mechanism (confirmed real in Bug #2's investigation) only preserves rank *within a single room*, never a true cross-room comparison, and isn't cumulative across multiple rounds — so two players with identical global standing could still end up on meaningfully different room-strength trajectories depending on which room they happened to start in. Needs its own dedicated investigation, not assumptions carried over from this entry.

**Resolved 2026-09-12 — see "Diversity + rematch-aware room reseeding" above.** `snakeSeed` was replaced by a new `tieredSeed` mechanism at the app's main generic transition call site (no-elim warm-up, Qualification Table, real single-elimination/team-format cuts) — per-room-rank tiering (fixing extremes-clustering), a greedy rematch-aware assignment search against full match history (fixing round-to-round staleness), mild recency weighting, and a diversity-to-balance taper approaching Semis. Double-elimination's WB/LB routing still uses `snakeSeed` unchanged — explicitly deferred as its own future piece of work, not resolved by this fix.

### Item #6 (impossible tournament-setting combinations) — live-verified, no bugs found
Reaching the Setup screen required clearing `curveFFA_state_v1` from `localStorage` directly rather than using the app's own "Reset"/"Clear roster" buttons — both are gated behind a native `confirm()` dialog, which the Claude Browser tool automatically declines (a known environment limitation noted earlier this project's history, not an app bug); the CFP admin login session itself was left untouched. All of Stage A/B's guards (see "Prevent impossible tournament-setting combinations — Stage A" / "— Stage B" above) were exercised directly against the real Setup UI and behaved exactly as those entries describe, no bugs found:
- **A1**: Pooling Phase correctly hides Swiss/Group Stage for `ffa-individual`; picking Swiss on `team-3v3` then switching format to FFA correctly resets Pooling Phase back to "None" instead of leaving a stale selection.
- **A2**: on `team-3v3` with Double elimination selected, switching Odd-count strategy to "Flex" correctly falls Schedule Logic back to Single elimination and swaps the dropdown to offer the FFA/Team double-elimination variant instead of the incompatible 1v1-style one.
- **A3**: Group Stage + Double elimination with 2 groups × 1 qualifier (seedTotal 2, needs ≥4) gave a clean rejection (*"Can't build a double-elimination bracket — only 2 players would actually enter the bracket phase (needs at least 4)."*); raising qualifiers to 2 generated successfully end-to-end (Group Stage rounds → WB/LB → Final).
- **B**: negative Semis override (-5) rejected (*"Semis size override must be a positive number — got -5."*); an oversized Final override (64) with no Semis override set rejected against the derived default Semis size (4); an oversized Group size (50) rejected against the upper bound (5).

### Testing performed
Full domain suite (`pnpm test`) run clean at 189/189 after Bugs #2 and #3's fixes (Bug #1 has no dedicated new unit test, see above). `eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on every changed file (`src/domain/tournament/transitions.ts`, `src/domain/tournament/bracket.ts`, `src/domain/tournament/__tests__/bracket.test.ts`). Live verification throughout via direct `localStorage` inspection and real Setup-UI interaction against a real CFP admin login, not just static code review.

### Explicitly out of scope
The parked `snakeSeed`-equity question (see above) — a future, separate conversation. A dedicated unit test for Bug #1's exact transition path (`transitions.ts`'s generic-seeding-path `isKingsValley` branch) — flagged as worth adding, not added this session. Items #7 (roster reset + duplicate-name blocking) were re-confirmed working as already documented but surfaced no new findings, so they aren't given their own subsection here — see "Reset roster button + disallow duplicate player/team names" above for the original build.

## Kings Valley (done — 2026-09-11)
A brand-new bracket/schedule-logic type, requested directly: rooms ranked top-to-bottom; each round, the top finishers in a room climb to the room above, the bottom finishers drop to the room below, the middle stays, and elimination happens only from the *bottom* room's bottom finishers — continuing until the field converges to a single Final room. `'kings-valley'` had existed as a reserved `ScheduleLogicKey` literal and a permanently-disabled Setup option ("coming soon") since the rework, with `generateTournament()` rejecting it outright — no generation or live-advancement logic existed anywhere. Per the organiser's own framing ("the app is expected to figure out the balance... until we reach the finals"), the exact promotion/demotion pacing and room-count convergence were left for this build to design, not pre-specified.

**Research before any design work**: three parallel Explore agents (every place a `ScheduleLogicKey` variant is registered/branched on, from `types.ts` through `generation.ts`/`schedule-generation.ts`/`SetupView.tsx`/`bracket.ts`; single/double-elimination's generation-time room math and live-advancement mechanics, specifically whether anything in the codebase already supported *persistent* per-player room membership across rounds; Bracket rendering + `computeRankings()`'s elimination-detection extensibility). **The central finding**: every existing seeding mechanism (`snakeSeed`, `randomSeed`, `swissFoldPair`) discards prior-round room membership and reseeds fresh from a flat global rank/standings list each round — Kings Valley's "move relative to your current room" mechanic had no partial version to extend; it's genuinely new machinery. The closest structural precedent was double-elimination's `winnersTo`/`losersTo` + `pendingBracketSeeds` routing pattern (multiple streams merging into one destination), needed at room-granularity instead of round-granularity. A second finding worked in Kings Valley's favor: `computeRankings()`'s existing elimination signal ("absent from next round's assignments," used for any round without `round.bracket` set) already correctly treats "still present next round, just demoted to a different room" as *not eliminated* — confirmed directly, no changes needed there beyond a small, tightly-scoped tiebreak addition (see below).

**The algorithm, designed and hand-verified before any code was written** (traced a 37-player FFA example through several rounds by hand to confirm sane convergence, catching and fixing a too-slow-pacing mistake in the process — see below): a room's occupants split by rank into a promote band (top), a demote band (bottom), and a stay band (middle) — except room 1's promote band redirects to itself (nowhere higher to go), and the bottom room's demote band is eliminated instead of demoted (nowhere lower to go). Band sizes: `bandCount(roomSize, fraction) = max(1, round(roomSize * fraction))`, with promotion/demotion everywhere using `KINGS_VALLEY_MOVE_FRACTION = 0.25` but the bottom room's cut using a separate, larger `KINGS_VALLEY_ELIMINATION_FRACTION = 0.5`. **Two fractions, not one, because the first hand-trace (using 0.25 for elimination too) converged far too slowly** — roughly 15+ rounds for a 37-player field, since population only shrinks via the bottom room's own small band each round while every other room stays roughly size-stable. Concentrating a larger cut in the bottom room specifically brought a 37-player field down to a Final in ~10 rounds instead, capped at `MAX_KINGS_VALLEY_ROUNDS = 14` with a graceful force-termination (whatever remains becomes the Final) if a pathological config never naturally converges. Room sizes are recomputed fresh each round via the existing `distributeRooms()` (same function every other schedule already uses) rather than tracked as a persistent, potentially-out-of-bounds per-room population — this means room *count* naturally shrinks as eliminations reduce the total, with no explicit "merge two rooms" step needed anywhere. The formula degenerates cleanly for head-to-head (room size 2) formats — promote 1 / demote-or-eliminate 1 / zero stay, a clean win-climbs/lose-drops ladder — so **Kings Valley needed no format-compatibility gate at all**, unlike either double-elimination variant.

A Plan agent then turned the hand-verified algorithm into exact, source-checked TypeScript, staged into four independently-checkpointed builds mirroring how "FFA/Team double-elimination generalization" (this app's previous largest single feature) was staged — the organiser explicitly asked to run through all four without stopping for check-ins this time, so all four landed in one session:

**Stage 1 — Generation.** New `src/domain/tournament/kings-valley.ts`: `kingsValleyBracketPhase(seedTotal, startRoundNum, config)` loops round-by-round, computing `distributeRooms()`-derived room sizes and per-room promote/demote/eliminate band counts, until the population fits one room (the dedicated Final round, `numGames` from the same `finalsGames` config every other schedule uses) or the round cap forces early termination. New additive `TournamentRound` fields: `isKingsValley`, `kvPromoteCounts`, `kvDemoteCounts`, `kvEliminateCount`. Wired into `schedule-generation.ts`'s dispatch switch and `getMinimumBracketUnits` (widened to accept `'kings-valley'`, reusing the existing generic `2 * roomSize.ideal` floor); the `generation.ts` rejection block deleted outright — no new validation needed, since every existing schedule-specific check is already scoped to the *other* schedule keys and never fires for Kings Valley. `mutations.ts`'s `rebuildFutureRounds` had an `Exclude<..., 'kings-valley'>` cast that was actively wrong (would have silently dropped Kings Valley from the no-elim-warmup roster-rebuild path) — removed.

**Stage 2 — Live advancement.** New `kingsValleyComputeAdvancement()` (`advancement.ts`) merges each room's promote/stay/demote-or-eliminate bands into one flat best-to-worst survivor order — hand-verified directly: every room's promote band counted exactly once (as inflow to the room above, or redirected to itself for room 1), every demote band exactly once (as inflow to the room below), the bottom room's cut band excluded from the merge entirely. New `sequentialSeed()` (`seeding.ts`) — unlike `snakeSeed`, doesn't reorder anything, just slices the already-ladder-ordered survivor list into the next round's already-precomputed room sizes. Dispatched from a new `advanceKingsValley()` in `transitions.ts`, alongside the existing `round.bracket` branch. `computeRankings()` (`rankings.ts`) gained a small, tightly-scoped fix: eliminated-unit entries now carry an optional `room` field, populated **only** when `round.isKingsValley` (never for single-elimination/Swiss/qual-table, whose room numbers are parallel seeding partitions, not strength-ordered — populating it there would have silently changed their existing, correct tie-break order), used as a tiebreak ahead of the existing `pct` comparison so a same-round elimination from a room closer to the top outranks one from further down.

**Stage 3 — Setup UI.** One line: removed `disabled` from the already-existing Kings Valley `<option>`. Confirmed by reading `changeFormat()`/`changeOddCountStrategy()` and every `scheduleLogic`-gated field in full — no new field, no new fallback-reconciliation branch needed, since Kings Valley is never invalidated by a format/odd-count-strategy change (it's compatible with every format) and every existing field-visibility gate (`isSingle`/`isRace`/`isShared`) already correctly excludes it from the Semis/Final-override/LB-qualifier fields while including it in "Finals format."

**Stage 4 — Rendering.** `BracketView.tsx`: new `promote`/`demote`/`stay` result-kind variants on the existing row-coloring scheme; a new `KingsValleyDisclaimer` component (copies the lucky-loser disclaimer's exact pattern — a static, per-room explanatory box computed purely from the round's stored band counts) wired into both the reached-round and future-round render paths; the per-room row classification gained a `round.isKingsValley` branch reusing every other piece of the existing scaffolding (tie badges, team rendering, score inputs) unchanged. `bracket.ts`: `bracketRoundLabels()` gained a "Round N (Kings Valley)" branch; `projectFutureRoundSlots()` gained an **explicit** `round.isKingsValley` bail-out rather than relying on the incidental token-count-mismatch fallback — without it, a Kings Valley round whose predecessor was the last pooling round would have hit the "qualifier-cutoff" branch first and shown a misleading "seed TBD" label instead of a plain dash. `RunningAdminScores.tsx`: **a real bug caught and fixed, not just a feature gap** — the existing lucky-loser computation was guarded only by `!round.bracket`, which Kings Valley rounds also satisfy (they never set `round.bracket` either), so without an explicit `&& !round.isKingsValley` guard, Kings Valley rounds would have silently run the single-pool cross-room lucky-loser mechanism against band data it was never designed for. New ▲ Promotes/— Stays/▼ Demotes/☠ Eliminated status derivation, reusing the table shell/tie-handling/score-input wiring verbatim. `src/components/ui/Table.tsx` gained a new `warning` row tone (mirroring the color `Badge` already uses for its own `warning` tone) so a "Demotes" row has a semantically correct color instead of reusing an unrelated tone.

### Testing performed
`eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on every changed/new file across all four stages. **187 domain tests, up from 170 at the start of this work** — 19 new: 6 in a new `kings-valley.test.ts` (the hand-traced band-count example, immediate-Final collapse, head-to-head degeneration, population conservation across a multi-round run, the force-termination cap, `getMinimumBracketUnits`), 2 new `generateTournament` integration tests, 2 new `kingsValleyComputeAdvancement` tests (a hand-derived 3-room merge and a 2-room top/bottom edge case, both matching the hand trace exactly on the first run), 3 new `sequentialSeed` tests, 2 new `advanceTournamentRound` integration tests (one chaining two Kings Valley rounds, one advancing directly into the Final), 2 new `rankings.ts` tests (the room-depth tiebreak firing for Kings Valley, and a regression confirming it's inert for every other schedule). Every hand-derived expectation matched the implementation's actual output on the first test run, confirming the earlier by-hand algorithm verification was accurate, not just lucky. Dev server verified to boot clean with zero console errors; a full live click-through (generating and playing an actual Kings Valley tournament through Admin) wasn't done this session — same CFP-login blocker noted throughout this project's history.

### Explicitly out of scope
Per-slot future-round room-of-origin projection for Kings Valley — the existing `projectFutureRoundSlots` model is a global-pool reseed, confirmed not extensible to a local persistent-membership room ladder; future Kings Valley rounds show room sizes and the static disclaimer, but individual slots stay blank dashes, matching the same graceful-degradation pattern already used for Swiss/pooling-cutoff cases. Any new organiser-facing Setup field for movement pace or starting room count — confirmed unnecessary, matching this app's "figure out the balance automatically" principle already applied to every other format. A dedicated Bracket accent color for Kings Valley rounds — uses the same unaccented styling as plain single-elimination rounds, a pure visual polish that can be added later without touching any logic. Compatibility restrictions by game format or pooling phase — Kings Valley works for every implemented format and every pooling phase with zero gating needed.

**Bug found and fixed 2026-09-12 — see "Live-testing pass" above**: the one-time hop from a no-elim/pooling round into the very first Kings Valley round used generic `snakeSeed`-based seeding (count-only, no per-room-size awareness), which could leave a room's actual occupancy off by one from the structural size its own promote/demote/eliminate bands were computed against. Every Kings-Valley-to-Kings-Valley hop was unaffected (already used `sequentialSeed`). Fixed by routing this one hop through `sequentialSeed` too.

## Bracket transparency: lucky-loser disclosure + projected room-of-origin (done — 2026-09-11)
Two related organiser requests for the player/spectator-facing Bracket tab (`src/features/bracket/BracketView.tsx`): (1) a disclaimer explaining how/why lucky losers qualify, plus the live calculation deciding who's currently leading for those spot(s), visible under the bracket for the round it applies to; (2) future rounds' bare dashed placeholder slots replaced with a label saying which room each slot is expected to come from, both for convenience and so players/spectators can independently verify the room-seeding math isn't quietly changed mid-tournament.

Investigated via two parallel Explore agents (the lucky-loser mechanism and how it's surfaced today across BracketView/ScoreboardView/RunningAdminScores; how future rounds render today and what's structurally knowable per schedule-logic/pooling-phase combination), confirmed directly against live source, then a Plan agent turned the confirmed facts into exact function bodies before any code was written — staged into two independently-checkpointed builds per this project's per-item check-in convention, since the second piece is materially larger/riskier than the first.

**Key finding driving the whole design**: `TournamentRound.advPerRoom`/`.luckyCount` are set once at schedule-generation time from room/seat counts alone (`luckyCount = <target> % <room count>`) — completely independent of live scores, and always `0` for pooling/no-elim/Final rounds by construction. So a single general predicate, `round.luckyCount > 0 && !round.isNoElim`, already correctly and exclusively targets single-elimination rounds, Semis, and double-elimination WB/LB rounds with zero schedule-logic branching anywhere in either build.

### Part 1 — Lucky-loser disclaimer + live standings
New `computeLuckyLoserStandings(state, roundIndex)` in `advancement.ts`, reusing the existing (already-private) `luckyLoserCandidate`/`scoreRoom`/`orderRoomByScore` helpers verbatim: ranks every room's near-miss candidate by their score as a share of their own room's total, live, marking the top `luckyCount` as `leading`. Works identically for single-elimination/Semis rounds and double-elimination WB/LB rounds — `doubleEliminationComputeAdvancement` already treats both bracket sides the same way, so no `round.bracket` check was needed. A static, structural explanation (`LuckyLoserDisclaimer`) now renders on any round — reached, current, or future — with a nonzero `luckyCount`; a live "Lucky loser race" standings panel (`LuckyLoserStandingsPanel`) renders additionally, current-round-only, updating as scores are entered.

### Part 2 — Projected room-of-origin for future rounds
Two real findings shaped the scope before any algorithm was designed:
- **Group-stage future rounds already have their exact real pairings computed at generation time** (`round.matches`/`round.groupByes`, `pooling.ts`) — sitting unused, since `PlaceholderRound` discarded them for generic dashes. This is a pure accuracy fix, not a projection — `PlaceholderRound` now renders the real names for group-stage rounds directly.
- **Room-of-origin for single/double-elimination is structurally derivable one hop at a time**, by replaying the same ordering rules `roomBasedComputeAdvancement`/`doubleEliminationComputeAdvancement` already use (direct `advPerRoom` slots per room, room order, plus `luckyCount` extra slots of unknown room appended last) through the real, already-exported `snakeSeed` — reused directly by feeding it synthetic tokens instead of real names, not reimplemented. New `projectFutureRoundSlots(state)` (`bracket.ts`) scans the whole `rounds` array once, resolving each future round's predecessor(s) via `winnersTo`/`losersTo` (or the implicit "next round" edge for non-bracket rounds — a double-elimination target can receive contributions from more than one source round, verified directly in `transitions.ts`'s pending-seed accumulation).

**Where the design deliberately doesn't guess**: a losers-bracket round's per-room attribution is genuinely fuzzy whenever its WB source has `luckyCount > 0` (exactly which near-miss finisher gets plucked into the lucky-winner pool is score-dependent, so the loser pool's *room* isn't knowable ahead of time) — so every LB-originated edge gets a round-identity label instead ("Dropped from WB Round 2" / "Advanced from LB Round 1"), sidestepping the ambiguity entirely while staying 100% accurate. Pooling-phase cutoffs (qual-table/Swiss/group-stage → the first bracket round) get one uniform label, "Qualifier from standings (seed TBD)" — that transition is a single ranked cutoff across the whole standings table, never room-attributable. Swiss rounds are untouched (pairing is decided live, already correctly flagged via the existing `pairingTBD` → "Pairings determined live"). Any hop where the structural token count doesn't match the target round's actual slot count — an odd-count "bye" pick, or the double-elimination first round's bracket-size bye concentration, neither of which is modeled — falls back to the plain `—` dash, and that fallback **poisons every round downstream of it in the same lineage** (once a hop's room layout is unknown, nothing built on top of it can be trusted either) rather than guessing.

### Testing performed
`eslint`/`prettier --check` clean on every changed/new file. **170 domain tests, up from 160** — 4 new for `computeLuckyLoserStandings` (ranking/leading order, the three `null`-guard cases, zero-score-room exclusion, confirmed unmodified for a double-elimination losers-bracket round) and 10 new for `projectFutureRoundSlots` in a new `src/domain/tournament/__tests__/bracket.test.ts` (the plain one-hop case cross-checked against `snakeSeed`'s known boustrophedon order; `advPerRoom > 1` room-major/rank-minor ordering with lucky tokens appended last; WB-losers-edge and LB-winners-edge round-identity labeling; both pooling-cutoff cases, deliberately constructed with a nonsense `advPerRoom`/`luckyCount` on the source round to prove they're ignored; Swiss and group-stage target exclusion; a mid-pooling-phase hop; a constructed token-count mismatch confirming both the `null` fallback and poison propagation to a further downstream round; multi-predecessor concatenation order). Dev server verified to boot clean and the Bracket tab renders with zero console errors in both its empty state and after triggering the real CFP admin sign-in modal (which appeared correctly, confirming the same login gate noted throughout this project's history) — a full live click-through against a real generated tournament with a nonzero lucky-loser count wasn't done this session for that reason; the domain-layer tests are the primary correctness backstop given how much of this build's real risk lives in the projection algorithm's edge cases, all of which are exercised directly by hand-derived worked examples.

### Explicitly out of scope
Attempting room-of-origin precision for a `isNoElim` predecessor (warm-up rounds) — falls back to the plain dash rather than a coarser "Room N, everyone advances" label; a reasonable future enhancement, not attempted here to keep the algorithm's rule set general rather than special-cased. Any UI change to `ScoreboardView.tsx`/`RunningAdminScores.tsx` — both already show a structural lucky-loser disclaimer of their own; this build only touched the Bracket tab, per the organiser's request. Predicting real player/team identities for a projected slot (only the room of origin is projected, never who's expected to win it).

**Two bugs found and fixed 2026-09-12 — see "Live-testing pass" above**: the `isNoElim`-predecessor bail-out described just above turned out to both null *and* poison the projection, and since the default "None" pooling phase's mandatory 2-round warm-up is itself `isNoElim`, this poisoned the entire rest of every default-configuration tournament's projections, not just the specific "attempting room-of-origin precision for warm-up rounds" case this section called out as deliberately skipped. Fixed to give warm-up rounds a real per-room projection after all. Separately, the projection's `snakeSeed`-based room bucketing (described in Part 2 above) could show a room fewer or more slots than its own declared size — fixed by chunking directly into each room's declared size instead (a deliberate, documented divergence from the real transition, which still uses `snakeSeed`).

## Reset roster button + disallow duplicate player/team names (done — 2026-09-11)
Two small, independent organiser requests, planned and built together.

**Reset roster**: switching a tournament's game format (e.g. FFA → Teams) previously meant deleting every player/team one at a time via "Manage Players"/"Manage Teams" — the existing "↺ Reset" button (Admin, post-start) resets live *progress* (scores/rounds), not the roster, and doesn't exist pre-start anyway. New `resetRoster()` (`mutations.ts`), mirroring `resetTournamentState()`'s style but clearing the complementary field set — roster plus anything derived from it that would otherwise go stale (the generated schedule preview, if "Generate Schedule" was already clicked pre-start) — while deliberately preserving `title`/`gameFormat`/`scheduleLogic`/`cfg`. New "↺ Clear roster" button in `SetupView.tsx` next to "Load roster & reserves," disabled when there's nothing to clear. `SetupView` is only ever rendered pre-`started` (`AppShell.tsx`), so this is implicitly pre-start-only with no extra gating needed.

**Disallow duplicate names**: investigated via two parallel Explore agents before designing anything (every place a name enters the system, and existing reset/state-shape precedent). Confirmed a real active bug, not just a hypothetical: for individual-format players, the roster name *is* the literal lookup key everywhere — `finalScores` keys, final rankings (`Map`/`Set` keyed by name), tie-break resolution. Two players sharing a name today means entering one player's Finals/Grand-Final score silently overwrites/reads the other's. For team formats, `teamId` (not `teamName`) is the real key, so a duplicate team *name* is confusing but not data-corrupting — member names within a team are purely cosmetic.

**Organiser's explicit scope decision** (via `AskUserQuestion`): hard-block duplicates in individual player names (the real bug) *and* team names (reduces confusion for players/spectators) — explicitly **not** team member names, "deemed to be unnecessary complexity."

Before implementing, found duplicate-name handling across the ~9 entry points was inconsistent: two had a *silent* no-op (no feedback at all), one had a *bypassable* `window.confirm`, most had *zero* check, and one (`addWalkUpIndividual`) had a working check but reported the wrong reason to the UI (`'room-cap'` instead of a naming conflict).

**One real correction made during implementation, not blindly following the approved plan**: the plan (written before this level of tracing) assumed `swapIndividual()` had a "missing reserves check" gap parallel to `renameIndividual()`'s. Re-reading the actual code line by line during implementation showed this was wrong — `swapIndividual()` already *removes* the incoming name from `reserves` as part of the same operation (silently promoting a bench player if the typed name happens to match one, mirroring the "+" reserve-add button's effect), so no duplicate can ever exist in the final state. Adding a reserves-block there would have broken that legitimate, already-working behavior for no reason. Left `swapIndividual()`'s domain logic untouched; added only a narrower UI-level pre-check (active-roster conflict only) so the *existing* silent no-op at least gets a visible alert. `renameIndividual()`'s dual check (players + reserves) *is* correct and necessary for its own code shape (a rename doesn't remove the target from wherever it already lives, so a match would create a real duplicate) — confirmed this by tracing both functions' actual mutation logic, not assumed from the plan text.

**Changes**: two new pure helpers in `roster.ts` (`isDisplayNameTaken`, `findDuplicateDisplayNames`) used consistently across every entry point — `SetupView.tsx`'s initial roster/reserves load (both individual and team branches, checking within-list and across roster-vs-reserves duplicates in one pass), `addWalkUpIndividual`'s reason corrected to `'duplicate-name'`, `swapTeam()` gained a real `teamName` uniqueness check (previously only checked `teamId`), and `RunningAdminRoster.tsx`'s Rename/Swap (individual) and Rename team/Swap team handlers all pre-check and give a clear `window.alert` instead of a silent no-op or a bypassable confirm.

### Testing performed
`pnpm exec eslint`/`pnpm exec prettier --check` on all four changed files, clean. Full domain suite (`pnpm test`) run clean at 156/156 both before and after — no regression, and no new domain tests added (this feature is UI/mutation-layer work with no existing test infrastructure for either, matching the approved plan's own verification scope). Live UI click-through was offered but not exercised this session (same CFP-login blocker as prior builds) — code-review-level verification only.

### Explicitly out of scope
Team member-name duplicates — organiser's explicit decision, not a gap. Any change to `swapIndividual()`'s reserve-consumption side effect (confirmed legitimate during implementation, not touched).
