## Prevent impossible tournament-setting combinations — Stage A (done — 2026-09-11)
Follow-up to "Restrict Group Stage to head-to-head formats" below: the organiser asked for a full audit of every setting-combination gap in the same class, not just Group Stage (Swiss was flagged as a known example). Investigated via three parallel Explore agents (domain-layer `throw`/room-size-assumption audit, `generation.ts` validation inventory, `SetupView.tsx` field-by-field gating audit) before any code changed. Organiser confirmed both issue classes found (impossible combinations, and a separate numeric-input robustness class) should be planned — staged as two independent approvals; this entry covers Stage A, done in four checked-in sub-steps.

**Key structural finding**: bracket-phase builders (single/double/shared-final elimination) compute their entire round structure once, inside `generateTournament()`'s own try/catch — so a throw inside them was already caught and shown as an Alert, never a crash. Swiss is uniquely dangerous because it computes each round's pairing *lazily*, mid-tournament, via `swissFoldPair()` (throws if `roomSize.ideal !== 2`) called from `advanceTournamentRound()`, which had **zero try/catch** at its only call site (`RunningAdmin.tsx`) — that's why it crashes the live Admin panel instead of showing a clean error.

**A1 — Swiss + multi-way format (confirmed crash, same class as Group Stage).** Gated in `SetupView.tsx` (generalized `groupStageCompatible` → `headToHeadOnly`, now filters both `'swiss'` and `'group-stage'`; `changeFormat()`'s reset condition extended to cover both) and `generation.ts` (upfront rejection mirroring the Group Stage message exactly). **Adjacent hardening**: converted the three legacy-only `!roomSize` throws in `transitions.ts` into the existing `{status:'blocked', reason, message, state}` shape (matching the `pending-ties`/`malformed-final` precedent already in that file) instead of throwing, and wrapped `RunningAdmin.tsx`'s `advanceTournamentRound()` call site in a try/catch as a last-resort backstop — so even an unanticipated future throw during round-advance can no longer crash the whole Admin panel. 4 new tests (3 rejection cases + 1 regression) plus 3 new tests confirming the blocked-not-thrown behavior directly.

**A2 — Stale `oddCountStrategy` → `scheduleLogic` desync.** Found during the audit, not previously known: `changeFormat()` was the only place reconciling `scheduleLogic` against compatibility — the Odd-count-strategy `<select>`'s `onChange` used the generic setter, so picking `team-3v3` + `'double-elimination'` then switching Odd-count-strategy to `Flex` silently left a stale, now-invisible `scheduleLogic` value in state. Fixed with a dedicated `changeOddCountStrategy` handler reusing `changeFormat()`'s exact reset logic, plus two new `generation.ts` upfront checks (non-head-to-head format; `'flex'` strategy) giving a clean message instead of the raw `doubleEliminationBracketPhase requires a head-to-head room shape...` exception. 2 new tests; mutation-tested by disabling the new guard and confirming both cases were *still* rejected, just via the confusing deep error — proving the fix is about message quality, not just correctness.

**A3 — Two double-elimination reachability gaps.** Both reachable through ordinary Setup UI clicks, previously only caught by the generic catch-all: Group Stage + Double Elimination with too few post-pooling qualifiers (`winnersRoundCount < 2` deep in `double-elimination.ts`), and an oversized Final-size override on `double-elimination-shared-final` (`requestedRounds < 1`). Fixed by hoisting a single correct `bracketEntryCount` value (the real post-pooling entrant count, accounting for Group Stage's independent groups×qualifiers formula) reused across three places that previously each recomputed or mis-derived it — **this also fixed a related latent bug not explicitly in scope**: the existing `semisOverride > overrideEntryCount` check was comparing against `config.qualAdv` even for Group Stage, where `qualAdv` is entirely unused/meaningless. Two new upfront checks added using the corrected value. 2 new tests; both mutation tests confirmed the hand-derived numbers exactly matched the real deep exception messages (`seedTotal 2`; `seedTotal 16, wbQualifiers 28`) before being caught cleanly instead.

**A4 — Empirical smoke-check (verification only, no fix needed).** `double-elimination-shared-final` + Group Stage, and + Swiss, both looked structurally fine on paper during the audit but were never executed. Added 2 new end-to-end anchor tests (`team-3v3` + `'flex'`, the only way either combination is UI-reachable for a head-to-head format) confirming both actually generate correctly — including catching and fixing an unrelated sizing mistake in the tests themselves (finalOverride sized in teams, not players, tripped the hard room-cap check for team-3v3's 3-per-team size).

**Domain suite**: 150 tests (up from 148 at the end of the previous build), all passing. Every new `generation.ts` guard mutation-tested. Eslint/prettier clean throughout.

### Explicitly out of scope (Stage A)
The third `double-elimination.ts` throw (losers-bracket routing failure inside `sharedFinalDoubleEliminationBracketPhase`) — no verified formula for its exact trigger condition was derived, so it still falls through to the generic catch-all rather than a polished upfront message; noted, not guessed at. Stage B (numeric-input robustness — negative overrides, missing upper bounds, an incomplete override-ordering check) — separate approval, not started as part of this entry.

---

## Prevent impossible tournament-setting combinations — Stage B (done — 2026-09-11)
Second and final stage of the audit started in Stage A above — numeric-input robustness, approved separately per this project's per-item check-in convention.

**Root cause, same across every field in scope**: every `type='number'` input in `SetupView.tsx` is unconstrained at runtime (`generate()` is a plain button click, not a form submit — HTML `min`/`max` never actually enforce anything), and `generation.ts`'s `parsed()` helper (`Number.parseInt(value,10) || fallback`) only replaces a *falsy* parse result (zero or `NaN`) with the fallback — a negative value like `"-5"` parses successfully and stays truthy, so it slips straight through untouched. Every existing bounds check on these fields only tested *relative* ordering (X vs Y), never plain positivity, so a negative value skipped them all.

**Investigated each field individually before adding a check** (not a blanket fix) — two fields turned out to already be safe and were correctly left alone:
- `lbQualifiers` already had a real positivity check (`!(lbQualifiers >= 1)`) — no new work needed.
- `qualAdv` looked risky but isn't — it's always run through `Math.min(Math.max(config.qualAdv, floorMin), config.n)`, and `Math.max` already absorbs a negative value up to `floorMin` with no crash risk. Decided to leave its existing clamp-not-reject behavior as-is (a deliberate, low-stakes judgment call, per the plan) rather than convert it to a hard rejection like every other override — the clamp is a genuine, safe convenience feature here, not a gap.
- `groupSize`'s existing lower-bound check (`< GROUP_SIZE_BOUNDS.min`, min=3) already excludes every negative value too — no separate positivity check needed, only the missing **upper bound** was a real gap.

**Six real gaps fixed**, each with its own upfront `generationError(...)` check in `generation.ts`:
1. Negative `semisOverride` — worst previously-possible case: `distributeRooms` returns `[]` for a non-positive count → `Math.floor(finalSize / 0)` → `Infinity`/`NaN` baked silently into the Semis round's `advPerRoom`.
2. Negative `finalOverride` — same class of risk.
3. Oversized `finalOverride` with **no** `semisOverride` set — previously only checked *against* `semisOverride` when the latter was truthy; now also compared against the derived default Semis size (`2 × roomSize.ideal`) when it isn't set.
4. `groupSize` exceeding `GROUP_SIZE_BOUNDS.max` — only the lower bound was ever enforced.
5. Negative `qualifiersPerGroup` — the existing `groupSize <= qualifiersPerGroup` relative check doesn't catch a negative value (a negative number is always `<` a positive `groupSize`, so the comparison silently passes).
6. Negative `grandFinalWbTarget`/`grandFinalLbTarget` — hoisted out of an inline `parsed()` call inside the `gamemodeConfig` object literal into named variables (matching how `lbQualifiers` was already handled) so they could be validated before use; scoped to `schedule === 'double-elimination'` (the only schedule where these fields are actually read) so a stale value left over from a different schedule selection can never block an unrelated generation — same "don't reject on dead data" principle Stage A's own `grandFinalWbTarget`/`LbTarget` audit finding established.

**Also moved `const roomSize = deriveRoomSize(...)` earlier in the function** (was previously computed only just before the double-elimination floor check) so the new `finalOverride`-vs-default-Semis-size check (#3 above) could use it — a pure reordering, no behavior change to anything that already used it.

**6 new tests**, one per gap. Mutation-tested all six: each guard temporarily disabled, confirmed the correct test (and only that test) failed, then reverted — `git diff --stat` confirmed zero residual diff after each revert.

**Domain suite**: 156 tests (up from 150 at the end of Stage A), all passing. Eslint/prettier clean.

### Explicitly out of scope (Stage B)
No change to `qualAdv`'s existing clamp-not-reject behavior (deliberate, explained above). No change to any UI-side HTML `min`/`max` attribute in `SetupView.tsx` — those were already known to be cosmetic-only; the real fix belongs in `generation.ts`, which is where it landed. This closes both stages of the impossible-tournament-setting-combinations audit — no further stages planned.

---

## Restrict Group Stage to head-to-head formats (done — 2026-09-11)
Closes an organiser request: Group Stage removed as a selectable pooling phase for `ffa-individual`, `team-2v2v2v2`, `team-3v3v3` — kept only for strictly head-to-head formats (`individual-1v1`, `team-3v3`, and any future 2v2/4v4-style duel format), since round-robin scheduling only makes sense as a series of 2-competitor duels.

**Investigated before fixing (two Explore agents + direct reads of the exact current code), and this turned out to be a real latent bug, not a hypothetical one**: `groupStagePoolingPhase()` (`src/domain/tournament/pooling.ts`) and everything it calls take no format/room-size parameter at all — they always build literal pairwise duel rooms (`GroupStageMatch.pair: [string, string]`, always exactly 2 occupants), regardless of the selected game format. So before this fix, picking Group Stage with FFA or either multi-way team format silently ignored that format's real room size and forced every group-stage room down to a 1v1-style duel — confirmed via a mutation test (temporarily disabling the new guard): those three format/pooling-phase combinations previously generated successfully (`status: 'generated'`) rather than erroring, producing the wrong room shape with no indication anything was off. No gate existed anywhere — not in the Setup UI's Pooling Phase dropdown (always listed all four options regardless of format) and not in `generateTournament()`'s validation (only checked group-size math, never format compatibility).

**Reused an exact existing precedent** rather than inventing a new pattern: this app already gates "Schedule Logic" (Single/Double elimination) by format compatibility in `SetupView.tsx`, using a hide-incompatible-options-entirely convention (`raceCompatible`/`scheduleOptions` built via `useMemo`, plus a fallback-to-a-safe-default in `changeFormat()` when the format change makes the current selection invalid). The new Pooling Phase gate replicates this exactly, keyed off the same `format?.idealRoomSize === 2` predicate that already exclusively identifies the two head-to-head formats today — no new field/allowlist needed, and it will automatically cover any future head-to-head format the app adds (a hypothetical `team-2v2`/`team-4v4`) without further changes.

**Changes**:
1. `src/features/admin/SetupView.tsx` — new `groupStageCompatible`/`poolingPhaseOptions` (mirroring `raceCompatible`/`scheduleOptions`), the dropdown now maps over the filtered option list instead of 4 static `<option>`s, and `changeFormat()` resets an in-progress `'group-stage'` selection to `'none'` when switching to an incompatible format.
2. `src/domain/tournament/generation.ts` — defense-in-depth: `generateTournament()` now refuses `poolingPhase: 'group-stage'` outright when `format.idealRoomSize !== 2`, with a clear message naming the format and the reason, so a stale/tampered `poolingPhase` value can never slip past the UI gate.
3. New test coverage in `src/domain/tournament/__tests__/generation.test.ts` (previously zero group-stage coverage existed anywhere in this suite): 3 rejection cases (one per disallowed format, table-driven via `it.each`) and 1 regression case confirming Group Stage still generates correctly end-to-end for `individual-1v1`.

No changes needed to `pooling.ts`'s actual scheduling math, `advancement.ts`, `transitions.ts`, `seeding.ts`, `schedule-generation.ts`, `mutations.ts`, `bracket.ts`, or `rankings.ts` — all already 100% format-agnostic and will simply never receive a `group-stage` config for a disallowed format now that it's gated upstream.

### Testing performed
`pnpm exec eslint`/`pnpm exec prettier --check` on all three changed/added files, both clean. Full domain suite (`pnpm test`) run clean at 137/137 (up from 133 — 4 new tests). Mutation-testing sanity check on the new `generation.ts` guard (temporarily disabled it, confirmed the 3 rejection tests correctly failed with `'generated'` instead of `'invalid'` — proving the guard is genuinely load-bearing and the previous silent-success behavior was real, not assumed — then reverted, confirmed via `git diff --stat` showing exactly the intended 5-line addition). Live UI click-through was offered but declined as unnecessary given the change reuses an exact, already-proven pattern in the same file.

### Explicitly out of scope
The Swiss + multi-way-format bug (a separate, pre-existing issue — `swissFoldPair()` throws uncaught when advancing into Swiss round 2 for a non-head-to-head format; not introduced or worsened by this change, flagged in `HANDOFF.md` for a future pass). Any change to `pooling.ts`'s round-robin scheduling math or any other already-format-agnostic domain file.

## Tighten the silent lucky-loser catch sites (done — 2026-09-10)
Closes `HANDOFF.md`'s long-standing "Silent error-swallowing around lucky-loser computation" gap and "Immediate next priorities" item 4. `RunningAdminScores.tsx` (`RoomScores`) and `ScoreboardView.tsx` both call `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()` (`src/domain/tournament/advancement.ts`) wrapped in `try { ... } catch { luckyNames = [] }` — any error there rendered identically to a legitimate "no lucky losers this round" state: every non-top-N player showed a confident "Eliminated" badge in Admin, or was silently excluded from `advances` on the public Scoreboard.

**Investigated before fixing, not assumed**: an Explore agent (then independently re-confirmed by direct reads of both component files and `advancement.ts`) found `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()`'s entire call graph — `isLastStandingsRound`, `computeQualificationStandings`, `applyQualCutoffOrder`, `detectQualCutoffTie`, `computeGroupStageAdvancement`, `computeGroupStandings`, `scoreRoom`, `orderRoomByScore`, `groupByScore`, `tieResolutionList`, `luckyLoserCandidate`, `pickLuckyLosers`, `fairPoints`, `getUnitScore`, `rosterKeys` — contains **zero `throw` statements**; every helper is written defensively (`?? []`, optional chaining, explicit early-return guards). `round` is already dereferenced by both callers before the try block runs, so it can't be `undefined` there either. The one non-UI call site (`transitions.ts:255`, inside `advanceTournamentRound`) calls the same function with **no try/catch at all** — the established pattern elsewhere in this codebase is to not guard this call. So `HANDOFF.md`'s original framing ("tighten to only swallow the expected precondition-not-met case") didn't actually apply — there was no such case in this call graph, just speculative defensive boilerplate with no known trigger.

**Decision** (confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`, choosing the minimal of two options): keep the `[]` fallback — this app has **no `ErrorBoundary`** anywhere, so removing the try/catch entirely (matching `transitions.ts`) would let a genuine future bug crash the *whole* live Scoreboard/Admin panel mid-tournament instead of just degrading one computation, a materially worse outcome for a live-event tool. But the catch no longer discards the error — it now `console.error`s it with round/room context, matching the existing `console.warn(message, error)` convention already used for non-fatal errors elsewhere (`TournamentProvider.tsx:87,112,206`, `firebase-client.ts:28`), upgraded to `.error` given this masks a real bug rather than a soft degradation. The organiser explicitly declined the more invasive alternative (a visible "⚠ Unknown" UI badge replacing "Eliminated" for affected rows) as out of scope for this pass.

**Changes**: `src/features/admin/RunningAdminScores.tsx` and `src/features/scoreboard/ScoreboardView.tsx`, one line each — `catch { luckyNames = [] }` → `catch (error) { console.error(...) }` (the `[]` fallback is already the initial value of `luckyNames`, so no reassignment is needed in the catch body). Zero change to the `try` block or anything downstream — the happy path (the overwhelming majority of renders) is byte-identical to before.

### Testing performed
`pnpm exec eslint`/`pnpm exec prettier --check` on both changed files, both clean. Live click-through verification was offered but declined by the organiser as unnecessary given the change's narrow, low-risk scope (only the unreachable-in-practice catch body changed; the try block — the actual computation and rendering logic — is untouched) — noting for the record that a full live verification would require signing into Admin with real CFP credentials, which this assistant does not do on the organiser's behalf per its standing credential-handling rule.

### Explicitly out of scope
The more invasive alternative (a visible in-UI "⚠ Unknown" indicator distinguishing a genuine computation failure from a legitimate zero-lucky-losers round) — organiser's explicit choice, not a follow-up gap. Adding an `ErrorBoundary` to the app — a real gap surfaced during this investigation, but a separate, larger concern than this specific fix. Any change to `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()` itself.

## Domain-layer test coverage — Stage 1 (done — 2026-09-10)
Closes the first stage of `HANDOFF.md`'s "Add domain-layer test coverage" priority. Full plan (all 3 stages, kept for Stage 2/3 reference) at `C:\Users\thbiaa\.claude\plans\all-right-we-ll-give-humble-stardust.md` under "Domain-layer test coverage". Researched via direct reads of every file in `src/domain/tournament/` (not summarized) plus a dedicated mining pass over this log's own legacy entries for concrete worked examples/formulas to use as regression fixtures — cross-checked each against the actual current TypeScript source rather than trusted blindly, since the rework is a faithful but not byte-identical port (see the "NOT concentrated" finding below).

**Scope**: `room-distribution.ts`, `seeding.ts`, `scoring.ts`, `single-elimination.ts`, `pooling.ts` — the pure-math/pure-function files with no dependency on the advancement/transition orchestration layer. `advancement.ts`, `double-elimination.ts`, `transitions.ts`, and `finals.ts` (found during planning — the grand-final race logic, not in the original 6-file list) are Stage 2, not started, per this project's "check in per item on large tasks" convention.

New shared helper `src/domain/tournament/test-fixtures.ts` (`sequenceRandom`, `buildRound`, `buildAssignments`) plus 5 co-located `*.test.ts` files, matching the sole existing test's conventions (explicit `vitest` imports, not globals; relative imports; no `__tests__` directory). **74 tests total** (up from the repo's original 2), all pure-function unit tests with concrete, hand-traced expected values — not property/shape checks alone. Every hand-traced value was independently verified by actually running the real implementation, not just written from the formula and trusted; one real transcription error was caught this way (a `[first,second]` pair-order mixup in a `pooling.ts` circle-method test) and fixed before it could pin a wrong expectation into the suite.

### Quality verification, not just "tests pass"
Per the plan's own standard, temporarily mutated 3 representative implementation lines and confirmed the intended test actually fails, then reverted (confirmed clean via `git diff --stat`, nothing left in the diff):
1. `room-distribution.ts`'s `Math.min(maxRooms, Math.max(minRooms, idealRooms))` clamp, bypassed to bare `minRooms`. **First attempt revealed a real, useful gap**: none of the originally-written test cases (including the N=17 anchor) actually exercised this line — N=17 takes the earlier `maxRooms < minRooms` branch and never reaches the clamp at all, and every other case happened to have `idealRooms === minRooms` by coincidence. Added a new case (`distributeRooms(20, {min:4,max:10,ideal:6})` → `[7,7,6]`, chosen so `minRooms(2) < idealRooms(3) ≤ maxRooms(5)`) specifically to close this gap — confirmed it passes normally and is the only test that fails under the mutation.
2. `seeding.ts`'s `randomSeed` Fisher-Yates shuffle, swapped for a naive `sort(() => random.next() - 0.5)` comparator shuffle — both of the function's tests correctly failed (the exact-permutation assertion and the room-fill assertion), confirming they're pinned to the real algorithm, not just "some shuffle happened."
3. `single-elimination.ts`'s round-0 bye handling, mutated to concentrate the whole shortfall into round 0 (mirroring `double-elimination.ts`'s `concentratedByeFirstRound`, computed via the same `nextPowerOf2AndRounds`-style doubling) — the "never concentrated" regression test correctly failed with `byeCount: 13` (matching the hand-computed `32 - 19` for the test's N=19 fixture) while every other test in the file stayed green.

### Explicitly out of scope for this pass
Stage 2 (`advancement.ts`, `double-elimination.ts`, `transitions.ts`, `finals.ts`) and Stage 3 (`generation.ts`/`schedule-generation.ts` end-to-end smoke tests) — planned in full detail but not started, pending a fresh go-ahead. No production code changes — this pass only added test files plus the shared fixture helper (the mutation-test edits to `room-distribution.ts`/`seeding.ts`/`single-elimination.ts` were all reverted, confirmed via `git diff --stat` showing zero remaining diff on those three files).

### Possible findings surfaced while writing these tests (flagged, not fixed here)
- **Confirmed by direct source read, now pinned by a test**: `single-elimination.ts`'s `singleEliminationBracketPhase` never concentrates byes into round 0 — every round, including the first, uses the same self-correcting `distributeRoomsWithBye` (at most 1 bye). This is a real, load-bearing divergence from `double-elimination.ts`'s race variant (which DOES concentrate all of round 0's shortfall via a dedicated `concentratedByeFirstRound` helper) and from the old legacy app's "Concentrate single-elimination's byes into the first elimination round" behavior. Not necessarily a bug in the port — may be a deliberate simplification — but worth a conscious decision at some point rather than staying an undocumented divergence discoverable only by reading both files side by side.
- `pooling.ts`'s `circleMethodSchedule` is not exported — tested indirectly through `groupStagePoolingPhase`'s output shape, per the plan.

### Documentation follow-up (same day, bundled with this build since it's one line)
`AGENTS.md`'s "Testing" section updated from a bare "we don't use automated testing" to note the domain layer's new exception — see the file itself.

## Domain-layer test coverage — Stage 2 (done — 2026-09-10)
Closes the second stage of `HANDOFF.md`'s "Add domain-layer test coverage" priority (see "Domain-layer test coverage — Stage 1" above for Stage 1). Same plan file, same section. Started only after an explicit fresh go-ahead ("Start Stage 2"), per this project's "check in per item on large tasks" convention.

**Scope**: `advancement.ts` (16 tests), `double-elimination.ts` (15 tests), `finals.ts` (9 tests), `transitions.ts` (9 tests) — **49 new tests**, bringing the domain-layer suite to **123 tests total** (up from Stage 1's 74). Same conventions as Stage 1 (explicit `vitest` imports, relative imports, co-located `*.test.ts`, reusing `createDefaultTournamentState`/`buildRound` from Stage 1's `test-fixtures.ts`).

`advancement.ts` and `finals.ts` both passed fully on the first run (16/16 and 9/9), a good sign the hand-tracing discipline built up during Stage 1 was holding. `double-elimination.ts` and `transitions.ts` each surfaced real issues — all caught by the same "verify against actual behavior, never trust a hand-derived assumption" discipline established in Stage 1, not by luck:

- **`double-elimination.ts`**: two of my own mistakes, both caught by running the test and diagnosing before "fixing" anything. (1) A plain arithmetic error (`distributeRooms(6, {min:2,max:2,ideal:2})` hand-computed as `[3,3,3]`, actually `[2,2,2]` since `base=floor(6/3)=2`) — fixed the assertion once confirmed. (2) A wrong assumption, not a slip: assumed FFA N=37 (WBQ=6/LBQ=2) would exhibit `sharedFinalDoubleEliminationBracketPhase`'s losers-bracket-round-creation deferral, based on it being "the legacy anchor case" — running it showed WB=7/LB=7, no deferral for N=37 under this config specifically. Rather than keep guessing, wrote a throwaway scratch test sweeping N=[32,37,43,27,20,25] to find which one actually deferred, confirmed N=32 (WBQ=6/LBQ=2) genuinely does (WB=8, LB=7) — and this matches what the legacy catalog's own worked example actually used, which I'd misattributed to N=37 in the plan. Switched the test to N=32, deleted the scratch file, re-ran clean.
- **`transitions.ts`**: two design issues in my own first-draft tests, found via the mutation-testing pass below rather than via a normal red/green run (both tests initially passed against the *correct* implementation, which is exactly why a passing test alone isn't proof of a good test): the `avoidSameGroupInFirstBracketRound`-gating test's original fixture (matching round-0 room membership to the defined groups) happened to produce a snake-seed result with no actual same-group collision to avoid, so the test couldn't distinguish "avoidance correctly skipped" from "avoidance incorrectly ran but had nothing to do" — confirmed weak via mutation (removing the `round.isGroupStage` guard entirely still passed the test). Redesigned the fixture with group membership independent of round-0's real room composition, chosen so the *raw* un-swapped snake-seed order is a genuine same-group collision in both rooms with a valid swap available to fix it — re-ran the same mutation, now correctly fails. Also found the bye-prepend-dedup test's fixture was missing `state.players` (defaults to `[]` in `createDefaultTournamentState`), which silently starved `computeQualificationStandings()`'s roster-accumulator seeding — the qual cutoff computed against an empty roster, producing a degenerate 1-name result that accidentally looked plausible. Fixed by populating `players: ['P1','P2','P3','P4']`.

### Quality verification, not just "tests pass"
Same standard as Stage 1 — 3 representative implementation lines temporarily mutated, confirmed the intended test fails, then reverted (`git diff --stat` on `transitions.ts` shows zero residual diff):
1. `transitions.ts`'s `advancing = uniqueKeepingLast(advancing)` bye-dedup line, removed entirely — the bye-prepend-dedup test correctly failed (`['P4','P4']` instead of one `'P4'`), confirming the dedup line is actually load-bearing and the test is pinned to it, not to some other coincidental behavior.
2. `transitions.ts`'s `if (round.isGroupStage) { seeded = avoidSameGroupInFirstBracketRound(...) }` guard, changed to `if (true)` — correctly caught by the redesigned fixture above (`['P1','P2']` instead of the expected raw `['P1','P3']`), where the original fixture would have missed it.
3. Full suite (`pnpm test`) run clean at 123/123 both before and after the mutation-and-revert cycle, confirming no cross-file breakage.

### Explicitly out of scope for this pass
Stage 3 (`generation.ts`/`schedule-generation.ts` end-to-end smoke tests) — still optional, still not started, needs its own fresh go-ahead. The plan's flagged dead-code branch (`transitions.ts`'s `bracketPhaseFirstRound`-triggered concentrated-bye seeding inside the ordinary room-based path) — confirmed still unreachable through any current generator, documented here rather than forced into a synthetic test reaching into unreachable internals. No production code changes beyond the mutation-and-revert testing cycle above (confirmed via `git diff --stat`).

`HANDOFF.md`'s "Immediate next priorities" #3 and "What is NOT yet built" updated to mark Stage 2 done, matching how Stage 1's completion was recorded.

## Domain-layer test coverage — Stage 3 (done — 2026-09-10)
Closes the third and final stage of `HANDOFF.md`'s "Add domain-layer test coverage" priority (see "— Stage 1" and "— Stage 2" above). Same plan file, same section — this stage was always flagged optional/lower-priority, and was started only after an explicit fresh go-ahead ("Start Stage 3").

**Scope**: `generation.ts` (`generateTournament`, the full config-synthesis/validation/pooling/bracket-composition/initial-seeding pipeline) + `schedule-generation.ts` (`getMinimumBracketUnits`, `buildTournamentProgression` — thin plumbing, folded in rather than given its own file per the plan). One new file, `generation.test.ts`, **10 tests**, bringing the domain-layer suite to **133 tests total**. Also added `fixedIdSource()` to `test-fixtures.ts` (a deterministic `IdSource`, needed for the first time in this stage since neither Stage 1 nor Stage 2 exercised `runtime.ids`).

Since `generation.ts`/`schedule-generation.ts` sit on top of every file covered in Stages 1–2, this stage is deliberately an *integration/smoke* layer, not a re-derivation of math already pinned elsewhere: `getMinimumBracketUnits` (3 cases, including a real subtlety — `'double-elimination-shared-final'` does NOT match the flat-4 branch, only the literal string `'double-elimination'` does, an exact-key check rather than "any double-elimination variant"); three validation-failure paths (no confirmed roster; below the format/schedule floor; a genuine `config.n`-vs-actual-bracket-entry-count disagreement under the "None" odd-count strategy — n=10 even but a clamped `qualAdv` of 5 is odd, constructed specifically so a validation that only checked `config.n` would wrongly pass); two round-0 seeding cases (an exact hand-traced Fisher-Yates permutation through the full pipeline, and confirmation that the Bye strategy's bench pick is always roster-position-0, independent of the random source); two full end-to-end anchors (FFA 37 players reproducing the known `[30,24,20,16]` sweep table plus the 2 no-elim warmup rounds `generateTournament` prepends that a raw `singleEliminationBracketPhase` call alone wouldn't show; individual-1v1 16 players through `double-elimination`, confirming the WB=4/LB=6/grand-final composition threads correctly end to end).

**9 of 10 tests passed on the first run** — a good sign given this stage's tests only had to compose already-verified pieces correctly, not re-derive new math. The one miss was a trivial casing mistake in an expected error-message substring (the app lowercases `unitLabelPlural` for its user-facing messages; my hand-written expectation used the type's own PascalCase field name instead) — caught immediately by running it, fixed, confirmed.

### Quality verification, not just "tests pass"
Same standard as Stages 1–2 — 2 representative `generation.ts` lines temporarily mutated, confirmed the intended test fails, then reverted (`git diff --stat` shows zero residual diff):
1. The Bye-strategy bench pick (`const firstBye = initialPool[0]`), changed to `initialPool[1]` — the bench-pick test correctly failed (`['P2']` instead of `['P1']`).
2. The "None"-strategy disagreement check's `isMultiple` computation, hardcoded to `true` — the constructed-disagreement validation test correctly failed (`'generated'` instead of `'invalid'`), confirming the test is actually pinned to that check firing, not to some other coincidental validation path.

Full suite (`pnpm test`) run clean at 133/133 both before and after the mutation-and-revert cycle.

### Explicitly out of scope for this pass
No production code changes beyond the mutation-and-revert testing cycle above. This closes all three stages of the domain-layer test-coverage plan — no further stages remain.

### Documentation follow-up (same day, bundled with this build since it's one line)
`HANDOFF.md`'s "Immediate next priorities" #3 and "What is NOT yet built" updated to mark all three stages done.

## Fix: hung Firebase Admin write freezes the UI (done — 2026-09-10)
Fixes the real bug found and root-caused in "Browser-based full test pass" below — read that entry first for the full diagnosis (a hung `.set()` call with no timeout, accumulating pending pushes until the browser's connection pool saturates and the UI stops responding to clicks, with no error surfaced beyond a permanently-stuck "🔄 Connecting…").

**Two changes, one server-side and one client-side, matching the two distinct failure modes the diagnosis found:**
1. `writeTournamentToFirebase()` (`src/features/sync/firebase-admin.server.ts`) now wraps the Firebase Admin SDK's `.set(payload)` call in a `withTimeout()` helper (`Promise.race` against a 10s timer) that rejects with a clear message (`Firebase write timed out after 10000ms — check FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON / Application Default Credentials.`) if the write never settles. This is the actual root-cause fix — it's the only place the original hang occurred. 10s was chosen as generous enough to never falsely trip on a real, slow-but-working Firebase write, while still being short enough that a genuinely broken credential surfaces within one debounce-and-a-bit rather than being invisible.
2. `SyncCoordinator.push()` (`src/features/sync/live-sync.ts`) gained a `pushInFlight` guard: if `push()` fires while a previous push is still settling (which — before the timeout fix — could mean "still settling, forever"), it coalesces into another `schedulePush()` call instead of firing a second concurrent write. This bounds the maximum number of simultaneous in-flight writes to exactly one, closing the "500+ accumulated pending requests" failure mode at its root rather than just shortening how long each one can hang for. Without this, a sustained burst of score edits during a Firebase outage could still queue up many timeout-bound (rather than infinite) requests in quick succession — bounded now, but still worth avoiding outright.

Both changes were verified via `eslint`/`prettier --check` on the two changed files (per `AGENTS.md`'s "Validating changes" — no full build/typecheck run) and via live re-testing in the browser against the exact same no-credential dev environment the bug was originally found in:
- Reproduced the hang (edited a score, confirmed the Live Sync card stayed on "🔄 Connecting…"), waited ~12s, and confirmed it correctly flipped to "🔴 Sync error — viewers may be seeing stale data (Firebase write timed out after 10000ms — check FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON / Application Default Credentials.)" — the exact new timeout message, proving the fix is live and the error correctly propagates all the way from the server function through `SyncCoordinator`'s existing (already-correct) catch-and-report path.
- Fired a rapid burst of edits (5 rounds across 11 inputs in one synchronous script) to simulate sustained editing during an outage, waited for settlement, and confirmed the status still correctly resolved to the same clear error state — not a silent hang, not an unbounded pile of requests.
- Confirmed "Next Round" and tab navigation stayed instantly responsive throughout, including immediately after the burst — the original symptom (clicks silently doing nothing) did not reproduce.
- Restarted the dev server to load the new server-side code fresh (TanStack Start server functions don't reliably hot-reload) before any of the above.

### Explicitly out of scope
No change to the underlying Firebase Admin SDK's own background reconnect/retry behavior (it keeps logging `FIREBASE WARNING: app/invalid-credential` on its own internal schedule even after our timeout gives up waiting on it) — that's a lower-level SDK concern that doesn't generate new pending requests to our own server, so it was left alone rather than trying to forcibly tear down the underlying connection. No change to the *read* path (`firebase-client.ts`'s direct client-side `onValue` subscription) — reads were never the ones hanging. No automated test added for this fix (matches `AGENTS.md`'s "We do not use automated testing in this codebase"); verification was the live-browser reproduction described above. The domain-layer test-coverage gap and the two silent `catch { luckyNames = [] }` sites (both flagged in `HANDOFF.md`) remain untouched — out of scope for this specific fix.

## Browser-based full test pass (done — 2026-09-10)
The first genuine in-browser verification of the reworked `src/` app — everything documented in the "App rework" entry below was based on static source comparison only; this entry replaces that gap. Set up locally: `npx pnpm@11.19.0 install`, a `.claude/launch.json` pointing at `pnpm dev`, and a `.env` overriding `VITE_API_ENDPOINT` to `https://api.curvefever.pro` (no local CFP API server available) so a real CFP login could be attempted. Real credentials were entered by the organiser directly into the browser pane — never by the assistant, per this project's standing rule against handling credentials. No `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`/Application Default Credentials were configured, so every admin write to Firebase was expected to fail; this shaped one major finding below.

### What was tested and passed cleanly
A full FFA tournament, 37 players (awkward count, per "One important principle" in `HANDOFF.md`), `single-elimination`, default settings:
- Setup: roster/reserve loading, schedule preview (derived round count 37→37→37→30→24→20→16→Final, matching legacy's variable-elimination-round-count logic), Confirm & Start.
- Round 1–2 (no-elim warm-up): all-advance behavior, snake-reseeding into Round 2 correctly redistributed players.
- Round 3 (first elimination round): deliberately constructed a same-room boundary tie (two players tied at the top-6/7th-place cutoff) — the tie-break banner, ⚠ TIE badges, disabled "Next Round" button (title "Resolve tie-breaks first"), and resolution flow (pick-who-ranks-next) all worked correctly; resolving correctly flipped the two rows to ADVANCES/ELIMINATED.
- Round 5 (`luckyCount: 2`): deliberately crafted three rooms' 7th-place (first non-advancing) candidates with different relative-to-room-total scores — the two higher-relative-score candidates were correctly marked ★ LUCKY LOSER, the third (lowest relative score, same nominal 7th-place finish) was correctly ELIMINATED. Confirms the cross-room relative-score lucky-loser selection ported exactly.
- Semis → Final: reached the 8-player multi-game (3 games) Final; entered deliberately non-monotonic per-game scores and hand-verified the Total tab's cumulative sums against every one of the 8 players — byte-exact match (e.g. P07: 270+320+80=670, correctly ranked 1st).
- Auto-archive: fired the instant the Final's last score landed ("Tournament archived automatically."), with zero manual action.
- Rankings (live and Archive detail view): finalists ranked 1–8 exactly matching Final totals, eliminated players correctly grouped and ordered by elimination round down to Round 3, all 37 players accounted for.
- Bracket: correct default collapse (only the round immediately before current stays expanded), lucky-loser ★ and tie badges correctly preserved on historical rounds after reopening them, "Follow a player" (searched "P24") correctly showed a "Following P24 — out in Round 6" banner, highlighted the row, and auto-scrolled/expanded to the relevant round.
- Archive: the auto-saved entry listed correctly (title, date, player/round counts); detail view reused the same Rankings/Bracket renderers as live; added an annotation successfully (timestamped, persisted).
- Scoreboard: current-round view correct, matching Admin's own data.
- A second smoke test — reset, switched to `team-3v3v3`, 11 teams (awkward count) — generated correctly (11→11→11→9→7→6→Final) and rendered the team-aware Admin UI correctly (per-member score inputs, "Manage Teams" with member-level rename/remove alongside team-level rename/swap/remove).
- Auth: real CFP login succeeded (organiser-entered credentials); a deliberately wrong login failed gracefully ("Failed to fetch" shown inline, no crash) when tested earlier against the unreachable local API default.
- **Zero application-level console errors** across the entire session — every error observed was `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`/`RESET` from the known-missing Firebase Admin credential (see below), nothing else.

### A real bug found, root-caused, and reproduced deterministically
**A hung Firebase Admin write eventually makes the entire UI unresponsive, not just the Live Sync status.** First noticed as "Next Round" appearing to silently do nothing after several minutes of active score-editing. Diagnosis, in order:
1. Suspected the domain logic first — extracted the exact stuck `TournamentState` from `localStorage` and replayed it through `advanceTournamentRound()` directly in a Vitest script, isolated from React/the browser entirely. It returned `status: 'advanced'`, `curRound: 2` correctly — **proving the tournament logic was never at fault**.
2. Read `TournamentProvider.updateState()`/`SyncCoordinator` — both are synchronous with a fire-and-forget async push; nothing should block a `setState` call on a hung network request by code-reading alone.
3. Restarted the dev server (clearing ~500 accumulated pending requests) and retried the identical click on the identical persisted state — it worked immediately.
4. Root cause: `writeTournamentToFirebase()` (`firebase-admin.server.ts`) does `await ...ref(...).set(payload)` against a Firebase Admin SDK instance that can't obtain credentials (`Could not load the default credentials`, logged repeatedly with exponential backoff by the underlying `google-auth-library`) — this `.set()` call **never resolves or rejects**, it just retries the connection forever. Every score edit re-triggers `SyncCoordinator.schedulePush()`, and each of those hung pushes becomes a permanent open HTTP request from the browser to the dev server's `_serverFn` endpoint. Once enough accumulate, the browser's per-origin connection limit is saturated, degrading the dev server's own responsiveness (`[vite] server connection lost. Polling for restart...` appeared in console) to the point that legitimate new interactions stopped being processed in reasonable time.

This is almost certainly **environment-specific to a misconfigured/unreachable Firebase Admin credential** — the real CFP deployment should have valid credentials and writes should resolve in milliseconds, never hitting this. But the underlying gap is real: nothing times out a hung write, and the `SyncStatus` UI has no "give up and show an error" state, so a genuine Firebase outage or an expired/revoked service-account credential in production would reproduce this exact failure mode, silently, with no diagnostic beyond a stuck "🔄 Connecting…" label. See `HANDOFF.md`'s "Immediate next priorities" #2 for the recommended fix (timeout + bounded retry + explicit error status).

### Explicitly out of scope for this pass
No code changes were made — this was a verification-only pass; the bug found above is documented, not fixed. Real cross-tab/cross-device live sync against a *working* Firebase credential was not tested (none was available in this dev environment) — worth doing once the CFP-hosted deployment is live. Domain-layer automated test coverage (flagged separately in `HANDOFF.md`) was not added. `last-man-standing`/`kings-valley` were not exercised (still unimplemented, as expected). The two silent `catch { luckyNames = [] }` sites were not fixed, only previously flagged.

**Fixed 2026-09-10 — see "Fix: hung Firebase Admin write freezes the UI" above.**

## Deployment target decision (done — 2026-09-10)
Resolved the "deployment target is currently undocumented" gap flagged the same day (see "App rework: migration to React/TypeScript" below) — the biggest open item that entry's investigation surfaced.

Asked the organiser directly (`AskUserQuestion`: CFP's own infrastructure vs. a self-set-up managed platform vs. an existing VPS vs. "walk me through the tradeoffs") rather than picking a platform unilaterally — real cost/control/maintenance tradeoffs with no code-derivable right answer, and a hint in `.env.example` (the CFP API's own `api-test.curvefever.pro`/`api.curvefever.pro` split) suggested the org might already have infra conventions worth following rather than inventing a new one.

**Decision**: a CFP developer will host it on Curve Fever Pro's own infrastructure, alongside `api.curvefever.pro`, with CI/CD set up on their end to auto-deploy on every push to `main` — preserving the exact git workflow the old GitHub Pages pipeline used (`git push origin main` → live). No repo changes were needed to make this decision (no hosting adapter/preset installed, no CI config added here) — it's entirely handled on the CFP infrastructure side, outside this repo's scope.

### Explicitly out of scope
Actually setting up or verifying the CI/CD pipeline — that's the CFP developer's side, not this repo's. Four operational details were surfaced as follow-ups to confirm with them once it exists, not resolved here: how `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`/Application Default Credentials reach that environment, whether `VITE_API_ENDPOINT` needs setting explicitly there, whether a staging/test target exists (mirroring `api-test.curvefever.pro`), and the eventual live URL. See "Deployment target" in `HANDOFF.md`.

## App rework: migration to React/TypeScript (done — 2026-09-10)
A full rewrite of the app landed via PR #1 ("rework" branch, merge commit `faea942`), 28 commits in one go, pulled into this local repo the same day. Not something built in this session — an externally-developed rework that was reviewed and merged by the organiser — but documented here per this project's "every build gets a dated entry" convention, since it's the largest single change in the app's history and every subsequent entry needs to be read against a completely different codebase layout.

**What changed, structurally**: the old single-file-turned-multi-file vanilla JS/HTML/CSS app (`index.html` + `js/*.js`) moved, unmodified, to `legacy/index.html` + `legacy/js/*.js`. A new React 19 + TypeScript app was added under `src/`, built on TanStack Start (SSR + file-based routing via TanStack Router) + Vite, Tailwind CSS v4, and `pnpm`/`eslint`/`prettier`/`vitest` tooling — the app's first build step, linter, and test runner ever. The domain/tournament-rules layer (`src/domain/tournament/*.ts`) was ported to pure TypeScript functions over a typed `TournamentState`, replacing the legacy untyped global `T`.

**Confirmed, by direct source comparison (not just commit-message trust), to be a faithful port, not a redesign**: every game format (`ffa-individual`, `team-2v2v2v2`, `team-3v3v3`, `team-3v3`, `individual-1v1`), pooling phase (`none`/`qual-table`/`swiss`/`group-stage`), and bracket phase (`single-elimination`/`double-elimination`/`double-elimination-shared-final`) carried over with matching room-size/odd-count-strategy rules (`src/domain/tournament/formats.ts` vs. `legacy/js/formats-and-primitives.js`). Formula-level details survived too — `fairPoints(rank, score) = rank - score / 100_000` (the corrected sign from the legacy-era "Fair Points sign fix," `src/domain/tournament/scoring.ts:150-152`) and the multi-game Semis key scheme (`r{ri}-rm{rm}-p{pi}-g{g}[-m{mi}]`, `getUnitScoreForGame`/`getUnitScore` in `scoring.ts`, matching `legacy/js/formats-and-primitives.js` byte-for-byte in logic shape) both ported exactly, meaning the rework was built from a legacy snapshot at or after the "Multi-game Semis" entry above (the last legacy-era build). Every viewer-facing Bracket feature (collapsible rounds, Follow-a-player, inline score entry, placeholder future rounds, unresolved-tie badges) and Rankings/Archive feature (pooling-rank badges, pre-schedule roster list, import/export/annotations) is present in the new `BracketView.tsx`/`RankingsView.tsx`/`ArchiveView.tsx` — verified via direct source read of each, not assumed from file existence. `last-man-standing` and `kings-valley` remain unimplemented — same as legacy, not a new gap.

**What changed on purpose, not as ported behavior**:
1. **Admin access — replaced entirely, not ported.** Legacy: one shared password, hashed client-side, verified against a Firebase Realtime Database rule. New: real per-user login against Curve Fever Pro accounts (`CfpLoginModal` → CFP API `/auth/login`), authorized by account role against an allowlist (`auth.shared.ts`'s `TOUR_ADMIN_ROLE_IDS`), re-validated server-side on every request (`getInitialAuth`, 60s cache) and re-checked server-side again on every privileged write (`requireTourAdminPermission()`) rather than trusted from client state. Legacy's `localStorage` unlock flags are actively deleted on hydrate.
2. **Live-sync writes — now server-mediated, not client-direct.** Legacy: the browser wrote the whole `T` blob straight to Firebase, gated by a DB rule checking a password-derived proof hash. New: `database.rules.json` denies all direct client writes (`.write: false` everywhere); a write goes through `writeTournament()`, a TanStack Start server function that validates the tournament ID, strips any legacy `adminProof` field, calls `requireTourAdminPermission()`, then writes via the Firebase **Admin** SDK with a service-account credential. Reads stayed exactly as before — direct, public, unauthenticated client-side Firebase reads (a shareable link is still the viewer access boundary). `SyncCoordinator` (`live-sync.ts`) otherwise ported the writer/viewer split, the debounced push, the null↔sentinel marshal/unmarshal for Firebase's array-null-pruning bug, and dirty-key score merging essentially unchanged.

**A real, currently-unresolved gap surfaced by this change, not present before**: the legacy app deployed as static files to GitHub Pages — no server, no secrets, `git push` to `main` was the entire deploy story. The reworked app's `pnpm start` runs a Nitro/TanStack Start server bundle (`node .output/server/index.mjs`) and requires `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` (or equivalent ADC) plus reachability to the CFP API for any admin write to succeed — it structurally cannot be served as static files anymore. Nothing in the repo (README, `.agents/skills/`, no CI/deploy config of any kind) documents where this is actually meant to run in production. This is the top item in `HANDOFF.md`'s "Immediate next priorities."

**Decided same day (2026-09-10) — see "Deployment target decision" below.**

**Other things worth flagging, found during investigation, not fixed in this pass** (all in `HANDOFF.md`'s "What is NOT yet built / known gaps," not re-duplicated here): `vitest` is wired up but only one test file exists in the entire `src/` tree (`auth.shared.test.ts`, role-allowlist logic only) — the pure-function domain layer that would benefit most from tests (`advancement.ts`, `double-elimination.ts`, `single-elimination.ts`, `pooling.ts`, `seeding.ts`, `scoring.ts`) has zero coverage despite being built in a shape that invites it. Two call sites (`RunningAdminScores.tsx`, `ScoreboardView.tsx`) silently swallow any error from `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()` into an empty lucky-loser list (`catch { luckyNames = [] }`) rather than distinguishing "no lucky losers this round" from "something actually broke."

### Documentation follow-up (same day)
Alongside pulling this rework, `CLAUDE.md` (this project's local, gitignored process-discipline file) was retired — its content merged into the new `AGENTS.md` the rework itself introduced (which only covered code-style/tooling conventions, not session workflow) as a new "Working process" section, with one real contradiction resolved in `AGENTS.md`'s favor: `CLAUDE.md`'s "verify independently... actually run it" guidance was reworded to explicitly defer to `AGENTS.md`'s existing "don't do a full compile/lint/build check, only check changed files" rule rather than implying a full verification pass. `HANDOFF.md` was then rewritten in full (not incrementally edited) to describe the new `src/` app's current state — the old version, describing the legacy app in exhaustive session-by-session detail, was structurally inapplicable to a codebase this different; its content isn't lost, since `legacy/`'s own build history lives on in every `HANDOFF_LOG.md` entry below this one.

### Explicitly out of scope (for the documentation follow-up)
No in-browser verification was performed — this entry, and the new `HANDOFF.md`, are based on static source comparison between `legacy/js/*.js` and `src/domain/tournament/*.ts`/`src/features/**/*.tsx` only. Nothing about the reworked app's actual runtime behavior (does a generated tournament really play through correctly end-to-end, does live sync actually work against production Firebase with the new server-mediated write path) was tested this session — flagged as `HANDOFF.md` priority #4. No code changes were made to `src/` or `legacy/` in this pass, only to `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`(deleted)/`HANDOFF.md`/`HANDOFF_LOG.md`. **The deployment-target gap flagged below was resolved later the same day — see "Deployment target decision" above.**

## Multi-game Semis (done — 2026-09-08)
The organiser asked for Semis to optionally be scored as several games summed, mirroring the existing "Finals format" dropdown. Researched via two parallel Explore agents (the Final's existing multi-game mechanism + Semis' own generation-time shape) and one Plan agent, then spot-checked directly against live source.

**Why the Final's existing multi-game mechanism ("Mechanism B", `T.finalScores`) couldn't just be reused**: its keys are flat and round-agnostic (`game{g}-{name}`, no round index) — a player playing both a multi-game Semis and a multi-game Final would collide on `game1-PlayerX`. Worse, Mechanism B's whole rendering model (`renderMultiGameFinals`/`buildFinalColumnHtml`/`finalsProgressState`) assumes ONE flat, undivided pool of entrants (the Final is always a single literal room) — Semis is 2+ independent rooms, each with its own room-based advancement and the existing cross-room lucky-loser mechanism.

**Decision: extend Mechanism A (`T.scores`, room/position-keyed) instead.** `T.scores`'s keys (`r{ri}-rm{rm}-p{pi}[-m{mi}]`) already carry round/room/position — extending them with an optional trailing game segment (`r{ri}-rm{rm}-p{pi}-g{g}[-m{mi}]`, used only when `round.numGames > 1`) has zero collision risk with `T.finalScores` (a wholly separate, untouched store) and zero migration need (an already-generated Semis round simply has `numGames: undefined`, unchanged behavior). The single load-bearing insight: **every existing consumer of a unit's round-score already routes through one function, `getUnitScore(state, ri, rm, pi, fallback)`** — `detectTieBreaks()`, `isRoundFullyScored()`, `computeLuckyLosers()`, `recalcRoom()`, `orderRoomByScore()`'s feeders, and `computeRankings()`'s elimination-detection loop all call it directly. Generalizing `getUnitScore()` itself (split into a new per-game building block `getUnitScoreForGame()`, summed by the now-thin `getUnitScore()` wrapper) made every one of those consumers multi-game-correct with zero changes of their own — confirmed directly by re-reading each, not assumed.

**UI — compact inline multi-input, extending `renderRooms()`'s existing per-cell pattern, not a new tabbed view.** Rejected porting the Final's tabbed Game1/Game2/.../Total UI (already used for the Final) to Semis: it would have turned the `round.isFinal && (round.numGames > 1 || round.bracket === 'grand-final')` routing condition — already byte-identically duplicated in 3 places — into a 3-way branch kept in sync everywhere, and would need `recalcRoom()`'s entire advance/eliminate/lucky-loser/tie-pill logic re-derived from scratch inside a new "Total" tab (the Final has no such concept). The chosen approach extends the Score cell's *existing* "stack multiple mini-inputs" pattern (already used for team-member sub-scores in this exact table) to also stack per-game inputs — needed **zero new write handler** (`scoreChanged()` was already 100% key-format-agnostic) and **zero routing change** (Semis was never routed to the Final's mechanism regardless of its own `numGames`).

**A real, independently-found data-integrity gap, required for this build, not a follow-up**: four roster-edit helpers (`removeTeam()`, `swapPlayer()`, `swapTeam()`, `removePlayer()`) shift or clear `T.scores` keys by room-position when an admin edits the roster *during the live round* — all built on a single-key-per-position assumption predating this feature. Fixed with a new shared helper, `scoreKeysForPosition(ri, rm, pi, round, teamSize)`, returning every key belonging to one position across every game of the round; each of the four sites now loops over its parallel from/to key arrays instead of touching one key. Without this, removing/swapping a player mid-Semis would silently strand games 2+'s already-entered scores under the wrong (or a deleted) position.

**A second gap caught during implementation, beyond the plan**: `saveState()`/`loadState()` (`js/core.js`) didn't originally persist/restore the new `cfg-semis-games` dropdown's value — every other setup field (`finalsGames`, `semisOverride`, etc.) already round-trips through a reload; `semisGames` needed the identical two-line addition to match, or a reloaded/resumed session would silently revert an organiser's chosen Semis format back to the default.

### Implementation
- `js/formats-and-primitives.js`: `getUnitScore()` split into `getUnitScoreForGame(state, ri, rm, pi, g, fallback)` (the original body, `g===null` reproducing today's exact key byte-for-byte) and a thin `getUnitScore()` wrapper that sums across `round.numGames` games when `> 1`, with the same `anyMissing`/`fallback===null` semantics the team-member loop already used, generalized one level up. New `scoreKeysForPosition()` helper (see above). `getFinalUnitScore()`/`T.finalScores` untouched.
- `js/bracket-phases.js`: `singleEliminationBracketPhase()`'s Semis round literal gains `numGames: descriptor.config.semisGames`, mirroring the Final round literal 3 lines below it.
- `index.html` + `js/generation-and-roster.js`: new "Semis format" dropdown (`#field-semis-games`/`#cfg-semis-games`), gated visible only for `single-elimination` (same as the existing Semis-size-override), **defaulting to "Single game," not Finals' own default of 3** — preserves today's exact behavior for anyone who never touches it. `cfg.semisGames`/`T.gamemodeConfig.semisGames` wired alongside `finalsGames`'s own pattern exactly.
- `js/core.js`: `semisGames` added to `saveState()`'s persisted setup object and `loadState()`'s restoration (the gap caught above).
- `js/render-admin.js`: `renderRooms()` renders `numGames` small inputs (+ a running total span) per unit's Score cell when multi-game, instead of one input — individual and team (member-stacked) branches both handled, vacant team slots keep a single disabled placeholder regardless of `numGames` (a deliberate column-alignment simplification). `recalcRoom()` tracks a partial-sum total (fallback `0`, growing live as games are entered, distinct from the rank-affecting fallback-`null` score) and writes it into the new `#total-{key}` span.
- `js/render-viewer.js`: `buildBracketHtml()`'s `rowsEditable` gains one defensive exclusion — `!(round.numGames > 1 && !round.isFinal)` — so an admin editing from Bracket during a multi-game Semis falls back to the existing read-only display instead of silently writing to a key `getUnitScore()` no longer reads. Read-only *display* needed zero changes (the existing `scoredList`/`showResults` logic already routes through the now-generalized `getUnitScore()`).

### Explicitly out of scope
Editable multi-game Semis score entry from the Bracket tab (read-only display only — mirrors how Finals multi-game entry was Admin-only first, with Bracket-side entry added as a later, separate build in this project's history). Any change to the Final's own `T.finalScores` mechanism. Multi-game support for any round type other than Semis. A per-game score breakdown (G1/G2/G3 chips) on Bracket's read-only display — the bare total is correct and complete.

### Testing performed
Real playthroughs via the actual UI/console-exposed functions, awkward counts (37/43 individual, 17 teams), fresh browser state each time:
- **Generation + setup UI**: "Semis format" correctly shown only for single-elimination, hidden for the shared-final double-elimination variant; a generated Semis round carries the configured `numGames`; the field's value now survives a page reload (the caught `loadState()` gap).
- **Multi-game sum correctness**: entered scores across all games for all 16 Semis entrants (2 rooms), cross-checked the displayed running total against a manual sum and against `getUnitScore()` directly for every single entrant — zero mismatches.
- **Lucky-loser cross-room comparison on multi-game totals**: constructed a case (odd Final-size override, `luckyCount:1`) where one room's rank-4 candidate had a higher *relative* 2-game total than the other room's — confirmed `computeLuckyLosers()` correctly picked the higher-relative-total candidate, not a single-game artifact.
- **Tie-break detection on totals, not single-game scores**: two players with different per-game splits (300+200 vs 100+400) summing to the identical total (500) — confirmed a tie was correctly detected and blocked advancement (`hasPendingTies()`) until resolved via the normal `resolveTie()` flow.
- **Team format, both scoring rules**: `designated-player` — confirmed the team total equals only the defender's summed score across all games, completely ignoring a non-defender's (deliberately absurd) scores, and that the defender stays fixed across every game of the round (no per-game re-selection). A vacant member slot rendered as a single disabled placeholder, not `numGames` of them.
- **Partial completion**: a room with some but not all games entered correctly showed `null` (not yet complete, blocking `isRoundFullyScored()`/advancement) via the null-fallback read, while the live total display correctly showed a growing partial sum via the zero-fallback read — the same "two different fallbacks for two different purposes" pattern already established elsewhere in this codebase.
- **Roster-edit-mid-round fix**: removed a team from a room with partially-entered multi-game scores (some but not all games/members filled) — confirmed every game's score data correctly repacked to the shifted position, with zero stale keys left at the vacated last position.
- **Bracket**: confirmed the live/read-only total matches Admin exactly; confirmed an unlocked admin sees zero editable inputs for a multi-game Semis round (defensive gate fires correctly) while a normal single-game current round still shows full editable inputs (regression-clean).
- **Regression — the Final's own mechanism, in a tournament with BOTH a multi-game Semis (2 games) and a multi-game Final (3 games)**: confirmed `T.finalScores` stayed completely empty (zero keys) before the Final was reached — no cross-contamination from the Semis' scoring — then scored the Final via its own unchanged mechanism (`game{g}-{name}` keys, no round prefix) and confirmed `computeRankings()` correctly ranked all 8 finalists; confirmed the 8 Semis-eliminated players' `eliminatedList` entries correctly used their multi-game totals for the relative-score tiebreaker.
- **Archive**: a saved snapshot including the completed multi-game Semis rendered correctly read-only in Archive's detail view — correct summed totals shown, zero editable inputs.
- **CSS sizing**: confirmed no horizontal overflow in the tightest case (4 games × 3-member team) via computed layout measurements (visual screenshot verification wasn't available this session due to a Browser-pane rendering issue, not an app problem — flagged for a quick eyeball look in a real browser).
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario above.

## Merge Players tab into Rankings (done — 2026-09-08)
Organiser feedback: the Players tab reads as redundant with Rankings — both list every roster unit, so having two separate tabs for that felt like duplication. Investigated with two parallel Explore agents (full read of both tabs' actual implementations) and one Plan agent (detailed file-by-file design), confirmed against live source. `computeRankings()` (`js/advancement.js`) does already return every roster unit via `stillActive`/`finalists`/`eliminatedList` — but Players had one genuinely non-cosmetic capability Rankings lacked: a still-active unit's current round + room ("Round 3 — Room B", or "BYE — advances automatically"), explicitly one of only two documented viewer-tab success criteria in `HANDOFF.md` ("convenience — zero-click, which room you're in"). Plus two smaller things: a live pooling-phase rank badge (Qual/Swiss/Group standing) per still-active unit, and a pre-schedule flat "Registered/Reserve" list (Rankings showed nothing until a schedule existed). Organiser confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: keep the pooling-rank badge, don't add a new search box (Bracket's existing "Follow a player" is already a strictly better version of Players' own `filterPlayers()` — matches team members too, persists, highlights everywhere), and preserve the pre-schedule list.

**`computeRankings(state)` (`js/advancement.js`)** — the three `lastAsgn.forEach(p => stillActive.push(p.name))` call sites now push the whole raw assignment entry (`stillActive.push(p)`), since `p.room`/`p.isLucky` were already sitting right there, just discarded — no parallel computation needed, this data was always in scope. Fixed `stillActiveSet` to stay name-keyed (`new Set(stillActive.map(p => p.name))`) since it gates `eliminatedList` membership — the one real correctness trap in this whole change, since a silently-broken elimination-exclusion set would have re-added every still-active unit to `eliminatedList` too. Added a new `poolRankMap` lookup generalized from Players' old `fpMap` logic (`state.qualTable`/`state.groupStandings`/`state.cfg.poolingPhase`, with defensive `||[]`/`||{}` guards — new, since `computeRankings()` is reused against arbitrary Archive snapshots including ones from before these fields existed, matching the exact "may be missing" precedent already established at `js/archive.js:217`). `stillActiveOut` entries now carry `room`/`isLucky`/`poolRank` alongside the existing `label`/`members`; `lastRound`/`lastRi` are exposed once at the top level of the return object (every still-active unit is, by construction, from that exact same round). All changes purely additive — no existing field renamed, removed, or given different semantics; `finalists`/`eliminatedList` entries are untouched.

**`buildRankingsRows()`/`rankingRowHtml()` (`js/render-viewer.js`)** — `rankingRowHtml()` gained an optional 5th `subHtml` param (rendered right after the unit cell); the two existing call sites (finalists, eliminated) pass no 5th arg, so their output is byte-identical to before. New `rankingStillActiveSubHtml()`/`rankingPoolRankBadge()` helpers render the round/room line (reusing `roundShortLabel()` — more granular than Players' own cruder Final/Semis/Round-N-only labeling, so Qual/Swiss/Group rounds now get a correctly-specific label here too) and the pool-rank badge for a still-active row. New `buildRosterOnlyRows()` builds the pre-schedule Registered/Reserve list through Rankings' own `.rk-row`/`.rk-columns` idiom — deliberately not a resurrection of Players' `playerCard()` avatar-card shape. `renderRankings()` gained a pre-schedule branch: when `computeRankings()` returns `null` but a roster is loaded, shows the roster-only list (hiding the description paragraph and "Download rankings image" button, now `#rk-desc`/`#rk-download-row`, via new ids — nothing to describe/export yet); the truly-empty case (no roster at all) keeps `#rk-empty`, reworded to Players' old copy since the condition it now guards has genuinely changed meaning.

**Removed entirely**: `renderPlayers()`, `filterPlayers()`, `playerCard()` (`js/render-viewer.js`); the Players nav button, `#view-players` block, and `.player-card-v`/`.p-avatar`/`.p-info`/`.p-name`/`.p-room`/`.p-status` CSS (`index.html`); the `switchTab()` 'players' dispatch (`js/core.js`); both `activeTab === 'players'` dispatch lines (`js/sync.js`, the writer-update and viewer-sync render paths). A stale comment reference to `#pl-search` and two stale `renderPlayers()` code-comment mentions (in `js/advancement.js`'s `lastAssignedRound()` doc comment and `js/render-viewer.js`'s `bracketFollowStatus()` doc comment) were also cleaned up.

### Testing performed
Real playthroughs via the actual UI/console-exposed functions, awkward counts throughout (37/43/23 individual, 13/23 teams — never clean round numbers), fresh browser state each time (cleared `localStorage`, re-fetched scripts with `cache:'no-store'` to sidestep this project's known browser-pane JS-caching issue):
- **Truly-empty state**: zero roster, zero reserves — `#rk-empty` correctly shown with the reworded copy.
- **Pre-schedule roster loaded** (37 players + 3 reserves, no schedule yet): 40 correctly-sorted "Registered"/"Reserve" rows, description/download-button correctly hidden.
- **Mid-tournament, pooling=none** (37 individual, single-elimination): cross-checked every still-active unit's displayed room directly against `T.assignments` — zero mismatches, at both an early round (37 active) and after a real elimination round (30 active, 7 correctly ranked/eliminated).
- **Team format + Group Stage** (13 teams, team-3v3v3): visually confirmed the two-sublines-plus-badge stacking (member names, then round/room, then "Grp X #N" pool-rank badge) with zero overlap, including a real bye ("BYE — advances automatically") rendering correctly within a group-stage round.
- **Qualification Table** (43 individual) and **Swiss** (23 individual, odd count, "Bye" strategy): pool-rank badge confirmed correctly populated (`#N · Y.YYY FP`) and matching `T.qualTable`'s live order; zero room/round mismatches.
- **Grand-final race** (16 individual, 1v1 double-elimination, mid-race not yet decided) — the one item the plan flagged as unverified from static reading alone: confirmed the two still-active finalists carry `room: 1` and render as "🏆 Final — Room A", a sensible label.
- **Archive reuse**: saved a real mid-tournament checkpoint, opened it in Archive's detail view, confirmed the enhanced round/room content rendered correctly there too (not just live). Separately fed `computeRankings()` a hand-built minimal snapshot missing `cfg`/`qualTable`/`groupStandings` entirely (simulating a pre-refactor archive) — confirmed no crash, `poolRank` gracefully `null`.
- **PNG export**: confirmed `buildRankingsImageCanvas()` still builds a valid image with sane dimensions from the enhanced `computeRankings()` output (its `rowName()` only ever read `.label`/`.members`, confirmed unaffected by the new fields).
- **Bracket's "Follow a player"**: confirmed completely unaffected — resolved and highlighted correctly after the merge.
- **Full nav click-through** (all 5 remaining tabs) + zero console errors throughout every scenario above.
- **Repo-wide grep** for every deleted symbol/id/class: zero hits outside this log entry.
- One unrelated pre-existing issue hit and worked around during testing, not a regression from this change: reusing a `started` `T` across sequential test generations without going through Reset first left stale team data that crashed `renderManageTeams()` (`js/generation-and-roster.js`, untouched by this build) — resolved by clearing `localStorage` and reloading between tests, the same thing a real organiser would do via the Reset button.

### Explicitly out of scope
Multi-tab live-sync propagation of the new Rankings content was not verified against a real two-tab production-Firebase round-trip (no real admin password available in this session) — the `sync.js` dispatch lines that would carry it were left completely untouched (only the neighboring `'players'` lines were removed), so this is a low-risk gap, consistent with how this project has flagged the same kind of untested-but-low-risk gap before. Reinstating any search/filter on Rankings (organiser's explicit decision). Any change to `computeRankings()`'s existing ranking/elimination logic, tie-handling, or the grand-final race's winner-first ordering. Any change to the Rankings PNG export's visual content.
