## Admin setup panel: decluttered Schedule Logic dropdown + reorganized field order (done — 2026-09-08)
Organiser feedback after Stage B4 landed: FFA — Individual (and every other non-1v1/3v3 format) showed BOTH "Double elimination (needs a 1v1 or 3v3 format)" and "Double elimination — FFA/Team" simultaneously in the Schedule Logic dropdown — the first, disabled-but-visible option was pure noise once the second (the actually-usable one for that format) existed alongside it. Plus two layout requests: move "Registered players" — a read-only derived display, not an input — out of the settings card and into the Roster/Reserves card where it conceptually belongs; reorder the settings card's fields to Game format → Schedule logic → Pooling phase → Scoring system → Finals format.

**Schedule Logic decluttering** (`refreshScheduleLogicAvailability()`, `js/generation-and-roster.js`): previously only toggled `.disabled` + relabeled the classic `'double-elimination'` option with a reason suffix; now toggles `.hidden` (the standard HTML boolean attribute that removes an `<option>` from a `<select>`'s rendered list entirely, not just greys it out) in addition to `.disabled`, for BOTH `'double-elimination'` and `'double-elimination-shared-final'` — the two are now always exact mirror images of each other's visibility, never both shown, never both hidden. For 1v1/3v3 (where the classic race-format Grand Final is compatible), the shared-final generalization is hidden too, even though it's structurally compatible with every format — this matches the original plan's own "explicitly out of scope" stance (1v1/3v3 keep the original race format as their intended experience; exposing the shared-final variant there as a second opt-in option was left as a future follow-up, not built). Reworded the reason-suffix away entirely — once an incompatible option is hidden rather than disabled, there's nothing left to explain inline.

**A real load-bearing detail, caught before it could ship broken**: this app's own documented "static HTML alone governs first paint" rule (`loadState()` returns immediately on a genuinely fresh browser with no saved state, never calling `toggleFormatFields()`/this function at all) meant the static default in `index.html` had to bake in the SAME compatibility rule for the default Game Format (FFA — Individual) — `<option value="double-elimination" hidden disabled>` now ships hidden by default, not just disabled-with-a-suffix as before. Verified directly: cleared `localStorage`, reloaded, confirmed the classic double-elimination option is `hidden:true` before any JS-driven toggle ever runs.

**Field reorganization** (`index.html` only, pure markup reordering, no id/behavior changes): "Registered players" moved from the top of the settings card into the roster card, directly above the "Roster" heading. The settings card's fields regrouped so each schedule-logic-dependent or pooling-phase-dependent conditional field now sits immediately after the field that governs its visibility, rather than being scattered near the bottom: Game format (+ its Odd-count-strategy/Team-scoring conditionals) → Schedule logic (+ its Semis-override/Final-override/LB-qualifiers/Grand-Final-targets conditionals) → Pooling phase (+ its Group-size/Round-robin/Qual-adv/Qualifiers-per-group conditionals) → Scoring system → Finals format. Also fixed a small stale-wording nit noticed while touching this block: the Pooling-phase hint still said "Classic elimination" (retired in the 2026-09-08 rename) — now says "Single elimination".

### A caching gotcha, hit and resolved mid-testing (worth restating, not new)
The first round of format-matrix testing showed backwards-looking results (`.disabled` correct, `.hidden` seemingly not applying) — turned out to be this project's already-documented browser-pane JS-caching issue, not a real bug: even a freshly-opened tab was still executing a stale, pre-edit copy of `refreshScheduleLogicAvailability()` (confirmed by printing the live function's own source and finding the old `.textContent`-based implementation, not the new `.hidden`-toggling one). Resolved via the established fix (`fetch(path, {cache:'no-store'})` + `(0,eval)(text)` for every script file), then re-ran the full test matrix cleanly.

### Testing performed
- **Fresh-load default** (cleared `localStorage`, hard reload): default format FFA — Individual, classic `double-elimination` correctly `hidden:true, disabled:true`, shared-final variant correctly visible/enabled, Single elimination selected.
- **Full format matrix** (`ffa-individual`, `team-2v2v2v2`, `team-3v3v3`, `team-3v3`, `individual-1v1`): confirmed exactly one "Double elimination" variant visible per format, correctly mirrored — classic visible only for `team-3v3`/`individual-1v1`, shared-final visible only for the other three.
- **`team-3v3` + `'flex'` odd-count strategy** (the one live edge case where compatibility can flip without a format change): confirmed both options' visibility correctly flip together.
- **Selection-preservation fallback, both directions**: selected `double-elimination` under `team-3v3`, switched to FFA — correctly fell back to `single-elimination` (the now-hidden option isn't left silently selected); repeated the reverse (`double-elimination-shared-final` under FFA, switched to `individual-1v1`) — same correct fallback.
- **Layout**: confirmed "Registered players" now updates correctly from its new location (loaded a 37-player roster, `cfg-n-display` showed 37) and renders directly above the Roster heading in the roster card; confirmed the settings card's visible field order matches the requested Game format → Schedule logic → Pooling phase → Scoring system → Finals format sequence via screenshot.
- **Regression — generation itself**: both `single-elimination` (37-player FFA, 8 rounds) and `double-elimination-shared-final` (same roster, lbQualifiers=2, 17 rounds) generated cleanly after the reorg — confirms this was a pure markup/JS-toggle change with zero effect on `proceedGenerateSchedule()`'s own logic.
- Zero console errors throughout.

### Explicitly out of scope
Exposing `'double-elimination-shared-final'` as an opt-in second option for `team-3v3`/`individual-1v1` (still a deliberately deferred follow-up, not this build's job — see the plan's own "Explicitly out of scope" section). Any change to Kings Valley's always-visible-but-disabled "(coming soon)" treatment — not part of the reported clutter, left as-is.

### Follow-up 2026-09-08 — full field order refined; a design question raised, deferred
The organiser reviewed the initial reorder above and gave the COMPLETE intended field order (the first pass had only used a partial 5-item list — Game format/Schedule logic/Pooling phase/Scoring system/Finals format — and I'd guessed at where the remaining conditional fields belonged). The real, full order, confirmed explicitly: **Game format → Schedule logic → Team scoring → Pooling phase (+ its own Group size/Round-robin/Advance to bracket/Qualifiers per group sub-fields) → Odd-count strategy → Scoring system → Finals format → Grand Final win-targets → Semis size override → LB qualifiers into the Final → Final size override**. Two real differences from the first pass: **Team scoring** moved from right after Game format to right after Schedule logic; **Odd-count strategy** moved from right after Game format to right after the whole Pooling-phase group, immediately before Scoring system. Pure markup reorder again (`index.html` only) — every id/onchange/conditional-visibility JS untouched, re-verified the full show/hide matrix for a `team-3v3` + Group Stage + Double elimination combination (every conditional field correctly shown/hidden) and a full `team-3v3` generation (17 teams, Group Stage + double-elimination, 13 rounds, zero errors) after the reorder.

Before touching this, I'd asked (via `AskUserQuestion`) whether the Grand-Final win-targets field should be hidden, or the whole `double-elimination` option blocked, whenever Team scoring is "Sum of all members" — the organiser's original report called the field's appearance there "illogical." **Resolved: no code change** — the organiser clarified the real issue isn't a bug in what's shown, but a genuine ambiguity in what "Sum of all members" *means* once games get compared across a whole match: it could mean "sum a team's members' scores within one game, to decide that game's winner" (what the app actually does, both today's Grand Final race AND every normal room), or "sum every member's score across every game played" (what the ordinary multi-game Final already does via "Finals format: N games (sum)" — a different, existing mechanism the Grand Final race never uses). Not building a toggle for this now — explicitly deferred by the organiser, recorded as a parked idea in HANDOFF.md — and current field labels ("Sum of all members", "Grand Final — wins needed to take the title") are staying as-is unless a future wording suggestion is proposed and approved first.

### Follow-up 2026-09-08 — clarifying line added (Suggestion A), Semis flagged for the future
Offered three wording options for resolving the ambiguity above (a standalone clarifying line under the Grand Final field; a "(per game)" qualifier on the Team-scoring dropdown option itself; relabeling the Grand Final field to say "race to N game wins"), with a recommendation for the first (most targeted, doesn't touch the Team-scoring label everywhere else it appears). **Organiser chose Option A.** `index.html`'s `field-grand-final-targets` helper text now ends with: "Each game's team score follows the 'Team scoring' rule above, applied per game — not summed across the whole match." Pure text addition, no logic/id changes — verified the rendered `textContent` directly (no HTML-escaping issue from the embedded straight quotes, since they're inner text, not an attribute value) and confirmed zero console errors.

The organiser also flagged, for the record only — **Semis may eventually be settled over multiple games too**, and if that's ever built, the same per-game-vs-summed-across-the-match clarification would need to apply wherever Semis' own multi-game config surfaces, not just the Final/Grand-Final fields. Nothing to build today: `round.isSemis` (single-elimination's fixed Semis/Final floor) is currently always a single-game, ordinary room-based round with no multi-game concept of its own at all. Recorded as a parked idea in HANDOFF.md so a future "give Semis its own multi-game format" build starts from this reminder rather than rediscovering the same ambiguity.

## FFA/Team double-elimination — Stage B4: rendering verification pass (done — 2026-09-08)
Last of 4 staged checkpoints (see "Stage B1"/"Stage B2"/"Stage B3" below). The plan flagged this as "expected to need no changes... verification-only unless something turns up" — something turned up: a real, previously-invisible bug in how lucky-loser winners are tracked/displayed for a double-elimination round, found via the plan's own prescribed checks (the targeted grep for stray positional indexing, and confirming `computeLuckyLosers()`'s call sites weren't excluding bracket-tagged rounds) plus one further check that check surfaced: whether the persisted `T.luckyLosers` record — a *different* mechanism from `computeLuckyLosers()`'s live preview — was ever actually written for a double-elimination round at all.

**What was confirmed clean, no changes needed**: `renderMultiGameFinals()`/`renderFinalsTotal()` (`js/render-admin.js`) and `finalsProgressState()`/`buildFinalColumnHtml()` (`js/advancement.js`/`js/render-viewer.js`, the Bracket-tab Finals-scoring machinery from an earlier build) all iterate generically over `asgn`/`progress.units` — zero positional `[0]`/`[1]` indexing anywhere, so they already handle an 8-entrant shared Final exactly like a 2-entrant grand-final, no special-casing found or needed. `checkGrandFinalRace()` and the `isGrandFinal`/`race` derivations in both files correctly gate on `round.bracket === 'grand-final'`, which is `undefined` (not `'grand-final'`) for the shared Final — confirmed it correctly falls through to the ordinary multi-game-Final path rather than trying to run race logic against 8 entrants. `bracketRoundLabels()` (`js/render-viewer.js`) labels WB/LB columns purely off `round.bracket === 'winners'/'losers'`, which both `doubleEliminationBracketPhase()` and `doubleEliminationSharedFinalBracketPhase()` tag identically — confirmed directly in `js/bracket-phases.js` (lines ~512/519 and ~754/761) — so column labeling needed no changes either.

**The real bug, found by asking "does `computeLuckyLosers()`'s OTHER mechanism — the persisted `T.luckyLosers[]` array — ever get written for a double-elimination round?"** Answer: no, never, in EITHER double-elimination variant. `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()` (the generic path) returns `{ advancing, luckyNames }`, and `advanceRound()` writes `T.luckyLosers[ri+1] = result.luckyNames`. `doubleEliminationComputeAdvancement()` computed `luckyNames` internally but never returned it, and `advanceDoubleEliminationRound()` never wrote to `T.luckyLosers` at all. For the old 1v1 `double-elimination` this was invisible — `luckyCount` is always 0 for a strict head-to-head room, so there was never anything to write. For the new FFA/Team shared-final generalization, `luckyCount` is regularly nonzero (confirmed in Stage B1/B2's own testing), making this a real, reachable, user-visible bug: a lucky-loser winner in a WB/LB room would render with the `'elim'` class (grey, struck-through, 50% opacity) on their own round's Bracket card — indistinguishable from someone who was actually eliminated — despite having genuinely advanced. Admin's own "Room Assignments" seed panel (`js/render-admin.js:174`, `T.luckyLosers[ri]`) would silently omit the ★ marker for the same reason.

**A second, independent bug surfaced while fixing the first**: Bracket's read side (`js/render-viewer.js`) assumed `T.luckyLosers[ri+1]` is always "the round `ri`'s results fed into" — true for every non-bracket schedule logic (which always advances strictly sequentially, `nextRound = T.rounds[ri+1]`), but **false in general for a WB/LB round**, whose actual destination is `round.winnersTo` — not necessarily the literal next array slot, since an interposed round from the *other* bracket can sit between a WB/LB round and its own `winnersTo` target in `T.rounds`' array order (the interleaved `wb_0, [lb...], wb_1, [lb...], ..., FINAL` sequence from Stage B1). Confirmed directly in live testing (below): every single lucky-loser event in a real 37-player playthrough had `winnersTo !== sourceIndex + 1` — the gap was never zero. So even after fixing the write side to use the correct destination, the *read* side needed a matching fix or it would still look in the wrong slot.

**Fix, both sides**:
- `doubleEliminationComputeAdvancement()` (`js/advancement.js`) now returns `luckyNames` alongside `winners`/`losers`, matching `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()`'s return shape.
- `advanceDoubleEliminationRound()` now writes `T.luckyLosers[round.winnersTo] = (T.luckyLosers[round.winnersTo] || []).concat(result.luckyNames)` in its "validated, safe to commit" section — **concatenated, not overwritten**, because `round.winnersTo` can be the shared Final for *both* the winners' bracket's own last round and the losers' bracket's own last round (the merged-Final topology), so a second contribution must add to the first rather than clobber it. No equivalent write for `losersTo` — the lucky-loser mechanism only ever decides who escapes *up* into winners; nobody "lucks" their way into the losers bracket, so there's nothing to record there.
- Bracket's read (`js/render-viewer.js`, inside `buildBracketHtml()`) now computes `luckyLookupIdx = (round.winnersTo != null) ? round.winnersTo : ri + 1` before looking up `state.luckyLosers[luckyLookupIdx]` — a genuinely general rule (read a structural field the round object already carries when present, fall back to the sequential convention otherwise), not a `round.bracket`-specific branch. Admin's own seed-panel read (`T.luckyLosers[ri]`, unchanged) needed no corresponding fix — it already reads whatever landed at the *current* round's own index, which the write-side fix now populates correctly regardless of which code path wrote it.

### Testing performed
Real playthroughs via the actual UI/console-exposed functions (`proceedGenerateSchedule()`, `startTournament()`, `advanceRound()` — not hand-built `T`), fresh browser tab (no stale-cache risk this time — a brand-new server process and tab, confirmed via a fresh `node --check` pass on both edited files beforehand):
- **FFA, 37 players, `double-elimination-shared-final`, lbQualifiers=2, seeded-random scores**: played all 16 pre-Final rounds via `advanceRound()`. `T.luckyLosers` ended up with real, nonempty entries at 4 different indices — confirmed **every one** of them had a source round whose `winnersTo` was NOT the literal next array index (the gap was always 2, i.e. one interposed round from the other bracket sat between them) — direct proof the `ri+1`-assumption bug was genuinely reachable, not theoretical. Rendered the full Bracket HTML (`buildBracketHtml()`) and confirmed, for a winners-bracket source round: all 4 real lucky-loser names rendered with `bracket-player-row lucky` (not `elim`) on that round's own card, and cross-checked all 4 names were genuinely present in the round they'd advanced to (not actually eliminated). Repeated for a losers-bracket source round (a different code path — LB's own `winnersTo` feeding the shared Final): the lucky name there also rendered correctly as `lucky`. **Directly confirmed the bug would have fired without the fix**: replaying the old `state.luckyLosers[ri+1]` lookup formula against the same data returned `[]` for the winners-bracket case checked above (real data was at `ri+4`, not `ri+1`) — this is the concrete "before" proof, not just an inference. Visually confirmed in the actual rendered Bracket tab too (screenshot): a real WB round shows "★ P10" in purple exactly where a struck-through grey "eliminated" row would otherwise have appeared. Completed the tournament (scored a 3-game Final for all 8 finalists) — `computeRankings()` returned `finalComplete:true` with 8 correctly-ranked finalists, matching Stage B2's own prior verification of the Final mechanics.
- **Regression — `individual-1v1`, 16 players, unchanged `double-elimination`**: full 12-round playthrough via `advanceRound()`, reached the grand-final correctly (13 rounds total), `T.luckyLosers` confirmed empty at every index throughout (matching pre-fix behavior exactly, since `luckyCount` is always 0 for a strict head-to-head room) — zero regression from either fix.
- Zero console errors throughout every step.

### Explicitly out of scope
Nothing deferred — this closes Stage B4, the last of the 4 planned checkpoints. All 4 stages of the FFA/Team double-elimination generalization plan are now complete and independently verified.

## FFA/Team double-elimination — Stage B3: setup UI (in progress — 2026-09-08)
Third of 4 staged checkpoints (see "Stage B1"/"Stage B2" below — generation-time bracket building and live advancement, both done). This stage wires the new `'double-elimination-shared-final'` schedule logic into the actual setup panel an organiser uses (`index.html` + `js/generation-and-roster.js`), landing largely as originally planned — no redesign needed, unlike Stage B1.

**`index.html`**: new `<option value="double-elimination-shared-final">Double elimination — FFA/Team</option>` in the Schedule Logic dropdown, positioned right after Double elimination, unconditionally enabled (no `disabled` attribute — see below). New `field-lb-qualifiers` block ("LB qualifiers into the Final", a plain number input defaulting to 2), styled like the existing Grand-Final-targets field. The old combined `field-semis-final-override` (one field wrapping a two-column Semis+Final flex row) was split into two independent fields, `field-semis-override` and `field-final-override`, since Final's override is now shared between two schedule logics while Semis' stays exclusive to one.

**`js/generation-and-roster.js`, `toggleScheduleLogicFields()`**: rewritten to branch on all three schedule-logic values instead of just the double-elimination/other split it had before. Grand-Final-race fields stay exclusive to `'double-elimination'` (the shared-final generalization merges WB+LB survivors into an ordinary fixed-`numGames` Final, so it uses "Finals format" like single-elimination, not a race). Semis override stays exclusive to `'single-elimination'` (neither double-elimination variant has a "Semis" round concept). Final override now shows for both `'single-elimination'` and `'double-elimination-shared-final'` (both read `gamemodeConfig.finalSize`). LB-qualifiers shows only for the new key.

**`refreshScheduleLogicAvailability()`**: deliberately left almost untouched — it only ever gated the `'double-elimination'` option, and the new key needs no gating at all (per the plan: "available to every registered format, not gated by `idealRoomSize===2`" — `doubleEliminationSharedFinalBracketPhase()` uses only `computeTargets`/`distributeRoomsWithBye`/`computeCleanTargets`, the same format-agnostic primitives Single elimination already uses safely). Confirmed directly in testing (below) that the new option's `disabled` state stays `false` across every registered format.

**`proceedGenerateSchedule()`**: `semisOverride` is now read only for `'single-elimination'`; `finalOverride` is read for `'single-elimination'` OR `'double-elimination-shared-final'`. New `lbQualifiers` validation block, placed alongside the existing Semis/Final override validation (i.e. *before* any `T` mutation, matching this function's established "validate everything, then mutate" discipline) — recomputes a prospective Final size locally via `deriveRoomSize()` (the same helper `T.gamemodeConfig.finalSize` itself will be built from a few lines later) rather than waiting for that assignment to happen, so the check can run at the same point every other pre-mutation validation does. Refuses generation with a clear message when `lbQualifiers` isn't `>= 1` and `< finalSize`. `T.gamemodeConfig.lbQualifiers` is set right alongside where `semisOverride`/`finalOverride` are applied — `wbQualifiers` is deliberately NOT stored separately; `doubleEliminationSharedFinalBracketPhase()` already derives it as `finalSize - lbQualifiers` internally (confirmed directly in `js/bracket-phases.js`), so storing a second, independently-computed copy here would just be a redundant, driftable source of truth.

### Testing performed
All via the real UI flow (`toggleScheduleLogicFields()`/`toggleFormatFields()`/`loadRoster()`/`proceedGenerateSchedule()` called exactly as the actual click handlers would, not by constructing `T` by hand) — the browser-pane JS cache was stale from a prior tab (`BRACKET_PHASE_MIN_UNITS[...]` briefly resolved to a non-function, i.e. Stage B1's own registration wasn't loaded), caught immediately and fixed by re-fetching every script file with `cache:'no-store'` before continuing, not worked around silently.
- **Field-visibility matrix**, all three Schedule Logic values: confirmed Single elimination shows Semis+Final override, hides LB-qualifiers/Grand-Final-targets, shows Finals-format; Double elimination shows only Grand-Final-targets; the new shared-final key shows LB-qualifiers+Final override+Finals-format, hides Semis override and Grand-Final-targets — matching the plan's spec exactly for all three.
- **Availability gating across every registered format** (`ffa-individual`, `team-2v2v2v2`, `team-3v3v3`, `team-3v3`, `individual-1v1`): the shared-final option's `disabled` stayed `false` in every case; Double elimination's own existing format-gating (`true` for the three FFA/team formats, `false` for the two 1v1/3v3 formats) was unaffected — confirmed no regression from touching the neighboring function's comments.
- **Real generation, FFA 37 players, `double-elimination-shared-final`, lbQualifiers=2**: `loadRoster()` → `proceedGenerateSchedule()` through the actual DOM, zero validation errors, resulting `T.gamemodeConfig.finalSize === 8`, `T.gamemodeConfig.lbQualifiers === 2`, 17 rounds total, Final round correctly `isFinal:true`/`players:8`/`rooms:[8]` with no `round.bracket` tag — matching Stage B1/B2's own prior verification of this exact case, now reached through the real setup form instead of hand-built `T`.
- **`lbQualifiers` validation, both boundary cases**: `lbQualifiers = finalSize (8)` correctly refused ("must be... less than the Final size (8)"); `lbQualifiers = 0` correctly refused ("must be at least 1..."); confirmed `T.gamemodeConfig`/`T.rounds` were untouched by either failed attempt (still reflecting the prior successful generation), proving the validate-before-mutate ordering actually held in the real function, not just on paper.
- **Regression — `single-elimination`, FFA 37 players, no overrides**: generated cleanly (8 rounds, `finalSize:8`, `semisSize:16`), and `T.gamemodeConfig` correctly has no `lbQualifiers` key at all.
- **Regression — `individual-1v1` 16 players, unchanged `double-elimination`**: generated cleanly (13 rounds, last round `bracket:'grand-final'`), confirming the Semis/Final-override field split didn't disturb the untouched 1v1 grand-final path.
- Zero console errors throughout.

### Explicitly out of scope (deferred to Stage B4)
The rendering verification pass (`js/render-admin.js`/`js/render-viewer.js`) — expected to need no changes (WB/LB/Final rendering already exercised and confirmed correct during Stage B1/B2's own playthrough testing), but the targeted grep for stray positional indexing and the `computeLuckyLosers()` call-site check called for in the plan haven't been done yet.

## FFA/Team double-elimination — Stage B2: live advancement (in progress — 2026-09-08)
Second of 4 staged checkpoints (see "Stage B1" below for the first — generation-time bracket building, done). This stage covers the LIVE advancement engine (`js/advancement.js`) — the code that actually runs when an admin clicks "Next Round" mid-tournament.

**`doubleEliminationComputeAdvancement(ri, round, descriptor)`** generalized from the 1v1-only version, which hardcoded `scored[0]` as the sole winner and `scored[1]` as the sole loser per room, unconditionally. Now reads `round.advPerRoom`/`round.luckyCount` directly, mirroring `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()`'s own direct-advancer + cross-room lucky-loser split — a room-based round can have several direct winners and a lucky-loser winner drawn from below the cutoff, and everyone else (including a lucky-loser candidate who didn't win the cross-room comparison) becomes an explicit loser. Confirmed byte-compatible with the old 1v1 behavior when `advPerRoom:1, luckyCount:0` — this is what makes reusing ONE function for both the old `doubleEliminationBracketPhase` and the new `doubleEliminationSharedFinalBracketPhase` safe, rather than duplicating it.

**`advanceDoubleEliminationRound`**: the hardcoded "next round must be the grand-final with exactly 2 entrants" check generalized to "the next round, if it's ANY kind of Final (`isFinal:true` — true for both the old grand-final and the new shared Final), must have exactly `T.rounds[nextIdx].players` entrants" — reads the round's own frozen expected size instead of a literal `2`, correctly covering both shapes with one check. The odd-pool bye-correction is now scoped to STRICT room sizes only (`roomSize.min === roomSize.max` — the old 1v1/3v3 head-to-head shape, or a format under a strict odd-count strategy) and generalizes from a single bye unit to an array (`poolAtNext.length % roomSize.ideal`) — a FLEXIBLE room size (FFA/2v2v2v2/3v3v3's normal default shape) never needs this correction at all, since `distributeRooms()`'s own min/max window already forms a valid room split for essentially any positive count, exactly the same reason `distributeRoomsWithBye()` itself never produces a bye for those formats at generation time either.

**`finalizeDoubleEliminationRound`**: `byeChosen` parameter generalized from a single unit (or null) to an array, since a room-based round can leave more than one unit short of a clean split, unlike the old head-to-head-only case where at most one ever could. The grand-final `wbFinalistName` stamping block is untouched and correctly never fires for the new shared Final (which carries no `round.bracket` tag).

**`computeRankings()`'s elimination-detection for a `round.bracket`-tagged round** — the real, necessary fix flagged since the original plan: `var targetNames = pi === 0 ? winnersToNames : losersToNames;` was purely positional (rank-0-in-room assumed the winner). Replaced with a membership check (`eliminated` iff the unit's name is in neither `winnersToNames` nor `losersToNames`, regardless of room position) — necessary because a lucky-loser winner can sit at any position below the direct-advance cutoff, not just position 0. Identical result to the old check for the original 1v1 shape (verified directly, not just asserted — see testing below), so this is a strict generalization, not a behavior change for that case.

**Also extracted a shared lucky-loser-selection helper** (`luckyLoserCandidate()` + `pickLuckyLosers()`), since this build was about to add a THIRD independent hand-maintained copy of the exact same "find the borderline candidate, compute its relative dominance, sort, slice" logic (the first two already existed in `computeLuckyLosers()` — display-only — and `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()`). All three call sites (those two plus the new `doubleEliminationComputeAdvancement()`) now share the same selection math; each keeps its own per-room loop (since each does other things in that loop too), so `orderRoomByScore()` isn't recomputed redundantly.

### A verification mistake caught and corrected mid-testing — worth recording honestly
An early pass at verifying the `computeRankings()` fix checked `rankings.eliminatedInfo[name]` and got a clean "not eliminated" result for a known lucky-loser winner — which turned out to be a false positive: `computeRankings()`'s actual return field is `eliminatedList` (an array), not `eliminatedInfo` — the check was silently reading `undefined` regardless of the real answer, on both the buggy and fixed code paths alike. Caught by re-reading the function's own `return` statement directly rather than trusting the first "looks correct" result, then redone against the real field — this time genuinely tracing whether the lucky-loser winner's eventual elimination (found later, at a real subsequent loss) was attributed to the correct *later* round rather than the round they actually won. Worth keeping visible as a reminder that a verification script can have its own bugs, same as the code it's checking — a passing check that was never actually exercising the right code path is worse than no check at all, since it looks like coverage.

### Testing performed
Two tournaments played through via real `advanceRound()`/`advanceDoubleEliminationRound()` calls (Stage B3's setup UI doesn't exist yet, so `T.rounds`/`T.gamemodeConfig`/`T.scheduleLogic` were constructed directly, mirroring what `proceedGenerateSchedule()` will do once that stage wires it up):
- **FFA, 37 players, `double-elimination-shared-final`, wbQualifiers=6/lbQualifiers=2**: all 14 WB/LB rounds advanced with zero errors. The shared Final correctly received exactly 8 entrants — confirmed by cross-referencing every finalist against every LB round's assignments: exactly 6 never appeared in any LB room (pure WB path) and exactly 2 did (LB path), matching the configured split exactly. The Final correctly triggers Mechanism B (multi-game scoring via `T.finalScores`, since `numGames:3` from `finalsGames`) with zero special-casing needed — `computeRankings()` correctly ranked all 8 finalists once scored. **The specific case the `computeRankings()` fix targets, verified directly**: a real lucky-loser winner (won their room's cross-room comparison from a below-cutoff position) was confirmed present in `T.assignments` for the round immediately after their lucky win (i.e. genuinely advanced, not eliminated there), and their eventual `eliminatedList` entry correctly pointed to a much later round where they actually lost a second time — not the round they won.
- **Regression — `individual-1v1`, 16 players, unchanged `double-elimination`**: all 12 pre-grand-final WB/LB rounds advanced cleanly (byte-identical round count/shape to pre-Stage-B2 verification). The grand-final race itself replayed correctly (`computeGrandFinalRaceState()` tracking `wbWins`/`lbWins` correctly across 2 games, deciding at the default `wbTarget:2`), and `computeRankings()` correctly ranked the race winner as champion.
- Zero console errors throughout.

### Explicitly out of scope (deferred to later stages)
The setup-panel UI (`cfg-lb-qualifiers`, Schedule Logic dropdown option, gating) and the rendering verification pass — Stages B3–B4, not started.

## FFA/Team double-elimination — Stage B1: generation-time bracket building (in progress — 2026-09-08)
First of 4 staged checkpoints for the FFA/team double-elimination generalization (see "Classic-elimination rename" below for the sibling rename this same session also did — that one's finished; this is the larger, staged piece). This stage covers pure generation-time logic only (`js/bracket-phases.js`) — no live advancement, no UI. New `doubleEliminationSharedFinalBracketPhase()` registered as `'double-elimination-shared-final'` in `SCHEDULE_LOGICS`/`BRACKET_PHASES`/`BRACKET_PHASE_MIN_UNITS`, coexisting with the untouched `'double-elimination'` (kept exclusively for `individual-1v1`/`team-3v3`'s Grand-Final-race experience — see the plan file for the full "why a new key, not a branch" reasoning).

**The plan's own pre-verified algorithm needed a real redesign during implementation, not just coding — worth recording honestly.** The plan (already worked-example-verified by a Plan agent that actually executed the primitives, not hand-math) called for the losers' bracket to compute one target per round via `computeTargets(pool, LBQ, roundsLeft, roomSize)`, `roundsLeft = R - k` tied directly to the winners' bracket's own round count `R`. Direct testing of the actual implementation (not the plan's own three worked examples, which only checked that WB/LB totals converged correctly — not that every individual round made genuine progress) surfaced two further wasted rounds beyond the one already found and fixed during the Classic-elimination rename:
1. **A WB round could itself be wasted** even for FFA's own room size 8 (not just the room-size-2 case the rename's fix already covers) — narrowing a 37-player field all the way down to a small `wbQualifiers` value (6) requested more rounds than `computeTargets()`/`snapFriendly()` could fill with genuinely-decreasing values, once the room count got small near the tail.
2. **An LB round could independently be wasted too**, even when WB's own targets were already clean — confirmed with a 32-player FFA field, where an early LB round computed a target equal to its own input. This is a structurally different problem from #1: LB recomputes fresh every round from a *live*, round-by-round-varying incoming pool (this round's own WB drop-ins plus the prior LB round's survivors), not from one static upfront array, so a wasted round can occur anywhere in the sequence, not just as a snapping artifact in one big precomputed list.

**Fix, in two parts:**
- **`computeCleanTargets(total, floor, requestedNumRounds, roomSize)`** (new, in `js/bracket-phases.js`, right after `computeElimRoundCount()`): iteratively tries fewer rounds than requested until `computeTargets()` returns a strictly-decreasing sequence (every target genuinely less than the one before it). Cheap (bounded by `MAX_ELIM_ROUNDS=8`), needs no closed-form "how many rounds are achievable" formula, and is a confirmed no-op for every already-working case (FFA/team-2v2v2v2/team-3v3v3's existing Semis-floor narrowing, and the post-rename `single-elimination` head-to-head case) — applied to both `singleEliminationBracketPhase`'s own elimination-round loop (replacing a bare `computeTargets()` call, with `numElim` now derived from the actual — possibly further-reduced — clean sequence length, not trusted from `computeElimRoundCount()` alone) and the new WB loop below.
- **LB's per-round computation now defers round creation until it would make genuine progress**, rather than creating one round per WB round unconditionally. A WB round's drop count accumulates (not discarded) across as many WB rounds as it takes until the combined pool can actually be narrowed by at least one unit; only then is a real LB round created, and *every* WB round whose drops fed that accumulation gets routed to it. This means `lb.length` is no longer forced to equal `R` (WB's own round count) — confirmed empirically this produces materially *tighter* brackets too, not just correct ones (e.g. a 2v2v2v2 case dropped from 7 requested WB rounds to 5 actually-useful ones once WB's own clean-targets fix applied). The interleaving step was reworked to route each WB round's losers to whichever LB round actually resolved them (tracked via `afterWbIndex` per LB round + a `wbLosersLbIndex` map), rather than assuming a fixed 1:1 WB-to-LB position correspondence.
- **The shared Final always uses a literal single room (`rooms:[FINAL]`)**, not `distributeRooms(FINAL, roomSize)` — an inconsistency caught during testing (a 2v2v2v2 example produced a Final split across `[3,2]` rooms). Matches `singleEliminationBracketPhase`'s own existing Final precedent exactly — the rest of the app (Bracket/Scoreboard/Rankings) treats "the Final" as one shared room where every finalist's score is directly comparable, and an oversized `FINAL` is already caught by the existing `validateRoomCap()` check that runs generically after `buildProgression()`, not this function's job to pre-empt.

### Testing performed
Two layers: (1) isolated Node execution of the real `js/bracket-phases.js`/`js/formats-and-primitives.js` source (stubbing only the cross-file function references not needed for pure generation-time logic), and (2) the same checks repeated live in-browser through the real `BRACKET_PHASES` registry, confirming identical results:
- **Zero wasted rounds anywhere**, across 5 format/room-size combinations: FFA 37p (WBQ6/LBQ2), FFA 32p (WBQ6/LBQ2 — the case that surfaced the LB bug), team-2v2v2v2 16t (WBQ4/LBQ1), team-3v3v3 27t (WBQ5/LBQ2), and `individual-1v1` 16p (WBQ7/LBQ1 — confirming the new phase works correctly for a head-to-head format too, even though it's not surfaced as that format's default).
- **Full routing-chain self-consistency**, not just endpoint totals: simulated walking every round's `winnersTo`/`losersTo` and accumulating into a `pendingBracketSeeds`-equivalent map, then verified every LB round's *actual staged* incoming pool exactly matches its own `players` count, and the shared Final's staged pool exactly matches `FINAL` — this is the check that would have caught the original plan's routing assumptions being wrong, had this redesign not already been necessary for the wasted-round bugs.
- **Zero regression** on `single-elimination` (formerly Classic elimination): FFA 37p and `individual-1v1` 16p both reproduce byte-identical output (same round count, same `advPerRoom`/`luckyCount` sequence) to what was verified immediately after the Classic-elimination rename, confirming `computeCleanTargets()` is a true no-op for those cases, not a silent behavior change.
- Zero console errors throughout.

### Explicitly out of scope (deferred to later stages, per the plan's own staging)
Live advancement (`js/advancement.js` — `doubleEliminationComputeAdvancement`, `advanceDoubleEliminationRound`, `finalizeDoubleEliminationRound`, `computeRankings()`'s positional winner/loser bug), the setup-panel UI (`cfg-lb-qualifiers`, Schedule Logic dropdown option, gating), and the rendering verification pass — Stages B2–B4, not started.

## Classic-elimination rename: "Classic elimination" → "Single elimination" (done — 2026-09-08)
First of two pieces parked from an earlier admin-cleanup session (see "Fair Points sign fix..." below for the context that led here). The organiser wanted "Classic elimination" gone as a name, keeping its actual mechanism (gradual room-based cuts + lucky-loser buffer) for FFA/team formats, with the old, narrower head-to-head-only "Single elimination" folded away. `classicEliminationBracketPhase()` was renamed to `singleEliminationBracketPhase()` (reusing the name the old, retired function vacated); `SCHEDULE_LOGICS`/`BRACKET_PHASES`/`BRACKET_PHASE_MIN_UNITS` collapsed from three registered schedule logics to two (`single-elimination`, `double-elimination`); the old narrow `singleEliminationBracketPhase()` (pure recursive halving, no lucky-loser mechanism, no Semis/Final floor) was deleted outright.

**A real bug was found and fixed during verification, not just a rename.** The plan's own justification for the rename being "safe" — that Classic's mechanism degenerates cleanly to the old narrow mechanism's behavior for a strict head-to-head room shape — was asserted from hand-calculation during planning and turned out to be **wrong**. Direct testing (generating a 16-player `individual-1v1` tournament under the newly-renamed `single-elimination`) produced **11 total rounds instead of the expected 6**, including one round whose own computed target equaled its input — a genuine "elimination round" that eliminated nobody. Root cause: `computeElimRoundCount()`'s pacing formula (`TARGET_ROUND_SURVIVAL_RATIO = 0.80`, tuned for FFA's 6-8-per-room granularity, which can express a wide range of per-round survival ratios) has no way to express itself in a room of exactly 2, which can only ever cut to *exactly* half — there's no room-size-2 equivalent of a gentle 80%-survival cut. Requesting more elimination rounds than pure halving needs (7 requested vs. 2 actually needed, for 16→4) forced `computeTargets()`/`snapFriendly()` to pad the schedule with near-degenerate rounds, one of which came out exactly zero-progress.

**Fix**: `computeElimRoundCount(total, floor, roomSize)` gained a third parameter and now caps its result at the room's own natural pure-halving round count (`Math.ceil(Math.log(total/floor)/Math.log(2))`) **specifically and only when `roomSize.ideal === 2`** — deliberately not generalized to every room size, since `team-3v3v3`'s `ideal:3` (`(3-1)/3 ≈ 0.667`) and `team-2v2v2v2`'s `ideal:4` (`(4-1)/4 = 0.75`) both sit *below* `TARGET_ROUND_SURVIVAL_RATIO = 0.80` too — a naive room-size-aware generalization would have also changed round pacing for those two already-shipped, already-tested formats, which was never asked for and is explicitly out of scope. Confirmed via direct testing that FFA (`ideal:8`, `0.875 > 0.80`, cap never triggers), `team-2v2v2v2`, and `team-3v3v3` are all byte-identical to their pre-fix output — the cap is provably a no-op for every format except the two newly-migrated head-to-head ones (`individual-1v1`, `team-3v3`).

**A note on how this was caught, worth keeping visible**: the rename's plan file explicitly claimed the equivalence was "verified by hand" — it wasn't actually verified, just asserted from an unchecked mental calculation, exactly the mistake this project's own standing rule ("verify by direct enumeration, don't trust the formula blind," already written into this same function's neighboring code for the *original* double-elimination LB round-count formula) exists to prevent. The fix only happened because the plan's own verification section required actually generating a tournament and inspecting the real output before calling the build done, not because the equivalence claim was re-examined on paper. Worth remembering for the double-elimination generalization work this session also touches — see below.

### Testing performed
Local dev server, direct console-driven testing, zero console errors throughout:
- **FFA, 37 players**: byte-identical round shape before and after both the registry rename and the `computeElimRoundCount` fix (8 rounds, same room/advPerRoom/luckyCount values at every round) — confirms zero regression for the format this mechanism was originally built around.
- **`individual-1v1`, 16 players, `'none'` odd-count strategy**: before the fix, 11 rounds with a genuine zero-progress round; after the fix, exactly 6 rounds (2 warm-up + 4 pure-halving bracket rounds), `advPerRoom:1, luckyCount:0` throughout (lucky-loser math correctly never firing) — matching the old, retired mechanism's output exactly. Played a full tournament through to the Final via direct `advanceRound()` calls; `computeRankings()` and Bracket both rendered without error.
- **`team-3v3`, 16 teams, `'none'`**: 6 rounds, same shape as the 1v1 case (confirms the fix applies correctly to both `idealRoomSize:2` formats, not just individual ones).
- **`team-3v3v3`, 27 teams**: 11 rounds, no zero-progress round anywhere, `advPerRoom` values all meaningfully cut the field each round — confirms the fix's `roomSize.ideal===2` scoping correctly leaves this format (`ideal:3`) untouched.
- **`double-elimination` (unchanged 1v1 mechanism), 16 players**: byte-identical 13-round WB/LB/GF structure before and after this build — confirms the registry consolidation didn't disturb the sibling schedule logic that shares the same `SCHEDULE_LOGICS`/`BRACKET_PHASE_MIN_UNITS` registries.
- Schedule Logic dropdown: `single-elimination` always enabled regardless of format (matching how `classic-elimination` never was gated); `double-elimination` still correctly gated by format + odd-count-strategy compatibility, unchanged from the prior admin-cleanup build.

### Explicitly out of scope
Applying `computeElimRoundCount`'s new room-size-aware cap to any room size other than exactly 2 (would change already-shipped `team-2v2v2v2`/`team-3v3v3` pacing, not requested). Any change to `doubleEliminationBracketPhase`/`doubleEliminationComputeAdvancement` — that mechanism is untouched by this build.

## Fair Points sign fix + Raw score removal + Schedule Logic gating (done — 2026-09-08)
Three small, independent, organiser-approved fixes bundled into one build since none touches enough surface area to warrant its own entry. The organiser asked to clarify the Admin setup panel ("has become quite messy"); the underlying scoring/schedule-logic mechanics were explained directly from the actual code, which surfaced two real bugs beyond the naming confusion — the organiser independently caught the Fair Points sign issue too, matching what a direct code read found. One genuine design fork (how to fix the tiebreak) was put to the organiser via `AskUserQuestion` — decided: the minimal, room-size-blind fix over a room-size-scaled alternative.

**Fair Points sign fix**: `fairPoints(rank, score)` (`js/formats-and-primitives.js`) was `rank + score / 100000` — under the ascending sort every qual/group-standings table uses, a HIGHER score at the same rank produced a WORSE (higher) total, backwards from the intended "higher score wins a tie" behaviour. Changed to `rank - score / 100000`. Deliberately minimal: sort direction, `detectQualCutoffTie()`/`detectGroupCutoffTie()`/`applyQualCutoffOrder()`/`applyGroupCutoffOrder()`, and Rankings' `fpMap` (`js/render-viewer.js`) are all untouched and remain correct as-is, since they only depend on ascending-sort-by-`totalFP`, which this change doesn't alter. **Rejected alternative**: room-size-scaled positional points (an 8-room's winner scores 8, a 6-room's winner scores 6, higher-total-wins) — fixes the same bug but reintroduces room-size sensitivity into the *primary* ranking signal, contradicting the app's existing "same rank = same standing regardless of room size" property, which matters here specifically because this app's whole reason for existing is handling uneven room splits from awkward registration counts. Would also have required flipping sort direction and re-verifying every cutoff-detection/null-handling call site. Organiser agreed the minimal fix was correct.

**Raw score removal**: deleted the `<option value="raw">` from `cfg-scoring` (`index.html`) — closes the standing gap flagged in "Audit resolutions" above ("Raw-score qualification metric": neither documented scoring system literally described what `updateQualTable()` actually did under "raw" — it summed rank only, dropping the score-based tiebreak entirely, producing *more* ties than Fair Points, not a genuinely different metric). `T.cfg.scoring === 'fairpoints' ? fairPoints(...) : rank` simplified to an unconditional `fairPoints(...)` call at both call sites (`js/advancement.js:268`, `:347`). `cfg.scoring` itself is left in place in the save/restore/generate code (`js/core.js`, `js/generation-and-roster.js`) — now vestigial (never read again) but harmless, not worth the churn to remove. **Known consequence, not fixed**: a tournament already mid-flight with `scoring:'raw'` saved in some organiser's `localStorage` will have its live standings silently recompute under Fair Points from the moment this ships — accepted given this app's low concurrent-user count.

**Schedule Logic gating**: Single/Double elimination in `cfg-schedule-logic` are now disabled (with an inline "(needs a 1v1 or 3v3 format)" reason appended to the label) whenever the selected Game Format's `GAME_FORMATS[...].idealRoomSize !== 2` or the selected Odd-count strategy is `'flex'` — the exact same two conditions `singleEliminationBracketPhase`/`doubleEliminationBracketPhase` (`js/bracket-phases.js`) already hard-throw on, just surfaced at selection time instead of at Generate-Schedule time. New `refreshScheduleLogicAvailability()` (`js/generation-and-roster.js`) recomputes both options' `disabled`/label on every call, resets the selection to `classic-elimination` only if the CURRENTLY selected option just became invalid (preserves an already-valid choice otherwise — e.g. a restored double-elimination team-3v3 setup survives `loadState()` untouched), and is called from the end of `toggleFormatFields()` (covers format changes and the `loadState()` restore path) plus directly from a new `onchange` on `cfg-odd-count-strategy` (covers the one case where format stays fixed at team-3v3 but the organiser flips to 'flex' afterward). Static HTML defaults (`index.html`) were also updated to start both options pre-disabled, matching the default Game Format (`ffa-individual`) — required because a genuinely fresh page load with no saved `localStorage` state never calls `toggleFormatFields()`/`toggleScheduleLogicFields()` at all (`loadState()` returns immediately on no saved data), so the static markup alone governs first paint.

### Testing performed
Local dev server, direct console-driven testing, zero console errors throughout:
1. **Fair Points sign**: `fairPoints(1, 1306)` = 0.98694, `fairPoints(1, 1200)` = 0.988 — the higher score (1306) correctly sorts first under the existing ascending sort, confirming the previously-backwards tiebreak is fixed.
2. **Raw Score removed**: `cfg-scoring` shows only the Fair Points option, correctly relabeled. Injected a stale saved setup with `scoring:'raw'` directly into `localStorage` and called `loadState()` — no error thrown; the scoring dropdown (now single-option) shows as unselected rather than falling back to Fair Points, a minor cosmetic quirk of a stale value matching no existing option (standard `<select>` behavior) — does not affect actual scoring, since `fairPoints()` is now called unconditionally regardless of what sits in that field. Accepted, not fixed, per the plan's own scoping.
3. **Fresh load gating**: with `localStorage` cleared and the page freshly navigated, Single/Double elimination show `disabled:true` with the reason suffix by default, matching the default `ffa-individual` format — confirms the static-HTML-default approach works without relying on any init call.
4. **Format switch gating**: switching to `individual-1v1` or `team-3v3` (odd-count-strategy defaulting to `'none'`) correctly enables Single/Double with their plain labels; switching back to `ffa-individual` correctly re-disables both with the reason suffix restored.
5. **Flex edge case**: with `team-3v3` + Double elimination selected (grand-final-targets field visible, finals-games field hidden), switching Odd-count strategy to `'flex'` correctly re-disables both Single and Double, falls back the selection to Classic elimination, and correctly re-syncs the dependent field visibility (`field-grand-final-targets` hides, `field-finals-games` and `field-semis-final-override` show) — confirmed via `toggleScheduleLogicFields()` being re-invoked as part of the fallback.
6. **Selection preservation**: saved a `double-elimination` + `team-3v3` + `'none'` setup via `saveState()`, perturbed the live DOM to a different format/logic, then called `loadState()` — the restored selection correctly came back as `double-elimination` (not force-reset to Classic elimination), with Single elimination correctly showing enabled to match the restored `team-3v3` format.
7. **Regression**: a 37-player FFA tournament (awkward count) generated and started cleanly under Classic elimination (8 rounds, zero errors). A 16-player 1v1 tournament generated cleanly under both Single elimination (6 rounds: 2 fixed warm-up + 4 bracket rounds, matching `log2(16)`) and Double elimination (13 rounds: 2 warm-up + 4 WB + 6 LB + 1 grand final, matching the `2R-2` LB formula) — both zero errors.
8. Zero console errors across every step above.

Small follow-up requested alongside a parked idea for a bigger future feature (see "Parked ideas" in HANDOFF.md — showing the actual seed source for each future room slot, not just the room's size; the organiser hasn't decided whether they want it yet, so it's noted, not built). Every room heading in Bracket now shows its player count in parentheses — "Room A (8)", "Group B · Room C (2)" — for both real, already-seeded rooms and Feature 5's placeholder rooms alike, in both individual and group-stage label shapes.

Scoreboard's own separate room-heading builder (`sbRoomHeading`) was deliberately left untouched — the organiser asked for this on "the brackets" specifically.

Group stage (17 players, group size 4): "Group A · Room A (2)" through "Group D · Room H (2)" all correct. Zero console errors.

## Bracket: unresolved-tie indicator (done — 2026-09-07)
Last of the five-feature request from the organiser (see "Roster delimiter"/"App rename"/"Fold all Bracket rounds"/"Projected/placeholder bracket" above for the other four). Adds a red ⚠ TB? marker in Bracket for any unit whose result depends on a tie the organiser hasn't resolved yet — both a per-room tie and a qualification/group-stage cutoff tie — matching the color of Admin's own existing unresolved-tie banner rather than reusing amber, which is already the *resolved*-tie badge's color in this same column.

**The real work was Archive-purity, not the rendering.** `getAllTies()` (`js/advancement.js`) actively calls `updateQualTable()`/`updateGroupStandings()` as a side effect — both WRITE into live `T` by reading live `T.rounds`/`T.assignments`. Calling it unmodified from Archive's read-only `buildBracketHtml(snap)` render would have silently recomputed and corrupted the *live* tournament's standings as a side effect of merely opening an old snapshot. Fixed by state-parameterizing `getAllTies()`, `isTieResolved()`, `detectQualCutoffTie()`, `detectGroupCutoffTie()`, and `hasPendingTies()` (all default to `T` when called with no state, so every existing call site — Admin's tie banner, `renderStandingsTable()`, `advanceRound()`'s own gate — needed zero edits), with an `isLive = (state === T)` gate deciding whether the two mutating recompute calls actually run. A snapshot's `qualTable`/`groupStandings` were already frozen correctly by `performArchiveSave()`'s deep clone at save time, so skipping the recompute for a snapshot is not just safe but correct — the frozen numbers are exactly what should be shown.

Rendering integration (`buildBracketHtml()`, `js/render-viewer.js`): computes `unresolvedTieNames` right after the existing resolved-tie-badge (`tbNames`) block, gated on `showResults` (same partial-room-privacy rule the resolved badge already follows), and layers a `tie-pending` class + badge onto the row — deliberately only setting `color`, never `border-left-color`, so it composes with the existing adv/elim/lucky/is-followed signals instead of overriding them (same reasoning already established for `.is-followed`). CSS placed after the existing `.adv`/`.elim`/`.lucky` rules so it wins the same-specificity source-order tie-break — an elimination that depends on a still-unresolved tie isn't actually settled, so the red should win over the strikethrough/heavy-fade.

### Known, accepted limitation found during testing — not fixed
For a group-stage tournament with **unequal group sizes** (a real, supported, awkward-count scenario — see "Projected/placeholder bracket" above, which hit the same asymmetry), a group whose round-robin finishes before the group-stage phase's overall final round can have an unresolved cutoff tie that **never visually shows** in Bracket, because `getAllTies()` only attaches a group's cutoff tie to the *globally* last group-stage round's `ties` dict (matching `advanceRound()`'s own gating, which correctly waits until every group has finished before applying any group's cut), and that group's own players have no rows left to attach a badge to by that point — they stop appearing in the round columns once their own matches are done. **This does not affect correctness**: Admin's tie banner and `hasPendingTies()`'s advance-block both still correctly detect and block on the tie regardless of which round it's checked from; only Bracket's cosmetic red marker fails to reach it. Confirmed via direct testing (5-member vs 4-member groups) and confirmed the *symmetric* case — equal group sizes, the common case — works correctly, including two independent simultaneous group ties resolving independently of each other. A proper fix would mean attaching each group's cutoff tie to that group's own last-appearance round instead of the phase's global last round, which risks changing `hasPendingTies()`'s gating semantics for an edge case and needs its own isolated pass, not a tack-on to this build — noted here for a future session, not attempted now.

### Testing performed
Local dev server, direct console-driven state manipulation (matches this session's established testing pattern — real click-through is unreliable in this harness), zero console errors throughout. Hit and recovered from the documented Browser-pane JS-caching issue mid-session (a `location.reload()` + `navigate()` combo silently re-served stale cached scripts; re-confirmed via `isTieResolved.toString()` showing the old 2-arg signature, fixed by the established `fetch({cache:'no-store'})` + `eval` force-refresh pattern) — a reminder that any real navigation mid-session needs a fresh force-refresh before trusting results.
- **Per-room tie**: two players tied at the top score in an 8-player room — both show red + `⚠ TB?`, Admin's own tie banner independently confirmed as showing the identical cluster; `resolveTie()` flips both to the existing amber `⚖ TB` with the red marker correctly cleared.
- **Partial room** (`showResults` gate): an incomplete room with a would-be tie shows zero markers of any kind, matching existing partial-room behavior exactly.
- **Qual-cutoff tie**: a 16-player qual-table tournament (raw scoring) crafted to tie exactly at the `qualAdv` boundary — confirmed the tie genuinely blocks `advanceRound()` (not staged, a real block), shows red + badge on the last qual round's card for the correct two names (drawn from different rooms — the cutoff spans rooms, unlike a per-room tie), and resolving it un-blocks advancement.
- **Group-cutoff tie, symmetric groups** (4 groups of 4, the common case): all four groups independently tied at their own `qualifiersPerGroup` boundary — all 12 affected names correctly marked simultaneously; resolving one group's tie clears only that group's markers, the other three stay red — the exact "render independently" check the plan called for.
- **Group-cutoff tie, asymmetric groups** (one group of 5, three of 4): found and documented the limitation above.
- **`.elim` + tie-pending combination**: two bottom-ranked (eliminated) players tied — confirmed via computed style, not just class name, that the row renders `color: rgb(255,77,106)` (`--red`), `text-decoration: none` (strikethrough correctly suppressed), `opacity: 0.85` (legible, not washed out).
- **Team format**: a 4-team room with the top two tied — `.bracket-team-name` picks up red (computed style confirmed), `.bracket-team-members` stays muted, both team rows correctly `adv` + `tie-pending` simultaneously.
- **Final/grand-final column**: not separately live-tested this pass — guaranteed by construction, confirmed by direct code reading: `detectTieBreaks()` returns `{}` unconditionally for `round.isFinal`, and neither `isLastStandingsRound` nor `isLastGroupStageRound` can ever be true for a Final round, so `unresolvedTieNames` is structurally always empty there.
- **Archive-purity — the critical test**: saved a mid-tournament checkpoint via `performArchiveSave()` while a qual-cutoff tie was genuinely unresolved; resolved it live and advanced further so live `T` diverged from the snapshot; called `buildBracketHtml(entry.snapshot)` exactly as `js/archive.js`'s real call site does (no `collapseMap`/`follow`/`editable`); confirmed the archived view still shows the tie **unresolved** (red) while the live Bracket render of the same round shows it **resolved** (no marker) — the two views disagreeing is the proof the snapshot is genuinely being read, not live state; confirmed `T.curRound`/`T.tieResolutions`/`T.qualTable` were **byte-identical** immediately before and after opening/rendering the archive — zero mutation from merely viewing it.
- **Regression**: Admin's tie banner and resolution buttons, `hasPendingTies()`'s advance-block, and Scoreboard's own render all confirmed unaffected.

## Projected/placeholder bracket for future rounds (done — 2026-09-07)
Fourth of a five-feature request from the organiser (see "Roster delimiter"/"App rename"/"Fold all Bracket rounds" above for the other three landed so far); paused after this one at the organiser's request, with Feature 3 (red unresolved-tie indicator) left for a follow-up session. Replaces Bracket's flat `"Not yet seeded"` text for any round beyond wherever the tournament has actually reached with generic dashed placeholder slots matching that round's already-known room structure.

**Scope decision, locked in the plan before coding started**: room structure is confirmed knowable at generation time for every format — including, for group-stage and double-elimination, genuinely real projected pairings, not just room shape. This build deliberately shows uniform generic placeholder slots for every format regardless, matching the organiser's literal wording ("placeholders instead of players' names") rather than building a projection engine nobody asked for. One code path across five formats, not five special cases.

**Implementation**: new pure function `buildPlaceholderRoundHtml(state, round)` in `js/render-viewer.js`, placed right after `buildBracketHtml()` closes — for each of `round.rooms`, emits a room label (reusing the exact `Group X · Room Y` / `Room Y` heading logic the real room loop already uses) followed by one dashed placeholder row per slot. A round with `pairingTBD` (Swiss round 2+, where the room *count* is known but pairings aren't — same distinction `renderPreview()` already draws) gets an appended "Pairings determined live" note; no name/seed/order is ever implied for it. `buildBracketHtml()`'s `if (!asgn.length) { ...'Not yet seeded'... }` branch now calls this new function instead. Two small CSS rules added next to `.bracket-room-label` for the dashed placeholder row and the Swiss note. Pure function of its two arguments — preserves Archive's `buildBracketHtml(snap)` purity guarantee exactly like the rest of this file; composes with the existing fold-any-round feature for free since collapse is checked before any body content is generated.

### Testing performed
Local dev server, direct console-driven generation (not click-through — matches this session's established testing pattern), zero console errors throughout:
- **Classic elimination, 37 players** (awkward count): every future round's placeholder room/slot counts cross-checked directly against `renderPreview()`'s own room-distribution summary — exact match at every round (e.g. round 4: preview says "30 players, 2×8+2×7"; placeholder render shows the same 30 slots across 4 rooms).
- **Double-elimination, 17 teams**, `odd-count-strategy=bye`: WB/LB/GF future columns all show placeholders with the correct header accent colour per bracket; incidentally exercised the defensive `rooms.length === 0` fallback for real (an LB round with no rooms yet scheduled in this particular bye configuration correctly fell back to the "Not yet seeded" message rather than rendering an empty room-group).
- **Swiss, 37 players**: rounds 2+ show correct room-count placeholders plus the "Pairings determined live" note; confirmed by direct text-content inspection that no player name leaks into any future Swiss round's placeholder body.
- **Group stage, 17 players**, group size 4: correct `Group X · Room Y` labels; incidentally surfaced that this app's round-robin scheduling can leave some groups with more rounds than others (a group of 5 needs an extra round versus groups of 4) — confirmed this is pre-existing scheduling behaviour, not a placeholder bug, and the placeholder builder correctly reflects whatever `round.rooms`/`round.roomGroups` the schedule actually produced for that round.
- **Round transition**: advanced a live 37-player tournament from round 0 into round 1 — the newly-reached round switched cleanly from placeholder to real scored rows on the very next render, with round 2 still correctly showing placeholders.
- **Archive purity**: saved a mid-tournament snapshot via `performArchiveSave()`, called `buildBracketHtml(snap)` exactly as `js/archive.js`'s real call site does (no `collapseMap`/`follow`/`editable` arguments) — future rounds correctly showed placeholders, fully expanded (no chevrons, nothing clickable, matching Archive's existing no-collapse-state behaviour), reached rounds showed real rows. Cleaned up the test archive entry afterward.
- **Completed tournament**: played a small bracket through to its final round and confirmed zero placeholder rows appear anywhere once every round has real data — the placeholder path only ever fires for a round that's genuinely still unseeded.

### Out of scope (deliberate)
Real projected matchups for any format (see Scope decision above) — including double-elimination's technically-derivable WB/LB routing and group-stage's technically-derivable round-robin pairings. Bye projection — `round.byeCount` is itself a shifting projection per `js/render-admin.js`'s own existing comment; stays out of the placeholder builder, matching how the existing bye-card code path only ever runs once a round has real assignments.

## Fold all Bracket rounds — current and future, not just past (done — 2026-09-07)
The organiser asked to make it possible to fold ANY round in Bracket, not just past ones — with the explicit requirement that a folded *current* round keep showing the cyan "this is the one being played" indicator. Investigation found this was almost entirely a one-line change: `renderBracketNow()`'s `collapseMap`-building loop was hard-bounded to `ri < T.curRound`, and current/future rounds got no map entry at all (not `collapsed:false` — structurally absent, which is what made them non-collapsible). Widened the bound to `ri < T.rounds.length`.

**The cyan indicator requirement needed zero new code** — confirmed by reading the emission order directly before writing anything: both `.current-col` (the card's cyan border/glow) and `.current-hdr` (cyan header text) are already written into the markup *before* `buildBracketHtml()`'s collapse early-return, and are derived from `isCurrent` completely independently of `isCollapsed`. Verified directly in-browser (not just read): toggled the current round of a real 16-round double-elimination tournament closed, confirmed via computed style that both the card border and header text stayed exactly `#00E5FF`.

`bracketRoundDefaultCollapsed()`'s existing formula (`ri < curRound - 1`) needed no change either — it already evaluates to "not collapsed" for the current round and everything after it, the moment it's consulted for them, which only started happening once the loop bound widened. Defaults are unchanged; only manual folding is newly possible.

One small polish addition: `.collapsible:hover{color:var(--text)}` now applies to the current round for the first time and would otherwise wipe its cyan on hover — added a more specific `.current-hdr.collapsible:hover{color:var(--cyan)}` override.

### Testing performed
Real 17-team, double-elimination, team-3v3 tournament (odd bracket, forced to `"Bye"` odd-count strategy since double-elimination's default `"None"` strategy correctly refuses a non-power-of-2 count — a real, correct validation catch during test setup, not a bug) advanced to round 3 of 16. Confirmed defaults unchanged before touching anything (rounds 0–1 collapsed, round 2 expanded, round 4+ not collapsed). Folded the *current* round — cyan card border and cyan header text both confirmed via `getComputedStyle` to be exactly `#00E5FF` on the collapsed strip. Folded a future round; folded the *last* round of 16 (`T.rounds.length - 1`, the off-by-one case the new loop bound could have gotten wrong) — confirmed exactly 16 columns render, matching `T.rounds.length`, no missing or extra column. **The composed case most likely to surprise**: folded a round while it was still future, advanced the tournament until that exact round became current — confirmed it stayed folded *and* correctly picked up the `current-col` class at the same time. Archive re-verified unaffected: opened an archived entry with the same 16-round tournament, confirmed zero `.collapsible` headers, zero chevrons, zero collapsed columns — `buildBracketHtml(snap)` still passes no `collapseMap`, so nothing there changed. Zero console errors throughout.

### Explicitly out of scope
Changing what's collapsed by default — confirmed via direct testing that the existing heuristic already does the right thing for current/future rounds without any change; this build is purely about *permitting* manual folding, not changing defaults.

---

## App rename: "Curve FFA" → "Curve Fever Pro Tour Hub" / "CFP Tour Hub" (done — 2026-09-07)
The organiser asked for the app's displayed name to change everywhere relevant, with "CFP Tour Hub" as an accepted shortening for tight spots. Four user-visible occurrences, all confirmed exhaustively via `grep`: `<title>` (full name), the sticky header `.logo` (short form — cyan `CFP` + plain `Tour Hub`, preserving the existing "brand word cyan, qualifier plain" split rather than the initially-assumed-but-verified-wrong "initialism highlighted" pattern), and the Rankings PNG export's in-image logo (short form, mirroring the header) + footer caption (full name).

**A real mobile regression found and fixed during verification, not assumed away**: "CFP Tour Hub" is wider than "CURVE FFA" was, and a reduced-font-size-only fix (16px/2px inside the existing `@media(max-width:700px)` block) wasn't sufficient on its own — at 375px with a long tournament title, the logo text wrapped onto three lines instead of staying on one. Fixed with `white-space:nowrap;flex-shrink:0` on `.logo`, which correctly shifts the squeeze onto `.hdr-title`'s own pre-existing ellipsis truncation (exactly what it's designed for) instead of letting the logo itself wrap. Verified directly (not just visually) that `document.body.scrollWidth === window.innerWidth` at 375px even with a deliberately extreme 45-character tournament title — zero actual horizontal overflow.

**Explicitly out of scope, decisions not oversights**: localStorage key prefixes (`curveFFA_state_v1`, `curveFFA_admin_unlocked`, `curveFFA_admin_proof_hash`, `curveFFA_archive_index`/`curveFFA_archive_{id}`, `curveFFA_bracket_follow`) — invisible technical identifiers; renaming would silently orphan every organiser's already-saved data in their own browser for zero user-visible benefit. GitHub repo name and Firebase project identifiers (`js/sync.js`) — live external-service identifiers; renaming breaks the deployed URL and live sync. Archive export JSON filename prefixes — confirmed these already say "curve-tournament", never "curve-ffa", so there was nothing to change. Every "FFA" that names the Free-For-All *game format* (`GAME_FORMATS['ffa-individual']`, its dropdown label, related comments throughout `js/formats-and-primitives.js`/`js/generation-and-roster.js`) — a different concept from the application's own name, confirmed untouched.

### Testing performed
Wide viewport: cyan `CFP` + plain `Tour Hub`, no overlap with title/round display. Narrow (375px) with a deliberately long tournament title: logo stays one line; confirmed via direct DOM measurement (not just a screenshot) that the page has zero real horizontal overflow. PNG export actually rendered and visually inspected (not just generated) — a real 37-player tournament exported to canvas, in-image logo shows correct cyan/white split with no collision with the right-aligned player/round-count text, footer caption reads "Generated by Curve Fever Pro Tour Hub · &lt;date&gt;". Negative checks: game-format dropdown still reads "FFA — Individual"; `grep -rin "curve ffa"` across `index.html`/`js/` returns zero real hits. Zero console errors.

## Roster delimiter: "|" → "," (done — 2026-09-07)
The organiser found `|` (pipe) an inconvenient character to type when entering team rosters — nobody naturally reaches for it. Replaced with `,` (comma) everywhere the "TeamName | P1 | P2" syntax is parsed, typed, or taught: `parseTeamLines()`'s own split (`js/generation-and-roster.js`), `promptWalkupTeam()`'s prompt text and pre-filled default, `toggleFormatFields()`'s four hint/placeholder strings, `loadRoster()`'s empty-team refusal alert, and matching comments. `parseMemberLine()` (parses "Name (userId)" *after* the outer split) needed no change — it has no delimiter concept of its own.

**Known, accepted tradeoff, not solved**: `parseMemberLine()` does no escaping. A player display name containing a literal comma would be silently mis-parsed into an extra garbage "member." No existing comma-delimited convention exists anywhere else in this codebase to collide with — this is a new risk introduced by the organiser's own explicit choice, not a collision with something already established. Not fixing this (no quoting/escaping scheme added) — over-engineering for what was actually asked.

### Testing performed
Team-3v3 format, 17 teams (awkward count), varied comma spacing across lines (`Team1,P1a,P1b,P1c`, `Team B , P16 (uid_x) , P16b , P16c` with spaces both sides and a userId parenthetical) — all loaded correctly, names trimmed, userId preserved. A short line (`Team Q, OnlyOne`) correctly null-padded to `[OnlyOne, null, null]`. An all-vacant line (`Team Empty,,,`) correctly refused the whole load with the updated comma-wording alert, index unchanged. `promptWalkupTeam()`'s prompt text and default value both confirmed comma-separated directly. Zero console errors.

### Explicitly out of scope
`Past files/curve_tournament_v2.html` (legacy pre-split monolith, confirmed not loaded by the live app, never touched). `HANDOFF_LOG.md`'s own earlier dated entries mentioning the old `|` syntax — append-only, not rewritten.
