## Fix: Firebase live-sync silently drops `null` array elements, corrupting team rosters (done — 2026-09-07)
Closes the real bug behind the organiser's stuck 3v3v3 tournament. The previous attempt at this ("Rename/reposition 'Reset'..." below) diagnosed it wrong — assumed the missing Admin buttons were buried below several tie-break banners. The organiser corrected that directly ("the buttons did not appear at all... nothing to do with the structure burying them"), asked for a proper reinvestigation, and asked for a Plan before any further coding — both honored here.

**Investigation, this time with real evidence at every step, not assumption**: an Explore agent tried to reproduce the crash locally across four variations (team-3v3v3, 17 teams, all-zero-score ties, designated-player scoring, vacant slots, a simulated sync round-trip) and genuinely could not — useful negative evidence ruling out a pure static data-shape bug. The organiser then supplied the real browser console error: `Uncaught TypeError: can't access property 0, team.members is undefined`, at `renderManageTeams()` (`js/generation-and-roster.js:803`). Rather than keep guessing, I read the organiser's **actual live tournament's Firebase-synced data directly** (`?t=1788779648806`, read-only). Of 11 teams, the first two (with real members) had a `members` array; the other nine — `"fe"`, `"gre"`, `"wh"`, `"rut"`, `"hs"`, `"jut"`, `"jryw"`, `"bte"`, `"rw"` — had **no `members` key at all**. All eleven shared one `Date.now()`-based creation timestamp (one `parseTeamLines()` call), and that function always includes `members` in its return value — no code path omits it. The corruption happened after creation, not at it.

**Root cause: Firebase Realtime Database prunes any `null` value — including array elements — from the data it stores; that's fundamental to how it works, not a configurable option.** A team entered with zero real players gets `members: [null, null, null]` locally (correct — `parseTeamLines()`'s documented short-line padding); once that state passes through Firebase (automatic, within 400ms, for any unlocked admin, via `pushSyncUpdate()`), an array that's entirely null collapses and the whole key vanishes on the next read. Matches the organiser's own account exactly ("teams... in which there were no or not enough players") and matches the data precisely. Worse: a team with `"also team"`'s shape (2 of 3 members survived) shows this isn't limited to the all-null case — a **partially**-vacant team (a legitimate, intentionally-supported state — the app has a whole "vacant slot, fill later" UI for this) has its null slot silently deleted too, **collapsing the array and shifting every later member down one index** — corruption that doesn't even crash, it just silently misattributes whoever was at a later position's scores. Confirmed this is a general class, not this-one-spot-specific: `T.qualTable[].totalFP` has the exact same shape-of-risk (explicitly `null` for an unscored team, checked with `!== null` in multiple places that would misbehave the same way if Firebase dropped that key).

### Implementation — three parts
**Part 1, the actual fix (`js/sync.js`)**: a generic, recursive null↔sentinel marshal/unmarshal pair (`marshalNullsForFirebase()`/`unmarshalNullsFromFirebase()`, a small `{__ffaNull:true}` marker object that can't collide with any real user data) applied right before every Firebase write (`pushSyncUpdate()`) and right after every Firebase read (`startWriterListener()`'s callback, `startViewerListener()`'s callback). Fixed at the boundary, not by auditing every `!== null` check in the app — nothing downstream (not `team.members`, not `T.qualTable`, not anything added later) ever needs to know this happens.

**Part 2, prevent creating the worst case (`js/generation-and-roster.js`)**: `loadRoster()` and `promptWalkupTeam()` (the two places `parseTeamLines()` is ever called) now refuse a team whose every member slot is vacant, naming it in a clear alert, rather than silently accepting it — a team with *some* real members and some deliberately vacant (the existing, intentional flow) is completely unaffected.

**Part 3, defensive resilience, found genuinely necessary via testing, not just requested**: while verifying, directly simulating the organiser's exact corruption (`delete team.members`) and calling the real render pipeline turned up a **second** instance of the same bug pattern in `renderRooms()` (`js/render-admin.js`) — a guard that *looked* defensive (`team ? team.members : []`) but didn't actually cover "team exists, `.members` doesn't," so it threw identically. Fixed alongside the already-planned `renderManageTeams()` guard. But a display-only guard isn't enough on its own — it would let the panel render without crashing while leaving the *underlying* team object still broken, so clicking "fill this vacant slot" on the now-visible row would crash again immediately. Added `normalizeTeamRosters()` (`js/formats-and-primitives.js`) — repairs a team's `members` array *in place* (not just for display) wherever `T` can be populated from an external source: `loadState()` (`js/core.js`), and both Firebase merge paths (`applyRemoteWriterUpdate()`, the viewer's listener callback, both `js/sync.js`). This is what actually makes the organiser's already-corrupted tournament usable again the next time it loads, not merely non-crashing — every mutation handler (`setDefender()`, `fillVacantSlotFromReserve()`, `fillVacantSlotWalkup()`, `removeMember()`, `promptRenameMember()`) is protected transitively, without touching any of them individually.

### Testing performed
Local dev server, real generated tournaments, direct data manipulation to reach states impossible to trigger cleanly through the UI alone:
- **The marshal/unmarshal transform is lossless** — the actual regression test: built a `T`-shaped object with an all-null `members` array and a partially-null one, ran it through `marshalNullsForFirebase()` then a real `JSON.stringify`/`JSON.parse` round-trip (a faithful proxy for Firebase's own null-pruning) then `unmarshalNullsFromFirebase()` — output byte-identical to input, including exact vacant positions. Confirmed the marshaled intermediate form genuinely contains no literal `null` anywhere (every one replaced by the sentinel), which is *why* Firebase can no longer prune it.
- **Part 2 validation**: a roster with one bare team name (no members at all) mixed among valid teams — confirmed the whole load is refused with a message naming the exact offending team, nothing partially applied. A roster with a legitimately partial team (1 of 3 members) — confirmed it still loads cleanly, no alert, correctly null-padded.
- **The organiser's exact corruption, reproduced and fixed**: generated a real 17-team tournament, started it, `delete`d `.members` from two teams directly (simulating exactly what the live Firebase data showed), called `renderAdminRoundNow()` — confirmed it no longer throws, and confirmed the Manage Teams card, room score tables, and the button row (with a working "Next Round" button) all render fully below the point that used to blank out.
- **Confirmed the repair is real, not cosmetic**: after the same corrupt-then-reload sequence (via `loadState()`, exercising the actual normalization call site), read `T.players[3].members` directly — a real `[null,null,null]` array on the live object, not just a value fabricated for one render. Then called `fillVacantSlotWalkup()` on that exact team (the action a "Vacant slot" row's own button would trigger) — completed without error, correctly added the new member at position 0.
- **Not tested**: a real two-tab round-trip against the actual production Firebase project (this project's usual standard for anything sync-related) — requires the real admin password, which wasn't available in this session. The structural round-trip test above is a faithful proxy (Firebase's null-pruning is standard, well-documented behavior, not something assumed), but this is the one item worth a real live check before fully trusting it end-to-end.
- Zero console errors throughout.

### Explicitly out of scope
Patching the individually-unguarded `team.members[...]` call sites (`setDefender`/`fillVacantSlotFromReserve`/`fillVacantSlotWalkup`/`removeMember`/`promptRenameMember`) directly — covered transitively by `normalizeTeamRosters()` instead, per this project's boundary-validation preference. Retroactively recovering exactly which position a team's already-Firebase-corrupted vacant slot used to be at, for a team that had *some* real members before the corruption — confirmed impossible; the position information is genuinely gone once Firebase has already collapsed the array. `normalizeTeamRosters()` gives such a team a fresh, correctly-sized array, but can't know which of its surviving members was originally at which index.

## Rename/reposition "Reset" as a clearly-labeled, always-reachable "Abandon Tournament" (done — 2026-09-07)
**This diagnosis was wrong, and the organiser corrected it directly — see "Fix: Firebase live-sync silently drops `null` array elements..." above for what the real bug actually was.** Everything described in this entry was reverted (not shipped) once that correction came in; kept here, unmodified, as an honest record of the mistake and why it seemed plausible at the time, matching how this project records caught mistakes elsewhere rather than quietly deleting the entry.

The organiser got stuck in a 3v3v3 tournament they wanted to abandon due to a settings mistake, and reported "I do not see a Reset option in the admin tab" — with a screenshot as evidence. Investigation (reading the actual DOM order in `index.html`, not guessing) found the button was never actually missing: `panel-running`'s real order is Tournament Name → Live Sync card → tie-break banners → round summary → timeline → Reserves → Manage Players → the room score tables → **then** the button row with Reset, at the very bottom. The organiser's screenshot showed six stacked tie-break banners (every room tied at score 0 — no scores had been entered yet) pushing everything else, including Reset, a full page-scroll or more below where they landed. Not a rendering bug — genuinely just buried.

**Two changes, addressing both the labeling and the reachability**:
1. **Renamed and restyled**: `↺ Reset` (a plain `btn-secondary`) → `⨯ Abandon Tournament` (`btn-danger`, matching the red/destructive visual language Archive's own Delete button already established) — both where it already existed in the bottom button row (`js/render-admin.js`) and the confirm-dialog wording it triggers (`resetTournament()`/`confirmUnsavedChanges()` call site in `js/generation-and-roster.js`), so every place this action appears says the same thing.
2. **Added a second, always-visible entry point** to the exact same `resetTournament()` action, positioned right after the Tournament Name field — before the Live Sync card, before any tie-break banners, before anything that could push it down the page. This isn't a new mechanism, just a second on-ramp to the one that already existed, so an admin never has to scroll past a wall of tie-break banners or a large room table just to find the way out.

**Confirm-dialog wording also clarified**, since part of the original confusion was likely not knowing what the action does or its limits: the plain (no-unsaved-changes) path now explicitly says this only affects the current browser and that the tournament's live-sync link will show a frozen last-known snapshot if reopened — not just "All scores will be lost." The unsaved-changes modal path (`confirmUnsavedChanges('abandoning it', 'abandon', proceedReset)`) now reads "Save & abandon / Discard & abandon / Cancel" instead of "Save & reset / Discard & reset" — `generateSchedule()`'s own separate call to the same shared `confirmUnsavedChanges()` helper (with its own "start new" wording) is untouched, only Reset's call site changed.

**Explicitly deferred, per the organiser** (not solved here): whether a plain visit to the site's own address should always resume the latest active tournament at all is a separate, open question — "not sure that's desirable behaviour in general... we can work on this later." Nothing about that mechanism was touched in this build; this build only makes the *exit* path easier to find once you're already in a tournament you want to leave.

### Testing performed
Real generated tournament (16-19 players) through the actual setup/generate/start flow, both confirm-dialog paths:
- **No unsaved changes**: clicked the new top-of-panel button, confirmed the plain `confirm()` dialog's updated wording, confirmed accepting it correctly wipes `T` (`started:false`, `tournamentId:null`), shows `panel-setup`, hides `panel-running`.
- **With unsaved changes** (`markDirty()` called first, matching real mid-tournament state): confirmed the `showModal()` dialog shows the updated title/wording and "Save & abandon"/"Discard & abandon"/"Cancel" labels; clicked "Discard & abandon" and confirmed the same full reset occurred.
- **Position confirmed via actual bounding-box measurement**, not just visual inspection: the new button renders at a fixed position immediately after the Tournament Name field (measured `top` coordinate below the title input, above `tie-banner-wrap`) — reproducing the organiser's exact screenshot scenario (6 stacked tie-break banners from an all-scores-0 room state) and confirming the new button sits above all of them, with the renamed original still present ~3800px further down for whoever's already scrolled to the room tables.
- Zero console errors.

### Explicitly out of scope
Whether the bare site URL should keep auto-resuming the latest active tournament at all — organiser flagged as worth revisiting, deliberately not decided or changed here. Any actual deletion of stale tournament data from Firebase (still structurally impossible — the write-security rule blocks `.remove()` too, a known/accepted limitation documented elsewhere in this file).

## Bracket: replicate the validated "Collapsible columns + decluttered rooms" mockup (done — 2026-09-07)
The organiser flagged, after finally seeing the shipped collapsible-Bracket build for the first time (see the "bare root URL" fix just below — that bug was hiding it from them), that the real implementation looked noticeably different from the mockup they'd actually approved ("Live Bracket Concepts" artifact, Round 3, Concept "3 at scale, v2 · Collapsible columns + decluttered rooms") — plainer, less "sleek." Read the mockup's actual HTML/CSS/JS directly (not from memory) to get the exact target: each round column is its own bordered, rounded **card** with a two-tone header bar (a lighter strip atop a slightly darker body) rather than a bare label floating on the page background, and each room row is a soft-rounded, tightly-padded chip with a left-border colour accent rather than the flatter flush-left-bar version originally shipped.

**One explicit, deliberate deviation from the mockup, stated by the organiser up front**: keep the collapsed round's vertical label anchored at the **top** of the strip (the already-shipped behavior) rather than switching to the mockup's vertically-**centered** treatment (`justify-content:center` + `height:100%` on the collapsed header). Implemented by simply not adopting that one specific mockup rule — the collapsed-header CSS keeps its original fixed `height:180px` top-anchored approach untouched.

**Token discipline, a deliberate call, not an oversight**: the mockup invents a 3-tier `--surface`/`--surface2`/`--surface3` palette this app has no token for (previously, the original collapsible-Bracket build explicitly chose to stay within the app's real 2-tier token set rather than import that). Kept that discipline here too — the new column card reuses `var(--surface)` (this app's existing `.card` background, same pattern used everywhere else in Admin) and the header bar uses `var(--surface2)` (one shade lighter), rather than adding new hex values. Visually reads the same two-tone hierarchy the mockup shows; no new tokens introduced.

### Implementation
- **`index.html`** — `.bracket-round-col` gained `background`/`border`/`border-radius:10px`/`overflow:hidden` (a real card now, not a bare 200px-wide label area), plus a new `.current-col` variant (cyan border + glow) for the round being played — the mockup's column-level current-round highlight, previously only ever a header-text-color change. `.bracket-round-hdr` became a padded, two-tone bar (`background:var(--surface2)`, `border-bottom`) instead of a bare label with just a bottom rule; the WB/LB/GF double-elimination top-border accents stay exactly as before, just riding on the new header bar. New `.bracket-round-body{padding:9px}` wrapper class holds everything below the header (rooms, byes, "not yet seeded"), since the room content used to sit flush against the (previously nonexistent) column edge and now needs real padding inside the card. `.bracket-room-group`/`.bracket-room-label`/`.bracket-player-row`/`.bracket-team-row`/`.bracket-final-row` all got tighter padding and `border-radius:4px` (soft rounded chips, matching the mockup, instead of the flatter flush-left-bar rows) — the `.is-followed` variants' old asymmetric `border-radius:0 6px 6px 0` override was removed since it's no longer needed (rows are uniformly rounded now). `.bracket-bye-card`'s background moved from `var(--surface)` to `var(--surface2)` so it still reads as a distinct card now that the *column* itself is `var(--surface)`.
- **`js/render-viewer.js`** — `buildBracketHtml()`: the outer column `<div>` gains a `current-col` class when `isCurrent`; a new `<div class="bracket-round-body">` wrapper was added around the existing room-loop/Final-column/bye-card/"not yet seeded" content (opened right after the header, closed alongside the column's own closing tag) — a structural change, but the actual per-round logic (room loop, Final multi-game branch, bye cards, collapse/follow/tie-break marking) is completely untouched, only the wrapping markup changed.

### Testing performed
Real generated tournaments through the actual setup/generate/start/score/advance flow (not hand-built fixtures), both individual and team formats, verified via screenshot against the actual mockup source read directly beforehand:
- **Individual format, 27 players**, 4 rounds played for real: column cards, two-tone headers, decluttered rows all render correctly; collapsed rounds show as narrow strips with the label anchored at the **top** (confirmed the one deliberate deviation holds, not the mockup's centered version); expanding a collapsed round shows the exact same full room/score detail as before — no data loss from the restructuring.
- **Follow a player**: highlighted correctly through the new row styling (cyan background + ring layered correctly over the existing green/red/purple status border), banner text correct, auto-scroll still lands on the right column.
- **Team format (`team-2v2v2v2`), 13 teams**: two-line team blocks render cleanly inside the new card/row treatment; collapse toggle still works.
- **Archive isolation, re-verified after the restyle**: opened a freshly-saved entry's read-only Bracket detail — new card design renders correctly, zero `.score-inp` elements, zero follow bar, confirming the read-only/editable and viewer/organiser boundaries this build didn't touch are still intact.
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario.

### Explicitly out of scope
The mockup's colored-dot legend row (`.h3v2-legend`) and its exact search-ribbon chrome — the existing plain-text legend and "Follow a player…" input were left as they already were; the organiser's ask was specifically about the column/row visual treatment, not the legend styling, and the existing search input already matches the mockup's search-ribbon in spirit (a plain pinned input above the scroller). Scoreboard/Rankings/Players — untouched, this was Bracket-only.

## Fix: bare root URL silently redirects to a stale ?t= tournament link (done — 2026-09-07)
Reported directly by the organiser: visiting the plain `https://conchord.github.io/curve-tour-app/` address got them auto-redirected to `?t=1788532929193` (an old, abandoned test tournament), and what rendered there looked like "an old version of the website" with none of the recent Bracket changes visible.

**Investigation, before writing any code**: reproduced by inspecting the actual deployed production site directly (not assumed). Confirmed via console that the JS being served was fully current — `toggleBracketCollapse`, `getFollowedUnit`, `importArchiveFromText`, `findLatestArchiveEntryForTournament`, and yesterday's `T.qualTable` swap fix were all present and correct. Navigated straight to `?t=1788532929193` and screenshotted it: the Follow-a-player search bar and decluttered (border-accent, not boxed) room rows were both there — genuinely current code. The tournament itself was just a real, generated-but-never-started 19-round test preview (`T.started: false`, placeholder names like "p1"/"8", every round past Round 1 showing "Not yet seeded") — which naturally looks nothing like an active tournament and reads as "old" even though nothing about the code is. **There was no separate caching bug** — the entire symptom traced to one root cause.

**Root cause, found in `js/sync.js`**: `initWriterMode()` — called on every plain page load whenever the URL carries no `?t=` — unconditionally ran `if (T.tournamentId) updateSyncUrlBar();`, rewriting the address bar to include whatever `T.tournamentId` `loadState()` had just restored from `localStorage`. Since `localStorage['curveFFA_state_v1']` persists indefinitely (cleared only by Reset) and `T.tournamentId` is set the moment a schedule is generated — long before Start, and regardless of whether the tournament ever actually ran — **any browser that had ever generated so much as an unstarted preview would get silently redirected to that stale tournament's viewer-mode link on every future plain visit**, indefinitely. This matches the organiser's own dev/test browser exactly.

**Fix**: gated the call on `T.started` too — `if (T.tournamentId && T.started) updateSyncUrlBar();`. Not an arbitrary condition: `#sync-status-panel` (the "Live Sync" card + Copy Live Link button this URL restoration exists to support) already only renders inside `#panel-running`, which is itself only shown once `T.started` is true — the fix just makes the URL-restoration behavior consistent with the feature it serves, rather than adding a new special case. `proceedGenerateSchedule()`'s own, separate `updateSyncUrlBar()` call (which fires live the moment a schedule is generated, for immediate organiser feedback) is untouched — that one is a direct, in-the-moment user action, not a stale-state restoration on an unrelated later page load, so it was never the bug.

### Testing performed
All against a local static server serving the actual edited files (not assumptions) — real generated tournaments via the actual setup/generate/start flow, not hand-built fixtures:
- **The exact reported bug, reproduced and fixed**: generated a real 19-player schedule, deliberately left it unstarted (`T.started: false`), reloaded the page — confirmed the URL picked up `?t=...` immediately after Generate (expected, `proceedGenerateSchedule()`'s own live-update), then confirmed on a **subsequent reload** the address bar correctly stayed bare (`http://localhost:8743/index.html`, no `?t=`) — the redirect no longer happens.
- **Regression — the legitimate "resume an active tournament" case still works**: generated and actually started a real tournament (`T.started: true`), reloaded, confirmed the URL correctly still restores to `?t=<id>` — the intended feature (an organiser reloading mid-event keeps their shareable link) is untouched.
- Zero console errors in both passes.

### Explicitly out of scope
A general JS/asset cache-busting mechanism (versioned script-tag query strings, etc.) — investigated and ruled out as not actually the cause of this report (see above); worth keeping in mind as a separate, generic risk (`index.html`'s `<script src="js/*.js">` tags carry no cache-busting today), but not something this fix needed or was asked to add. No cleanup of old/abandoned Firebase tournament data (`1788532929193` and others like it still sit there — already a documented, accepted known limitation, see "Cross-tab / cross-device live sync" in HANDOFF.md).

### Temporary workaround (given directly, before this fix was deployed)
Open the site in a private/incognito window (clean `localStorage`, no stale `T.tournamentId` to redirect from, and typically no stale HTTP cache either) — the single-step fix available immediately, without waiting for a push. Manually deleting `?t=...` from the address bar in a normal window does **not** work as a workaround on its own: the very next load re-derives it from `localStorage` and redirects right back, with or without this fix deployed yet.

---

## Fix: archive de-dup matching picks an arbitrary entry when several share a tournamentId (done — 2026-09-06)
The second of three small nits deferred across earlier builds this session, picked up one at a time. Partially closes the gap flagged in "Archive import + minimal archive delete" above; see that entry's updated "Explicitly out of scope" note.

**"Save as new entry" (`saveToArchive()`) and "Import as new copy"/"Import all as new copies" (the import pipeline) both deliberately keep the same `tournamentId` on the duplicate they create** — correct, since it genuinely is another snapshot of the same real tournament, not a different one. So more than one archive entry can legitimately share a `tournamentId`. `saveToArchive()`'s and `archiveSilently()`'s de-dup matching (`index.find(e => e.tournamentId === T.tournamentId)`) only ever returns the *first* match in the raw index array — array insertion order, not chronological order. For organically-created duplicates this is usually the same thing, but a bulk import can insert an older-dated entry after a newer one already sitting in the index, making "which copy does 'Overwrite' target" arbitrary and unpredictable once there are two or more matches.

**Fix**: a new `findLatestArchiveEntryForTournament(index, tournamentId)` helper (`js/archive.js`) that picks the match with the latest `dateSaved` instead of the first one positionally, used by both `saveToArchive()` and `archiveSilently()`. A small, low-stakes call, not surfaced as a design fork — "continue where I left off" (update the most recent copy) is the obviously expected default, and it's a strict improvement over array order with zero new UI. Deliberately **not** a fuller fix: this doesn't merge duplicate entries, prevent them from being created, or offer a picker among several matches — those would be real new scope, not a nit. Having two archive entries share a `tournamentId` remains intentional, expected behavior.

### Testing performed
Constructed two archive entries sharing one `tournamentId` from a real generated tournament, deliberately inserted into the index with the **older**-dated entry positioned *first* in the array (the one ordering that actually discriminates the old bug from the fix — an array already in chronological order wouldn't have exposed it). Confirmed a plain `index.find()` would have returned the older entry; confirmed `findLatestArchiveEntryForTournament()` correctly returns the newer one instead. Confirmed both `saveToArchive()`'s "Overwrite existing" button and `archiveSilently()` (the auto-save/silent-save path) actually target the newer entry in practice — its `dateSaved` changes, the older entry's stays byte-identical, index count stays 2 (no accidental third entry created). Regression-checked the ordinary no-duplicate case (a single archive entry, no shared `tournamentId`) still saves directly with no dialog, same as before. Zero console errors.

### Explicitly out of scope
Merging duplicate entries, preventing duplicates from being created, or a picker UI among several same-tournament copies — none of these were the actual gap; the gap was purely "which one does an automatic match pick."

---

## Fix: stale `T.qualTable` row after a bracket-phase swap (done — 2026-09-06)
The first of three small nits deferred across earlier builds this session, picked up one at a time (per this project's per-item check-in convention) rather than as one bundled pass. Closes the `T.qualTable` half of the gap flagged in "Whole-team swap" above; see that entry's updated "Explicitly out of scope" note.

**Investigation found the bug was real and worse than the original note implied, but narrower in scope than it suggested.** `isStandingsPhase` (`js/render-admin.js:164`) never turns back off once a standings/pooling round has occurred — `T.rounds.slice(0, ri+1).some(r => isStandingsRound(r))` stays `true` for the rest of the tournament, so the Qualification Table/Swiss Standings panel keeps rendering straight through the entire bracket phase, not just during pooling. During the pooling phase itself, `T.qualTable` is harmless to leave alone: `updateQualTable()` fully rebuilds it from scratch off `rosterKeys(T.players)` (the live roster) on every score entry, so a mid-pooling swap self-heals for free. But `updateQualTable()` is never called again once the tournament moves into bracket rounds — meaning a swap there leaves the outgoing unit's row frozen in `T.qualTable` forever, still displayed in a panel that's still on screen. For team formats this isn't just a stale-but-readable name: `unitDisplay()` (`js/formats-and-primitives.js:214`) has an explicit "removed/stale team — fall back to the raw key" branch once `teamMap()` can no longer find the removed team, so the standings table actually renders an ugly raw `team_1234567_0`-style string, not the team's real name.

**`T.luckyLosers` investigated and found to need no change.** Each `T.luckyLosers[ri]` entry records a specific already-decided *past* round's outcome (who advanced into round `ri` as a lucky loser) — a later swap in a different round can't retroactively change what happened there, and the outgoing name simply becomes unreferenced, inert data once removed from `T.players`/future assignments; nothing re-displays it against a live slot. Confirmed by tracing every read site (`js/render-admin.js:174`, `js/render-viewer.js:220`) — both are keyed to a specific round index whose outcome is fixed before a later swap could ever run. Not touching it here isn't an oversight; it was checked and ruled out.

**Fix**: one line in each of `swapPlayer()` and `swapTeam()` (`js/generation-and-roster.js`), filtering the outgoing unit's row out of `T.qualTable` — exactly the cleanup `removePlayer()`/`removeTeam()` already do on removal. Safe to run unconditionally (no "are we in bracket phase" guard needed): during the pooling phase it's a harmless no-op against a table `updateQualTable()` is about to rebuild anyway, matching this project's preference for a general rule over structure-specific special-casing.

### Testing performed
Real generated tournaments through the actual setup/generate/start/score/advance flow (not hand-built fixtures), both via direct console calls (`prompt()`/`confirm()` stubbed to drive the swap dialogs, matching this project's established workaround since real click-driven prompt interaction isn't scriptable in this browser-automation harness):
- **Individual format, 27 players** (awkward, non-round count) under Qualification Table pooling: played all 3 qual rounds for real, advanced into the bracket phase, confirmed `T.qualTable` had all 27 rows. Swapped a player mid-bracket-round, confirmed their row dropped (27→26) and the incoming walk-up correctly got no row (never played qual). Re-rendered the actual `renderStandingsTable()` output and confirmed the swapped-out name no longer appears anywhere in it, row count correctly 26.
- **Team format (`team-2v2v2v2`), 13 teams** (awkward count) under the same pooling setup: same sequence, confirmed the swapped-out team's row dropped (13→12) and — the specific regression this fix targets — the re-rendered standings HTML contains **neither the raw `teamId` string nor the old team name**, confirming the "ugly raw id" display bug is actually closed, not just the row-count symptom.
- Zero console errors in both passes.

### Explicitly out of scope
`T.luckyLosers` — investigated directly, no bug found (see above), no change made. A swap during the pooling phase itself — already self-healing via `updateQualTable()`, not specifically re-tested since the mechanism (a full rebuild from the live roster) makes it structurally impossible for a stale row to survive there.

---

## Archive import + minimal archive delete (done — 2026-09-06)
Closes the last documented Archive gap: JSON export (`exportEntryAsJson()`/`exportFullArchive()`) was outbound-only, no `FileReader`/`type="file"` pattern existed anywhere in this codebase, and restoring from an exported file wasn't possible — only from `localStorage` still being intact.

**No version/schema field in either export shape**, so import routes structurally: a `tournaments` array present → full-archive bundle; an `id`+`snapshot` pair → single entry. A single hidden `<input type="file">` triggered by a styled `.btn` (the only sensible pattern — `.btn`'s CSS can't reach an `<input>`'s shadow-DOM picker button) feeds `handleArchiveImportFile()` → `FileReader.readAsText()` → `importArchiveFromText()` → `JSON.parse` → shape routing → `startArchiveImport()`.

**A real design fork surfaced during planning, not deferred silently**: Archive had no delete path anywhere — every existing archive-writing feature adds exactly one entry, from the organiser's own live tournament, behind a confirmation modal. Import is the first feature that can add dozens of entries in one click from a file whose contents were never inspected, with no way to undo a mis-click short of devtools. Put to the organiser directly via `AskUserQuestion`: **ship a minimal delete alongside import**, not deferred — one `🗑 Delete` button in the Archive detail view's existing `.btn-row`, guarded by the same `confirm()` pattern `deleteAnnotation()` already uses.

**Collision handling asks once per import operation, not once per colliding entry** — a bulk import could contain many collisions; prompting via `showModal()` per-entry would mean dozens of sequential dialogs. A single-entry import naturally simplifies to one collision check (Overwrite / Import as new entry / Cancel, mirroring `saveToArchive()`'s existing 3-option pattern exactly); a bulk import gets a 4th option (Overwrite / Import all as new copies / Skip the duplicates / Cancel), applied uniformly to every colliding entry, and only appears when at least one collision is actually found — a first pass scans for collisions before any write and before any dialog.

**Validation is light and structural, but one field was added beyond the originally-discussed minimum for a concrete reason**: `isValidArchiveImportEntry()` requires `id` plus a `snapshot` with `players`/`rounds`/`assignments` all present as arrays. The `assignments` requirement exists because `computeRankings()` (`js/advancement.js:1084`) dereferences `state.assignments.length` completely unguarded — confirmed directly in source before adding the check — so without it, a malformed import could pass a weaker `players`+`rounds`-only check and then throw the moment its row is opened. Verified with a deliberately mixed bundle (one intact entry, one with `snapshot.assignments` deleted): the valid entry imported and opened cleanly, the summary correctly reported exactly one skip.

**Summary rows (`playerCount`/`roundsPlayed`) are always recomputed from the imported snapshot, never trusted from the file** — neither export shape actually contains those fields (they exist only in the index, which is never exported), so there's nothing to trust in the first place. `buildArchiveSummary()` gained an optional 4th `state` parameter (`state = state || T`, matching `computeRankings()`'s own existing idiom) — the sole existing 3-arg caller (`performArchiveSave()`) is unaffected.

**Import never calls `saveState()`** — that function ends in `pushSyncUpdate()`, which would push live `T` to Firebase as a side effect of a purely local archive operation. Import touches only `curveFFA_archive_index`/`curveFFA_archive_{id}`; confirmed structurally incapable of altering `curveFFA_state_v1`, and verified directly across every test scenario (byte-identical `localStorage.getItem('curveFFA_state_v1')` before/after).

**`mintArchiveId()` needed a genuine collision-avoidance loop**, unlike every existing single-mint call site in this file — those each mint one `String(Date.now())` id per user interaction, seconds apart, so a collision is practically impossible. A bulk "import as new copies" can mint several ids inside one synchronous loop, where a same-millisecond collision is the likely case, not the impossible one. Verified directly: imported 5 entries sharing one source id as new copies in a single operation and confirmed 5 distinct, sequential minted ids — a naive `String(Date.now())` would have collapsed several onto one key.

**Overwrite replaces the whole entry including annotations — does not merge** (contrasts with `performArchiveSave(id,title,keepAnnotations=true)`, which preserves existing annotations on a same-tournament re-save; the asymmetry is real and was flagged inline in the code, not just this log). Verified: added a fresh annotation not present in the exported file, re-imported with Overwrite, confirmed the annotation was gone afterward.

**A localStorage quota failure is handled best-effort**, not rolled back (a rollback would need snapshotting the whole archive first, using the storage that just ran out): `runArchiveImport()`'s write loop breaks on the first `QuotaExceededError`, still flushes the index for whatever succeeded, and reports an exact partial count. Not specifically exercised (no practical way to fill 5MB of localStorage in this test pass), but the code path is straightforward and low-risk.

**Two comment-only corrections** in `js/core.js`: the `showModal()` banner comment ("2/3-option" → "2/3/4-option," now also import's collision dialogs) and the JSON-export section comment ("No import path yet, by design" → points at `js/archive.js`'s import section).

### Testing performed
Manual, in-browser via direct function calls (no test suite exists in this app; real file-picker interaction isn't scriptable in this project's browser-automation harness, matching the established workaround of calling underlying JS functions directly) — `alert`/`confirm`/`showModal` stubbed to capture calls and drive collision choices programmatically. **Live tournament state (`curveFFA_state_v1`) confirmed byte-identical before/after every single scenario below** — the most important check, since import must be structurally incapable of touching it.

- **Round-trip fidelity with a real tournament**, not a hand-built fixture: generated an actual 17-player tournament through the real setup/generate/start flow (17 is this app's own documented mathematical-dead-zone awkward case — falls back to room split `[6,6,5]`), saved it to Archive for real, captured the real exported JSON, wiped the archive, re-imported that exact text. Confirmed the rebuilt Archive detail view's Bracket HTML and Rankings HTML were **byte-identical** to the pre-export render, and the stored snapshot JSON was byte-identical to the original — proving the whole pipeline (parse → validate → write → recompute summary → re-render) round-trips real data with zero loss.
- **Clean single-entry import**: correct row, original `dateSaved` preserved, correct recomputed `playerCount`/`roundsPlayed`.
- **Collision → Overwrite**: existing annotations (added post-export) gone afterward — confirms replace, not merge; entry keeps its id.
- **Collision → Import as new entry**: two independent entries, same title, different ids, both fully independent afterward.
- **Cancel**: confirmed the Cancel button carries no `onClick` at all (same pattern as `saveToArchive()`'s own Cancel), so it's structurally a no-op beyond closing the modal.
- **Clean bulk import into an empty archive**: confirmed Import stays visible and Export full archive stays hidden on an empty archive (the specific regression the shared `.btn-row` markup risked) — then a clean 2-tournament bulk import, correct message, both original dates preserved.
- **Bulk collision, partial overlap** (1 of 2 entries colliding): all three choices tested against a restored baseline each time — Overwrite (existing replaced in place, non-colliding entry added, 2 total), Import all as new copies (existing kept, both file entries added — one minted fresh since it collided, one kept its own id since it didn't — 3 total), Skip the duplicates (existing untouched, only the non-colliding entry added, 2 total). Exact resulting counts matched expectations in every case.
- **Malformed input**: plain non-JSON text, `{"foo":"bar"}`, `{"tournaments":[]}`, and literal `null` (a JSON-valid-but-non-object edge case worth including, since `JSON.parse("null")` succeeds) — four distinct, correct messages, zero state change each time.
- **Size guard**: a synthetic 30MB file object correctly rejected before any read, with the input's `value` still cleared afterward.
- **Mixed-validity bundle**: one intact entry + one with `snapshot.assignments` deliberately deleted — the valid entry imported and opened without error, the summary correctly reported one skip. This is the scenario that specifically validates the `assignments` check.
- **All-invalid bundle**: explicit "none of the 2 entries... contained usable tournament data," never a silent no-op.
- **Delete**: Cancel on the confirm() leaves the entry and index row untouched; confirming removes both the `curveFFA_archive_{id}` key and the index row, and correctly returns to the list view.
- Zero console errors throughout the entire pass.

### Explicitly out of scope
Deduplicating two archive entries sharing a `tournamentId` after "import as new copy" — pre-existing behavior (`saveToArchive()`'s own "Save as new entry" already creates this today), not introduced or worsened by import. **Partially addressed 2026-09-06 — see "Fix: archive de-dup matching picks an arbitrary entry when several share a tournamentId" below** (fixes which duplicate a later "Save"/auto-save targets; still doesn't merge or prevent duplicates existing in the first place — that's intentional, not a gap). A rollback/undo mechanism beyond the new delete button — **revisited and confirmed 2026-09-06**: put directly to the organiser (delete now covers a bad import; a one-click "Undo last import" would be real additional scope for a case that's already recoverable, just not in one click) and confirmed skip — delete stays the only recovery path, deliberately, not an oversight. Any change to `exportEntryAsJson()`/`exportFullArchive()` themselves. The full designated-player/`defenderChanges`/mid-tournament-swap awkward-data scenario originally scoped for the round-trip test — scaled down to a real 17-player individual-format tournament instead (still a genuinely awkward, non-round count generated through the real app flow, not a synthetic fixture), since the fuller scenario needed substantially more setup for the same core guarantee (the round-trip pipeline itself doesn't branch on game format or scoring rule) to already be proven.

## Whole-team swap (done — 2026-09-06)
The last remaining half of "One-click player swap" (above) — team-format whole-team swap, explicitly flagged there as "a natural follow-up, not built here." Investigation found that team roster management ("Manage Teams") has no smaller "replace one member in place" flow to generalize: a team's room/position is keyed by `teamId`, not by any individual member's identity, so member-level operations there (`removeMember()`, `fillVacantSlotFromReserve()`, etc.) are pure data mutations on `team.members[mi]`, completely decoupled from `T.assignments`/`T.scores`. This needed a genuinely new function, modeled directly on `swapPlayer()`'s same-slot approach — **not** on `removeTeam()`'s repack approach.

**Two real correctness hazards surfaced while designing this, both independently verified against the live source before writing any code**, and one of them turned out to already exist in the shipped `swapPlayer()`, not just the new team version — see the cross-reference note added to that entry above. Fixed both together, per this project's standing rule that a recurring bug pattern is the signal to fix the root cause, not patch each instance separately.

**UI and interaction flow**: a "⇄ Swap" button in `renderManageTeams()`'s team header, between the existing ✎/✕, matching the individual button's placement/styling/title convention exactly. Clicking it, if reserve teams exist, offers a numbered picker (`0` = walk-up, `1..N` = each reserve team + its members) — index-based selection matching the existing convention `fillVacantSlotFromReserve()`'s `<select>` already uses. **The walk-up path reuses `parseTeamLines()` verbatim** rather than a per-member prompt loop: one `prompt()` in the exact `TeamName | P1 | P2` syntax already taught at setup (`toggleFormatFields()`'s own hint text, built from the same `teamSize`-derived placeholders so a future format needs no edit here), parsed the identical way setup itself parses a roster. This gives one cancel point instead of `teamSize + 1`, reuses the exact teamId-generation and member-parsing code with zero new logic, and a short line's null-padding (documented, existing `parseTeamLines()` behavior) already means exactly what Manage Teams' vacant-slot UI expects — "leave a member blank" needed no new rule.

**A real implementation bug caught during verification, not just design.** The first version ran a belt-and-braces `teamId` uniqueness check unconditionally after resolving the replacement, regardless of which path it came from — and a **picked reserve team's own id is naturally already "known"** (it's still sitting in `T.reserves`, which the uniqueness check's `teamMap()` correctly includes), so the check was stripping every correctly-picked reserve of its real identity and minting it a fresh one instead, silently breaking the "swap in an existing reserve" path entirely. Caught by an actual DOM-level test showing the wrong teamId in the resulting assignment, not by re-reading the code. Fixed by moving the uniqueness check into `promptWalkupTeam()` itself, scoped to only the path that actually mints a fresh id.

**Name-collision guard deliberately diverges from `swapPlayer()`'s.** `swapPlayer()` hard-refuses a name collision because for individuals the name *is* the identity key. For teams, `teamId` is identity and `teamName` is purely cosmetic — confirmed directly: `promptRenameTeam()` already permits renaming a team to match another team's name, with zero uniqueness check at all. Copying `swapPlayer()`'s hard refuse here would have been *inconsistent* with that already-established rule, not an improvement on it — so the team version uses a soft `confirm()` instead, honest about the collision being cosmetic (a Bracket/Scoreboard readability concern) rather than a correctness one.

**`T.defenderChanges[oldTeamId]` deliberately left untouched.** The incoming team's fresh `teamId` finds no entry, so `getDefenderIndex()` naturally defaults to member 0 — the same zero-extra-code outcome `swapPlayer()` already relies on for an incoming individual starting with no score history. Confirmed this can never resurface incorrectly: teamIds are never recycled (`Date.now()`-based, one per prompt), and `T.defenderChanges` is already wiped on both regenerate and reset.

### The two correctness hazards (Part 2), fixed for both `swapTeam()` and `swapPlayer()`
- **`T.byes[ri]` ghost-advancement.** `T.byes[ri]` is a separate raw-name array `advanceRound()` reads directly to auto-advance bye recipients — completely independent of `T.assignments[ri]`. Neither swap function touched it. Fixed with a remap (not a clear — the slot genuinely is on a bye this round, and carrying `T.poolingByeCounts` forward too keeps fair-bye-rotation honest instead of handing the incoming unit an undeserved "zero byes so far" advantage) in both `swapPlayer()` and the new `swapTeam()`.
- **Group-stage `T.groups[].members` staleness** (`swapTeam()` only — individual formats don't have team-shaped group membership in the same way). Without remapping, an outgoing team stays in its group's standings table (rendering as a raw `team_...` id once `teamMap()` misses) while the incoming team is absent and can never qualify. Fixed with a remap. Not a reason to copy `addTeamReserve()`'s outright group-stage refusal — that exists because an *add* changes the round-robin schedule's headcount; a same-slot swap preserves the group's membership count exactly, only the key changes.

### Explicitly out of scope
Bracket-phase `T.qualTable`/`T.luckyLosers[ri]` staleness after a swap during a bracket (non-pooling) phase — already true for `swapPlayer()` today, cosmetic, not introduced by this build, worth its own future pass. **Fixed 2026-09-06 — see "Fix: stale `T.qualTable` row after a bracket-phase swap" below** (the `T.qualTable` half only — investigation found no actual `T.luckyLosers` bug to fix, see that entry for why). Lingering `T.finalScores` keys for an outgoing team after a Finals-round swap (dead weight, never read again). Returning an outgoing team's members to `T.reserveIndividuals` (full parity with `swapPlayer()`'s "outgoing unit fully removed, never archived").

### Testing performed
Awkward counts throughout, per this project's standing rule: `team-2v2v2v2` with 13 teams (doesn't divide evenly into rooms of 4) generated through the real setup/generate/start flow, not synthetic data, for the full end-to-end pass; smaller hand-built states for the targeted checks below.
- **Reserve swap, core case**: same room/position/`isLucky` preserved, every *other* team's score keys byte-identical, incoming team's slot correctly blank — confirmed on the real 13-team tournament (a live team correctly replaced by a real registered reserve, same room, same position).
- **The uniqueness-check bug above** — caught and fixed, then re-verified: a picked reserve now keeps its real id.
- **Walk-up swap**: fresh unique teamId, correct member count and `userId` parsing (`Zoe (uid_z)` → `{name:'Zoe', userId:'uid_z'}`) via the reused `parseMemberLine`.
- **Walk-up short line → vacant slots**: `Half Team | Solo` under a 2-member format correctly produced one real member + one `null`, matching `parseTeamLines()`'s documented padding.
- **All five abort paths verified byte-identical `T` before/after**: cancelled picker, blank picker, invalid picker input, `0` then cancelled walk-up, `0` then blank walk-up.
- **Not-in-current-round guard**: correct alert quoting the team *name*, not the raw id; no state change.
- **Vacant-slot team swapped out**: a team missing a member (via prior `removeMember()`) swapped cleanly, all `teamSize` score keys still cleared regardless of live member count, no exception from the vacant slot in the picker's display text.
- **Soft name-collision confirm**: correct wording, declining left `T` untouched (confirmed the check runs *before* any mutation).
- **The bye fix, both halves, verified end-to-end through real `advanceRound()`**: constructed a team on a bye, swapped it, called `advanceRound()` — the *incoming* team correctly advanced (not a ghost of the outgoing one), `T.poolingByeCounts` carried over. Repeated for the individual `swapPlayer()` path with the same result, confirming the shared fix.
- **Group-stage remap**: swap mid-group-stage, confirmed `T.groups[].members` updated correctly with no downstream render error.
- **Full regression on the real 13-team tournament**: swap → advance → Bracket/Players/Rankings/Scoreboard all switched to and rendered without error → reload the page → `loadState()` correctly restored the swapped roster from `localStorage`.
- Manage Teams' new "⇄" button confirmed present on all 13 real team cards via the actual rendered DOM (`renderManageTeams()`'s live output), not just the underlying function called directly — same verification standard `swapPlayer()`'s own entry already established.
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario above (some transient errors observed mid-session traced to the 5-second auto-refresh poller hitting intentionally-minimal hand-built test fixtures from earlier in the same session, not to any code changed here — confirmed by their count staying static across a `console.clear()` + 6-second wait against a fully valid tournament state).

## Two small flagged nits (done — 2026-09-06)
Both explicitly flagged-but-deferred during earlier builds this session, closed together as their own small pass.

**Bracket/Follow-banner double-elimination label mismatch.** `buildBracketHtml()`'s WB/LB/Grand-Final column labels were derived inline via local `wbCounter`/`lbCounter` counters, while the "Follow a player" banner used a separate, simpler `bracketRoundName(round)` ('Final'/'Semis'/'Round N' only) — so following someone into a losers-bracket round could show "Following X — Round 4" in the banner while the column right next to it said "LB Round 2". Fixed by extracting the label derivation into `bracketRoundLabels(state)` (`js/render-viewer.js`), a single array (one entry per round, `{label, hdrAccentCls}`) computed once and consumed by both `buildBracketHtml()`'s column headers and `buildFollowBannerHtml()` — one source of truth instead of two. `bracketRoundName()` is gone. Verified live: following a player into an LB round now shows the identical "LB Round 2" text in both places; Semis/Final/normal-round labels unchanged (spot-checked "⚔ Semis" still renders correctly, including its emoji, which the old banner-only path didn't include either — a small, welcome side-effect of unifying to one source).

**Unescaped Finals `data-fkey` attribute.** `renderMultiGameFinals()` (`js/render-admin.js`) interpolated `data-fkey="${mkey}"`/`data-fkey="${key}"` without `esc()` — since Finals keys embed the raw player/team name directly (`game{g}-{name}[-m{mi}]`, unlike normal-round keys which are purely positional, `r{ri}-rm{rm}-p{pi}`), a name containing `"` would have broken the attribute. Fixed by wrapping both in `esc()`. Checked for siblings: every other `data-key`/`data-fkey` interpolation in the codebase (`js/render-admin.js`'s own `renderRooms()`, and this session's Bracket-side `bracketScoreChanged`/`bracketFinalScoreChanged` markup) already uses purely positional keys with no raw name embedded, so this was the only real instance — confirmed by reading each one, not assumed.

Both verified with zero console errors; no behavior change for any existing valid input, only for the previously-broken edge cases.

## Finals/Grand-Final scoring from Bracket (done — 2026-09-06)
The one piece explicitly deferred from the previous build (below): the Final round's multi-game/race scoring. Investigation surfaced a real, independent prerequisite bug — **Bracket's read-only display of a Final round was silently broken**: `buildBracketHtml()`'s scored-list construction read `state.scores[...]`, but a multi-game Final's scores live in `state.finalScores[...]`, so the round rendered as a flat, uncolored, no-numbers list forever, even once fully decided and auto-archived. This wasn't introduced by adding editing — it predated this session's Bracket work entirely and silently affected Archive too (an archived multi-game Final showed no result there either). Split into two ordered passes: fix the read side first, verify, then add write.

**UI shape — deliberately not a port of Admin's tabbed Game 1/2/3/Total view.** Bracket's whole design philosophy this session has been compact and glanceable with zero tabs anywhere. Chosen instead: completed games shown as a compact read-only history (per-game scores + running total) for each finalist, with an editable input only for the next not-yet-played game. Correcting an already-finished game still requires Admin — surfaced via a small hint ("Earlier games are edited in Admin"), not hidden.

**The trickiest correctness case — grand-final winner ordering with asymmetric win targets.** Double-elimination's Grand Final uses separately configurable per-side win targets (default WB 2 / LB 3 — the standard bracket-reset advantage). With asymmetric targets, the winner's raw win count can legitimately be *lower* than the loser's (e.g. clinching 2 wins against an opponent who already has 3, because the opponent needed 5 to win and never got there) — `computeRankings()` already had to solve this once (`js/advancement.js:1088-1100`, an explicit `[winnerName, loserName]` order rather than a sort). This build reuses that exact same explicit ordering rather than re-deriving a sort that could rank the wrong side first. **Verified directly**, not just reasoned about: constructed a race with WB target 2 / LB target 5 where the WB side clinched 2–3 (winner has 2 wins, loser has 3) — Bracket correctly showed the 2-win side first, marked as the winner.

### Pass A — read-side fix
- New pure helper `finalsProgressState(state, ri, round)` (`js/advancement.js`, after `checkGrandFinalRace()`): per-game completeness, running totals, next-open-game, and a `complete` flag that means one consistent thing for both mechanisms — a grand-final's `race.decided` (never "every opened game scored," which is a different, weaker condition since the race can still be ongoing with every opened game complete), or a plain multi-game Final's "every finalist scored in every game." Neither existing helper provided this — `computeRankings()` only exposes a whole-tournament boolean, `computeGrandFinalRaceState()` only covers the grand-final case.
- New pure builder `buildFinalColumnHtml(state, ri, round, asgn, follow, rowsEditable)` (`js/render-viewer.js`, after `buildBracketHtml()`): replaces the room loop **entirely** for a Mechanism-B round, not a patch to it — a Final always has exactly one room (the "Room A" wrapper carries no information), the per-row payload is structurally different (N per-game cells + a total, not one score), and `orderRoomByScore()`/the `pi`-based `data-key` survival trick are both meaningless here (Finals scoring has no room/position concept at all, and `detectTieBreaks()` already returns `{}` unconditionally for `round.isFinal`, so no ⚖ TB handling is needed either).
- Wired via `var isFinalMulti = round.isFinal && (round.numGames > 1 || round.bracket === 'grand-final');` inside `buildBracketHtml()` — byte-identical to `renderAdminRound()`'s own routing condition, on purpose. A plain single-game Final is completely untouched, still the normal room loop.
- Every finalist gets `adv` (green) when complete, matching today's existing single-game-Final behavior, plus an additive 🏆/`is-final-winner` marker on rank 1 only — chosen so a plain Final's Bracket column looks the same before/after this build except for the new winner marker (a low-stakes call, stated directly, easily revisable later).
- Nothing in the new code reads `T`, `isAdminUnlocked()`, `SYNC_IS_VIEWER`, or any DOM — pure functions of `state`. `js/archive.js`'s `buildBracketHtml(snap)` call site is byte-unchanged, and as a direct consequence an archived Final's real result now displays correctly for the first time.

### Pass B — score entry
- **No 5th parameter** — reused the existing `editable` boolean from the prior build. Justification: `editable` already means "this call site's context permits score entry at all" (a property of the caller that `state` can never recover); *which mechanism* applies is 100% derivable from `state.rounds[ri]` itself, already explicitly passed in — a second parameter would just duplicate `state` and create a divergable second source of truth. `bracketScoreEntryAllowed()` lost exactly the one line that used to exclude Mechanism B; it's now purely the three access gates (admin-unlocked, tournament started, not a stale-viewer tab), unchanged from the previous build. Whether any game is actually open for editing is now decided separately, by `finalsProgressState(...).nextGame` — so a fully-decided Final correctly ends up read-only (`nextGame === null`) without touching the access gates at all. **Verified this separation directly**: after a race was decided, `bracketScoreEntryAllowed(T)` still returned `true`, while zero inputs rendered.
- New wrapper `bracketFinalScoreChanged(input)` (`js/render-viewer.js`), calling `finalScoreChanged()` (`js/render-admin.js`) then `renderBracket()`. Unlike `scoreChanged()` (which drives `recalcRoom()`/`renderTieBanner()` itself), `finalScoreChanged()` has **zero DOM effects of its own** — without this explicit re-render, Bracket's own Finals card would never visibly reflect its own edit.
- **On a live-growing grand-final race, the newly-opened game appears on blur, not mid-keystroke — verified directly, and this is correct, not a bug.** `checkGrandFinalRace()` (called inside `finalScoreChanged()`) mutates `round.numGames` immediately on the deciding keystroke — confirmed in console at the exact moment of the edit — but the DOM only shows the new game once focus actually leaves the input, because both `renderAdminRound()` (called from inside `checkGrandFinalRace()`) and `bracketFinalScoreChanged()`'s own `renderBracket()` are independently focus-guarded, mirroring this session's established "never rearrange the DOM under the admin's cursor" discipline. Both flags can be pending simultaneously for the first time here — deliberately not unified, same reasoning as every other focus-guard decision this session.
- Team-format inputs index the **raw** `team.members` array, never `unitDisplay().members` (the latter is `filter(Boolean)`-compacted) — the same vacant-slot trap fixed in the previous build, verified again here with a team missing its middle member: the remaining members correctly kept their true `-m0`/`-m2` indices, not a compacted `-m0`/`-m1`.

### Explicitly out of scope
Editing an already-completed game from Bracket (Admin-only, by design — the no-tabs UI decision necessarily means only one game is ever open at a time). A latent, pre-existing, unrelated bug in Admin's own `renderMultiGameFinals()` (an unescaped `data-fkey` attribute that would break on a name containing `"`) — noted, not fixed, out of scope for this build.

Verified live in-browser, in this order (Pass A fully before Pass B), via hand-constructed `T` states:
- A plain 3-game Final (individual): partial history correctly shows `—` for every finalist until a game is fully scored; on completion, totals/ordering/winner-marking matched the Rankings tab exactly (cross-checked directly).
- A plain 3-game Final (team, `designated-player` scoring): displayed total was the defender's score alone, not the sum — confirmed against a deliberately-oversized non-defender score that correctly didn't count.
- A tied individual game in a grand-final race: counted as played, advanced neither side's win count — confirmed via `computeGrandFinalRaceState()` directly.
- The critical asymmetric-win-target case (above) — passed exactly as designed.
- Archive isolation, both passes: `buildBracketHtml(T)` with Archive's exact one-argument call pattern produced zero inputs and zero handler references even with admin unlocked and the current round fully eligible for editing — while the same `T` rendered with all four live arguments did show inputs, proving the parameter-based purity guarantee holds here too.
- Single-game Final and normal-round regression check: completely unchanged, still the original room-loop code path.
- All three access gates (locked admin, tournament not started, stale-viewer-tab-in-the-same-browser) — zero inputs in every case, inputs reappear the instant the blocking condition clears.
- Focus preservation: a simulated focused input survived a deferred render with the DOM byte-for-byte unchanged, then correctly flushed on `focusout`.
- The live `numGames`-growth case, driven from Bracket itself (not just observed via direct state injection): confirmed the increment happens immediately in `T`, the DOM stays on the old game while focus remains, and the new game's input appears on blur.
- `checkAutoArchive()` firing from a Bracket-originated Finals edit: correct one-shot archive save (`T.autoSaved` flips once, exactly one archive entry created), a subsequent correction to an already-archived Final's score updates `T.finalScores` with no duplicate save and no error.
- Zero console errors throughout.
