## Score entry from the Bracket tab (done — 2026-09-06)
The last piece of the Bracket mockup arc, always flagged as the most speculative one: an authenticated admin can now type scores directly into Bracket's current-round rows instead of switching to Admin. The mockup notes explicitly left the access-control question unresolved ("who can see the input controls — deliberately not resolved in a mockup"); this build resolves it, plus two more that only surfaced once the real code was investigated — one of which is a genuine silent-data-loss bug the feature would otherwise have introduced.

**Scope, decided with the organiser up front**: normal rounds only. This app has two structurally different scoring mechanisms — confirmed against `js/render-admin.js`'s own routing condition, `round.isFinal && (round.numGames > 1 || round.bracket === 'grand-final')` — a single-room-per-round `T.scores`/`scoreChanged()` path (this build), and a completely different multi-game/race `T.finalScores`/`renderMultiGameFinals()` path for the Final (default: 3 games) and always for double-elimination's Grand Final. Bracket detects that exact condition for the current round and stays fully read-only whenever it's true — the Final remains Admin-only.

**Three access gates, not one**:
- `isAdminUnlocked()` — the organiser-confirmed baseline, matching every other admin-gated capability in the app.
- `state.started` — Admin structurally can't score a generated-but-not-yet-started tournament either (`#panel-running` stays hidden pre-start); Bracket matches that existing boundary rather than inventing a new capability.
- **`!SYNC_IS_VIEWER` — a real bug found during design, not a defensive extra.** `SYNC_IS_VIEWER` is computed once at page load; `isAdminUnlocked()` reads `localStorage` live and is shared across every tab of one browser. A tab that opened a `?t=` link *before* being unlocked keeps `SYNC_IS_VIEWER === true` even after Admin is unlocked in a *different* tab of the same browser — only `promoteViewerToWriter()`, run in the tab that actually unlocks, clears it. Without this gate, that stale-viewer tab would render live inputs whose edits silently vanish: `saveState()`/`pushSyncUpdate()` both no-op for a viewer, and the *next* incoming Firebase snapshot hits the viewer listener's blanket `Object.assign(T, payload)` (`js/sync.js`) — confirmed to have **no dirty-key protection at all**, unlike the writer path's merge. A typed score would be overwritten with no error and no indication. `!SYNC_IS_VIEWER` is exactly the predicate `pushSyncUpdate()` itself already gates on.

**Why `editable` had to be an explicit fourth parameter to `buildBracketHtml`, not an internal `isAdminUnlocked()` call — the highest-stakes case yet for a discipline this session already established twice.** `buildBracketHtml(state, collapseMap, follow)` already keeps its extra behavior opt-in via explicit params so Archive's read-only call site (`buildBracketHtml(snap)`, no extra arguments) can never inherit them. Here the stakes are worse than either prior case: Archive's nav is hidden entirely for viewers, so in practice *every* Archive visitor is an unlocked admin — an internal call wouldn't just risk exposing Archive occasionally, the bad path would be the *only* path. And `buildBracketHtml` has no way to tell a snapshot from live `T` (snapshots carry no marker field), so it can't self-guard even in principle. Concretely: an Archive row's `data-key` would be built from the *snapshot's* round indices — the same key namespace live `T.scores` uses — so typing into an archived tournament's Bracket would silently corrupt the **live** tournament's scores and push that corruption to Firebase, while the snapshot itself stayed untouched (the mistake invisible at the point it happened). `editable` stays a fourth param, computed only inside the live-only `renderBracketNow()`; Archive's call site passes neither, so it's `undefined` → falsy, structurally, independent of `localStorage`.

**Implementation**:
- `pi` (the room-position index a correct `data-key` needs) is now stored on each `scoredList` entry (`js/render-viewer.js`), not just used inline — necessary because `orderRoomByScore()` re-sorts that array the instant a room completes, after which array position no longer matches room position. Verified this is the load-bearing fix via the single most important test below.
- Individual rows: an `<input>` replaces the read-only score display in both the unrevealed and revealed states — not gated on `showResults`, since that flag is a viewer-privacy rule (don't leak a partial room's standing), not an editing rule, and a completed room's score must stay correctable, matching Admin's own always-editable behavior.
- Team rows: `teamSize` mini-inputs on a new line below the member names, keyed `r{ri}-rm{rm}-p{pi}-m{mi}`. **Indexed against the raw `team.members` array, never `unitDisplay().members`** — the latter is `filter(Boolean)`-compacted, so with a vacant slot its indices no longer match the `-m{mi}` key space `getUnitScore()`/Admin's own `renderRooms()` both use; indexing the wrong array would silently write a score under the wrong member's key. All `teamSize` inputs render regardless of `teamScoringRule` (including "designated-player"/Save your Buddy, where only one member's score actually counts) — `renderRooms()` itself has no such branch, and skipping inputs would change scoring semantics, out of scope.
- `bracketScoreChanged(input)` is a thin wrapper — calls the existing `scoreChanged()` (`js/render-admin.js`) verbatim (confirmed safe regardless of which tab is active: every DOM touch point inside it is either null-guarded or hits a static always-present container), then triggers a focus-guarded Bracket re-render. Deliberately not a per-keystroke live update: because the input that just fired is still focused, the re-render always defers until focus leaves every score input, producing exactly one repaint per room edited, not one per keystroke — the calm, correct moment, given `orderRoomByScore()` would otherwise yank rows out from under the admin's cursor mid-type.
- Focus preservation is its own mechanism (`bracketRenderPending`, a delegated `focusout` listener), symmetric to but deliberately separate from Admin's own `adminRenderPending` — they guard different render functions and can never both be pending at once, so unifying them would need a "which view" discriminator for no behavioral gain. Reuses `isScoreInputFocused()` (`js/render-admin.js`) verbatim rather than duplicating it.
- CSS: the same shared `.score-inp` class Admin uses (keeps `scoreChanged()`/`isScoreInputFocused()` unified), with sizing overridden only via an ancestor selector scoped to Bracket's rows — Admin's own 90px/18px styling is untouched.

**Explicitly out of scope**: Finals/Grand-Final scoring (see above). Past-round correction (Admin doesn't support this either — not a new limitation this build introduces). A surgical per-keystroke live re-render (Admin's own live feel comes from `recalcRoom()`'s targeted DOM updates against a completely different DOM shape; duplicating that here wasn't warranted — the one-repaint-on-blur behavior is the deliberate design). Changing "designated-player" scoring semantics.

Verified live in-browser via hand-constructed `T` states (awkward counts, per CLAUDE.md), checking every gate and both correctness traps independently:
- **The `pi`-preservation fix — the single most important test.** Filled every score in a 6-player room but one, then the last one: confirmed the room re-sorted (a player who typed a top score correctly jumped to rank 1, `adv`/`elim` colors applied correctly) **and** that same player's input still carried its original, correct `data-key` after the reorder — this is exactly the check that would have silently failed (writing scores to the wrong player) without the fix.
- **The vacant-slot indexing trap.** A 3-member team with a vacant middle slot: confirmed the two real members' inputs got `-m0`/`-m2` (their true raw indices), not `-m0`/`-m1` (what indexing the compacted `unitDisplay().members` array would have wrongly produced) — the check that would have failed without indexing `team.members` directly.
- **All three Mechanism-A/B cases**: a Final at the default 3-games setting (0 inputs), a double-elimination Grand Final with `numGames` still at 1 — the case a naive `numGames > 1` check would have wrongly let through (0 inputs), and a Final explicitly set to "Single game" (inputs present, correctly routed to Mechanism A).
- **All three access gates independently**: locked/non-admin viewer (0 inputs), pre-start (0 inputs, inputs appear immediately after Confirm & Start), and the stale-viewer scenario (`SYNC_IS_VIEWER` forced true with admin unlocked — 0 inputs; flipped back to false — inputs reappear on the very next render, confirming the gate is read fresh, not cached).
- **Archive isolation, directly**: called `buildBracketHtml(T)` with Archive's exact one-argument call pattern against a live `T` where every gate above was satisfied (admin unlocked, started, Mechanism A) — zero inputs in the output, versus the same `T` rendered with all four live arguments producing inputs. Proves the parameter-based purity guarantee holds even in the specific condition where a naive internal check would have leaked editable fields into Archive.
- **Focus preservation**: simulated a focused `.score-inp` (`document.activeElement` override, the established technique for this project's automation harness) and confirmed `renderBracket()` leaves the DOM byte-for-byte unchanged while "focused," then correctly flushes the deferred render once a `focusout` event fires.
- **Multi-admin merge interaction**: simulated an in-progress unpushed edit (`syncDirtyScoreKeys`) for one key while a remote `applyRemoteWriterUpdate()` payload arrived with new values for both that key and a different one, all while a score input was "focused" — confirmed the dirty key was preserved (not clobbered by the remote value), the other key merged in correctly, and the DOM stayed untouched until focus left, all via the pre-existing dirty-key mechanism plus the new focus guard, with zero additional code needed at that call site.
- Zero console errors throughout.

---

## Fix: duplicate 5s auto-refresh interval (done — 2026-09-06)
Found while designing "Follow a player" (below): `js/core.js` and `js/render-viewer.js` each contained a byte-identical `setInterval(...)` block (same comment banner, same body) polling every 5 seconds to re-render whichever of Scoreboard/Bracket is the active tab — a leftover duplicate from the "Split into multiple files" refactor (2026-09-10) that was never de-duplicated. Both script tags load on every page view, so this wasn't theoretical: `renderScoreboard()`/`renderBracket()` genuinely fired twice every 5 seconds. Harmless by itself, but wasteful, and it meant every render-path addition (Follow-a-player's highlight/banner/auto-scroll, and the score-input work planned next) would silently run twice as often as intended.

Removed the copy in `js/core.js`, kept the one in `js/render-viewer.js` — that file already owns `renderScoreboard()`/`renderBracket()`/`renderPlayers()`/`renderRankings()` and this session's Follow-a-player logic, so it's the natural home for a "keep viewer tabs fresh" concern; `core.js` already calls into these same functions from `switchTab()` regardless, so nothing about `core.js`'s own responsibilities changed.

Verified live in-browser: wrapped `renderBracket()` to count invocations, opened the Bracket tab with a minimal tournament loaded, and confirmed exactly 1 call in a 5.5-second window (previously would have been 2) — proving both that the duplicate is gone and that auto-refresh itself still works. Zero console errors.

---

## Bracket: Follow a player (done — 2026-09-06)
The "Follow a player" search/highlight feature three rounds of mockup exploration converged on but explicitly deferred from the collapse/declutter build above. Built directly on top of that build: a search input above the bracket scroller highlights a chosen player/team's row in every round they appear in, plus a "where are they right now" banner. Matches HANDOFF.md's already-binding "Follow a player, never who are you" rule — anyone (a player following themselves, a spectator, an admin) can follow any name.

One open design fork was put to the organiser directly: does following someone auto-expand a currently-collapsed past round that contains them, or does collapse state stay entirely under the viewer's manual control with just an indicator? **Answered: indicator only** — a collapsed round's header turns cyan if the followed unit is inside, but `toggleBracketCollapse`/`bracketCollapseOverride` are never touched by following.

**Resolution** — `resolveUnitQuery(state, query)` (`js/formats-and-primitives.js`, immediately after `unitDisplay()`, its exact inverse): format-agnostic by construction (built on `unitDisplay()` itself, so individual vs. team needs no separate branch), matches a team name OR any individual member's name to the same `teamId`, exact matches beat substring matches at each query length (so "Al" isn't shadowed by "Alex"). Reserves are included on purpose — following an unseeded reserve is valid, it just shows "not in this bracket yet".

**State** — `js/render-viewer.js`, `BRACKET_FOLLOW_KEY = 'curveFFA_bracket_follow'` + `getFollowedUnit()`/`setFollowedUnit()`/`clearFollow()`. Stores the **resolved key**, not raw typed text, so a roster change degrades gracefully instead of resolving to the wrong unit. **`localStorage`, not in-memory** — the opposite choice from `bracketCollapseOverride`, and for the opposite reason: losing "who I follow" on reload is a real cost for a returning viewer, matching `ADMIN_UNLOCKED_KEY`'s reasoning. Cleared in `proceedReset()` but deliberately **not** in `proceedGenerateSchedule()` — the same friend group across several regenerated tournaments should keep who they like to follow, unlike collapse state (tied to one specific round structure). Intentionally still writes in viewer mode — the key never enters `T`, never reaches `pushSyncUpdate()`, and viewers are the feature's primary audience.

**Rendering** — `buildBracketHtml(state, collapseMap, follow)` gained a third, optional parameter (a `bracketFollowStatus()` result). `js/archive.js`'s call site (`buildBracketHtml(snap)`) is untouched — passes neither, so Archive never highlights anything, confirmed by calling `buildBracketHtml(T)` with one argument directly while a follow was active and checking the output contained zero `.is-followed`/`.has-followed` classes. `bracketFollowStatus()` is a standalone pre-pass over `state.assignments` (not folded into `buildBracketHtml`'s own per-round loop) — needed before the round-column loop runs either way, and keeps `buildBracketHtml` a pure function of its inputs. "Eliminated" uses the exact same `lastAssignedRound()` rule `renderPlayers()`/`computeRankings()` already use. A followed unit's row/team-row/bye-card all gain `.is-followed`; a bye needed explicit handling since a bye recipient never appears in the room loop (`room:null` is filtered out there) — without it the highlight would silently vanish on any round the followed unit received a bye.

**Highlight styling** — background + inset ring (`var(--cyan-dim)` + `#00E5FF44`), deliberately **never `border-left-color`** — that's already load-bearing for the adv/elim/lucky result signal from the collapse/declutter build; overriding it would destroy that signal instead of adding to it.

**Scope decision — skipped a label-extraction refactor.** The banner needs to name the same column the highlight is in, but `buildBracketHtml`'s WB/LB/GF column labels are derived inline via local counters rather than a reusable per-round label function, so a double-elimination banner can say "Round 4" while the column header says "LB Round 2". The clean fix (extract a shared `bracketRoundLabels(state)`, verify separately, then build on top) was a legitimate candidate for its own pass, but was deliberately skipped to keep this build's scope contained — the banner instead reuses `renderPlayers()`'s simpler existing label logic (`Final`/`Semis`/`Round N`), which is correct everywhere except double-elimination, where the mismatch is cosmetic-only (the highlight itself is unaffected). Worth a follow-up later.

**Fixed 2026-09-06 — see "Two small flagged nits" below.**

**Explicitly out of scope**: the Players tab's existing `filterPlayers()`/`#pl-search` — untouched, independent. Sub-highlighting a specific matched member's name within a team's member line — following resolves to the whole team, whichever name matched (team or member) highlights the entire row uniformly. Rename-tracking — if a followed unit is renamed mid-tournament, the stored key silently stops resolving and the banner degrades to "not in this bracket"; no hook added to `renamePlayer()`.

Verified live in-browser, via hand-constructed `T` states covering the cases most likely to be missed:
- **Individual format, awkward count (17 players)**: partial-name typing (`resolveUnitQuery`) correctly resolved and highlighted; the current round's row highlighted correctly even with no score/colour yet.
- **Bye handling**: a followed unit's bye card (odd-count "Bye" strategy) correctly gained `.is-followed`; banner correctly read "Round N · BYE, advances automatically".
- **Eliminated unit**: seeded a Final excluding some Round-2 players; banner correctly read "out in Round 2" for an excluded unit.
- **Collapsed round containing the followed unit**: confirmed the round stayed collapsed (`isCollapsed === true`) with zero room-group HTML generated, while its header correctly gained `.has-followed` — collapse state genuinely untouched, exactly matching the organiser's "indicator only" answer. Manually clicking it still expanded normally afterward.
- **Team format, by team name and by a member's name** ("Ash" on "Team Rocket"): both resolved to the same `teamId`; whole two-line row highlighted, no sub-highlighting of the individual member.
- **Ambiguous names** ("Al" vs. "Alex" both on roster): exact match won over substring at each query length, confirmed via direct `resolveUnitQuery()` calls.
- **Persistence across reload**: followed a team via a member's name, called `saveState()`, reloaded the page, called `loadState()` — the highlight, banner, and input box (repopulated with the *resolved* label "Team Rocket", not the originally-typed "Ash") were all correctly restored.
- **Clear**: the ✕ button removed the highlight, banner, and input text, and the `localStorage` key was confirmed actually gone (not just visually cleared).
- **Archive isolation**: confirmed directly, as described above under "Rendering".
- **Stale key**: set a follow key with no matching roster entry — banner degraded to "Following X — not in this bracket" with no console errors.
- **No `T`/sync involvement**: confirmed the follow key never appears anywhere in `T`'s own serialization.

Explicitly not exercised this pass: a live two-tab Firebase viewer-mode session (confirmed correct by code review instead — `getFollowedUnit()`/`setFollowedUnit()` carry no `SYNC_IS_VIEWER` guard and no `T` write, so there's nothing for `pushSyncUpdate()` to ever pick up).

## Bracket: collapsible past rounds + decluttered room chrome (done — 2026-09-06)
Three rounds of mockup exploration (see "Round 2"/"Round 3" below) converged on a direction for the real viewer-facing Bracket tab: keep today's horizontal round-column layout as-is structurally, but let past rounds collapse to save space, and declutter the per-room chrome. A dedicated 48-player/8-round scale mockup ("Live Bracket Concepts" artifact, outside the repo) stress-tested this and surfaced what the smaller mockups couldn't show: a round with 6 stacked rooms of 8 players is both unavoidably tall and, in the organiser's own words, "cluttered with those room 'boxes.'" This build implements that direction for real. Score-entry integrated into this view and a real "follow a player" search/highlight feature were both explicitly confirmed deferred to separate future stages, not part of this build.

**Collapsible past rounds** — a past round's column collapses to a ~34px vertical-text strip on click, re-expands on click. Current and future rounds are structurally never collapsible — not defaulted to expanded, but given no `collapsible` class, no chevron, and no `onclick` at all — enforcing the existing "Design intent for viewer-facing tabs" rule (future rounds always fully visible; reopening a collapsed round must show identical full detail, never a summary) as a hard invariant rather than a convention to remember.

- `buildBracketHtml(state, collapseMap)` (`js/render-viewer.js`) gained an optional second parameter: `{ [roundIndex]: boolean }`, present key = collapsible with that value, absent key = not collapsible at all. Archive's read-only call site (`js/archive.js`, `buildBracketHtml(snap)`) passes no second argument, so every round renders exactly as before this change — zero chevrons, zero clickable headers, always fully expanded. `buildBracketHtml` itself stays a pure function of its inputs with no dependency on module state.
- A collapsed round's room-loop HTML is never generated at all (an early `return` inside the `forEach`, not a CSS `display:none`) — so a re-expand always runs the exact same render code path every other round uses, making "identical full detail on reopen" true by construction.
- New module-level `bracketCollapseOverride` (`js/render-viewer.js`) stores only explicit user toggles, keyed by round index; an absent entry falls back to `bracketRoundDefaultCollapsed(ri, state)` — every past round collapses by default except the one immediately before current (the round someone landing on Bracket is most likely to still care about). Chosen **in-memory, not `localStorage`** — losing it on reload is a real non-cost (unlike `ADMIN_UNLOCKED_KEY`), matching the `adminRenderPending`/`currentArchiveEntry` per-browser-only precedent already named in "Design intent for viewer-facing tabs" above.
- **Real gotcha found during design, not hypothetical**: the Bracket tab's 5-second auto-refresh poller fully replaces `#br-rounds`'s `innerHTML` on every tick. A collapse toggle that lived only in the DOM would be silently wiped within 5 seconds. Collapse state had to live in JS and be threaded back into `buildBracketHtml()` on every render — confirmed by a live test (see below).
- `bracketCollapseOverride` is cleared in `proceedGenerateSchedule()` and `proceedReset()`, alongside the existing per-generation resets (`T.groups`, `T.defenderChanges`) — a stale round-index override must never bleed into a differently-shaped tournament.

**Decluttered room chrome** — `.bracket-room-card`'s bordered/background box and `.bracket-room-name`'s boxed header bar (the literal "room boxes" the organiser named) are replaced with a plain small-caps `.bracket-room-label` plus a left-border colour accent per row (`.bracket-player-row`/`.bracket-team-row` — green=advanced, muted=eliminated, purple=lucky loser), the same accent pattern Rankings' `.rk-row` already uses elsewhere in this app. Pure rename in `buildBracketHtml` (`bracket-room-card`→`bracket-room-group`, `bracket-room-name`→`bracket-room-label`); no information removed — every name, score, ★ lucky-loser mark, ⚖ tie-break badge, and team member/🛡 defender line renders exactly as before. `.bracket-bye-card` was deliberately left unchanged (already distinct, not one of the "boxes" the complaint was about). No new CSS tokens needed — stayed inside the app's existing palette rather than importing the standalone mockup's invented one.

Verified live in-browser (local static server), via hand-constructed `T` states covering every format this app supports plus the specific stress case that motivated this build — since the render logic, not the pre-existing advancement logic, is what changed:
- **Classic single-elimination, 6 rounds**: confirmed default collapse (rounds 1–3 collapsed, round 4 — immediately before current — expanded, Semis/Final structurally non-collapsible with no chevron), click-to-expand via a real mouse click reopening a round with identical detail to before, ★ lucky-loser mark and ⚖ tie-break badge both correctly preserved, and the current round's "roomComplete" all-or-nothing reveal gating (one scored room shown in colour, one unscored room shown plain) untouched.
- **5-second poller survival**: manually expanded a collapsed round, waited 7s untouched — still expanded, confirming `bracketCollapseOverride` (not DOM state) is what the poller's re-render reads.
- **Double-elimination**: WB/LB/Grand-Final header labels and colour accents (`wb-hdr`/`lb-hdr`/`gf-hdr`) correct both collapsed and expanded; collapsing a WB round didn't disturb LB round numbering.
- **Team format (designated-player scoring)**: two-line rows fully legible with the border accent, 🛡 defender mark preserved, `adv`/`elim` classes correct.
- **Group stage**: multi-group room labels ("Group A · Room A" etc.) render correctly under the new label treatment.
- **48-player/6-room stress case** (the scenario that motivated this build): confirmed collapsed rounds generate zero room-group HTML (not just hidden), and collapsing the two heaviest early rounds visibly and substantially shortens the horizontal scroll distance to the current round — the actual problem this build set out to fix.
- **Archive unaffected**: called `buildBracketHtml(snap)` with no second argument directly — zero `.is-collapsed`/`.collapsible`/chevron/`onclick` anywhere in the output, every round fully expanded, exactly as before this change.
- **Reset**: confirmed `proceedReset()` actually clears `bracketCollapseOverride` to `{}` (not just present in source) by calling it directly and inspecting the variable before/after. While testing formats back-to-back without going through a reset between them, a stale override from an earlier synthetic scenario visibly leaked into a later one at the same round indices — a live demonstration of exactly the bug the reset-point clears are there to prevent, not just a theoretical concern.

**Explicitly out of scope**: score-entry integrated into this view; a real "follow a player" search/highlight feature (the mockup's search ribbon was inherited boilerplate, confirmed declined for this build via a direct question to the organiser) — both remain future, separately-scoped stages.

## Rankings image export (done — 2026-09-02)
A "Download rankings image" button — on the live Rankings tab and on the Archive detail view — exports the current standings as a PNG, styled to match the app's dark theme, for pasting into Discord result posts or an external website. Scoped to Rankings only (no Bracket image); explicitly not a PDF or print-dialog flow — the organiser wanted a pasteable image, not a document.

**Why canvas, not a screenshot library**: the app has no backend and no external JS libraries, and there's no native browser API to rasterise an arbitrary DOM subtree into an image without one. `buildRankingsImageCanvas(state)` hand-draws the graphic onto an offscreen `<canvas>` with the 2D drawing API instead — same "no external dependencies" constraint the rest of the app follows, and it gives full control over the layout (closer to a clean shareable graphic than a literal screenshot of the HTML table would be).

- **Shared with the on-screen table**: calls the same `computeRankings(state)` used by `renderRankings()`/the Archive detail view, so the image can never show different standings than what's visible on screen. Live tab calls it with `T`; Archive detail calls it with `currentArchiveEntry.snapshot` — same pattern as `buildBracketHtml`/`buildRankingsRows`'s existing live/archive sharing.
- **Layout**: 720px-wide canvas (2x-rendered for crisp high-DPI/Discord-embed quality), `CURVE FFA` logo, player/round count, tournament title, one row per player — full list, not a top-N cutoff, matching the on-screen table's scope exactly. Champion row (rank 1, once the Final is complete) gets a green left-accent + tinted background, mirroring the `.rk-champion` row style. Long names truncate with an ellipsis (`truncateToWidth()`, binary search over `measureText()`) rather than overflowing.
- **Colours are literal hex, mirrored from the CSS custom properties** in `<style>` — canvas can't read CSS vars via `fillStyle`, so `RANK_IMG_COLORS` duplicates them. Keep the two in sync if the theme ever changes.
- **Font loading**: `document.fonts.load(...)` is awaited for every Rajdhani/Inter weight used before drawing, since canvas silently falls back to a system font if the requested one isn't marked ready — guards against an inconsistent export if the button is somehow the very first thing to touch these fonts on the page (in practice they're already loaded well before Rankings is reachable, but this makes it correct regardless of timing).
- **Download mechanism**: `canvas.toBlob()` → object URL → temporary anchor `.click()` → revoke, the same pattern `downloadJson()` already used for archive exports, just `image/png` instead of `application/json`. `downloadCanvasAsPng()` wraps the callback-based `toBlob()` in a Promise so the whole chain is properly awaitable.
- Reuses the existing `sanitizeFilename()` helper for the download filename (`{title}-rankings.png`).

Verified: pixel-level inspection of the generated canvas (exact hex matches for the cyan logo text and the champion green accent, not just "some non-background pixels somewhere"), a full visual render at both a normal roster size and with an intentionally long player name (confirmed truncation), the empty-state guard (alerts rather than downloading a blank image when no schedule exists yet), and both download paths (`downloadRankingsImage()` from the live tab, `downloadArchiveRankingsImage()` from a saved snapshot) producing a correctly-typed and non-trivially-sized `image/png` blob.

---

## Audit resolutions (2026-08-30 decided, 2026-08-31 implemented)
An implicit-assumptions audit was run against the app (every judgment call made during implementation that wasn't explicitly spec'd). The organiser reviewed all findings, made the decisions below, and — in a follow-up session — asked to go further on tie-break scope than originally planned (universal detection, no Semis carve-out). All 7 planned changes and both confirmed bugs are now implemented and verified via live playthrough testing.

### Confirmed correct as-is, no change needed
- **Scoring edge cases**: the Fair Points formula's implicit 100000-score ceiling, the lack of negative-score validation (input `min="0"` is cosmetic only), and the internal `''`-vs-`null` inconsistency between `T.finalScores` and `T.scores` are all accepted as they are.
- **Seeding**: lucky losers seeding as the lowest tier in the next round's snake order (regardless of their actual relative performance) is correct.
- **Reserves/lucky losers**: excluding an all-zero-score room from lucky-loser candidacy (rather than dividing by zero) is correct.
- **Ranking calculation**: the existing assumptions (qual-eliminated players' relative-score proxy, ties sharing a rank, still-active players ranked last, etc. — already documented above under "Rankings") are reasonable as implemented.
- **Archive**: the millisecond-timestamp archive ID scheme, and the `roundsPlayed` summary field showing the current round number rather than a completed-round count, don't need action.

### Implemented
1. **Round 2 now snake/interleave-seeds off Round 1**, same as every later round. `advanceRound()`'s `ri === 0 ? randomSeed(...) : snakeSeed(...)` special case is gone — every advancement past the very first (random) draw into Round 1 uses `snakeSeed()`. Verified with an exact match against an independently-computed expected seeding, for both standard FFA and qual mode's Round 1→2 transition.
2. **Reserve window now closes only once the tournament has advanced past the first elimination-capable round**, not the moment it becomes current. `checkReserveWindow()` finds the round index whose *advancement* first eliminates someone — for qual mode that's the last qualification round (the cut happens on advancing out of it, before any `!isNoElim` round even begins), for standard elimination it's the first `!isNoElim` round — and keeps the window open while `T.curRound` is at or before that index, closing only once you've advanced past it. Verified reserves can now actually be added mid-Round-3 (previously blocked), and that the window correctly stays open through all of a qual tournament's 3rd (or however many) qualification round.
3. **`computeLuckyLosers()` now returns `[]` until every room in the round has a complete set of scores** (`isRoundFullyScored()`), since lucky-loser selection is inherently cross-room. Fixing the function itself (rather than each caller) automatically gates all 3 real call sites — `renderRooms()`/`recalcRoom()` (Admin's room status pills) and `renderScoreboard()`. Bracket and the Admin "seed panel" turned out not to need changes: both only ever display the already-*finalized* `T.luckyLosers[ri]` record from when a round was seeded, never a live preview. Verified the ★ stays hidden with one room still unscored and appears correctly the instant the last room completes.
4. **Tie-break detection is now universal**: any two or more players sharing an identical score anywhere in a room's ranking is a tie needing resolution, in every round except the Final — not just a tie at the advance/eliminate cutoff. This is a wider scope than originally planned: the first draft kept a Semis carve-out (only the 4th/5th cutoff matters there, since all of Semis' top 4 land in the same single Final room regardless of order), but the organiser asked to drop that too, since locking behaviour to Semis' current shape risks breaking if the schedule structure changes in a future version. `T.tieResolutions[key]` changed from a single winner-name string to an **ordered array**, built one click at a time via `resolveTie()` ("this player ranks next among the remaining tied ones") — a 2-way tie still resolves in one click exactly as before; a cluster of size K needs K−1 clicks before the last name is implied automatically. Keys changed from `r{ri}-rm{rm}` to `r{ri}-rm{rm}-s{score}` (score-value-scoped), which incidentally closes most of Bug B below for free. New shared helpers `groupByScore`/`orderRoomByScore`/`tieResolutionList` (the latter also reads an old single-string resolution as a one-element list, so an in-progress tournament or archived snapshot from before this change won't break). The resolved order now threads through everywhere a room's exact order matters: `recalcRoom()`, `renderScoreboard()`, `buildBracketHtml()` (live and archived), `updateQualTable()`'s Fair Points ranking, and `advanceRound()`'s seeding. Verified: a tie well within the always-advancing tier of a no-elim round now blocks; a 3-way tie correctly needs exactly 2 clicks with the right "ranked so far" banner text at each step; a Semis top-4 tie now blocks too (confirming no carve-out).
5. **Qualification-round ties, both layers:**
   - **Per-round, per-room** — falls out of item 4 automatically, since qual rounds are no longer excluded from `detectTieBreaks()`. Verified a qual-round tie blocks advancement.
   - **Qual-table cutoff** — new `detectQualCutoffTie()` checks whether the player at the `qualAdv` boundary shares an exact `totalFP` with the next player; `applyQualCutoffOrder()` splices the resolved order into the qual table before `advanceRound()` slices it. Both plug into a new `getAllTies(ri, round)` (per-room clusters, plus the cutoff cluster only on the actual last qual round) that `hasPendingTies()`/`renderTieBanner()` now use instead of `detectTieBreaks()` directly, so blocking and the banner UI cover it for free — same multi-way peel-off mechanism. `renderQualTable()` also shows a "⚠ Tie" pill on the tied rows (scoped to only the last qual round, to avoid flagging a premature/likely-to-resolve-itself tie from incomplete cumulative totals earlier in qual). Verified end-to-end with an engineered exact Fair-Points tie: correct banner text, `advanceRound()` blocked with the right alert, and — critically — that resolving it determines who *actually* keeps the qualifying spot, not just cosmetic ordering. Also hit a genuine 4-way qual-cutoff tie by coincidence during regression testing, confirming the multi-way path.
6. **`QUAL_ROUNDS = 3`** is now a single named constant used by `buildProgression()`'s qual loop and the qual-table's "played/N" display; `advanceRound()`'s qual-cut trigger uses the structural check `round.isQual && !(T.rounds[ri+1] && T.rounds[ri+1].isQual)` instead of the hardcoded `ri === 2`. Verified changing `QUAL_ROUNDS` to 4 required zero further code changes anywhere — the cut correctly waited for the real last qual round, the display updated, and the reserve-window fix (item 2, itself already structural) adapted along with it.
7. **Archive de-duplication now uses a stable `T.tournamentId`**, not the title string. Generated fresh in `proceedGenerateSchedule()`, cleared in `proceedReset()`, self-healed in `loadState()` for a tournament generated before this change. `saveToArchive()` now distinguishes "you've saved this exact tournament before" (same `tournamentId` — unchanged Overwrite/Save-as-new/Cancel modal) from "a *different* tournament happens to share this title" (new "Title already used" modal, offering only Save-as-new/Cancel — never Overwrite). `archiveSilently()` (auto-save and the unsaved-changes "Save & ..." paths) matches by `tournamentId` only and never prompts, so it can no longer silently clobber a differently-identified tournament. Verified all three paths, including that a title collision between two genuinely different tournaments now correctly produces two separate archive entries instead of one overwriting the other.

### Bugs fixed
- **Lucky-loser candidate selection ignoring tie-break substitution** — resolved as a structural side effect of item 4's rewrite: `advanceRound()` now reads both the direct-advancer cutoff and the lucky-loser candidate slot from the same tie-resolved `orderRoomByScore()` array, so there's no separate single-slot "patch" step left to go stale. Verified with the exact scenario from the audit (tie-break winner originally sitting at the post-cutoff array position) — the winner now appears exactly once in the next round's assignment, correctly flagged as a direct advancer, not double-counted as a lucky loser.
- **Stale tie resolutions surviving a score correction** — the new score-scoped key format (`r{ri}-rm{rm}-s{score}`) already closes the scenario from the original report (a different pair tying at a different score gets an independent key). The narrower residual case — a brand-new tie recurring at the *same* score value with different players — is handled by `invalidateStaleTieResolutions(ri, rm)`, wired into both `scoreChanged()` (the reported scenario) and `removePlayer()` (same class of staleness). Verified by deliberately reconstructing the residual case: a resolved A/B tie at score 1000, then edits that moved A away and brought two different players to that same score — the stale resolution was correctly cleared and the genuinely new tie correctly flagged as pending.

### Testing performed
Full regression across all 9 changes together: standard FFA at N=37 (reserve addition mid-first-elimination-round, an engineered advancing-tier tie, and a lucky-loser scenario, all in one playthrough) and N=43 (with an exact snake-seeding verification), plus qual mode at N=41 (verifying the reserve window through all qual rounds and the post-cut closure). All three reached the Final, rendered Scoreboard/Bracket/Rankings/Players/Archive correctly, and produced zero console errors.

### Parked for later review (organiser wants to revisit, not decided yet)
- **Round 1 randomness quality**: the `Array.sort(() => Math.random()-.5)` shuffle is "good enough for now" but not a provably-uniform permutation (a proper Fisher-Yates would be) — flagged to revisit later, no action for now.
- **Reserve overflow confirmation**: `addReserve()` shows `confirm('All rooms have 8 players. Adding this reserve will create a 9-player room. Proceed?')` when the smallest available room is already at the 8-player cap. Organiser wasn't sure what this referred to when reviewing the audit — noted here with the specific code location so it's easy to revisit and decide on (leave as a confirm dialog? block outright? something else?). **Decided 2026-09-12 — see "Individual-reserve 'None'-mode refusal"**: FFA (no real invariant at stake) keeps the confirm dialog exactly as-is; `individual-1v1` (which does have a real "None"-mode invariant, same as team formats) now refuses outright instead.
- **Raw-score qualification metric**: when the organiser picks "Raw score only" as the overall scoring system, `updateQualTable()` currently still sums per-round *rank* (1st/2nd/3rd...) across the 3 qual rounds — a golf-style lowest-total-rank metric — rather than summing raw scores. Neither documented "scoring system" literally describes this. Needs a decision on what "raw score" should actually build the qual ranking on. **Resolved 2026-09-08 — see "Fair Points sign fix + Raw score removal + Schedule Logic gating" above**: "Raw score only" removed as a selectable option entirely, closing this gap by elimination rather than by deciding the metric.
- **Duplicate player names**: not straightforward to resolve now, since two different real players sharing a name isn't implausible. A future version will connect player names to real userIDs; in the meantime, a possible interim approach is giving same-named players a temporary/session-local ID to disambiguate them internally (score attribution, rename/remove targeting) until the real userID system is wired in. No action for this version — noted for the userID-integration work.

## Persistence (done)
`saveState()`/`loadState()` in the script serialize the full tournament state (plus setup-form fields and active tab) to `localStorage` under `curveFFA_state_v1`, called after every mutating action. A refresh restores exactly where you left off, including which nav tab was open. Single-device/browser only for now — kept as an isolated layer so it's a clean swap point for a backend call once the hosted multi-user version is built.

## Edge case testing (done — room distribution & target computation)
Tested via automated sweep (not manual UI clicks) across N=16–150, both with and without Qualification mode, plus full simulated playthroughs for N=17, 23, and 45(qual). Found and fixed three real bugs:
- `computeTargets` could produce a **non-monotonic** elimination sequence (player count increasing mid-tournament, e.g. N=16–21 would jump 16→24→20→16) — rewritten to clamp forward so counts only ever decrease.
- `distributeRooms` could produce rooms **above FFA's own configured 8-player ceiling** (N=9–11 → single room of 9–11; N=17 → a room of 9) — rewritten to treat that configured max as a hard ceiling, only falling below the 6-floor for the one unavoidable case (N=17). (This was FFA's own preferred ceiling being enforced, not the game's actual technical limit — see "Room cap correction" further below, added later once that distinction came up.)
- Qualification mode used the configured `qualAdv` (default 24) to size the post-qualification bracket even when fewer players were registered than that — now clamped to `[16, N]` at generate time.
Also added a hard minimum of 16 registered players (Semis is fixed at 16 in 2×8 rooms — below that the format can't run). Originally reflected via the `cfg-n` input's `min` attribute, but `cfg-n` was later replaced by a read-only display (see "Registered player count" above) — the 16-player floor is now enforced in `generateSchedule()` itself (inline error), and `cfg-qual-adv` still carries a matching `min="16"`. Verified separately that room-count shape is stable under ±1 player changes across N=17–100 — no destabilizing jumps — so a single late add/drop-out won't reshape the bracket for everyone else.

## Auto-refresh (done — Scoreboard & Bracket)
A single `setInterval(…, 5000)`, started once at page load, checks which nav tab is currently active and calls only that tab's render function directly (`renderScoreboard()` / `renderBracket()`) — bypassing `switchTab()`, so it doesn't trigger an extra `saveState()` write every tick. Admin, Players, and Rankings are untouched by the interval (Players/Rankings still only refresh on tab visit, as before); Admin's own score inputs are unaffected since the interval never calls anything Admin-related.
- **Scoreboard**: unchanged rendering logic — it already showed partial/in-progress rooms live, so wiring it to the interval was the whole job.
- **Bracket**: got a new "room complete" gate — a room's scores, sort order, and advance/eliminate colour-coding only appear once *every* player in that room has a score entered; until then it renders exactly like an unplayed room (plain name list, original order, no colours), even if some scores already exist. Verified per-room independence (one room can show full results while its neighbour, still partially scored, stays blank) and confirmed the flip happens automatically on the next 5s cycle with no manual re-render.
- **Scope note**: this polls the browser tab's own in-memory `T`, not localStorage — deliberately, per the spec that commissioned it. That means it only has something new to show when *something in that same tab* changed `T` (e.g. `goBack()`/`advanceRound()`/a tie resolved) since the last tick; it does not pull in changes made by an admin on a *different* tab, browser, or device — that gap is unchanged and tracked separately as "Cross-tab / cross-device live sync" above.

---

## Code consolidation pass (done)
The script (by then ~2230 lines) was reorganized for maintainability with **no intended behaviour change** — verified afterward via a full automated playthrough in the browser (generate → start → score → forced tie-break resolution → lucky losers → advance through all 8 rounds → multi-game Final → Rankings/Bracket/Scoreboard/Players render → Archive save/duplicate-title/list/detail/overwrite → Reset and Generate-new unsaved-changes modals → reserve add/remove and player rename/remove, several with apostrophes in the name to stress-test escaping) with zero console errors.
- **Section comments**: the ~32 existing fine-grained `// ═══` banners were consolidated/relabelled under the broader taxonomy (State & Configuration, Roster & Player Management, Room Distribution & Progression, Seeding, Scoring & Tie-breaks, Round Rendering, Scoreboard & Bracket, Players & Rankings, Archive, Archive Rendering, Persistence, Navigation, Helpers & Utilities, Auto-refresh, Init) without physically relocating code — functions tightly coupled to where they're called (e.g. Reserves/Manage Players rendering, embedded in the live Admin round view) were left in place and clearly labelled rather than forced into strict topical contiguity, since JS hoisting makes physical order irrelevant to behaviour and moving interleaved live-round logic risked more than it was worth.
- **Live vs. archive rendering, explicitly separated**: the data layer (`getArchiveIndex`/`saveArchiveEntry`/`performArchiveSave`/etc.) now ends where DOM rendering begins; `renderArchiveList()` onward is its own "ARCHIVE RENDERING" section, clearly marked as reusing the same pure builders (`computeRankings`, `buildBracketHtml`, `buildRankingsRows`) the live Scoreboard/Bracket/Rankings tabs use, just fed an archived snapshot instead of live `T`.
- **Dead code removed**: `T.tieBreaks` state field (declared + reset in two places, never read — fully superseded by `detectTieBreaks()`); `advExtra:0` (written in all 5 round-object constructions in `buildProgression()`, never read anywhere); CSS `.g3` and `.msg-warn` rules (defined but never applied to any element).
- **Duplicate logic consolidated** into six new shared functions: `renderTieBanner(ri, round)` (tie-break banner HTML, previously copy-pasted between `renderAdminRound()` and `scoreChanged()`); `escAttr(s)` (apostrophe-escaping for inline `onclick` attributes, previously duplicated 3× with an inconsistent non-global regex in two of the three — now uniformly global); `scoreOrDefault(raw, fallback)` (the null/undefined/empty-string-then-`parseInt` score lookup, previously repeated in 8 places); `confirmUnsavedChanges(...)` (the "unsaved tournament in progress" 3-option modal shared by Generate Schedule and Reset, exact button/message wording preserved); a local `doSave` helper inside `saveToArchive()` (collapsed 3 copies of the save-then-status-message pairing into 1); `getActiveTab(fallback)` (the "find the active nav button" lookup, previously duplicated 3×).
- **Inline comments** added to functions whose behaviour wasn't already documented (e.g. `renderTieBanner`, `confirmUnsavedChanges`, `scoreOrDefault`, `escAttr`, `getActiveTab`, plus clarifying notes on why Scoreboard's and Bracket's score-sorting look similar but intentionally differ — Scoreboard always shows partial results, Bracket gates on room completion).
- Net effect: total line count stayed roughly flat (new shared functions + the inline documentation the pass added roughly offset the duplicate code removed) — the real win is that six previously-copy-pasted patterns and two dead state fields now exist in exactly one place each, and every section of the file is clearly labelled.

## Gamemode architecture (done — 2026-09-01)

FFA classic elimination is now one registered module within a gamemode system, not the entire app. The codebase was originally built with FFA as the only mode and its rules baked throughout — the progression logic, scoring, seeding, room structure, and rendering all assumed individual FFA players. Rather than scattering `if (mode === 'FFA') ... else ...` conditionals everywhere (the exact fragility problem this app was built to replace), the core functions were split into a thin **dispatcher** (shared plumbing, unchanged for every mode) plus a **classic-elimination module** (today's exact logic, moved verbatim, reading its parameters from a descriptor instead of hardcoded literals). This is a structural refactor only — the app behaves identically to before it when run, verified below.

### Two independent dimensions
The architecture must treat these as separate parameters, combinable freely:

**Schedule logic** (how rounds are structured and players progress):
- **Classic elimination** — what is currently built: gradual cuts toward a fixed Semis/Final structure
- **Kings Valley** — hierarchical rooms per round; players move up or down based on results; only losers of the bottom room are eliminated; best performers in lower rooms are promoted, worst performers in higher rooms are demoted
- **Swiss rounds** — all players play a fixed number of rounds paired by record; top N advance at the end
- **Group stage into knockout** — pool play followed by a bracket

**Game format** (what is played in each room):
- **FFA individual** — currently built; 6–8 individual players per room
- **2v2v2v2** — 4 teams of 2 per room; next to be built after the refactor
- **3v3v3** — 3 teams of 3 per room
- **3v3** — 2 teams head-to-head
- **Last Man Standing** — FFA variant; last-place player per game is eliminated progressively within the match
- **1v1** — bracket or group stage

A Kings Valley tournament could be played in FFA or in teams. A Swiss tournament could be FFA or 1v1. Every combination must be possible without special-casing.

### Build order after the refactor
The second combination to build (validating the architecture beyond FFA) was **2v2v2v2 with classic elimination**, since it appears most frequently in real tournament records. The refactor was designed with this as the concrete second case, not an abstract future one — it is much easier to generalise correctly from two real examples than from one. **Done (2026-09-03)** — see "2v2v2v2 team format" below.

### What "team" means in this context
In team formats, the **unit of competition is a named team**, not an individual player. A team has a name and a fixed roster of N players. Scores are entered per team per room, not per individual. Seeding, advancement, lucky losers, and rankings all operate on teams. The player roster and reserve system will need a team-aware equivalent.

### Implementation
**State**: `T.scheduleLogic` (default `'classic-elimination'`), `T.gameFormat` (default `'ffa-individual'`), `T.gamemodeConfig` (default `{}`, populated at generation time from the chosen format's defaults) — added to the base `T` declaration, so `saveState()`/`loadState()` and archive snapshots (`performArchiveSave()`'s `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(T))`) all carry them automatically, no per-field plumbing needed. Backward compatible the same way `T.tournamentId` already was: a saved state or archive from before this refactor simply lacks these keys, so `Object.assign(T, saved.T)` leaves the base declaration's defaults in place — verified by loading a state with the three fields deliberately stripped and confirming the app kept working normally.

**`T.cfg` was deliberately left untouched** — `qual`/`qualAdv`/`scoring`/`finalsGames`/`n` stay where they were. `T.gamemodeConfig` is scoped narrowly to what was actually hardcoded in code before this refactor: room-size bounds (previously literal `6`/`8` inside `distributeRooms`/`snapFriendly`) and qual-round count (previously the global `QUAL_ROUNDS` constant, which still exists as the *default* value `gamemodeConfig.qualRounds` is populated from at generation time).

**Two registries** (`GAME_FORMATS`, `SCHEDULE_LOGICS`), each with exactly one entry today (`'ffa-individual'`, `'classic-elimination'`), plus `getGamemodeDescriptor()` composing the live descriptor from current state — `{ scheduleLogic, gameFormat, schedule: <behaviour>, format: <behaviour>, config: T.gamemodeConfig }`. Registry entries live near the state declaration for discoverability; the functions they point to stay in their natural topical sections (the classic-elimination progression builder is with Room Distribution & Progression, its advancement logic is with Round Rendering) — the registry is a lookup table, not where the logic lives.

**Dispatcher pattern** for the two functions with real per-mode logic:
- `buildProgression(cfg)` → thin dispatcher → `classicEliminationBuildProgression(cfg, descriptor)` (the original body, verbatim, now reading `descriptor.config.roomSize`/`.qualRounds` instead of hardcoded `8`/`QUAL_ROUNDS`)
- `advanceRound()` → thin dispatcher (tie-break block check, snake-seed call, `T.curRound++`, render/save — unchanged) → `classicEliminationComputeAdvancement(ri, round, descriptor)` (the original qual-cut-branch + per-room direct/lucky-loser body, verbatim, returning `{advancing, luckyNames}`)

`distributeRooms(n, roomSize)`/`snapFriendly(n, roomSize)` now take a `{min, max, ideal}` object instead of a bare `ideal` number, replacing every hardcoded `6`/`8` inside them — every call site (all inside `classicEliminationBuildProgression`/`computeTargets`) updated accordingly. `snakeSeed(players, numRooms, formatDescriptor)` gained an optional 3rd parameter, accepted but unused by FFA's already-format-agnostic interleave algorithm. `renderRooms()`/`renderScoreboard()`/`renderQualTable()` gained an optional format-descriptor parameter (default: the current gamemode's format), used only to source the dynamically-generated "Player" column header text — verified byte-identical output for FFA (`unitLabel: 'Player'` matches the old hardcoded string exactly). `renderBracket()`/`buildBracketHtml(state)` and `renderRankings()`/`computeRankings(state)` needed no new parameter — `state` already carries the mode fields now that they're part of `T`.

**Setup panel**: two new selectors ("Schedule logic", "Game format") in the Tournament Settings card, visible from the start with one enabled option each and the rest `disabled` (native browser styling, no new CSS) — signalling more formats are coming rather than hiding the concept. Persisted through `saveState()`/`loadState()`'s existing `setup` object, same pattern as the other setup fields.

### Team format hooks
12 `// TEAM FORMAT HOOK` comments originally marked every point where FFA-individual assumptions were baked in. All 12 have since been resolved with real team-aware logic — see "2v2v2v2 team format" below for what replaced them.

### Testing performed
Re-ran the exact regression suite from the audit-resolutions pass — standard FFA at N=37 (reserve mid-first-elimination-round, an engineered tie, a lucky-loser scenario) and N=43 (exact snake-seeding match against an independently-computed expectation), plus qual mode at N=41 (reserve window through all qual rounds and the post-cut closure) — all reaching the Final, archiving correctly (snapshots confirmed to include the three new fields), and rendering Scoreboard/Bracket/Rankings/Players with zero console errors. Additionally confirmed `T.rounds`' full structure (room counts, `advPerRoom`, `luckyCount` per round) for N=37 matches byte-for-byte against a hand-computed expectation using the pre-refactor hardcoded constants, and confirmed the Admin/Scoreboard room tables still render the "Player" header unchanged.

## 2v2v2v2 team format (done — 2026-09-03)
`team-2v2v2v2` is now a second registered `GAME_FORMATS` entry, running on the existing `classic-elimination` schedule logic — the concrete second combination the gamemode architecture refactor was designed to validate. All 12 `// TEAM FORMAT HOOK` seams from that refactor are now real team-aware logic.

### Shared fix: Semis/Final sizing is derived, not hardcoded
`classicEliminationBuildProgression` no longer hardcodes `FINAL=8, SEMIS=16` — it reads `descriptor.config.semisSize`/`finalSize`, computed once at generation time in `proceedGenerateSchedule()` as `2 * roomSize.ideal` / `1 * roomSize.ideal` and stored as explicit `gamemodeConfig` fields (not recomputed inline), so a future manual-override feature (organiser types a custom Semis/Final size before generating) just has to overwrite these two fields — the override UI itself isn't built. For FFA (`roomSize.ideal = 8`) this reproduces `16`/`8` exactly — verified byte-for-byte identical round structure for N=37/43/qual N=41. For 2v2v2v2 (`roomSize.ideal = 4`, team-units) this gives Semis = 8 teams (2 rooms of 4) / Final = 4 teams (1 room of 8 players) — verified at N=27. The confirmed-count floor check in `proceedGenerateSchedule()` is likewise derived from the *currently selected* format's default room size and built from its `unitLabelPlural`, rather than a hardcoded "16 players" message — for 2v2v2v2 that floor is 8 teams, not 16.

### Data model: teamId is the stable identity, teamName is just a label
For a team format, `T.players`/`T.reserves` hold team objects — `{teamId, teamName, members:[{name,userId}]}` — instead of plain name strings. Every place that used to key by a player's `.name` as identity (assignments, scores, tie-resolutions, lucky-losers, qual table, finals) keeps doing exactly that, unchanged — it just keys by a team's `teamId` instead of a name string. This is the load-bearing design choice that kept the diff manageable: `.name` fields throughout the app are opaque identity strings regardless of format, so seeding, advancement, tie detection, and lucky-loser selection needed **no structural changes**, only a generic score-reading layer (see below). A shared `rosterKey()`/`rosterKeys()` pair normalises either roster shape (bare string, or `{teamId,...}` object) into that identity string wherever "the roster" is iterated generically (qual table, rankings, seeding).

Renaming a team is just `team.teamName = newName` — no propagation across every array the way `renamePlayer()` has to for individual formats, since teamId (not teamName) is what's actually stored everywhere else. Members are a **fixed-length array of size `teamSize`**, with `null` for a vacant slot (a removed member, or a short input line at roster-parse time) — this is what Manage Teams' vacant-slot UI expects, and what a genuinely short/malformed roster line degrades to gracefully instead of blocking the whole roster load.

Display resolution is centralised in `unitDisplay(state, key)` / `renderUnitCell(state, key, withMembers)` — looks a key back up to its current team (`teamMap(state)`, scanning both `state.players` and `state.reserves`) and returns `{label, members}`, always resolved fresh so a renamed team's history shows under its current name. `computeRankings(state)` resolves and attaches `label`/`members` to every row it returns, so downstream consumers (`buildRankingsRows`, the rankings image export) never need their own format lookup.

### Score storage: per-member, team score is derived
Score **entry** stays one input per individual player (two per team, stacked under a single room-position row rather than duplicating a whole extra `<tr>` per team — this keeps `recalcRoom()`'s existing per-position DOM targeting, row tinting, and pos/status pills completely unchanged). Keys gain a member suffix: `r{ri}-rm{rm}-p{pi}-m{mi}`. `getUnitScore(state, ri, rm, pi, fallback)` is the one new generic reader every score-consuming function now goes through (`isRoundFullyScored`, `computeLuckyLosers`, `detectTieBreaks`, `recalcRoom`, `updateQualTable`, `classicEliminationComputeAdvancement`, `renderScoreboard`, `buildBracketHtml`, `computeRankings`) — for an individual format it's the exact same lookup as before (byte-identical for FFA); for a team format it sums (`TEAM_SCORING_RULES['sum-members']`, the only rule built) each member's score. `getFinalUnitScore()` is the Finals equivalent (keys `game{g}-{teamId}-m{mi}`).

`gamemodeConfig.teamScoringRule` (default `'sum-members'`) is set at generation time the same way `roomSize`/`qualRounds`/`semisSize`/`finalSize` are. Per Part 5's spec, no rule-selector UI is built — one enabled entry, structurally ready for more (see "Parked" below).

**A genuinely vacant member slot never blocks scoring** — it contributes 0 to the team's derived score rather than counting as "not yet entered." A team down to one member (mid-tournament removal, not yet replaced) can still be scored and advance normally; only a slot that *has* a member but no score yet counts as missing. Verified both in-round and in Finals.

### Two reserve mechanics — genuinely different, not just different granularity
- **Team reserve** (`addTeamReserve`/`removeTeamReserve`): a whole new team waiting in the wings, joins the smallest available room as a unit — direct generalisation of FFA's `addReserve`, same reserve-window gating (`checkReserveWindow()`, unchanged), same smallest-room placement (`smallestRoomInCurrentRound()`, factored out and shared by both). The room-cap confirm dialog now reads the active format's `roomSize.max` instead of a hardcoded 8, so a team format's smaller per-room cap (4 team-units) warns at the right threshold.
- **Individual reserve** (`T.reserveIndividuals`, a new state field — `[{name,userId}]`): a substitute for one missing member of a team that's already seated — not a room-join action, there's no room to join. Entered via a new "Individual reserves" textarea (`PlayerName (userID)` per line, shown only for team formats), consumed via a targeted `fillVacantSlotFromReserve(teamId, memberIndex, reserveIndex)` or `fillVacantSlotWalkup(teamId, memberIndex)` (types a brand-new name), both gated by the same reserve window, both defensively refusing to overwrite a slot that isn't actually vacant.

### Manage Teams (replaces Manage Players for team formats)
`renderManagePlayers()` is now a dispatcher — team formats render `renderManageTeams()` into the same `#admin-manage-players` container instead. Each team is a block: team name (✎ rename / ✕ remove-whole-team) plus one row per member slot, each with its own ✎/✕, or — if vacant — the reserve-individual dropdown + "＋ New" walk-up button described above. Whole-team removal (`removeTeam`) parallels FFA's `removePlayer()` re-packing exactly, just shifting every member-suffixed score key for each room position after the removed team (one extra index level than an individual player's removal has to handle). Past rounds' scores are left untouched either way — same rule FFA's `removePlayer()`/`renamePlayer()` already establish.

### Rendering
One row per team everywhere a room/roster is listed — Admin score-entry (`renderRooms`), Scoreboard, Qual table, seed panel, Rankings (team name **and both member names**, per the confirmed spec), and the Players tab all show the resolved team name (+ members, where there's room). ~~Bracket is the one exception — its cards are narrow enough that member names were dropped to keep the compact per-room layout readable; it shows team name only.~~ **Closed out (2026-09-04)** — Bracket now shows member names too, via a two-line layout; see "Players tab / marker / Rankings / Bracket update", Part 4. The live Rankings tab and Archive detail view's table headers used to say "Team" instead of "Player" for a team tournament (`unitLabel`, resolved fresh per format on tab switch / archive open) — this was a leftover hook from the gamemode refactor ("left for whenever team rankings are built"); Rankings was later restructured away from a table entirely (see the same Part 3), so this specific header no longer exists, but the underlying "team format shows team-aware content" intent it was closing out is still satisfied by the row content itself.

### Testing performed
Full playthrough at N=27 teams (not evenly divisible by 4): verified room distribution (Semis = 8 teams in 2 rooms of 4, Final = 4 teams in 1 room of 8 players) and the format-aware floor-check message (8 confirmed teams minimum, not 16); a 6-slot lucky-loser scenario resolved correctly at team level even with a pending same-round tie; a 2-team tie-break at score 500 (both above the room's advance cutoff — confirmed both correctly advance once resolved, winner appearing exactly once, no double-counting); an individual-member removal + reserve-individual substitution in Round 1 (no-elim, before any elimination); a whole-team removal + team-reserve substitution (room slot freed then correctly refilled); Scoreboard/Bracket/Rankings/Players/Manage Teams all rendering with zero console errors, confirmed via both scripted assertions and live UI interaction (typed real values into the Admin score inputs, watched the pos/status pill update correctly); Archive save (auto-fired on Final completion), JSON export, and re-open of the completed tournament (team names/members intact in the reopened Rankings/Bracket, rankings image export producing a correctly-sized PNG from the archived snapshot). Then re-ran the FFA regression suite (N=37 full playthrough including a 3-way tie needing exactly 2 clicks, N=43, qual mode N=41) to confirm the shared Part 1 change left FFA byte-for-byte unchanged.

**Bracket's team-name-only compact row (below) has since been replaced with a two-line layout showing member names too — see "Players tab / marker / Rankings / Bracket update", Part 4.**

### Parked — logged, not built
- **Admin-facing manual override of Semis/Final room boundaries** (e.g. "Semis: 3 rooms of 6") — `gamemodeConfig.semisSize`/`finalSize`/`roomSize` are stored as explicit fields specifically so this is cheap to add later; the override UI itself isn't built. **Done 2026-09-12 — see "Semis/Final headcount override"**: scoped to a headcount override (not literal room-count/size control), which turned out to be the organiser's own preferred scope anyway.
- **`'designated-player'` team scoring rule** ("Save your buddy" mode, reading only one member's score) and its selector UI — the `TEAM_SCORING_RULES` registry seam exists (`sum-members` is the only entry), the second rule doesn't. **Done 2026-09-12 — see "Designated-player team scoring rule"**: always the first-listed member (no per-team designation choice this pass).
- Also out of scope per the build spec: 3v3v3, 3v3, Last Man Standing, 1v1; Kings Valley/Swiss/group-knockout schedule logics; admin password protection, cross-device live sync, one-click swap, archive import.

### Noticed during testing — fixed 2026-09-04
The viewer-facing Players tab could show a unit as "Eliminated"/"Out" the moment its current round was *fully scored*, even before the organiser clicked "Next Round" — it inferred "current round" as "the latest round with a score still missing," which didn't distinguish "moved on to the next round" from "just finished being scored." See "Players tab / marker / Rankings / Bracket update (in progress — 2026-09-04)" below for the fix.
