## Players tab / marker / Rankings / Bracket update (done — 2026-09-04)
A four-part follow-up prompt (`260901_prompt_v2_players_markers_rankings_bracketcards.md`), explicitly scoped as four independent changes so that no one part's design would influence another's. All four parts are done and verified.

### Part 1 — Players tab "Eliminated" bug (done)
Root cause: `renderPlayers()` built `playerStatus` by scanning every round's assignment and recording a unit's status only when it found an assignment with a score still missing — the moment every score in a unit's current room was entered, that condition was never true again for them anywhere, so they fell through to the default `null` status and rendered as "Eliminated," regardless of whether the organiser had actually advanced the round.

**Fix**: a new shared `lastAssignedRound(state)` helper (also now used by `computeRankings()`, replacing its local copy of the same computation) finds the index of the last round with any assignment at all. `renderPlayers()` now finds each unit's *latest* assignment round via a single pass (later rounds overwrite earlier ones in a `latestAppearance` map — a plain overwrite is correct here since a unit's last appearance is always their most recent one), then marks a unit "still active" only if that latest round equals `lastAssignedRound()`; anyone whose latest appearance is an earlier round is genuinely eliminated (a later round's assignment exists and doesn't include them). This exactly mirrors `computeRankings()`'s existing elimination rule instead of maintaining a second, subtly different implementation.

**Bug caught during testing (in this fix, not the original code)**: a first draft tried to determine status by re-checking, on each round's assignment, whether the unit appeared in the *next* round's assignment — but only ever *set* status on a match, never cleared a stale one, so a unit who was "still in" for an earlier round (e.g. advanced from Round 1 into Round 2) kept that stale Round-1 status forever, even after later actually being eliminated in Round 3 — the bug this pass was supposed to fix would have been replaced by a worse one (nobody ever showing as eliminated at all). Caught by testing genuine eliminations, not just the repro case; fixed by switching to the "track latest appearance, then compare to the overall last round" approach above.

**Testing performed**: reproduced the exact HANDOFF repro (20-player FFA, one room fully scored, no round advanced) and confirmed the affected players now show their current round/room, not "Eliminated." Ran a full 8-round FFA playthrough (N=20) and checked all 20 players' rendered status against ground truth (who's actually in the last round's assignment) — zero mismatches. Repeated both checks for a 16-team 2v2v2v2 tournament — repro fixed, zero mismatches across all 16 teams through a full playthrough to the Final.

### Part 2 — Lucky-loser/tie-break markers: Bracket only, round of origin (done)
**The rule**: both markers describe something that happened within one specific round's own outcome (survived despite not directly qualifying / needed a manual tie resolution) — by the next round it's irrelevant, so the marker belongs on *that round's own* Bracket card, nowhere else, except Admin's live in-progress views.

**The round-index correction (the easy-to-get-wrong part)**: `T.luckyLosers[ri+1]` is written by `advanceRound()` for the round *arrived into* — it's the record of who was saved *leaving* round `ri`. `buildBracketHtml()` previously read `state.luckyLosers[ri]` while rendering round `ri`'s own card — off by one, showing the wrong round's data (and only barely visible, since Bracket never rendered a lucky-loser-specific visual treatment distinctly from the ★ used elsewhere). Fixed by reading `state.luckyLosers[ri + 1] || []` while rendering round `ri`'s card — that's the record of who was saved *advancing out of* round `ri`, which is exactly the round whose card should show it. Round `ri+1`'s card no longer references `luckyLosers` at all; a unit who arrived as a lucky loser renders there exactly like any other participant, purely on round `ri+1`'s own outcome.

**Lucky-loser badge on Bracket**: a lucky-loser candidate now always renders in the purple "lucky" state on its own round's card, *regardless of* whether `adv` (the rank-based direct-cutoff check) is true — by definition they rank outside the direct cutoff, but they did actually advance via this mechanism, so folding them into plain `elim` would be wrong.

**Tie-break badge on Bracket (new — didn't exist there before)**: a small amber "⚖ TB" badge (`.bracket-tb-badge`, deliberately not purple, so it can render alongside the ★ lucky badge without looking like the same thing or an error state) now appears on round `ri`'s card for every participant belonging to a tie cluster that round actually has an entry for in `state.tieResolutions`. Getting the cluster's *full* membership (not just the resolved subset — a K-way tie's resolution array only ever holds K−1 explicit names, the last is implied) needed `detectTieBreaks(ri, round, state)` to gain a third, optional `state` parameter (defaults to `T`, so all 3 existing call sites are unaffected) — the same "pure builder, accepts live T or an archived snapshot" pattern `computeRankings`/`buildBracketHtml` already use, needed here so the Archive detail view's Bracket can compute this too, not just the live tab.

**Scoreboard** (`renderScoreboard()`): the three-way Advances/★Lucky Loser/Eliminated pill collapses to two states — a unit that's either directly advancing by rank *or* present in `computeLuckyLosers()`'s result now shows the plain "Advances" pill/`adv-row`, indistinguishable from a direct advancer. `computeLuckyLosers()` itself is still called (still needed to correctly determine *who's* advancing) — only the separate ★-badged visual branch is gone.

**Players tab**: the ` ★ LL` pill fragment is removed from `statusHtml`; `status.isLucky` is still computed and carried (harmless, unused).

**Explicitly untouched (2d)**: the Admin seed panel (`T.luckyLosers[ri]`, the round just arrived into — still correct, unaffected by the Bracket fix since it's reading a different index for a different purpose) and `recalcRoom()`'s live in-progress scoring pill (`computeLuckyLosers(ri, round)` called directly, unaffected) — both are legitimate "admin panel during resolution," left exactly as they were, confirmed via `Grep` that neither function was touched by this change.

**Testing performed**: engineered a round with both a 2-way tie among directly-advancing units *and* a lucky-loser scenario in the same round (FFA N=37 round 5: Player24/Player12 tied at 900 — both already ranking inside the cutoff, tie resolved in one click; Player5/Player16 as the 2 lucky losers) — verified Bracket's round-5 card shows Team-equivalent ⚖ TB on the tied pair (green `adv` class, tie is cosmetic-order-only here since both already qualify) and ★ purple on both lucky losers, round-6's card shows plain rows for everyone with no markers at all; Scoreboard (checked both the now-historical round 5 via `goBack()` and the new current round 6) shows the lucky-loser unit merged into "Advances" with zero distinguishing mark and no tie-break marker anywhere; Players tab confirmed to contain no `★ LL` text anywhere; Admin seed panel and live scoring pill confirmed unchanged both by direct testing and by `Grep`-searching for `Lucky Loser` occurrences in the file. Repeated the entire scenario for a 27-team 2v2v2v2 tournament (round 3: Team4/Team18 tied at 900, Team7/Team19/Team2/Team5/Team14/Team17 as the 6 lucky losers) with identical results — screenshot-verified the Bracket visual (green `adv` rows with amber "TB" tags, distinct from the purple ★ lucky rows, both rendering cleanly with no overlap). Zero console errors throughout.

### Part 3 — Rankings: dropped "Relative score," responsive multi-column layout, DOM and PNG (done)
**DOM**: Rankings is no longer a `<table>` — `buildRankingsRows()` now returns block-level `.rk-row` divs wrapped in `.rk-columns` containers (CSS `column-width:340px`, deliberately not a fixed `column-count`, so the browser fits up to ~3 columns when there's room and collapses to fewer as the viewport narrows, with zero JS/breakpoints). `break-inside:avoid` on `.rk-row` keeps a row from splitting across the column break. Still-active and ranked (finalists + eliminated) are two independent `.rk-columns` blocks with their own `.rk-section-title` heading, not one shared container — otherwise a section's own rows could start mid-column while its heading is still finishing the previous column, which reads as broken. The "Relative score" column (which doubled as the finalists' raw score display, per the original single-column design) is gone entirely — a finalist's row now just shows the "🏆 Reached Final" badge with no number; an eliminated unit's row shows its elimination-round badge, no percentage. The old `rk-th-unit`/`ar-rk-th-unit` "Team"/"Player" column-header labels were removed along with the table itself (no longer meaningful without table columns) — the row content (team name + members vs. plain name) already communicates the format. Applies to both the live Rankings tab and Archive detail view via the one shared builder.

**PNG export**: `buildRankingsImageCanvas()` no longer draws `r.value` at all (the badge stays). Rows now lay out into up to 3 newspaper-style columns per section (`pickRankColumnCount(n)`: ≤6 rows → 1 column, ≤14 → 2, else 3 — a row-count-based proxy for the DOM's viewport-width-based collapse, since a fixed-width canvas export has no viewport to auto-collapse against) via a new `layoutRankSection()`/`drawRankRow()` pair. Canvas width grew from 720px to 1100px — needed to fit 3 columns with badges comfortably; still a fixed, reasonably-sized export for pasting into Discord or a website, not blown out arbitrarily. Canvas height is now derived from *rows-per-column*, not total row count — with columns in play, the old total-row-count formula would leave large empty vertical bands. Still-active and ranked render as two separately-laid-out, vertically-stacked sections, same structural split as the DOM.

**Testing performed**: DOM — verified no `<table>` remains, no "Relative score" text/value anywhere, a full 37-player FFA playthrough with a deliberately-tied Final (two finalists tied at the same total) correctly shares rank 7 with no gap-then-8 anomaly; column count measured directly via `getBoundingClientRect()` at three viewport widths — 1400px → 3 columns, 1000px → 2, 800px → 1, confirming the 3→2→1 collapse; Archive detail view confirmed byte-identical row output to the live tab for the same snapshot. PNG — pixel/visual verification (same approach used when this export was first built) via the base64-extraction-to-real-file technique: a 37-player completed tournament (3-column "Final Standings" section, champion row correctly accented, tied rank-7 pair both showing, zero relative-score/value text anywhere); a 16-player tournament still mid-way through its no-elimination rounds (all 16 correctly "Still in tournament," laid out in 3 columns since 16 > the 14-row threshold); a 27-team 2v2v2v2 tournament with both sections present at once (20 still-active teams in 3 columns, 7 round-3-eliminated teams in 2 columns) — team name + member names ("Team1 (Alpha1 & Beta1)") rendering correctly in the single canvas name slot in both sections. Zero console errors across all of the above.

### Part 4 — Bracket room cards: two-line team display (done)
Team-format Bracket rows are now a small stacked block (`.bracket-team-row`) instead of the single-line flex row individual formats still use: **line 1** is team name + status coloring (adv/elim/lucky, plus Part 2's tie-break badge) with the score right-aligned, same visual language the old single-line row used; **line 2** is every member's name, comma-separated, in a smaller muted style below. This needed a genuine restructure, not a CSS tweak to the existing row — `buildBracketHtml()`'s row-rendering loop now branches on `getGamemodeDescriptorFor(state).format.teamSize` (computed once per call, not per row), individual formats taking the exact old `.bracket-player-row` path untouched.

**Long names wrap, they don't truncate or overflow** — `.bracket-team-members` uses `white-space:normal` (the default, stated explicitly) and `overflow-wrap:anywhere` (so one absurdly long single "word" still breaks rather than pushing the layout wider), with no fixed height anywhere in the block, so a team with long names simply makes that row taller.

**A real bug surfaced by this, not present before**: wrapping *only* works if the row has an actual width to wrap against. `.bracket-round-col` was `min-width:200px` with no upper bound — a flex item with no `max-width`/`width` will happily grow past its min-width to fit unwrapped content, so the long-name test initially overflowed horizontally instead of wrapping (confirmed via `scrollWidth` exceeding `clientWidth`). Fixed by making it `width:200px` (not just `min-width`), which gives every room card in every round column a genuinely fixed width to wrap text against — harmless for individual formats (names have always fit comfortably inside 200px) but load-bearing for team formats' member-name line.

**Member count isn't assumed to be 2** — the renderer maps over `team.members` directly (via `unitDisplay(state, key).members`, already null-filtered) rather than indexing by the registered format's `teamSize`, so it renders whatever length the array actually is with no code change needed for a future 3v3v3-style format.

**Testing performed**: a 16-team 2v2v2v2 tournament with one team's two members deliberately renamed to long strings ("Superduperlongfirstname Wontfitonasinglelineatallforsure", "Anotherextremelylongsecondmembernamealsoverylong") — confirmed via direct `getBoundingClientRect()`/`scrollWidth` inspection that the card is a fixed 200px wide, the members text has zero horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth === clientWidth`), wraps to multiple lines (measured height 56px for the members line, ~84px total row height vs. ~42px for an unaffected neighbor in the same room, confirming only the long-named team's row grew); a temporarily-added 3rd member on a different team rendered correctly as "Alpha2, Beta2, ThirdMemberExtra" with no code assuming exactly 2; re-verified the full Part 2 marker set (★ lucky purple, ⚖ TB amber) renders correctly integrated into the two-line layout using the same engineered tie+lucky-loser scenario as Part 2's own testing (the long-named team turned out to be that round's lucky loser — "★ Team1" with `lucky` class, correct wrapped members line, and no markers at all on its arrival-round card, exactly matching Part 2's rule); confirmed FFA (individual format) renders exclusively via the unchanged `.bracket-player-row` path (0 `.bracket-team-row` elements ever appear for a non-team tournament). Zero console errors throughout.

### All four parts done (2026-09-04)
Players tab bug fixed, lucky-loser/tie-break markers relocated to Bracket's round of origin, Rankings restructured to drop the relative-score column and flow into responsive columns (DOM and PNG), and Bracket team cards now show member names via a two-line layout. See `260901_prompt_v2_players_markers_rankings_bracketcards.md` for the original spec.

## Variable elimination-round count (done — 2026-09-05)
`classicEliminationBuildProgression` no longer hardcodes `MAX_R = 8` (which forced exactly 4 elimination rounds — rounds 3–6 — between the no-elim warm-up and Semis, regardless of registration size). The elimination-round count is now **derived from how much cutting is actually needed**, shared across every format via the schedule-logic layer — not part of the 3v3 work, doesn't touch anything format-specific.

**The problem this fixes**: a field just above the Semis floor (e.g. FFA N=17) was forced through 4 "gradual" rounds where 3 of them eliminated nobody at all (target-clamping already absorbed this silently, but it was still 3 pointless rounds shown to the organiser); a very large field got the same 4 rounds regardless, forcing steep, blunt cuts each round.

**The mechanism**: `computeTargets(start, end, numRounds, roomSize)` already did the actual hard part correctly — geometric interpolation of a smooth per-round cut, with the monotonic-clamp and `snapFriendly` edge-case handling from the earlier edge-case-testing pass — and needed no changes. The only thing wrong was that it was always called with `numRounds = 4`. A new `computeElimRoundCount(total, floor)` replaces that hardcoded 4:
```js
var TARGET_ROUND_SURVIVAL_RATIO = 0.80; // ~20% of the field eliminated per round — provisional default
var MAX_ELIM_ROUNDS = 8; // hard cap so a huge field doesn't produce an absurdly long tournament

function computeElimRoundCount(total, floor) {
  if (total <= floor) return 0;
  var ideal = Math.log(floor / total) / Math.log(TARGET_ROUND_SURVIVAL_RATIO);
  return Math.min(Math.ceil(ideal), MAX_ELIM_ROUNDS);
}
```
`Math.ceil` is deliberate — the actual per-round cut ends up at least as gentle as the target ratio, never steeper, except once `MAX_ELIM_ROUNDS` caps it for very large fields (a steeper-than-ideal cut is the accepted tradeoff over an unbounded tournament length). No explicit minimum is needed — `Math.ceil` of any positive value is already ≥1, and the `total <= floor` guard handles "already at or below the Semis floor" the same way `computeTargets`' own fast path does.

**Both constants are provisional defaults, not settled numbers** — named and commented clearly in code so they're easy to find and retune once there's real usage data to react to. `TARGET_ROUND_SURVIVAL_RATIO = 0.80` was chosen because it reproduces today's 4-round result for FFA's most-tested case (N=37) almost exactly (confirmed in the sweep below) — a reasonable starting point, not a derived truth.

**Wiring**: `numElim` is now `computeElimRoundCount(total, SEMIS)` (`total` here is already the post-qual-cut count when qual mode is active, since the qual branch reassigns `total = cfg.qualAdv` earlier in the same function). `MAX_R` — and therefore `semisRound`/`finalRound` — is now computed as `(elimStart - 1) + numElim + 2` instead of the literal `8`. **The subtle part**: `elimStart - 1` is *not* simply "the fixed warm-up round count of 2" substituted in — it generalises correctly to qual mode too, where `elimStart` is `1 + qualRounds` rather than `1 + 2`. Using a literal `2` there would have silently reintroduced a one-round gap/overlap in qual-mode round numbering; using `elimStart - 1` keeps the round numbers contiguous (no gaps, no overlaps) between the warm-up/qual phase, the elimination phase, Semis, and Final, regardless of which phase-length combination produced `elimStart`. The no-elim warm-up round count itself (still fixed at 2) and qualification mode's `qualRounds` (still its own separate config field, default 3) are both unchanged — this pass is specifically about the elimination-round count that comes after either phase.

**A real bug caught by the "grep the whole file" check**: `renderPreview()`'s schedule-preview table hardcoded the Semis row label as `'⚔ Semis (R7)'` — true only because Semis was always round 7 before this change. Fixed to `'⚔ Semis (R'+r.roundNum+')'`, reading the round's actual computed number. Grepped the whole file for any other literal round-number assumption (`MAX_R`, `semisRound`, `"8 rounds"`, `"Round 8"`, `"R7"`, etc.) — this was the only one; every other Semis/Final label already branches on the `isSemis`/`isFinal` flags and prints `round.roundNum` dynamically, not a literal.

**`checkReserveWindow()` verified, not assumed**: it finds "the round whose advancement first eliminates someone" via `round.isNoElim`/`isQual` flags on the rounds array, not a hardcoded round index — confirmed unchanged and working correctly (reserve window stays open through the whole first elimination round while it's current, closes correctly once advanced past it) via a scripted regression, not just code reading.

### Sweep results (actual, from testing — not just target computation)
**FFA (floor = 16 players)**:

| N | Elim rounds | Total rounds | Per-round targets |
|---|---|---|---|
| 17 | 1 | 5 | 16 |
| 20 | 1 | 5 | 16 |
| 25 | 3 | 7 | 22, 19, 16 |
| 30 | 3 | 7 | 24, 20, 16 |
| 37 | 4 | 8 | 30, 24, 20, 16 |
| 43 | 5 | 9 | 35, 29, 24, 19, 16 |
| 50 | 6 | 10 | 41, 34, 28, 23, 19, 16 |
| 60 | 6 | 10 | 48, 39, 31, 25, 20, 16 |
| 80 | 8 (capped) | 12 | 65, 53, 44, 36, 29, 24, 20, 16 |
| 100 | 8 (capped) | 12 | 80, 63, 50, 40, 32, 25, 20, 16 |

**2v2v2v2 (floor = 8 teams)**:

| N (teams) | Elim rounds | Total rounds | Per-round targets |
|---|---|---|---|
| 9 | 1 | 5 | 8 |
| 12 | 2 | 6 | 10, 8 |
| 16 | 4 | 8 | 13, 11, 10, 8 |
| 20 | 5 | 9 | 17, 14, 12, 10, 8 |
| 27 | 6 | 10 | 22, 18, 15, 12, 10, 8 |
| 35 | 7 | 11 | 28, 23, 19, 15, 12, 10, 8 |
| 50 | 8 (capped) | 12 | 40, 32, 25, 20, 16, 13, 10, 8 |

**Previously-tested N values that now produce a different round count** (an intended, visible consequence of this change, not a regression): FFA N=37 stays at 4 elimination rounds/8 total — confirms the `0.80` default was picked well against the most-tested case. FFA N=43 goes from 4 elimination rounds to **5** (9 total rounds instead of 8) — visibly gentler cuts for a field that size. 2v2v2v2 N=27 goes from 4 elimination rounds (fixed, old behaviour) to **6** (10 total rounds instead of 8).

### Testing performed
Ran the sweep above via `classicEliminationBuildProgression()` directly (both formats, all N values from the spec) and confirmed monotonically-decreasing, floor-clamped targets throughout. Full playthroughs to a completed Final: FFA N=37 (8 rounds, Semis on round 7, champion/rankings computed correctly, zero console errors across Admin/Scoreboard/Bracket/Players/Rankings) and 2v2v2v2 N=27 (10 rounds, Semis on round 9, champion/rankings correct, zero console errors) — both driven through real score entry and `advanceRound()`, not just target computation. Separately re-verified, on a fresh FFA N=37 tournament, that an engineered tie-break in the first elimination round still correctly blocks `advanceRound()` and resolves via `resolveTie()`, and that `checkReserveWindow()` still keeps the reserve window open through that same round and closes it correctly afterward — confirming the round-count change didn't disturb tie-break or reserve-window behaviour, both of which are flag-based rather than round-count-based. Verified the `renderPreview()` label fix directly: a 100-player FFA preview now shows "Semis (R11)" instead of a stale "(R7)".

### Explicitly out of scope (unchanged)
The 3v3 format and its room-shape choice; retuning `TARGET_ROUND_SURVIVAL_RATIO`/`MAX_ELIM_ROUNDS` from real usage (a future decision once there's actual data); scaling the no-elim warm-up round count (still fixed at 2).

## Odd-count strategy (None / Bye / Flex) + registering team-3v3 (done — 2026-09-06)
A follow-up prompt (`260901_prompt_3v3_oddcount_strategy.md`), building on the variable-round-count work above. 3v3's natural room shape is strict head-to-head (2 teams/room) — much narrower than FFA (6–8) or 2v2v2v2 (3–4 teams), neither of which has ever actually failed to fit an uncomfortable registration count somewhere in their range. A 2-per-room format doesn't have that slack, so this adds an explicit, organiser-chosen answer (**None** / **Bye** / **Flex**) for what happens when the count doesn't divide evenly, at generation or any later round. Built as five genuinely separate parts, per the spec. All five are done — `team-3v3` is now a real, generatable format.

### Part 1 — `oddCountStrategy` config and setup UI (done)
`GAME_FORMATS[x].supportedOddCountStrategies` (a subset of `'none'|'bye'|'flex'`) and `GAME_FORMATS[x].idealRoomSize` are new, additive fields — a format that doesn't declare them (FFA, 2v2v2v2) gets no dropdown and no behaviour change. New `ODD_COUNT_STRATEGIES` label registry, mirroring `TEAM_SCORING_RULES`'s pattern. `toggleFormatFields()` rebuilds the "Odd-count strategy" dropdown's options from the selected format's own declared list (not a fixed 3-option `<select>`) every time the format changes, showing the field only when that list has more than one entry, defaulting to "None." `T.gamemodeConfig.oddCountStrategy` is set at generation time only for a format that declares the dropdown. Persists through `saveState()`/`loadState()`, restored *after* `toggleFormatFields()` rebuilds the options (otherwise the rebuild clobbers it back to the default).

**Testing**: registered a temporary fake 3-strategy format at the console (not `team-3v3` yet — that's Part 5) — dropdown show/hide, option population, selection→`gamemodeConfig` wiring, and the save/restore round-trip all verified directly; confirmed FFA/2v2v2v2 stay completely unaffected even with the fake format also registered.

### Part 2 — Flex (done)
New shared `deriveRoomSize(format, oddCountStrategy)`: a format with a static `defaultRoomSize` (FFA, 2v2v2v2) uses it unchanged; a format that instead declares `idealRoomSize` derives `roomSize` fresh every generation from `idealRoomSize` + the chosen strategy. For `'flex'`: `{min: ideal, max: ideal+1, ideal}`. `proceedGenerateSchedule()` now computes `oddCountStrategy` *before* `roomSize` (the derivation needs it) and calls `deriveRoomSize()` instead of always reading `defaultRoomSize`.

**Testing**: registered a temporary fake head-to-head format (`idealRoomSize:2`, no `defaultRoomSize` — `team-3v3`'s eventual shape) and ran a full scripted playthrough at N=13 (odd) through the real `distributeRooms`/`computeTargets`/`snapFriendly`/`advanceRound()` pipeline — rounds 1–3 correctly produced one 3-team room each, reached the Final, zero console errors. Confirmed an even count (N=16) stays pure 2-per-room, no unnecessary 3-team rooms.

### Part 3 — None (done)
`deriveRoomSize()`'s non-flex branch already returns the fixed-point `{min:ideal,max:ideal,ideal}` shape Bye also needs (see Part 4) — traced (and scripted) `snapFriendly`/`distributeRooms`/`computeTargets` against it directly rather than assuming: every even input stays untouched, `distributeRooms` produces exclusively `idealRoomSize`-sized rooms for every N tested, `computeTargets` sequences stay entirely on multiples of `idealRoomSize` — no new logic needed there, confirming the spec's claim.

**Generation-time validation**: refuses to generate (same inline-error style as the confirmed-count floor check) when the count entering the elimination phase (`cfg.qualAdv` if combined with qual mode, else `cfg.n`) isn't an exact multiple of `idealRoomSize`.

**Mid-tournament withdrawal**: `removeTeam()` blocks removing a team still live in the current round if doing so would leave a live count that isn't a multiple of `idealRoomSize`, with an explanatory alert. Deliberately a blanket "the live count this round must stay a multiple of idealRoomSize" rule for *any* round type, rather than trying to predict whether a specific elimination round's `advPerRoom`/lucky-loser cushion would happen to absorb the oddness (which depends on scores that may not be final yet) — matches "None" mode's own stated philosophy of refusing rather than guessing. **Consequence worth knowing**: since a live "None" round's count is always even by construction, removing exactly one team always makes it odd — so a live team can never be removed alone; the organiser must add a replacement reserve *first* (temporarily odd), then remove the old team (back to even). Verified this exact workaround succeeds, and that removing an already-eliminated team is always allowed (doesn't touch any future room).

**Known gap, not fixed (adjacent to, not part of, Part 3's scope)**: `addTeamReserve()`'s existing room-cap confirm dialog ("...will create a 3-team room. Proceed?") still lets an organiser explicitly click through and create an over-cap room even under "None" — reproduced directly. Tightening that touches a function shared by every format and wasn't asked for here, so left as-is. **Fixed 2026-09-11 — see "Small flagged-gap fixes."**

### Part 4 — Bye (done)
The substantial part — a genuinely new mechanic, not a room-size variation. Room size is identical to Part 3's (fixed `{min:ideal,max:ideal,ideal}`, same `deriveRoomSize()` branch).

**The mechanism, precisely**: immediately before distributing a round's incoming pool into rooms, if the pool is odd, the single highest-seeded unit (first in whatever list feeds that round's distribution) is pulled out as that round's bye — recorded in a new `T.byes[ri]` (mirroring `T.luckyLosers`'s per-round-array convention; `null` = no bye that round, **not** `undefined` — see the JSON round-trip bug below) — and represented as a real entry in `T.assignments[ri]` with `room: null` (not simply omitted), specifically *because* the generic "present in round ri's assignment" logic that drives Players-tab/Rankings active-detection only cares about presence, not room — giving the bye unit a `room:null` entry is what lets that logic treat them as correctly still-active with zero special-casing, exactly as the spec predicted (verified directly, not assumed — see Testing). The remaining even pool distributes into rooms normally. `T.byes[ri]` is indexed by the round the bye entry itself appears in (not `ri+1`, unlike `T.luckyLosers`'s established off-by-one convention) — deliberately, to avoid re-introducing that exact class of confusion; Bracket/Scoreboard/Admin all read `state.byes[ri]` while rendering round `ri`'s own card, no adjustment needed anywhere.

**Seeding forward**: the bye recipient is unshifted to the front of the next round's seeding pool, treated as a clean 1st-place finisher — a fairness convention, not a derived fact, exactly as the spec flags it; a different rule (e.g. seeding them at their *last actual* rank) would be a legitimate alternative if the organiser prefers one after seeing this in practice.

**Why generation-time computation needed changes too (not just live seeding) — the part that was easy to get wrong**: `classicEliminationBuildProgression` already calls `distributeRooms` directly with a headcount that can be odd (a raw registration count for Round 1, or an elimination round's own incoming target) — left unguarded, this would put a genuinely broken room of size 1 straight into the generated schedule, before any live bye logic ever runs. New `distributeRoomsWithBye(n, roomSize, oddCountStrategy)` wraps every such call site (no-elim loop, qual loop, elimination loop), reserving exactly `n % idealRoomSize` as that round's projected `byeCount` and distributing only the clean remainder. **The subtler bug this surfaced**: an elimination round's own `advPerRoom`/`luckyCount` are computed to reconstruct its *own* smooth advancement target (`pOut`) exactly from room-based selection alone — if that round *also* has an incoming bye recipient (who advances automatically, outside room-based selection), leaving the target unadjusted means the next round silently receives one *more* unit than its own generation-time projection expects. Hand-traced this: left unfixed, the +1 surplus never resolves on its own — it recurs identically every subsequent round, cascading all the way through Semis and corrupting the Final. Fixed by reducing a bye-having round's own room-based target by its `byeCount` (`advTarget = pOut - byeCount`) before computing `advPerRoom`/`luckyCount`, so room-based selection *plus* the automatically-advancing bye recipient reconstructs the original, unmodified `pOut` — this is what makes the cascade self-terminate the moment a round's own projected target is already clean (normally within a round or two of whatever triggered the original oddness). Semis' own incoming pool (`SEMIS = 2×idealRoomSize`) is provably always even regardless — proved by induction from this same fix, then verified directly (not just assumed, per the spec's explicit ask) via a full playthrough reaching the Final with exactly `idealRoomSize` teams and no bye ever recorded there.

**A real, more serious bug found only through adversarial testing (removal + Bye), fixed as a safety net**: the analytical proof above assumes no *permanent* change to the live population — a genuine mid-tournament team removal (Bye mode has no removal-time guard the way "None" mode's Part 3 does) permanently shrinks the pool in a way the frozen generation-time targets can't retroactively account for, and the resulting oddness does **not** self-resolve — it recurs every round indefinitely (verified: the same team got 6 consecutive byes after one mid-tournament removal). The first implementation let this cascade reach the Final-transition guard and silently seed a **3-team Final** (skipping the bye-peel there since a Final structurally can't take one, but not catching what that meant for the leftover odd unit). Fixed by turning that guard into an explicit block: if the pool heading into the Final is ever unexpectedly odd, `advanceRound()` now refuses to advance with a clear alert ("...usually means a team was removed mid-tournament; check Manage Teams...") rather than either corrupting the Final or inventing a nonsensical "Final bye." Re-tested the same removal scenario — now stops cleanly at the round before Final instead of producing a broken bracket.

**Two more real bugs, both plain display bugs, not logic bugs**: (1) `roomLabel(null)` (called on a bye entry's `room:null`) silently produces the single character `"@"` instead of throwing — found in both the Room-Assignments seed panel and the Players tab's "Room X" line; both now check for `room === null` first and render "BYE" instead. (2) `T.byes` is serialized via `JSON.stringify` (in `saveState()` and Archive snapshots) — a JSON round-trip silently turns an `undefined` array element into `null`, so the very first draft (which used `undefined` as the "no bye" sentinel) would have made **every** round falsely show a bye card after any page reload or archive reopen. Switched the sentinel to `null` everywhere (`T.byes[i] != null` — a loose-equality check, so it still correctly treats a never-populated/backward-compatible array's `undefined` holes as "no bye" too) and verified the exact failure mode directly: saved mid-tournament, reloaded the page, confirmed the bye-card count in Bracket stayed correct (3, matching the actual bye rounds) rather than appearing on every round.

**Rendering**: Bracket gets a new `.bracket-bye-card` (amber dashed border, matching the visual language of other amber markers) rendered after a round's real room cards, no room card generated for the bye unit. Scoreboard gets an analogous `.sb-bye-card`, rendered outside the room grid, "Advances" pill, no score. **Admin's own live round view and seed panel were also updated, though not explicitly itemized in the spec's rendering checklist** — the spec's own framing ("every place that currently assumes every advancing unit came from a scored room") clearly extends to the organiser's primary working view, and leaving it silently blank (or broken, per the `roomLabel(null)` bug above) there would be a real, organiser-visible gap.

**Testing performed**: hand-traced (and then scripted, matching exactly) a full N=13 `team-3v3`-shaped playthrough — rounds 1–3 (roundNum) each correctly reserved 1 bye for the same team (the mathematically expected, self-resolving pattern once oddness starts), rounds 4+ clean, reached the Final with exactly 2 teams and zero byes, champion computed correctly, zero console errors. A separate scenario (N=12, even — no bye initially, then a live mid-round team removal) confirmed a *different* team correctly receives the resulting bye (not hardcoded to always pick the same unit — it's genuinely "whoever's first in the live pool at that moment"), and separately triggered + confirmed the new Final-transition safety block described above. Verified directly (not assumed): Bracket/Scoreboard/Admin/Seed-panel all show a correctly-labelled BYE card for the exact right unit and round; Players tab shows the bye unit as active with a "BYE — advances automatically" line, never "Eliminated"; `computeRankings()` correctly never classifies a bye unit as eliminated (traced the code path directly — a bye entry's presence in the *next* round's assignment is what the existing generic "present in ri, absent in ri+1" check already keys off, so it needed no special-casing, confirming the spec's claim); Archive save/reopen preserves `T.byes` through the snapshot and renders identically via the same shared `buildBracketHtml()` builder the live tab uses; the null-vs-undefined JSON round-trip bug reproduced and then re-verified fixed via an actual `saveState()` → page reload → re-render cycle.

**Not tested / lightly reasoned through only**: a bye occurring on the qualification table's own last-round cutoff transition (qual mode + Bye combined) — the code path is designed to handle it generically (the bye-carryover-unshift in `advanceRound()` runs after *either* branch of `classicEliminationComputeAdvancement`, qual-cutoff or room-based, uniformly), but wasn't exercised by an actual qual+Bye playthrough; flagging rather than claiming it's verified.

### Part 5 — Register `team-3v3` (done)
`GAME_FORMATS['team-3v3']` is now real: `{ label:'3v3', unitLabel:'Team', unitLabelPlural:'Teams', teamSize:3, idealRoomSize:2, supportedOddCountStrategies:['none','bye','flex'] }`, no `defaultRoomSize` — exactly the shape Parts 2–4 tested against temporary fake formats throughout. Setup dropdown's `<option value="team-3v3">` is no longer `disabled`.

**The one real fix this part needed**: `proceedGenerateSchedule()`'s confirmed-count floor check read `selectedFormat.defaultRoomSize.ideal` unconditionally — reproduced the exact crash Parts 2–4 had been dodging (by giving their temporary test formats a `defaultRoomSize` too, or bypassing `proceedGenerateSchedule()` entirely) the moment `team-3v3` was registered for real and actually driven through the floor check. Fixed with a one-line branch (`selectedFormat.defaultRoomSize ? selectedFormat.defaultRoomSize.ideal : selectedFormat.idealRoomSize`) — the two are equivalent for this purpose since `deriveRoomSize()`'s `roomSize.ideal` is always exactly `idealRoomSize` regardless of which of None/Bye/Flex ends up chosen, so this needed no format-specific special-casing, just the fallback. Verified both the below-floor message and the exact-floor-boundary case produce correct, format-aware text for `team-3v3` ("...at least 4 confirmed teams (Semis is fixed at 4 teams in 2 rooms of 2)..."), and re-verified FFA/2v2v2v2's own floor messages are byte-identical to before this change.

**Also fixed (adjacent, not explicitly itemized, but directly affects whether `team-3v3` is actually usable)**: `toggleFormatFields()`'s roster/reserve hint text and placeholders hardcoded a 2-member example line (`TeamName | Player1 | Player2`) regardless of format — harmless coincidence for 2v2v2v2 (`teamSize:2`) but actively misleading for `team-3v3` (`teamSize:3`), since `parseTeamLines()` expects exactly `teamSize` member segments per line. Rebuilt the hint/placeholder generation from `format.teamSize` directly (a loop building `Player1 | Player2 | ...`) instead of the literal 2-item string, so it's correct for any future `teamSize` too, not just 3. Verified 2v2v2v2's hint text is unchanged and `team-3v3`'s now correctly reads "TeamName | Player1 | Player2 | Player3".

**Testing performed**: drove all three odd-count strategies through the actual UI-facing `proceedGenerateSchedule()` path (not manual state construction, unlike Parts 2–4's necessarily-improvised testing) — None at N=12 (even), Bye at N=13 (odd, round 1 correctly gets a bye), Flex at N=13 (odd, rooms correctly flex to 3 where needed) — all three reached a complete Final with **Semis = 4 teams / Final = 2 teams identically across all three modes**, confirming the spec's explicit ask. Verified None's odd-count generation-time refusal fires correctly through the real UI (N=13 with "None" selected → clean inline error, no crash). Verified the floor-check fix directly against team-3v3 (N=3, below the 4-team floor) and re-confirmed FFA/2v2v2v2 unaffected. Visually confirmed the setup panel (Game Format = "3v3", Odd Count Strategy dropdown populated and visible, correct 3-member roster hint/placeholder) via a screenshot. Zero console errors throughout, including a clean localStorage-cleared reload.

### All five parts done (2026-09-06)
`oddCountStrategy` config + setup UI, Flex, None (+ generation-time validation and mid-tournament withdrawal guard), Bye (+ generation-time room/target math, live seeding, rendering across Bracket/Scoreboard/Admin/Players/Rankings/Archive, and two real bugs — a Final-corrupting cascade from mid-tournament removal, and a JSON-round-trip sentinel bug), and `team-3v3` itself now fully registered and generating real tournaments end-to-end under all three strategies. See `260901_prompt_3v3_oddcount_strategy.md` for the original spec.

## Room cap correction (done — 2026-09-07)
While reviewing the `team-3v3` build, a question came up about whether Flex mode's `idealRoomSize + 1` fallback could ever exceed the game's actual per-room limit. **The organiser has now confirmed the real number: the game's hard technical cap is 10 players per room — not 8.** 8 is FFA's own *competitively preferred* room size (`GAME_FORMATS['ffa-individual'].defaultRoomSize.max`), a design choice independent of the technical ceiling, and not necessarily shared by every future format — 2v2v2v2 and 4v4 both happen to land at 8 too, but that's their own design choice, not evidence of a shared technical limit. `team-3v3`'s Flex fallback (3 teams × 3 players = 9) was never actually over the real line — but nothing in the code had ever checked that; it worked out by luck (the arithmetic happened to fit), not because any guard caught it. That's the actual bug here — a documentation/understanding error, not a functional one, with 3v3v3 (9 players, the same shape) coming right behind it.

**Corrected throughout**: `distributeRooms`'s section comment (previously read "the max is treated as the hard constraint (the game itself doesn't support more per room)") now explicitly distinguishes a format's own configured `roomSize.max` (a design preference) from the game's real technical limit. HANDOFF's own N=17 dead-zone note (under "Known issues / things to revisit") and the "Edge case testing" section's historical bug-fix description both previously described the `[6,6,5]`/8-player-ceiling logic as enforcing "the game's hard per-room cap" — both corrected to say what's actually true: FFA's own preferred ceiling, kept for competitive-design reasons (not reverted to `[9,8]` — see below), not a technical necessity.

**New runtime safety net**: `HARD_ROOM_PLAYER_CAP = 10` (a clearly-named, easy-to-find global constant) plus `validateRoomCap(rounds, format)`, called once in `proceedGenerateSchedule()` right after `buildProgression()` returns — checks every room of every round the whole generated schedule would actually produce (`room-unit-count × (format.teamSize || 1)`) against the real number, for any format, current or future. A violation refuses to generate with a clear, specific inline error (same pattern as the other generation-time refusals) rather than ever letting a broken schedule reach the preview — verified this directly by registering a temporary fake over-cap format (4-player teams, 3-team Flex fallback = 16 players/room) and confirming it's refused cleanly, with the preview panel never showing. This should be structurally impossible to trigger with any format registered today — confirmed via a full regression sweep (FFA N=37, 2v2v2v2 N=27, `team-3v3` under all three odd-count strategies) that none of them come anywhere close, let alone violate it.

**`team-3v3` Flex re-verified as a checked fact, not an assumption**: generated an actual `team-3v3` Flex-mode schedule and confirmed directly (not just by arithmetic in a comment) that its 3-team rooms (9 players) pass `validateRoomCap()` cleanly — `9 ≤ 10`, with real margin to spare.

**FFA's `[6,6,5]` N=17 fallback: kept as-is, deliberately** — now that a 9-player room is confirmed technically fine, `[6,6,5]` (rather than `[9,8]`) is a genuine competitive-preference choice (staying inside FFA's own 6–8 range), not a workaround for a technical limit that turns out not to apply. No functional change here — see the corrected HANDOFF note above for why.

**Testing performed**: regression sweep across FFA (N=37), 2v2v2v2 (N=27), and `team-3v3` under None (N=12), Bye (N=13), and Flex (N=13) — recorded each mode's actual max room-player-count (FFA/2v2v2v2 top out at 8, `team-3v3` None/Bye at 6, `team-3v3` Flex at 9), all well clear of the real 10-player cap, zero false positives. Registered a temporary fake format specifically shaped to violate the cap and confirmed `validateRoomCap()` catches it with a clear, specific error and no broken schedule ever reaching the preview panel. Zero console errors throughout, including a clean reload.

## 3v3v3 team format (done — 2026-09-08)
`team-3v3v3` — 3 teams of 3 per room — is now a real, generatable `GAME_FORMATS` entry (`260901_prompt_3v3v3.md`, planned the prior session in `260901_plan_3v3v3.md`). Registration itself was exactly as lightweight as the spec predicted: `{teamSize:3, defaultRoomSize:{min:2,max:3,ideal:3}}`, the same static-range pattern `team-2v2v2v2` already uses (not `team-3v3`'s `idealRoomSize`/`oddCountStrategy` mechanism — 3v3v3's room genuinely has the same min–max flex slack FFA/2v2v2v2 have, unlike 3v3's strict head-to-head shape). Flex only goes *down* (`min:2`, not `max:4`) — a hard requirement, not a style choice: `ideal:3` teams is 9 players; flexing up to 4 teams would be 12, over `HARD_ROOM_PLAYER_CAP` (10) outright.

**But verification did turn up something real — a genuine bug, not just confirmation.** `classicEliminationBuildProgression`'s Semis construction computed `semisAdv = FINAL / semisRooms.length` — a bare division, not the floor+remainder split every regular elimination round already uses. This silently assumed `FINAL` (= `roomSize.ideal`) always splits evenly across Semis' (always exactly 2) rooms — true for every format built before this one only because their `roomSize.ideal` (FFA 8, 2v2v2v2 4, `team-3v3` 2) happened to be even. `team-3v3v3`'s `ideal:3` is the first odd one: `FINAL(3) / 2 = 1.5`, a non-integer that JavaScript's `Math.min(1.5, scored.length)` + a `for (i=0; i<thisAdv; i++)` loop silently let **2** teams through per room (not the intended fractional 1.5) — 4 teams reaching the Final instead of 3. Caught by exactly the check the spec asked for ("verify directly that a 3-team final plays and scores correctly... rather than assuming the existing generic logic definitely holds") — a scripted playthrough showed `finalTeamCountActual: 4` against an expected 3, not by reading the code and assuming it was fine.

**The fix**: Semis' `advPerRoom`/`luckyCount` are now computed with the exact same `Math.floor(FINAL / semisRooms.length)` / `FINAL % semisRooms.length` split the regular elimination-round loop already uses, instead of a bare division with `luckyCount` hardcoded to `0`. This reuses the already-built, already-tested lucky-loser mechanism to resolve the remainder — for `team-3v3v3`: 1 direct advancer per Semis room (2 total) + 1 lucky-loser slot awarded to whichever 2nd-place team across both rooms has the best relative score = 3 total, correctly. Verified this generalizes correctly, not just for the new case: for every format with an even `roomSize.ideal`, `FINAL % 2` is always `0`, so `luckyCount` computes to exactly `0` and `advPerRoom` to exactly the old value — confirmed byte-identical Semis behaviour (`semisLuckyCount:0`, same `advPerRoom`) for FFA, `team-2v2v2v2`, and `team-3v3` in the same regression pass that caught the bug for `team-3v3v3`. This is a genuinely new, visible behaviour for `team-3v3v3` specifically (a Semis round can now show a ★ lucky-loser marker, something no prior format's Semis round has ever done, since it was never structurally needed before) — rendering it required no new code, since Bracket/Scoreboard/Admin's room views, and `computeLuckyLosers()`/`classicEliminationComputeAdvancement()`, all already branch on `round.luckyCount` generically with no `isSemis` special-casing to work around.

**Genericity claims — verified, not re-audited, exactly as the spec asked**: Manage Teams renders 3 member rows per team (checked by counting `promptRenameMember` occurrences against `teamCount × 3`, not just eyeballing); two simultaneous vacant member slots on one team filled correctly, one from an individual reserve and one via walk-up; score entry stacks exactly 3 inputs per team per room position; Bracket's two-line team card renders and wraps 3 member names with no code changes; Rankings and the PNG export join 3 names sensibly ("M1_7 & M2_7 & M3_7"); the roster/reserve hint and placeholder auto-generated a correct 3-segment example with zero new code (confirmed still using the `format.teamSize`-driven generator built during `team-3v3`'s Part 5). None of these needed any fix — the only real gap this build found was the Semis arithmetic above.

**Room-cap safety net**: confirmed `team-3v3v3`'s real numbers (3 teams × 3 players = 9) pass `validateRoomCap()` cleanly, then proved the guard actually guards (not just "the number happens to be fine") by registering a deliberate throwaway over-cap format (`teamSize:4` on the same `{min:2,max:3,ideal:3}` shape → 12 players/room) and confirming it's refused with a clear, specific error — removed immediately after.

**Round-count sweep at the new floor (6 teams)**: N = 7, 9, 12, 18, 25, 35 — Semis/Final land at 6/3 in every case; elimination-round count grows gradually (1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 8-capped) with no wasted zero-elimination rounds near the floor and no runaway length at the top end (capped by the existing shared `MAX_ELIM_ROUNDS=8`).

| N (teams) | Elim rounds | Total rounds | Per-round targets |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7 | 1 | 5 | 6 |
| 9 | 2 | 6 | 7, 6 |
| 12 | 4 | 8 | 10, 8, 7, 6 |
| 18 | 5 | 9 | 14, 12, 9, 7, 6 |
| 25 | 7 | 11 | 20, 17, 14, 11, 9, 7, 6 |
| 35 | 8 (capped) | 12 | 28, 23, 18, 14, 12, 9, 7, 6 |

**Testing performed**: full playthrough at N=19 (deliberately awkward — forces the 3→2 team room flex; confirmed directly, not assumed, via `rooms.includes(2)` across multiple rounds) through the real `proceedGenerateSchedule()` UI path (not manual state construction), reaching a completed 3-team Final that played and scored correctly across 3 games (verified the actual per-game and total values against hand-computed expectations, not just "a champion got picked"), zero console errors. Bracket verified visually (screenshot) — Semis showed the expected ★ lucky-loser marker on exactly the right team, Final column showed exactly the 3 correct teams. Rankings and Archive save/reopen (byte-identical Bracket/Rankings re-render via the shared builders) both verified. Confirmed `team-3v3v3` never touches the Bye/odd-count-strategy machinery at all (no dropdown shown, `T.byes` stays all-`null`, `idealRoomSize`/`supportedOddCountStrategies` both `undefined` on the registry entry) — it's a pure `defaultRoomSize` format like FFA/2v2v2v2. Re-ran full playthroughs for FFA (N=37), `team-2v2v2v2` (N=27), and `team-3v3` (N=12) in the same regression pass that caught and verified the Semis fix — all three byte-identical to their pre-existing behaviour (`semisLuckyCount:0`, correct Final team counts, champions computed correctly). Zero console errors throughout, including a clean reload.

**Answering the spec's own question**: registration was exactly as lightweight as predicted — but verification was not a formality this time. The Semis/Final case specifically (Part 2 of the spec) is what caught a real, previously-invisible bug that had been silently correct for three formats purely by coincidence (their `roomSize.ideal` all happening to be even). This is a concrete data point in favour of the spec's own instruction to verify the Semis/Final case directly for every new format rather than assuming the generic logic holds — it would not have been caught by re-reading the code, only by an actual 3-team-Final playthrough.
