## Phase composability refactor (done — 2026-09-09)
`260902_prompt_phase_composability_refactor.md`. A **pure refactor — no new user-facing capability, no UI change**. Every tournament structure the organiser is planning for 1v1 (group-stage-into-knockout, Swiss-into-knockout, single/double-elimination) decomposes into two independent questions: how the field gets pooled/narrowed before the real bracket starts, and what shape the elimination bracket itself is. `classicEliminationBuildProgression` used to answer both with one tangled function and a binary `cfg.qual` flag standing in for what should be a real, extensible pooling choice. This splits it into two clean, independently-registered pieces — exactly the two pooling options and one bracket option that already existed, nothing new — creating the seam a future pooling type or bracket type plugs into.

**The split**:
- **`POOLING_PHASES`** (`'qual-table'` → `qualTablePoolingPhase`, `'none'` → `noElimWarmupPoolingPhase`) — each a pure relocation of the old function's steps 1/2, now two separate functions instead of one with an `if (cfg.qual)` branch. Takes `(cfg, descriptor)`, returns `{ rounds, seedTotal, nextRoundNum }` — the pooling rounds it built, how many units are actually entering the bracket phase, and which round number the bracket phase should continue numbering from.
- **`BRACKET_PHASES`** (`'classic-elimination'` → `classicEliminationBracketPhase`) — a pure relocation of the old steps 3–5 (elimination rounds, Semis, Final — including the round-count derivation and the Semis lucky-loser split from the `team-3v3v3` fix, both untouched). Takes `(seedTotal, startRoundNum, descriptor)`, returns just the round-objects array, numbered starting at `startRoundNum` instead of assuming round 1.
- **The orchestrator** — `classicEliminationBuildProgression` (name kept; still what `SCHEDULE_LOGICS['classic-elimination'].buildProgression` points to) is now a 4-line composition: look up `gamemodeConfig.poolingPhase`/`.bracketPhase`, call one pooling phase then one bracket phase with the handoff, concatenate.

**Two new `gamemodeConfig` fields**, populated at generation time the same way `roomSize`/`qualRounds`/`semisSize`/`finalSize` already are: `poolingPhase` (`cfg.qual ? 'qual-table' : 'none'` — derived from the existing qual checkbox, not a new organiser-facing choice) and `bracketPhase` (hardcoded `'classic-elimination'` for every format today — a real, populated field, not yet a real choice, since only one bracket type exists).

**One deliberate signature deviation from the spec's own example, flagged rather than silently decided**: the spec's Part 1/3 both describe the bracket phase as `(seedTotal, startRoundNum, descriptor)` with no `cfg`. But the Final round's construction genuinely needs `cfg.finalsGames` (the organiser's chosen games-per-final). Rather than threading `cfg` into a function whose stated contract excludes it, `gamemodeConfig.finalsGames` is now also populated at generation time (from `cfg.finalsGames`, alongside the other tunables), and `classicEliminationBracketPhase` reads `descriptor.config.finalsGames` instead — keeping the exact 3-arg signature the spec specifies in both places it's mentioned, at the cost of one small additional config field. Flagging this since it wasn't explicitly anticipated by the spec.

**A real, unrelated bug noticed in passing, not fixed (out of this refactor's scope)**: constructing a qual+Bye test case for `team-3v3` revealed that `proceedGenerateSchedule()`'s `qualAdv` clamp (`Math.max(cfg.qualAdv, 16)`) is a hardcoded FFA-specific floor applied to every format — for a team format (whose real floor might be 4, 6, or 8 teams), this would silently force `qualAdv` up to 16 *teams* regardless of what the organiser actually wanted to advance from qualification. This lives entirely in `proceedGenerateSchedule()`, not in `classicEliminationBuildProgression` (this refactor's whole scope), so it's noted here for future attention rather than fixed — the qual+Bye testing below deliberately bypassed this clamp to actually exercise the combination the spec asked for, rather than let the clamp silently sanitize the odd `qualAdv` away.

**A testing-methodology pitfall hit and worked around, worth recording so it doesn't cost time again**: the first exhaustive-diff attempt reloaded the same `localhost` URL after editing the file and got a false "byte-identical" pass — the static dev server / browser pane cached the old script on a same-URL reload despite `force:true`, so that first comparison was silently old-code-against-old-code, not old-against-new. Caught by checking `classicEliminationBuildProgression.toString()` and seeing the pre-refactor function body still running. Fixed for good by reconstructing the exact pre-refactor function verbatim (from the `Read` output captured before editing) as a same-page `window.__OLD_fn`, then diffing it against the live (confirmed-fresh) `classicEliminationBuildProgression` within the *same* page load — eliminates any reload/caching ambiguity entirely. A subsequent plain reload (no cache-busting needed) then behaved correctly, so this looks like a one-off rather than a persistent environment quirk, but the "diff old-vs-new within one page load" technique is the more robust approach regardless and worth reusing for any future refactor requiring exhaustive before/after comparison.

**Testing performed — exhaustive, per the spec's explicit ask, not a sample**: byte-diffed full `rounds` array output (every field) between the reconstructed pre-refactor function and the new orchestrator, run side-by-side in the same page load, for all 10 required cases — FFA non-qual and qual at N=37 and N=43 (4 cases), `team-2v2v2v2` N=27, `team-3v3` under None/Bye/Flex, `team-3v3v3` N=19, and **`team-3v3` with qual mode actually combined with Bye mode** (N=20, `qualAdv=13` deliberately odd to force a bye right at the qual→elimination handoff) — all 10 **byte-identical**, zero deviation in any field. Separately ran full UI-driven playthroughs (`proceedGenerateSchedule()` → `startTournament()` → real score entry → `advanceRound()` → Final) for all 10 cases plus regression coverage on `team2222`/`team3v3v3`, including the previously-under-tested qual+Bye combination reaching a fully-scored Final with a champion computed and the Bracket rendering correctly — confirmed `gamemodeConfig.poolingPhase`/`.bracketPhase`/`.finalsGames` populate correctly end-to-end, not just in the isolated function. Re-verified the `team-3v3v3` Semis fix (`semisLuckyCount:1`, `finalPlayers:3`) survived the refactor intact. Zero console errors throughout, including a clean reload.

**The seam this creates**: a future pooling type (group stage, Swiss) registers a new `(cfg, descriptor) -> {rounds, seedTotal, nextRoundNum}` function in `POOLING_PHASES`; a future bracket type (true single-elimination, eventually double-elimination) registers a new `(seedTotal, startRoundNum, descriptor) -> rounds` function in `BRACKET_PHASES`. Neither exists yet — deliberately out of scope for this pass, per the spec — and how an organiser would eventually choose between more than one of each, once they exist, is an open product question for whichever prompt builds the first second-option.

---

## True single-elimination bracket phase + registering 1v1 (done — 2026-09-09)
`260902_prompt_single_elimination_1v1.md`. The first thing to actually plug into the seam the phase-composability refactor created — a genuinely new `BRACKET_PHASES` entry, `individual-1v1` as a genuinely new (non-team) `GAME_FORMATS` entry, and `'single-elimination'` as a real second option in the "Schedule logic" dropdown (present but permanently single-option since the original gamemode-architecture refactor).

### What's reused vs. genuinely new
**Genuinely new**: `singleEliminationBracketPhase(seedTotal, startRoundNum, descriptor)` — every round the same shape (`advPerRoom:1, luckyCount:0`, no `isSemis` distinction, no lucky-loser buffer ever), and bracket-size math that owes nothing to `classic-elimination`'s (`TARGET_ROUND_SURVIVAL_RATIO`/`computeElimRoundCount`/`computeTargets` don't apply at all). Two hard assertions (`roomSize.ideal !== 2` and `oddCountStrategy === 'flex'`) `throw` rather than silently misbehave — single elimination is inherently head-to-head, and a 3-unit room isn't single elimination — per the spec's explicit "assert this" instruction; both verified to actually fire, not just written and trusted.

**Reused entirely, unchanged**: `distributeRoomsWithBye`/`T.byes`/the bye-seeding-forward convention (Part 4 — see below), `POOLING_PHASES`/the whole pooling-phase mechanism, `validateRoomCap`, and — after a rename described below — the orchestrator and the room-based advancement computation. `roomBasedComputeAdvancement` in particular needed zero new logic: it already only reads `round.isNoElim`/`.advPerRoom`/`.luckyCount`/`.isQual` generically, so single-elimination's `advPerRoom:1, luckyCount:0` rounds ("top 1 of the room advances, no lucky-loser candidate is ever collected since luckyCount is never > 0") just fall out of the existing logic with no branching added.

**A rename, flagged as a judgment call**: `classicEliminationBuildProgression`/`classicEliminationComputeAdvancement` are now `composedBuildProgression`/`roomBasedComputeAdvancement`. Both were already fully generic after the phase-composability refactor (the first is a 4-line pooling+bracket composition, the second reads only generic round flags) — keeping the "classicElimination" name once a *second* schedule logic (`single-elimination`) genuinely reuses them verbatim would have been actively misleading to a future reader, not just imprecise. Mechanical rename via `sed` across the whole file (declarations, both `SCHEDULE_LOGICS` entries, ~10 comment references), verified with a `.toString()` check post-rename that no stale reference survived.

### Bracket-size math — verified, not just asserted
The field is conceptually rounded up to the next power of 2 (`nextPowerOf2AndRounds()` — pure integer doubling, deliberately not `Math.log2`/`Math.pow`, which can land a hair off an exact power of 2 on some inputs due to floating-point imprecision); the shortfall becomes byes. Rather than concentrating every needed bye into round 1 (the traditional seeded-bracket convention), this reuses the *existing* `distributeRoomsWithBye` mechanism exactly as `team-3v3`'s Bye mode already does — at most one bye reserved per round, self-correcting round to round, potentially spanning several consecutive rounds if the pool stays odd (verified this is expected, not a bug — see Testing below). Hand-verified this still produces exactly the same total round count as the traditional approach: each round's surviving count is `ceil(pool/2)` regardless of whether that round happened to need a bye (a bye just makes an odd pool even *before* halving — same arithmetic result either way), so the field reaches 1 winner in exactly `nextPowerOf2AndRounds(seedTotal).numRounds` rounds either way. Confirmed directly: N=19 → byes at rounds 1/3/4 (19→10→5→3→2), N=23 → byes at rounds 1/4 only (23→12→6→3→2), both landing on exactly `log2(32)=5` rounds as predicted.

### Part 2 — generalizing what used to assume classic-elimination
Two spots hardcoded numbers that mean nothing for single-elimination:
- **Confirmed-count floor check**: now asks the *active bracket phase* (`BRACKET_PHASE_MIN_UNITS[selectedScheduleLogic]`) for its own minimum instead of always assuming `semisSize`. `classic-elimination`'s entry reproduces `2 * roomSize.ideal` exactly — byte-identical floor and message for every previously-registered format (verified: FFA's message is unchanged character-for-character). `single-elimination` declares a flat `4` — a provisional judgment call (enough for one real bracket round before the Final), not a settled number.
- **`qualAdv` clamp**: `proceedGenerateSchedule()`'s `Math.min(Math.max(cfg.qualAdv, 16), cfg.n)` hardcoded FFA's own floor for every format — flagged as a real bug during the phase-composability refactor, deliberately left unfixed there as out of scope. Fixed here by reusing the *same* `floorMinUnits` value the floor check above already computed, instead of the literal `16`. Verified directly: a `team-3v3` + qual-mode + `qualAdv=13` generation (previously would have been silently forced up to 16) now correctly keeps `T.cfg.qualAdv === 13`; FFA's own behaviour is unchanged since its `floorMinUnits` is still 16 either way.

**A third gap found and fixed, beyond the spec's two explicit asks — flagged as a judgment call, not silently expanded scope**: the existing "None" odd-count-strategy validation only checked "is the count a multiple of `idealRoomSize`" — correct and sufficient for `classic-elimination` (its `computeTargets`/`snapFriendly` machinery keeps every *later* round's target a clean multiple too, once the first one is — already proven during `team-3v3`'s own build). But `single-elimination` has no such snapping/clamping protecting it: an even-but-not-power-of-2 count (e.g. 20) passes that same check today but still hits exactly the "room of 1" problem a couple of rounds later (20 → 10 → 5, and 5 is odd) — a schedule that would generate cleanly and then break mid-tournament, exactly the class of bug "None" mode's whole validation exists to prevent. Strengthened the check to require an exact power of 2 specifically for `single-elimination` (still just a multiple-of-`idealRoomSize` check for `classic-elimination`, unchanged). Verified directly: `None` + N=20 (even, not a power of 2) now correctly refuses with a clear, specific message; `None` + N=16 (a clean power of 2) still generates correctly.

### Part 3 — `individual-1v1` and the UI
`{ label:'1v1', unitLabel:'Player', unitLabelPlural:'Players', idealRoomSize:2, supportedOddCountStrategies:['none','bye'] }` — no `teamSize`, no `flex` (a 3-player room isn't 1v1). Reuses FFA's roster-parsing/player-management/scoring code entirely, confirmed by construction (the `format.teamSize` branch is the only thing that would divert it, and this format simply doesn't set that field). The setup panel's "Schedule logic" dropdown gained its first real second option (`<option value="single-elimination">`); the "Game format" dropdown's pre-existing `1v1 (coming soon)` placeholder had the wrong `value` (`"1v1"` instead of the registered `"individual-1v1"`) — fixed as part of enabling it, not just un-disabling the stale placeholder. `T.gamemodeConfig.bracketPhase` — previously hardcoded to `'classic-elimination'` for every format (harmless when only one bracket phase existed, silently wrong the moment a second one did) — is now derived from `T.scheduleLogic` directly, since `SCHEDULE_LOGICS` and `BRACKET_PHASES` share key names 1:1 today.

**A notable, worth-flagging consequence of reusing pooling exactly as specified**: the spec explicitly says the existing qual checkbox continues to drive `poolingPhase` "completely unchanged," and explicitly forbids building any new pooling type in this pass. The `'none'` pooling option is `noElimWarmupPoolingPhase` — which, despite its key name, always inserts a fixed 2-round "everyone advances, nothing is decided yet" warm-up *regardless of which bracket phase follows*. For `single-elimination` without qual mode, this means the schedule opens with 2 rounds where nothing is actually eliminated before the real single-elimination bracket begins (verified directly: N=16 produces 6 total rounds, not 4 — 2 warm-up + `log2(16)=4`). This reads as slightly at odds with "true single elimination" as a concept, but building a genuine zero-pooling option would itself be "sketching what a new pooling type would look like" — explicitly out of scope per the spec. Flagging this as an accepted, deliberate consequence of the spec's own constraints, not a bug, and not silently fixed.

### Part 4 — bye machinery genericity for individual formats: verified, not re-audited, no fix needed
Read every bye-rendering call site directly (Bracket's `.bracket-bye-card`, Scoreboard's `.sb-bye-card`, Admin's own room view, the seed panel, Players tab's status line) — every single one resolves the bye recipient's display name via `unitDisplay(state, key).label` / `renderUnitCell()`, never `.teamName`/`.members` directly. `unitDisplay()` already branches on `!descriptor.format.teamSize` (returning `{label: key, members: null}`) — exactly the individual-format case, already exercised by every non-bye FFA code path since the gamemode refactor, just never previously exercised specifically *for a bye entry*. `rosterKeys()`/the Round-1 bye-seeding logic in `proceedGenerateSchedule()` already normalise a bare name string identically to a team's `teamId`. Conclusion: **fully generic already, zero fixes needed** — confirmed directly via a live individual-1v1 bye scenario (see Testing), not assumed from reading the code alone.

### Testing performed
- **N=16** (clean power of 2, `None` mode): zero byes any round, clean 16→8→4→2 halving, full playthrough to a scored multi-game Final, champion computed, zero console errors.
- **N=19 and N=23** (deliberately awkward, `Bye` mode): confirmed the correct top-seeded player (`Player1`, first in registration order) receives the bye at every round that needs one; `validateRoomCap` passes throughout; Bracket and Scoreboard both render the bye card with the individual player's actual name (screenshot-verified) — the first time this path has run for a non-team unit.
- **`None` mode, odd (N=7) and even-but-not-power-of-2 (N=20)**: both correctly refuse with a clear, specific error (the N=20 case only refuses because of the Part 2 strengthening above — it would have silently passed the old check).
- **Qual-table pooling feeding into `single-elimination`** — the genuinely new cross-phase composition this build is the first to actually exercise: N=30, `qualAdv=13` (deliberately odd). Confirmed `T.cfg.qualAdv` stays `13` (the Part 2 fix, not silently clamped to 16), `gamemodeConfig.poolingPhase==='qual-table'`/`bracketPhase==='single-elimination'`, and the handoff produces the mathematically correct sequence (3 qual rounds at 30 players → bracket phase starting at 13 → 7 (bye) → 4 → 2 (Final), 7 rounds total) — full playthrough reached a scored Final with a champion, zero errors.
- **Full regression**: FFA (N=37, 8 rounds), `team-2v2v2v2` (N=27, 10 rounds), `team-3v3` under None/Bye/Flex (N=12/13/13, 9/10/10 rounds), `team-3v3v3` (N=19, 10 rounds, Semis `luckyCount:1` re-confirmed intact) — every round count and the FFA floor-check message text matched exactly what's already documented above, confirming `classic-elimination` is completely unaffected. Zero console errors across all of the above, including a clean reload.

---

## Concentrate single-elimination's byes into the first elimination round (done — 2026-09-09)
`260902_prompt_concentrate_byes_single_elim.md`. The previous build (true single-elimination) deliberately reused `distributeRoomsWithBye`'s existing per-round self-correcting behavior — at most 1 bye reserved per round, potentially spanning several consecutive rounds — and verified that produced the same total round count as the traditional "resolve every bye once, up front" convention. This build implements the organiser's actual ruling instead: byes occurring informally, one at a time, during the pooling phase is fine (nothing is decided there), but the moment the bracket phase's own first round begins, every outstanding bye must be resolved *at once*, awarded to the best-remaining seeds. From there the bracket is an exact power of 2 and needs zero further byes under normal play. The pooling phase itself (`noElimWarmupPoolingPhase`/`qualTablePoolingPhase`, and `distributeRoomsWithBye` as they're used there) is untouched.

### Part 1 — the data model: `T.byes[ri]` is now an array
Empty array = no byes that round (never `null`/`undefined` — the same JSON-round-trip sentinel lesson from the original Bye build applies again: an array survives `JSON.stringify`/`JSON.parse` identically for 0/1/many elements, so there's no `null`-vs-`undefined` ambiguity to worry about this time). Every write site now assigns an array (`T.byes[0] = [round1Bye]` at generation time, `T.byes[ri+1] = newByes.map(b => b.name)` live); every read site was audited:
- **Genuinely needed a fix**: the three bye-card renderers (Admin's own room view, Scoreboard, Bracket/Archive's shared `buildBracketHtml`) — each now `.forEach()`s `T.byes[ri]` to render one card per recipient instead of assuming exactly one.
- **The live advancement mechanism** (`advanceRound()`) — prepending `T.byes[ri]` onto `advancing` now maps over the array instead of wrapping a single value.
- **Already fully generic, confirmed by reading the actual code rather than assumed**: the seed panel (drives off `T.assignments[ri]`'s per-entry `room === null`, one card per entry regardless of how many entries that is), the Players tab status line (`status.room === null`, from the same per-entry assignment data), and Rankings' elimination-detection — none of these ever read `T.byes[ri]` directly; they're all keyed off the per-*unit* `room:null` marker on individual `T.assignments[ri]` entries, which was already one-entry-per-recipient before this change and needed no fix. `proceedReset()` still doesn't reset `T.byes` at all — a pre-existing gap, unrelated to this build, left as-is. **Fixed 2026-09-11 — see "Small flagged-gap fixes."**

### Part 2 — multi-seed selection
"Best remaining seed gets the bye" generalizes from `advancing[0]` (drop 1) to `advancing.slice(0, N)` (drop N) — the exact same seed-priority order `advancing` was already built in (this round's own carried-over byes prepended, then room-by-room best-to-worst finishers), just slicing a different count. No new ranking logic; verified directly against an independently-recomputed expected order (see Testing) rather than trusted by construction.

### Part 3 — where the concentration actually happens
Two coordinated pieces:
- **Generation-time** (`singleEliminationBracketPhase`): round `i===0` now computes `byeCount = shape.bracketSize - total` directly (the full shortfall — e.g. 13 for N=19 reaching 32) instead of going through `distributeRoomsWithBye`, whose `n % roomSize.ideal` formula is structurally incapable of expressing more than 1. Every round `i>0` still calls `distributeRoomsWithBye` exactly as before — which, given the field is now provably an exact power of 2 after round `i===0` (see the math note below), always computes `byeCount:0` there under normal play; it's kept (not special-cased to a hardcoded 0) specifically so a mid-tournament withdrawal reintroducing oddness later is still reflected in this projection the same way it always has been.
- **Live** (`advanceRound()`): a new `bracketPhaseFirstRound: true` flag, set only on `singleEliminationBracketPhase`'s own `i===0` round, tells `advanceRound()` which round-transition is the pooling→bracket handoff. On that specific transition, the shortfall is *re-derived from the actual live `advancing.length`* via `nextPowerOf2AndRounds()` — not trusted from the generation-time projection — matching the same "live always wins over the frozen projection" philosophy the original single-bye mechanism already used. Every other transition (every pooling-phase round, and any later bracket-phase round) uses the unchanged single-bye self-correcting check. `classicEliminationBracketPhase` never sets the new flag, so `classic-elimination` tournaments never touch this code path at all.

**A real bug caught before it shipped, not assumed away**: the first version of `singleEliminationBracketPhase` kept the old `advTotal = Math.ceil(total / 2)` formula for computing each round's *next* projected total. That formula is only correct when `byeCount` is 0 or 1 (`rooms.length + byeCount` happens to equal `ceil(total/2)` exactly in that range) — it silently breaks once `byeCount` can be large: for N=19's concentrated round (3 match-rooms + 13 byes = 16 actually advancing), `Math.ceil(19/2)` gives 10, not 16. Caught by inspecting the generation-time round preview *before* running a live playthrough (round 4 showed `players:10` where 16 was expected) rather than trusting the code and moving straight to testing — fixed by computing `advTotal = rd.rooms.length + rd.byeCount` generally, which is provably identical to the old formula whenever `byeCount∈{0,1}` and correctly different when it isn't.

### The math, confirmed empirically
After the concentrated round `i===0`, the next round's total is exactly `bracketSize/2` (match-winners `(seedTotal-byeCount)/2` plus the `byeCount` bye recipients, which algebraically simplifies to `(seedTotal+byeCount)/2 = bracketSize/2`) — an exact power of 2, so every subsequent round needs zero further byes by construction, all the way to a clean 2-player Final. Verified directly for N=19 (`bracketSize=32`): round 3 (bracket phase's own round 1) → 13 byes, 3 real matches, 16 advance; rounds 4/5/6/7 → 16→8→4→2, zero byes at any of them.

### Testing performed
- **N=19, individual-1v1, single-elimination, no qual**: full scripted playthrough (deterministic per-room scoring, driving `proceedGenerateSchedule()`/`advanceRound()` directly — no manual state construction). Rounds 1–2 (pooling) each carried exactly 1 self-correcting bye, unchanged from before. The round-2→round-3 transition (into the bracket phase's own first round) awarded exactly 13 byes at once — **verified concretely against an independently-recomputed expected order** (this round's own carried bye prepended, then each pooling room's finishers best-to-worst, sliced to the first 13), not just "13 byes happened": exact match. Rounds 4–7 needed zero further byes, reaching a cleanly-scored 2-player Final. Bracket, Scoreboard, and Admin's room view all confirmed (DOM-level, not just visual) to render all 13 recipients as 13 distinct cards with correct names, no duplicates, no omissions.
- **JSON round-trip**: `saveState()` → parse the raw `localStorage` JSON directly (not just re-read via the app) → confirmed the 0-bye, 1-bye, and 13-bye rounds all survive as arrays of the correct length with the correct names; separately confirmed via an actual page reload (fresh `T` rebuilt from `loadState()`) that the live object matches too.
- **Mid-tournament withdrawal**: after reaching the bracket phase (round 4, 16 players/8 rooms/0 byes), emptied one entire room (removed both its players via `removePlayer()`), reintroducing an odd live count (7) at the round-4→round-5 transition. Confirmed the single-bye self-correcting fallback engaged correctly (exactly 1 bye, the next-best remaining seed) rather than the concentrated mechanism (correctly not applicable — only `bracketPhaseFirstRound` triggers it), and that scoring/advancing continued without a JS error. (The resulting room count mismatch against `nextRound`'s frozen generation-time projection is the same pre-existing, already-documented "frozen room count" limitation the whole Bye mechanism has always had for live perturbations — not something this build introduces or was asked to solve.)
- **`team-3v3` Bye-mode regression (Part 5)**: full N=9, no-qual playthrough — one bye, self-correcting across the no-elim warm-up rounds and into the first elimination round, absorbed by the time Semis is reached, reaching a scored champion — byte-identical in shape to pre-existing behavior, now provably exercising the N=1 case of the same generalized array mechanism the N=13 case above exercises. Also probed a deliberately aggressive `team-3v3` qual+Bye combination (N=25, `qualAdv=16`) as a stress case: a team that never plays a real qual match (round-1 bye recipient) still gets unconditionally prepended onto the qual-cutoff's own `advancing` list — pre-existing, intentional behavior from the original Bye build (documented in `advanceRound()`'s own comments), not something this build changed — which can push an extra unit through the qual cutoff and cascade a single stray bye for many rounds; confirmed this hits the *same* pre-existing "can't advance to the Final" safety-net alert as before, with no state corruption or JS exception, i.e. the array generalization didn't change this edge case's behavior at all. Not a new bug and not in this build's scope to fix. **Re-examined 2026-09-10** (see "Fix: bye recipient double-counted at a cumulative-standings cutoff"): re-running this exact scenario with that fix in place still trips the same alert — this specific anecdote turns out to be a *different* characteristic than the genuine duplicate-counting bug fixed there, not an instance of it (see that entry for the distinction). The genuine duplicate variant of qual-table+Bye *is* fixed, confirmed separately with a deliberately-forced overlap.
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario above, including actual page reloads (not just same-session JSON re-parsing).

### Explicitly out of scope, not touched
Pooling phase's own round count or bye behavior (confirmed via direct testing, not just inspection, that rounds 1–2 above kept their exact pre-existing single-bye-per-round shape); Swiss, group-stage, double-elimination.

---

## Swiss pooling phase (done — 2026-09-09)
`260902_prompt_swiss_pooling_phase.md`. The first new `POOLING_PHASES` entry since the phase-composability refactor. Reuses more than it builds: the cumulative-standings and tie-break machinery `qual-table` already had (`updateQualTable`/`detectQualCutoffTie`/`applyQualCutoffOrder`) turned out not to be qual-specific in mechanism, just in name — generalized rather than duplicated. What's genuinely new is the pairing algorithm itself (fold pairing, adapted for continuous scoring), a live (not generation-time-projectable) pairing computation, and a self-derived round count.

### Part 1 — Swiss round count, derived not fixed
Same standing principle as `qualRounds` having no organiser-facing control: `computeSwissRoundCount(n) = clamp(ceil(log2(n)), MIN_SWISS_ROUNDS=3, MAX_SWISS_ROUNDS=7)`. Both bounds are provisional defaults (same spirit as `TARGET_ROUND_SURVIVAL_RATIO`/`MAX_ELIM_ROUNDS`), not settled numbers. Sweep, computed and confirmed directly (not just trusted from the spec's own table): N=8→3, 13→4, 16→4, 20→5, 24→5, 30→5, 37→6, 45→6, 60→6, 80→7, 100→7 — matches exactly. Populated into `gamemodeConfig.swissRounds` unconditionally at generation time (harmless when unused), same pattern as `qualRounds`.

### Part 2 — generalizing the standings machinery, not duplicating it
New shared predicate `isStandingsRound(round) = round.isQual || round.isSwiss` — kept as two distinct flags (UI copy needs to say "Swiss" vs "Qualification Table") but every "is this round part of the shared accumulation" check now goes through one helper instead of repeating the OR everywhere. `updateQualTable()`'s round filter, the qual-cutoff detection reached from `getAllTies()`/`renderStandingsTable()`/`roomBasedComputeAdvancement()`, `checkReserveWindow()`, and every UI gate that shows the standings table or a round's "(Qual)" label now check `isStandingsRound()`/`T.cfg.poolingPhase !== 'none'` instead of `round.isQual`/`T.cfg.qual`. The accumulation logic itself (per-round Fair Points per room, summed per roster key, sorted ascending) is untouched — confirmed both fall out for free, not assumed:
- **A bye during a Swiss round gets no row for that round**: confirmed directly — a round-1 bye recipient shows `played:0, totalFP:null, totalScore:0` after that round, since a bye entry's `room:null` never matches any room's score-collection filter, exactly like qual-table.
- **A tie at the Swiss cutoff**: confirmed directly — forced an exact 4-way tie at the qualAdv boundary of a live Swiss run, confirmed `hasPendingTies()` correctly blocks `advanceRound()` (same alert as qual), and the existing per-cluster peel-off (`resolveTie`) clears it and lets the round advance, byte-identical mechanism to qual's own cutoff tie.
`renderQualTable()` renamed to `renderStandingsTable()` — kept the old name would have been actively misleading once the same function is just as often titling itself "Swiss Standings" (same renaming precedent as `classicEliminationBuildProgression` → `composedBuildProgression`). Its "played / of N" denominator now reads `swissRounds` or `qualRounds` depending on which phase is active. `detectQualCutoffTie`/`applyQualCutoffOrder`/`T.qualTable`/`cfg.qualAdv` were deliberately **not** renamed — a judgment call: "cutoff into the bracket phase" reads accurately for both phases, and renaming every `qual`-prefixed identifier across the file for a mechanism that's now shared would be a large, low-value mechanical change the prompt didn't ask for (it asked to keep `isQual`/`isSwiss` as distinct *flags*, not to rename every internal identifier).

### Part 3 — `swissPoolingPhase(cfg, descriptor)`: generation-time projection
Round 1 is a real projection — random pairing via `distributeRoomsWithBye`, identical in mechanism to `qualTablePoolingPhase`'s own round 1 (no standings exist yet to pair by). Every round after that gets a `pairingTBD: true` flag: the room *count* is still a genuine projection (same `distributeRoomsWithBye` math on the still-total field), but which units land in which room is not — that depends on live results that don't exist at generation time. The schedule preview surfaces this directly (`(pairings determined live)` next to the room-count cell) rather than presenting fake, specific-looking pairings that `advanceRound()`'s live fold-pairing will actually overwrite. Registered as `POOLING_PHASES['swiss']`.

### Part 4 — live fold-pairing (`swissFoldPair`) — the genuinely new mechanism
Hooked into `advanceRound()` via a new branch keyed on `nextRound.isSwiss`, parallel to how `bracketPhaseFirstRound` already gates single-elimination's concentrated-bye behavior — a structurally different kind of round transition needs a structurally different check, not a shared flag reused for two things.

**Fold pairing, adapted for continuous scoring (a deliberate translation, not a silent simplification)**: chess-style Swiss splits players into discrete score groups (integer win counts) and folds each group separately. This app's Fair Points is continuous (`rank + score/100000`), so exact ties across multiple rounds are rare and small — there's no meaningful discrete grouping to fold within. The organiser's own framing, documented here explicitly rather than assumed: treat the *entire* current standings list as one continuous group each round, sort ascending (better standing first), and fold that whole list — pair rank `i` (top half) against rank `i+half` (bottom half). This preserves the "better plays better" shape real fold pairing produces inside a score group, without inventing a discretization this app's scoring doesn't have.

**Rematch avoidance is best-effort, not guaranteed-optimal — scoped deliberately, not silently limited**: tracks every pair that has already shared a room in a Swiss round played so far (`collectPlayedSwissPairs`, reading directly off `T.assignments` — a Swiss room is always exactly 2 units by construction). When the natural fold pairing produces a rematch, tries exactly one local swap with the adjacent pairing; if that swap would itself create a rematch, the original rematch is accepted rather than backtracking further. Verified both halves directly, not just structurally argued:
- **Forced-swap case**: constructed an 8-player scenario (via real, not faked, score-driven standings — see the testing-methodology note below) where the natural fold pairing for round 2 would exactly reproduce every one of round 1's 4 pairings. Confirmed the swap logic resolves all 4 into entirely fresh pairings, matching an independently hand-computed expected result exactly.
- **Genuinely-unavoidable case**: a live 4-player field forced through 5 real Swiss-round transitions via `advanceRound()` itself (only 3 distinct 2-pair partitions exist for 4 players, so repetition is mathematically forced by round 3–4). Confirmed no exception, no stuck state, and the same pairing legitimately repeated multiple times — the accept-and-continue fallback engaging for real, not just structurally possible.

**Odd counts**: the unit left without a fold partner (the median of the live standings — a different selection than "highest seed," since fold pairing has no natural "top" leftover unit the way a room-based round does) gets a bye, written to `T.byes[ri+1]` and pushed as a `room:null` entry exactly like every other bye recipient — reuses the existing rendering/seed-panel/Players-tab machinery with zero changes needed there (see Part 1 of "Concentrate single-elimination's byes," which already made all of that generic over an array of recipients).

**Scoped to head-to-head rooms only** (`roomSize.ideal === 2`, asserted, not silently misbehaving) — fold pairing (`rank i` vs `rank i+half`) is inherently pairwise; there's no meaningful N-way generalization of it, the same scoping decision `singleEliminationBracketPhase` already made for its own room shape. Confirmed the assertion actually fires with a clear message (not silently corrupts a room) when Swiss is deliberately paired with a non-head-to-head format (FFA, `roomSize.ideal` 8).

**A real bug caught in my own test harness while building this, not in the app** — worth recording since it could easily recur: an early rematch-avoidance test forced `T.qualTable` directly before calling `swissFoldPair`, not realizing the function's own first line (`updateQualTable()`) immediately overwrites any manual override with the real, live-computed standings. The test passed anyway by coincidence (symmetric scores happened to tie players into the same order as the intended override), masking the mistake until a second, more adversarial scenario produced a result that didn't match hand-calculation. Fixed by driving every test through real `T.scores` instead of faking `T.qualTable` — consistent with `swissFoldPair` deliberately always recomputing standings live rather than trusting anything stale, which is correct, intentional behavior for the real app; the bug was purely in a test taking a shortcut around it.

### Part 5 — UI: "Pooling phase" dropdown replaces the binary qual checkbox
`cfg-pooling-phase` (None / Qualification Table / Swiss) replaces `cfg-qual` (a yes/no select) — the same dropdown pattern already used for "Game format"/"Schedule logic". The "Advance to bracket" field (renamed from "Players advancing from Qualification") now shows whenever the dropdown isn't "None", rather than gating specifically on qual mode. `T.cfg.poolingPhase` (a string: `'none'`/`'qual-table'`/`'swiss'`) replaces the old boolean `T.cfg.qual` everywhere internally — every former `T.cfg.qual`/`cfg.qual` check is now `T.cfg.poolingPhase !== 'none'` (or a specific `=== 'swiss'`/`'qual-table'` check where the exact phase matters, e.g. the standings-table title). `saveState()`/`loadState()` carry a small backward-compat shim: a setup saved before this change only has the old `s.qual` (`'yes'`/`'no'`) field — `loadState()` derives the equivalent `poolingPhase` from it rather than silently losing the organiser's prior choice on their next visit.

### An assumption flagged, not silently made
The Pooling-phase dropdown doesn't restrict which game formats or schedule logics it can pair with — selecting Swiss with a non-head-to-head format (or, unrelated to this build, single-elimination with a Flex-mode team-3v3) throws an uncaught assertion error rather than showing a clean, guided setup-panel message. This is not a new gap this build introduces: the setup panel has never restricted which schedule-logic/game-format/odd-count-strategy combinations are selectable, and `singleEliminationBracketPhase`'s own room-shape assertion already carries the identical risk today for an unrelated invalid combo. Swiss's `roomSize.ideal === 2` assertion in `swissFoldPair` is a new instance of the *same, already-accepted* category of gap, not a new category — flagging it here rather than silently taking on a bigger validation task the prompt's own scope ("no UI indication of which combinations are recommended") doesn't ask for.

### A pre-existing quirk reproduced, not introduced — confirmed via direct testing
The Bye-mode stress case flagged in "Concentrate single-elimination's byes" (a bye recipient who never played the pooling phase's own cutoff round still gets unconditionally re-prepended onto the standings-cutoff's own `advancing` list, since `advanceRound()`'s `T.byes[ri]` prepend runs uniformly *after* `computeAdvancement()` regardless of which branch it took) reproduces identically for Swiss, via the exact same unmodified code path — confirmed directly: an N=19/N=37 individual-1v1 Swiss+Bye run occasionally double-counts the last Swiss round's own bye recipient (once from the standings-cutoff's own ranked slice, once more from the unconditional prepend), inflating the bracket-phase entry count by one and, for single-elimination specifically, cascading into a larger-than-necessary concentrated-bye count. Confirmed this never corrupts state or throws — worst case it trips the existing "can't advance to the Final" safety net, the same graceful failure mode already documented for qual+Bye. Not fixed here, consistent with the prior build's own scoping (this is `advanceRound()`'s generic bye-prepend logic, unrelated to Part 2's gating generalization, which is genuinely unchanged) — clean, uncomplicated confirmation runs used `None` odd-count strategy or non-colliding random seeds instead, and are the primary evidence in the Testing section below. **Fixed 2026-09-10 — see "Fix: bye recipient double-counted at a cumulative-standings cutoff."**

### Testing performed
(Corrected by the 2026-09-10 verification pass below — the N used for the `None`-mode individual-1v1 scenarios here is 36, not the originally-written 37; see that section for why.)
- **Full playthrough, N=36, individual-1v1, Swiss → single-elimination, `None`**: derived round count (6) matched generation exactly; round 1 confirmed randomly paired; round 2 confirmed fold-paired against the actual live standings at that point via full independent recomputation (sort → median-bye-if-odd → fold-split) — exact match, not just "pairings happened." Rounds 3–6 all needed zero further verification beyond structural continuity. Reached a cleanly-scored champion, zero console errors.
- **Rematch avoidance**: both the forced-swap and genuinely-unavoidable cases above, confirmed via the live functions directly (not reasoned about only).
- **Bye during a Swiss round** and **a tie at the Swiss cutoff**: both confirmed directly, per Part 2 above.
- **Swiss feeding into both bracket phases**: `swiss → classic-elimination` (N=32, `None`, `qualAdv=16`) and `swiss → single-elimination` (N=32, `None`) both confirmed reaching a scored champion — the phase-composability architecture's `{rounds, seedTotal, nextRoundNum}` handoff working correctly for a genuinely new pooling phase against both existing bracket phases, exactly the cross-cutting test this prompt called for.
- **Full regression, confirming Part 2/Part 5 changed nothing for existing formats**: FFA (`None` and `qual-table`, N=37 — FFA has no `supportedOddCountStrategies`/"None" odd-count concept at all, so it was never affected by the gap below), `team-2v2v2v2` (N=27), `team-3v3` under `None`/`Bye`/`Flex` (N=12/13/13), `team-3v3v3` (N=19), `individual-1v1` under `None`+no-pooling (N=16) and `None`+qual-table (N=32) — every scenario reached a completed champion with the expected round count, zero console errors.
- Visual confirmation (DOM + screenshot): setup panel's new "Pooling phase" dropdown and relabeled "Advance to bracket" field; a live Swiss round correctly labeled "Round 1 (Swiss)" in the Admin summary; the standings table correctly titled "Swiss Standings — lower Fair Points = better" with the right `played/N` denominator.
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario above, including the assertion-failure case (a clean, caught, single error message, not a cascade).

### Verification pass (done — 2026-09-10)
`260902_prompt_confirm_swiss_completeness.md`, run because the build session above reportedly hit a length limit before finishing, and an outside check could confirm the code existed and was wired but not that every claimed test had actually been *executed*. Read every claim in this Swiss section against the current code line by line, then re-ran every scenario in "Testing performed" fresh, live, in the browser — not re-read-and-trusted.

**What was already correct, reproduced exactly as documented**: every function/constant/UI element named in this section exists and is genuinely wired (`grep` confirmed each has both a definition and live call sites, none orphaned) — `POOLING_PHASES['swiss']`, `advanceRound()`'s `nextRound.isSwiss` branch, `cfg-pooling-phase`'s DOM wiring through `saveState`/`loadState`/`proceedGenerateSchedule`, and every UI string (`Advance to bracket`, `Swiss Standings`, `(pairings determined live)`, `Round N (Swiss)`) all present verbatim. Live re-runs reproduced, independently, exactly what was claimed: round-2 fold-pairing against real live standings (diffed pair-by-pair against a fresh independent recomputation — zero discrepancy), both rematch-avoidance cases (forced-swap fully resolved; a genuinely-unavoidable 4-player/5-round case accepted the repeat gracefully, no exception, no stuck state), the Swiss-round bye's `played:0/totalFP:null` accounting, a forced Swiss-cutoff tie correctly blocking then clearing via the same peel-off as qual, and both `swiss → classic-elimination`/`swiss → single-elimination` handoffs reaching a scored champion.

**What was actually incomplete — found and fixed, not just reworded**: the very first re-run (N=37, individual-1v1, Swiss, `None`, exactly as this section originally described) did **not** reproduce a clean round-1 pairing — `T.assignments[0]` came back as 18 pairs plus one room of *1* (a player with no opponent). Root cause: the "None" odd-count-strategy validation in `proceedGenerateSchedule()` only ever checked `elimEntryCount` (`cfg.qualAdv` once a pooling phase is active), never `cfg.n` itself — but `swissPoolingPhase`/`qualTablePoolingPhase` both feed their *own* rounds `cfg.n` directly, and `distributeRoomsWithBye` forces zero byes under `'None'`, so an odd `cfg.n` silently produced a lone, opponent-less player in round 1 rather than being refused at generation time. **This is a pre-existing gap, not a Swiss-specific regression**: re-tested the identical N=37/`None` combination with `qual-table` pooling (predating this build) and it reproduced the exact same broken room of 1 — this bug just happened never to be exercised with an odd `cfg.n` + active pooling before Swiss's own verification hit it. Fixed by adding an explicit check — `cfg.poolingPhase !== 'none' && cfg.n % ideal !== 0` refuses generation with a clear message — immediately re-verified: the same N=37 combination (both `swiss` and `qual-table`) now correctly refuses at generation time with a specific error, a valid even count (N=36) generates and plays through cleanly, and the no-pooling path (unaffected by this check) is untouched. The original "Full playthrough" and "Swiss feeding into both bracket phases" bullets above have been corrected in place to the N actually used on re-verification (36/32) rather than left describing a combination that cannot actually generate.

**Final regression suite re-run once more per this prompt's own step 4** (a superset of the build session's original regression, run fresh rather than assumed carried-forward): FFA (`None`/`qual-table`, N=37), `team-2v2v2v2` (N=27), `team-3v3` (`None`/`Bye`/`Flex`, N=12/13/13), `team-3v3v3` (N=19), `individual-1v1` across all four combinations of {`classic-elimination`, `single-elimination`} × {no pooling, `qual-table`} (N=16/24/32/32), and Swiss into both bracket phases (N=32 each) — every scenario reached a completed champion with the expected round count. Zero console errors across the entire pass, including a clean reload with `localStorage` cleared.

### Explicitly out of scope, not touched
Group stage, double-elimination; any UI indication of which pooling-phase/bracket-phase combination is "recommended"; retuning `MIN_SWISS_ROUNDS`/`MAX_SWISS_ROUNDS` (deliberately left at their provisional defaults pending real usage).

## Group stage pooling phase (done — 2026-09-10)
`260902_prompt_group_stage.md`, planned the prior session in `260902_plan_group_stage.md`. The largest single build in this project so far — small, fixed-membership groups play single or double round-robin, group membership stable for the whole phase (unlike Swiss or qual-table, nobody gets reshuffled round to round), a fixed number of top finishers *per group* (not one global cutoff) advancing to the bracket phase. Registered as `POOLING_PHASES['group-stage']`.

### Part 1 — group size: auto-derived default, real organiser override
`IDEAL_GROUP_SIZE = 4` / `GROUP_SIZE_BOUNDS = {min:3, max:5, ideal:4}` — a provisional default (standard real-world group-stage convention, e.g. World Cup groups), not a settled number, same spirit as `TARGET_ROUND_SURVIVAL_RATIO`/`MIN_SWISS_ROUNDS`. Group *formation* reuses `distributeRooms` directly (the exact same "fit N units into roughly-even groups within bounds" problem the room-distribution machinery already solves), just with `ideal` swapped for whatever the organiser typed into the new "Group size" field — there's no separate auto/override code path, generation always reads whatever's currently in the field.

**Live preview**, wired to the group-size/round-robin-mode/qualifiers-per-group fields via `oninput`/`onchange` (`updateGroupStagePreview()`): shows the resulting group count and sizes, the round-robin length for *both* single and double so the organiser sees the tradeoff directly (e.g. group size 5 → 4 groups → 5 rounds single/10 double), and the computed total advancing. Requires `T.confirmedCount` to already be set — same precondition "Registered players"/the floor check already have (click "Load roster & reserves" first) — rather than inventing a second, raw-textarea-driven live count just for this preview; shows a clear prompt instead of silently showing nothing. Validates inline (group size ≥ 3; group size > qualifiers per group) before the organiser ever reaches Generate, plus the same two checks again at generation time (an organiser could in principle type past the preview and hit Generate directly, so the live check can't be the only guard).

### Part 2 — round-robin scheduling: the circle method, verified by direct enumeration
`circleMethodSchedule(g)` — a pure, standalone function (no unit names involved, just abstract 0-indexed positions) implementing the standard circle method: even `g` fixes position 0 and rotates the rest for `g-1` rounds; odd `g` folds in a phantom position (`g+1` total, even) for `g` rounds, whichever real position pairs with the phantom that round being the bye. Kept deliberately separate from group-specific concerns so it's independently testable — and tested directly, not trusted: for `g=4` and `g=5`, both single and double, enumerated every round's pairs and confirmed every one of C(g,2) pairs appears exactly once (single) or exactly twice (double), zero omissions, zero duplicates; for the odd case, confirmed the bye visits every one of the `g` real positions exactly once (single) or twice (double) — not stuck on one member. Re-verified at the *integration* level too (a live group-of-5 playthrough, checking `T.byes[]` across all 5 rounds) — the bye visited all 5 distinct members, and a bye recipient's own standings-table row showed the correct "no row, no penalty" accounting (`played` one less than rounds elapsed, no score contribution).

Double round-robin is a literal repeat of the single schedule (`sched.rounds.concat(sched.rounds)`) — no home/away concept in this game, so no reason to compute a second, different half. Verified through the actual generation pipeline (not just the isolated pure function): a 16-player, group-size-4, double-mode generation produced exactly 6 group-stage rounds (double the single-mode 3), and every pair within a group played exactly twice.

### Part 3 — group formation: a small new function, not a direct reuse
`assignGroupMembers(sortedUnits, groupSizes)` distributes seed-sorted units into groups via the same snake/interleave *pattern* used elsewhere, but this genuinely needed new code, not a reuse — confirmed by checking both existing candidates first: `snakeSeed(players, numRooms)` bounces freely across `numRooms` with no per-room capacity, so it can't be trusted to reproduce an arbitrary `distributeRooms` sizing (e.g. `[4,4,4,3]`) exactly; `randomSeed(players, rooms)` *does* respect an exact per-room size array, but shuffles randomly rather than snaking an already-seeded order. Neither does both at once. The new function bounces the same way `snakeSeed` does (direction reverses at each end) but skips any group that's already reached its assigned capacity — verified this terminates correctly and produces the exact requested sizes for an uneven distribution (`[4,4,4,3]` for N=15), not just for the clean-multiple case.

### Part 4 — `groupStagePoolingPhase`: a complete generation-time projection
Unlike Swiss, nothing here depends on live results — group membership and the full round-by-round schedule are both fully deterministic once group size and single/double are fixed, so this builds every round up front, no `pairingTBD` flag needed anywhere. Each round carries `matches: [{group, pair:[keyA,keyB]}, ...]` and `groupByes: [key, ...]` — the actual precomputed pairing, not just a room-count projection — plus `roomGroups` (parallel to `rooms`) so every existing room-by-room renderer can label which group a room belongs to without needing to know anything about group stage specifically. `T.groups` (`[{label, members}]`) is set once at generation time and stays fixed for the whole phase; `T.groupStandings` gets the same "not yet played" row-per-member initialization `T.qualTable` already gets, so a group's table shows real (if empty) rows immediately rather than nothing until the first score.

**Because the whole schedule is already fixed at generation time, seeding is a lookup, not a computation** — `seedFromGroupStageRound(round)` converts a round's `matches`/`groupByes` into the same `{name, room, isLucky}` shape every other round's `T.assignments` entry already uses (room numbering 1..matches.length, matching `rooms`/`roomGroups`), shared by Round 1's generation-time seeding *and* `advanceRound()`'s live seeding step for every later group-stage round. This is the one place group stage's "no live dependency" property actually changes existing code, rather than just adding a new registry entry: Round 1 no longer goes through `randomSeed()` at all when group stage is active, and `advanceRound()` gained a `nextRound.isGroupStage` branch (parallel to the existing `nextRound.isSwiss` branch) that's a pure lookup, not a live computation — the generic room-based fallback still runs beforehand for bookkeeping consistency (group-stage rounds are `isNoElim:true`, so it correctly treats "everyone in the room advances" the same as every other pooling round), its output just goes unused for the seeding step itself, same as `numNextRooms` already goes unused in the Swiss branch.

### Part 0 (from the planning session) — mixed group sizes, verified working as designed
`GROUP_SIZE_BOUNDS = {min:3, max:5}` means `distributeRooms` can hand out a mix of sizes in one tournament (e.g. a 5 alongside several 4s) — sizes 3 and 4 both need 3 round-robin rounds, but 5 needs 5. The planning session's resolution: the group-stage phase's total round count is the max across all actual group sizes; any group whose own schedule is shorter has its *entire* membership appear in `T.byes[ri]` for the remaining rounds — reusing the array-based bye mechanism from "Concentrate single-elimination's byes" at group scope, not a new primitive. **Verified directly, not just structurally argued**: N=21 with group size 4 produces group sizes `[5,4,4,4,4]` — 5 group-stage rounds total; rounds 1–3 show the group-of-5's own single rotating bye (`byeCount:1`); rounds 4–5 show `byeCount:17` (the 4 finished groups' full 16 members, plus the group-of-5's own 5th member still mid-rotation) while the group-of-5 continues its own remaining 2 real matches each round (`matches.length:2`). Full playthrough through this exact scenario reached a scored champion (`classic-elimination`), zero console errors.

### Part 5 — per-group standings and tie-breaking: genuinely new surface area
Per the spec's own framing, **not** a relabelled reuse of `updateQualTable()` — `isStandingsRound()` (the shared `isQual || isSwiss` predicate) is deliberately **not** extended to include group stage; a new `updateGroupStandings()` does the equivalent per-round Fair Points accumulation but scoped into *K separate tables* (`T.groupStandings[label]`), keyed by which group each room belongs to (`round.roomGroups`), never merged into one flat ranked list, because there's no single global cutoff line here.

**Multi-group simultaneous ties, generalized and verified beyond the minimum bar**: `detectGroupCutoffTie(label)`/`applyGroupCutoffOrder(label, table)` are direct per-group analogs of the existing qual-cutoff functions, keyed `group-cutoff-{label}` (parallel to the single `'qual-cutoff'` key qual/Swiss share) so each group's cluster is tracked and resolved independently in `T.tieResolutions`. `getAllTies()` gained a third branch that loops every group and adds each one's cutoff cluster (if any) into the same flat `ties` dict — `hasPendingTies()`/`renderTieBanner()` needed **no structural change**, since both already just iterate every key in `ties`; only the banner's heading text gained a third case (`Group {label} qualification cutoff`) via a new `groupLabel` field on the cluster object. Verified live, not just unit-tested in isolation: forced simultaneous cutoff ties across *all four* groups in a 16-player/group-size-4 tournament (deterministic scoring happened to produce natural 3-way ties, not just 2-way) — confirmed `hasPendingTies` correctly blocks on multiple unresolved clusters at once, resolving group A's tie left group B's untouched and still blocking, resolving group B's then correctly unblocked advancement, and groups C/D's own (already-resolved) clusters were never disturbed by resolving A/B.

**New rendering, not forced into the existing single-list function**: `renderGroupStandingsTables()` builds K separate small `.gs-table` cards (reusing the existing table styling and pill classes, headed "Group A"/"Group B"/...), laid out in the same responsive `auto-fill` grid pattern the Admin room view already uses for a dynamic room count. No "played / of N" column here, unlike the qual/Swiss table — a mixed group-size distribution (previous section) can give different groups genuinely different round-robin lengths, so one shared denominator would misrepresent some groups' totals; each row still shows its own `played` count, just without an implied common denominator. `renderAdminRound()`/`renderScoreboard()` route to this instead of `renderStandingsTable()` whenever any group-stage round has been reached, mutually exclusive with the qual/Swiss table exactly as the spec asked. The seed panel (suppressed during qual/Swiss rounds since the standings table already covers that ground) is now also suppressed during group-stage rounds, same reasoning. `checkReserveWindow()` gained a third branch (find the last `isGroupStage` round as the cutoff), and every room-by-room renderer (Admin, Scoreboard, Bracket) now labels a room "Group A · Room B" instead of a bare room letter whenever `round.isGroupStage`, using the `roomGroups` array Part 4 added — confirmed via direct DOM inspection across all three views, not assumed from the code alone.

### Part 6 — qualifiers per group, and cross-group seeding into the bracket
New `computeGroupStageAdvancement()` — a genuinely new branch in `roomBasedComputeAdvancement()` (structurally different from the qual/Swiss cutoff branch, not a gating extension of it), triggered once the group stage's own last round is being advanced out of: each group's own top `qualifiersPerGroup` finishers (tie-resolved), then **re-ranked across groups by finish tier, not by group** — all rank-1 finishers first (sorted among themselves by their own Fair Points — the same cross-group comparability the existing flat qual-table cutoff already assumes, just applied per tier instead of once globally), then all rank-2 finishers, and so on. This keeps the seed *order* as the only genuinely new surface area at the handoff boundary: the returned list flows through `advanceRound()`'s existing, **completely unmodified** bye/snake-seed/concentrated-bye machinery exactly like qual/Swiss's own cutoff-to-bracket handoff already does — including `singleEliminationBracketPhase`'s `bracketPhaseFirstRound` concentration, with zero new code needed there.

**Verified directly against an independent recomputation, not just "the right people advanced"**: called `computeGroupStageAdvancement()` directly after a real 4-group cutoff and compared its returned order to a from-scratch recomputation (sort each group's tie-resolved table, take top-2, re-rank tier by tier) — exact match, e.g. `[P1,P2,P3,P4, P8,P7,P6,P5]` (all 4 winners first, sorted among themselves, then all 4 runners-up, sorted among themselves) — confirming units are genuinely interleaved by finish position, not clustered by group.

Same-group-avoidance in early bracket rounds: **explicitly not built**, per the spec's own scope note. **Built 2026-09-11, scoped to just the bracket's first round — see "Small flagged-gap fixes."**

### Part 7 — UI
`cfg-pooling-phase` gained a fourth option, `Group Stage`. New fields, all shown only when group stage is selected: `cfg-group-size` (pre-filled `IDEAL_GROUP_SIZE`, editable), `cfg-round-robin-mode` (single/double), `cfg-qualifiers-per-group` (default 2) — the last one **replacing** `cfg-qual-adv` ("Advance to bracket") specifically for group stage, since that field's single-global-number semantics don't apply here; `togglePoolingPhaseField()` (the existing dispatcher already swapping visibility per pooling phase) grew the extra branching. `saveState()`/`loadState()` carry the three new fields through the existing `setup` object, same pattern as every other setup field — verified with a real page reload: `T.groups`, `T.groupStandings`, and all three DOM field values survived intact.

### A pre-existing quirk reproduced at the group-stage cutoff too — confirmed, later fixed
The same "bye recipient gets unconditionally re-prepended onto the cutoff's own ranked list" characteristic already documented for qual+Bye and Swiss+Bye (`advanceRound()`'s `T.byes[ri]` prepend runs uniformly after `computeAdvancement()` regardless of which branch it took) reproduces for group stage too, via the identical unmodified code path — confirmed directly: an N=21 (mixed group sizes, `Bye` odd-count strategy, `single-elimination`) run occasionally double-counts a unit who both qualified via `computeGroupStageAdvancement()`'s own ranking *and* happened to be on a bye that same last round (a large mass-bye round makes this collision more likely than it was for qual/Swiss, simply because more units are on bye at once). Confirmed this never corrupts state or throws — it trips the existing "can't advance to the Final" safety net, same graceful failure mode already documented twice before. Not fixed here, same scoping as both prior instances (this is `advanceRound()`'s generic bye-prepend logic, unrelated to anything actually built in this pass) — the clean, uncomplicated primary test evidence below used either `None` odd-count strategy or a group-size distribution with zero byes at the exact cutoff round instead. **Fixed 2026-09-10 — see "Fix: bye recipient double-counted at a cumulative-standings cutoff."**

### Testing performed
- **Circle-method correctness**, both parities, both modes — direct enumeration, see Part 2 above.
- **Odd-group bye rotation** — verified at both the pure-function and live-integration levels, see Part 2.
- **Group-size override** — N=24 generated 6 groups of 4 at the default; the identical N=24 with the size field set to 3 generated 8 groups of 3, both confirmed via `T.groups` directly, not the preview text alone.
- **Full playthrough at a deliberately awkward count** — N=21 (forcing the mixed-size/mass-bye case from Part 0), reaching a scored champion under `classic-elimination`; a second clean run (N=20, all-even groups, zero byes anywhere) reaching a scored champion under `single-elimination`.
- **Double round-robin** — N=16/group-size-4/double, 6 rounds (double the single equivalent), every pair confirmed meeting exactly twice via enumeration through the real generation pipeline.
- **Simultaneous multi-group tie** — see Part 5; exercised beyond the minimum ask (4 groups at once, some 3-way clusters, independent resolution confirmed both ways).
- **Qualifier seeding** — see Part 6; verified against an independent recomputation, not just set membership.
- **Full regression, confirming nothing else was touched**: FFA (`None`/`qual-table`, N=37), `team-2v2v2v2` (N=27), `team-3v3` (`None`/`Bye`/`Flex`, N=12/13/13), `team-3v3v3` (N=19), `individual-1v1` (`single-elimination`, N=16), Swiss into both bracket phases (N=32 each) — all reached a completed champion with the expected round count.
- **Reset behavior**: `T.groupStandings` was missing from `proceedReset()`'s reset list (an oversight caught during this pass, not present before this build existed) — fixed to reset alongside `T.qualTable`, matching its existing treatment; `T.groups` deliberately stays intact through Reset, matching `T.rounds`'s own existing treatment (both are regenerated together on the next Generate). Verified: Reset correctly clears standings, keeps group membership, and a fresh Generate afterward rebuilds and plays through cleanly.
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario above, including real page reloads (not just same-session state) and a clean `localStorage`-cleared load.

### Explicitly out of scope, not touched
Double-elimination; same-group-avoidance seeding into the bracket phase (Part 6 — **built 2026-09-11, see "Small flagged-gap fixes"**); any UI indication of which pooling-phase/bracket-phase combination is "recommended".

## Fix: bye recipient double-counted at a cumulative-standings cutoff (done — 2026-09-10)
`260903_prompt_fix_bye_double_count.md`. A small, isolated fix to a bug independently reproduced three times — qual-table+Bye, Swiss+Bye, and group-stage+Bye all tripped the same underlying issue, previously left unfixed in each of those three builds as out of scope.

### Root cause
`advanceRound()` calls `computeAdvancement()` to get the advancing list, then unconditionally prepends `T.byes[ri]` (this round's bye recipient(s)) onto it. Correct and necessary for a plain room-based elimination round — a bye recipient has no room result to rank by, so without the prepend they'd be missing entirely. Wrong for the three cumulative-standings cutoff branches (qual-table/Swiss/group-stage): there, a bye recipient's *cumulative* total from rounds actually played can already place them inside the cutoff's own returned list (the "no row for a bye round, but still accumulates from rounds played" convention), and the prepend then adds that same unit a second time.

### The fix — deduplicate, not branch
Per the prompt's own instruction: dedupe the final `advancing` list after the prepend, rather than teach the prepend step which branches need it. This is robust to *why* the overlap happened, not just today's three known cases — a fourth pooling-phase type added later is protected automatically. Implementation: iterate `advancing` from the end backward, keeping the **last** occurrence of each roster identity key (`name` — overloaded throughout this app to mean a player name or a team's `teamId`). This ordering choice matters: for a cutoff branch, the last occurrence is `computeAdvancement()`'s own correctly-ranked entry (the prepended duplicate, artificially shoved to the front, is what gets dropped); for a plain room-based round, the prepend is the only entry to begin with (a bye recipient's `room:null` means `computeAdvancement()`'s per-room loop can never also produce them), so the dedup is a verified no-op there. No branch-conditional skip was needed — the straightforward dedup was both simpler and more robust, so that's what shipped.

### A distinction worth being precise about, found while re-verifying the three prior write-ups
Re-running each of the three previously-documented anecdotes **exactly as originally described**, with the fix in place, showed the fix resolves two of them cleanly but not the third — not because the fix is incomplete, but because that third anecdote was never actually an instance of the duplicate-counting bug in the first place:
- **Swiss+Bye and group-stage+Bye**'s original write-ups explicitly confirmed (at the time) that the doubled unit "both qualified via the cutoff's own ranking *and* happened to be on a bye" — a genuine duplicate. Both now confirmed fixed, including re-running group stage's specific N=21 mixed-group-size mass-bye scenario.
- **qual-table+Bye**'s original write-up (`team-3v3`, N=25, `qualAdv=16`) described "a team that never plays a real qual match" pushing "an extra unit through the cutoff" — re-run verbatim with the fix in place, it still trips the same safety net. This is a *different* characteristic: a unit with **no accumulated standing at all** (never played a single real round) can never appear in `computeAdvancement()`'s own returned list in the first place (`T.qualTable`/`T.groupStandings` filter out `totalFP === null` entries before slicing) — so there's no duplicate for a dedup to remove. The prepend is, in this case, legitimately adding a genuinely new unit past the cutoff, exactly the same as the room-based case's own bye convention — just one that happens to inflate a standings-cutoff's count beyond `qualAdv`/`qualifiersPerGroup`. This is real, and can still trip the same "can't advance to the Final" safety net, but it's a distinct, still-open characteristic, out of scope for this prompt's specifically-described root cause (confirmed via direct re-run, not assumed from re-reading the code).
- The genuine duplicate variant of qual-table+Bye — an existing qualifier's cumulative total placing them in the cutoff list, deliberately re-forced as that round's own bye recipient too — **is** confirmed fixed (verified with a deliberately-constructed overlap, since natural random play can land in either case and the two are easy to conflate from the surface symptom alone).

### Testing performed
- **All three duplicate scenarios, deterministically forced** (not left to chance, per the lesson above): for qual-table+Bye, Swiss+Bye, and group-stage+Bye (the exact N=21 mixed-group-size mass-bye case from the group-stage build), directly forced a bye recipient to be someone already present in the raw `computeAdvancement()` output, then confirmed `advanceRound()` produces the correct count with zero duplicate names and no safety-net trip. Group-stage additionally stress-tested with *every* qualifier simultaneously forced as a bye recipient (worse than the real mass-bye scenario ever produces) — still zero duplicates, correct count.
- **The legitimate room-based case (Part 3)** — `team-3v3` Bye mode, no pooling: confirmed a genuine bye recipient still appears in the next round's assignment exactly once, `totalAdvanced` matching the expected no-elim carry-forward count.
- **Full regression, every format × pooling-phase × bracket-phase × odd-count-strategy combination that currently exists**: FFA (`None`/`qual-table`), `team-2v2v2v2`, `team-3v3` (`None`/`Bye`/`Flex`, into both `classic-elimination` and `single-elimination` where valid), `team-3v3v3`, `individual-1v1` (both bracket phases, no-pooling/`qual-table`/`swiss`/`group-stage`), and `team-3v3` Bye combined with each of `qual-table`/`swiss`/`group-stage` — all reached a completed champion with zero duplicate names at any transition. (The `team-3v3`+Bye+`qual-table`/`group-stage` combinations hit the separate, still-open "never accumulated a standing" characteristic described above when left to random scoring — re-run with `None` odd-count strategy instead to get a clean pass confirming the dedup itself introduces no regression; this is the same distinction, not a new finding.)
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario above.

### Documentation
The three original write-ups (in "Concentrate single-elimination's byes," "Swiss pooling phase," and "Group stage pooling phase") were corrected in place — Swiss and group-stage's own bye-quirk notes now point here as fixed; qual-table's note was corrected to clarify its specific anecdote was actually the separate, still-open "no accumulated standing" characteristic above, not the duplicate this fix resolves, with a cross-reference confirming the genuine duplicate variant of qual-table+Bye is fixed too.

### Explicitly out of scope
Any broader refactor of `advanceRound()`'s dispatch logic; the separate "bye recipient with no accumulated standing inflates a cutoff's count" characteristic identified above (real, still open, but not what this prompt's root cause describes); double-elimination or any other new feature work.
