## Fix: duplicate premature `loadState()` call (done — 2026-09-12)
Found in passing while reading `core.js` for the defender build below, flagged separately at the time (a suggested standalone task), then folded into this session directly at the organiser's own request instead.

`loadState()` was called twice on every page load: correctly from `index.html`'s own trailing inline `<script>` (after every `js/*.js` file has loaded), and a second time prematurely from a stray `loadState();` at the very tail of `js/core.js` itself — the FIRST script tag, so this ran before `generation-and-roster.js` (a later tag) had defined `togglePoolingPhaseField()` and friends, which `loadState()` calls partway through. That threw a `ReferenceError`, silently swallowed by `loadState()`'s own try/catch (`console.warn('Could not restore saved tournament state', ...)`) — harmless in effect, since the correct second call from `index.html` fully re-did the work, but wasted work every load and a risk that a genuinely new bug in `loadState()` could hide behind the same "known, ignorable" warning.

**Fix**: deleted the stray call and its comment block from the tail of `core.js`, replaced with a short comment explaining why `loadState()` belongs only in `index.html`'s trailing script (script load order) — `index.html`'s own call is unchanged and is now the only one.

### Testing performed
- A genuinely fresh load (cleared `localStorage`, hard-reloaded): no "Could not restore saved tournament state" warning in the console at all.
- A load with a real saved tournament present: no warning, and restore behaved correctly (`T.started`, `T.rounds`, `T.players`, and `panel-running`'s visibility all confirmed correct after reload).
- Zero console errors either way.

### Explicitly out of scope
Any other `loadState()`/`saveState()` behavior — this was purely the one stray call.

---

## Defender history for the 'designated-player' team scoring rule (done — 2026-09-12)
Follow-up to this session's earlier "Designated-player team scoring rule" build, which hardcoded the designated scorer as "always the first-listed member, no way to change it." Two organiser notes drove this: the designated player (in-game called **the defender**, from Curve Fever Pro's "Save your Buddy" mode) must be **changeable mid-tournament**, and — the real constraint — **a later change must never alter what an already-played round's score was computed from**. The organiser also asked for "defender" to be visually integrated wherever it matters to *participants* preparing to face a team: Manage Teams (so the organiser can set it), Bracket, and Scoreboard — deliberately not Rankings, since the stated need was specifically pre-match/live-match visibility, not the standings table.

### State: a change log, not a per-round snapshot
New `T.defenderChanges` ([js/core.js](js/core.js)) — `{ teamId: [{round, memberIdx}, ...] }`, append-only, per team. This was the key design choice: a dense per-round snapshot (mirroring `T.luckyLosers[ri]`/`T.byes[ri]`) would need hooking into every place a new round gets created — `advanceRound()`'s generic path, `finalizeDoubleEliminationRound()`, group-stage/Swiss seeding — to carry the current defender forward. A sparse log needs none of that: a lookup for round `ri` (`getDefenderIndex(state, teamId, ri)`, [js/formats-and-primitives.js](js/formats-and-primitives.js)) just finds the latest entry with `round <= ri`, so a change recorded at a *later* round can structurally never affect a lookup for an *earlier* one — the historical-integrity guarantee falls out of the data structure itself rather than needing to be remembered at every call site. Defaults to member index 0 when no entry applies, which also gives clean backward compatibility for free: a tournament or archived snapshot from before this feature existed simply has no `defenderChanges` field at all, and reproduces the original "always first-listed" behaviour exactly.

Reset in `proceedReset()` and freshly re-initialized in `proceedGenerateSchedule()` (alongside `T.groups = []`'s own "must never leak into a new generation" reset) — both checked explicitly against this project's own prior miss of exactly this class of bug (`T.byes` once being forgotten from the `proceedReset()` list). No special handling needed in `saveState()`/Archive: unlike the admin-password hash, which deliberately had to be *excluded* from `T`, this is ordinary tournament data that belongs in every snapshot, and the existing generic mechanism already includes it.

### Wiring: `computeTeamScore` gains a second parameter
`TEAM_SCORING_RULES['designated-player'].computeTeamScore(individualScores, designatedIdx)` now reads `individualScores[designatedIdx]` instead of hardcoding index 0; `sum-members` ignores the new parameter. `getUnitScore()`/`getFinalUnitScore()` ([js/formats-and-primitives.js](js/formats-and-primitives.js)) compute `designatedIdx = getDefenderIndex(state, teamId, ri)` for the round they're actually reading and pass it through — for `getFinalUnitScore`, `ri = state.rounds.findIndex(r => r.isFinal)` (true for the grand-final round too, so this is uniform across every schedule logic, no special-casing needed). New `setDefender(teamId, memberIdx)` ([js/generation-and-roster.js](js/generation-and-roster.js)) appends-or-updates (never duplicates) a `{round: T.curRound, memberIdx}` entry — **only ever writes the current round's own entry**, never touching a past round's already-recorded one. Not gated by the reserve window, same reasoning `removePlayer()`/rename already use — this manages the roster, it doesn't confer a competitive advantage.

**A real bug caught by testing the full pipeline, not just the new code in isolation**: the first version of `getUnitScore()` read `asgn[pi].name` directly to resolve the team for `getDefenderIndex()` — unguarded, unlike every other access in that function (`asgn[pi] && ...`). `computeRankings()`'s elimination-detection scan calls `getUnitScore()` for room positions that don't always have an occupant, which crashed the very first full-tournament regression attempted. Fixed by reusing the already-guarded `team` variable (`team && team.teamId`) instead of re-deriving from `asgn[pi]` a second, unguarded way — caught specifically because a full playthrough was run as part of verification rather than only the isolated defender-change scenario.

### Display: matched by name, not index
Both marking sites needed to identify the defender within an already-rendered member-name list — `unitDisplay()`'s `members` array has vacant slots filtered out, so a raw member index no longer corresponds 1:1 to a position in that list once any slot in the team is vacant. New `getDefenderName(state, teamId, ri)` resolves straight to the defending member's current display name, and a shared `markedMemberNames(state, key, ri, memberNames)` helper (both in [js/formats-and-primitives.js](js/formats-and-primitives.js)) marks whichever rendered name matches it — sidestepping the index/vacancy mismatch entirely rather than needing to re-derive it. Verified directly, not assumed: vacated a member slot positioned *before* the actual defender in the array and confirmed the correct (later) member still got marked.

- **Manage Teams** (`renderManageTeams()`): a 🛡 badge on the current defender's row, a "Make Defender" button on every other filled member's row — both only when the tournament's `teamScoringRule === 'designated-player'`.
- **Bracket** (`buildBracketHtml()`'s team-row block): marks whichever member was defending *for that round's own `ri`* — this is the one place the historical guarantee is directly visible, not just internally correct. Verified: after changing a team's defender partway through a tournament, that team's *earlier* Bracket cards kept showing the *original* defender, and only the round the change took effect in onward showed the new one.
- **Scoreboard**: `renderUnitCell()` ([js/formats-and-primitives.js](js/formats-and-primitives.js)) gained an **optional** 4th parameter, `ri` — only Scoreboard's own call site passes it (the live current round is all Scoreboard ever shows); every other existing call site (the seed panel, the reserve panel, the Finals total, standings tables) is untouched, since omitting the parameter skips the marking logic entirely.
- Relabeled the rule itself: `"Save your Buddy (defender only)"`.

### Testing performed
- **The core historical-integrity case** (the actual point of this build): a `team-3v3` tournament, defender left at the default (member 0) through rounds 1–2 (both scored and advanced), then `setDefender()` to member 1 starting round 3. Confirmed directly — not assumed — that rounds 1 and 2's team scores for that exact team, read fresh *after* the change, were byte-identical to before it; a *different* team (never touched) still used member 0 in round 3 too, confirming the change is genuinely per-team; round 3 onward correctly reflected member 1.
- Manage Teams: badge on the correct member, button correctly moves it, neither shown at all under `sum-members`.
- Bracket: the historical case above, verified visually in the actual generated HTML (round 1/2's cards marked member 0, round 3's card marked member 1) — and the vacant-slot-before-the-defender case (above).
- Scoreboard: defender marked for the live round; confirmed the reserve panel (which also calls `renderUnitCell` with member names shown) shows no marker at all, since it doesn't pass `ri`.
- `sum-members` regression: zero 🛡 markers anywhere across Manage Teams/Scoreboard/Bracket for a tournament generated under the default rule.
- Save/reload: `T.defenderChanges` round-trips correctly through `localStorage`; a simulated pre-feature snapshot (the field deleted entirely) correctly defaults every lookup to member index 0.
- **Full regression, both formats**: a complete `team-3v3` playthrough (with the vacant-slot-plus-defender-change scenario from above folded in) reached a scored champion — this is what caught the `asgn[pi].name` bug above, since the isolated defender-change test alone never exercised `computeRankings()`'s elimination-scan path. A separate full FFA (individual format) playthrough confirmed the shared `getUnitScore()`/`getFinalUnitScore()` changes left the non-team code path completely untouched.
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario above.

### Explicitly out of scope
Rankings (not requested — the stated need was specifically pre-match/live-match visibility for participants, which is Bracket/Scoreboard, not the standings table); any change to what happens if the defender's own slot is later removed via Manage Teams (the replacement at that same index silently becomes defender by position — an existing consequence of member slots being stable-indexed, not a new problem this build introduces, not solved here); the duplicate-member-name-within-one-team edge case (matches this project's existing accepted risk for duplicate names generally).

## Fix: future-round structure goes stale when reserves join mid-tournament (done — 2026-09-04)
HANDOFF.md's "Known issues" listed this as a Bracket-tab display quirk: "future rounds show projected structure but don't update if player count changes mid-tournament." Investigation showed it was bigger than that — a real advancement bug, not a display one.

### Why: every bracket phase freezes its projection at generation time
`classicEliminationBracketPhase`/`singleEliminationBracketPhase`/`doubleEliminationBracketPhase` ([js/bracket-phases.js](js/bracket-phases.js)) each compute room counts, advancement targets, Semis/Final, and — for double-elimination — the whole WB/LB routing table, once, from a single `seedTotal` captured at Generate Schedule time. `addReserve()`/`addTeamReserve()` ([js/generation-and-roster.js](js/generation-and-roster.js)) mutate `T.assignments`/`T.players` when a reserve joins but never touched `T.rounds`. `advanceRound()` then seeds each real round using that stale, frozen structure — so a reserve joining during the no-elim/pooling window didn't just make the Bracket tab's preview stale, it could run the actual tournament with the wrong room sizes and advancement math, and could have silently exceeded the game's 10-player hard room cap with zero warning (`validateRoomCap()` only ever ran once, at generation).

Organiser decision: **full auto-recompute** — whenever a reserve joins, silently re-derive every not-yet-reached round's structure from the new live headcount, matching the app's standing "the organiser should never have to think about headcount math" principle (see "One important principle" above).

### Fix: `recomputeFutureRounds()`
New function ([js/advancement.js](js/advancement.js), next to `checkReserveWindow()`) — called from `addReserve()`/`addTeamReserve()` right after the new unit is pushed, before render/save. Re-derives `T.rounds` from `T.curRound + 1` onward: resizes any still-remaining pooling-phase rounds to the new live headcount via the same `distributeRoomsWithBye()` every pooling phase already uses, then calls the active `BRACKET_PHASES` entry fresh at its own natural entry point (`seedTotal` = live headcount for `'none'` pooling, or the organiser's unchanged `T.cfg.qualAdv` for qual-table/Swiss — a reserve widens the pool competing for a fixed cutoff, it doesn't move the cutoff), `startRoundNum` = the pooling→bracket handoff round. `T.rounds[0..T.curRound]` (already-played, or the round currently being scored) is never touched — same historical-integrity principle as the defender-change log above. Re-validates the proposed tail with the existing `validateRoomCap()` before committing; on failure, returns `false` and the caller rolls back its own mutation (assignment/player/reserve-list restored) with an alert, rather than silently running an invalid schedule.

This works cleanly because `checkReserveWindow()` (existing code) already guarantees `T.reserveOpen` can never be true any later than the pooling→bracket handoff (or, for `'none'` pooling only, the first bracket round) — so recompute is never asked to regenerate a bracket phase mid-sequence, only from the one entry point `classicEliminationBracketPhase`/`singleEliminationBracketPhase`/`doubleEliminationBracketPhase` are already pure functions of. The organiser's Semis/Final override is reapplied automatically for free, since it lives in `T.gamemodeConfig` (untouched by this) and the bracket-phase functions read it from there every time they're called. `T.byes`/`T.luckyLosers` (dense per-round arrays) are re-sliced to match — safe because any index past `T.curRound` is guaranteed still at its untouched `[]` state. Round count can only ever grow, never shrink (`computeElimRoundCount` and the single/double-elim power-of-2 round count are both monotonically non-decreasing in total) — confirmed no code hardcodes a `T.rounds` index beyond `T.rounds[0]` or caches its length, so a longer array needed no rendering changes anywhere (Admin timeline, Bracket, Scoreboard all read `T.rounds` fresh every render).

**Judgment call, made directly (not re-asked)**: group stage is explicitly excluded from recompute and instead gets a hard refusal — `addReserve()`/`addTeamReserve()` now alert-and-refuse outright if `T.cfg.poolingPhase === 'group-stage'`. Group stage's `T.groups`/round-robin schedule is fixed membership set once at generation, not a headcount-derived rooms array like every other pooling phase, so "recompute" has no sound meaning there. This also closes a **pre-existing latent bug**, not just a newly-drawn scope boundary: before this fix, a reserve added during group stage was silently placed via `smallestRoomInCurrentRound()` into an arbitrary 2-player round-robin match room, corrupting that match — group stage simply had no reserve support that actually worked.

### Testing performed
All scenarios driven directly against the live app (function calls through the actual UI's own code paths, not a separate harness), verified via direct `T` inspection plus rendered-page checks:
- **Individual FFA, classic-elimination, `'none'` pooling, round-count threshold crossing**: 31 players (3 elimination rounds) → added reserves one at a time to 37 (4 elimination rounds) during no-elim Round 1. `T.rounds.length` grew 7→8 exactly at the 31→32 boundary (matches `computeElimRoundCount`'s own math); Round 1 (already in progress) stayed byte-identical throughout; the recomputed tail (rounds 2–8) was diffed against a completely independent fresh 37-player generation and matched **byte-for-byte** field-by-field (rooms/advPerRoom/luckyCount/players/byeCount/isSemis/isFinal for every round) — not just "looks reasonable," an actual ground-truth comparison.
- **Room-cap refusal + rollback**: forced `validateRoomCap()` to fail via monkey-patch, confirmed `addReserve()` fully rolled back (`T.players`/`T.reserves`/`T.assignments[T.curRound]` all restored to their exact pre-attempt state) and `T.rounds` was untouched, with the correct alert firing.
- **Group-stage guard**: confirmed the reserve add is refused with a clear message and `T.players` is unchanged, for a tournament actually mid-group-stage.
- **Qual-table pooling + classic-elimination**: 31 registered, `qualAdv=24` fixed, added reserves to 37 during qual Round 1. Qual Rounds 2–3 correctly resized to the new live total; the bracket portion of `T.rounds` (from the qual→bracket handoff onward) was confirmed **byte-identical** before and after the add, since `qualAdv` itself never changed.
- **Team format** (`team-3v3v3`, 13 teams): added 2 reserve teams mid-no-elim; confirmed the recompute correctly used team-unit counts (not player counts) throughout.
- **Single-elimination, `individual-1v1`, `'bye'` odd-count strategy, power-of-2 threshold crossing**: 15 players (bracket size 16) → 17 (bracket size 32) via reserves during no-elim Round 1; confirmed the concentrated-bye math on the recomputed first bracket round (1 active match, 15 byes) and the correct round-count growth.
- **Double-elimination, `individual-1v1`**: same 15→17 crossing; confirmed every recomputed WB/LB round's `winnersTo`/`losersTo` pointed to valid in-bounds indices and the grand final landed at the correct final index.
- **Save/reload round-trip**: saved mid-tournament after a recompute-triggering add, hard-reloaded the page, confirmed `T.rounds`'s grown structure (and everything downstream of it) survived `loadState()` intact.
- **Rendering**: the Bracket tab, checked against the live double-elimination scenario above, correctly displayed all 16 recomputed rounds (2 no-elim + 5 WB + 8 LB + grand final) with the already-reached round showing real (reserve-inclusive) assignments and every future round correctly showing "Not yet seeded" — no code changes were needed there, exactly as expected from `buildBracketHtml()`'s existing empty-assignment handling.
- Zero console errors across every scenario above.

### Explicitly out of scope
Cross-tab/cross-device live sync (separate, larger, pre-existing known gap — unrelated to this fix, still open, see "What is NOT yet built" above). Any change to the pooling-phase round *count* itself (`qualRounds`/`swissRounds`/the fixed 2-round no-elim warm-up) — deliberately kept invariant; only the elimination round count is allowed to grow, since it was already an openly-variable, derived quantity (see "Variable elimination-round count" above) unlike the pooling round count, which is an organiser-visible commitment. Any recompute *support* for group stage — explicitly blocked, not deferred as "later" (see judgment call above).

---

## Real static hosting + cross-tab/cross-device live sync (done — 2026-09-04)
The organiser asked to pick up the cross-tab/cross-device live-sync gap flagged in the previous entry. Investigating how viewers actually reach the app surfaced a blocking prerequisite: distribution was via a OneDrive share link, which turned out to only ever hand recipients a folder to download and open `file://` locally — never a genuinely live running page. Real-time sync has nothing to sync *through* if viewers aren't sitting in an actual connected browser tab, so this became two builds: real hosting first, then sync on top of it. Both were explicit forks surfaced to the organiser via `AskUserQuestion` before building anything (hosting model, sync scope — same-network vs. truly anywhere, backend approach) — see HANDOFF.md's new "Real static hosting" and "Cross-tab / cross-device live sync" sections for the full design writeup; this entry covers what was built and how it was tested.

### Real static hosting
`git init` + a new public GitHub repo (`github.com/conchord/curve-tour-app`), pushed to `main`, GitHub Pages enabled (root folder). `.gitignore` added, excluding `CLAUDE.md`/`HANDOFF.md`/`HANDOFF_LOG.md`/`Past prompts/` (internal working docs — organiser's own call, narrowed from "exclude Past prompts + local settings only" to "exclude all internal docs, ship just the app") and `.claude/settings.local.json` (machine-local tool-permission config, standard practice to keep out of git). Verified live end-to-end on the real `https://conchord.github.io/curve-tour-app/` URL: roster load → generate schedule → start tournament, Scoreboard and Bracket tabs, zero console errors.

### Cross-tab / cross-device live sync
Firebase Realtime Database via the organiser's own free-tier project (created through their own Firebase console access — account/project creation isn't something this session could do on their behalf). New `js/sync.js`; small hooks into `js/core.js` (`saveState()`, `switchTab()`, `proceedReset()`) and `js/generation-and-roster.js` (`proceedGenerateSchedule()`, `startTournament()`). Full design (writer/viewer mode split, the write-key security scheme, the shareable-link mechanism, resilience guarantees) is written up in HANDOFF.md's "Cross-tab / cross-device live sync" section rather than duplicated here.

**A real gap found and fixed during testing, not just argued**: the security rule's `.write` condition (`!data.exists() || newData.child('writeKey')... === data.child('writeKey')...`) technically also blocks a legitimate `.remove()` — a delete's `newData` doesn't exist, so it can never carry a matching key. Attempted to clean up this build's own test data from Firebase directly and hit `PERMISSION_DENIED` confirming it. Decided **not** to weaken the rule for this — the real app never needs to delete a live tournament's Firebase node (an organiser just moves on to the next one), so this is recorded as a known, accepted limitation (stale data persists in Firebase indefinitely) rather than fixed, to avoid loosening security for a capability nothing actually needs.

### Testing performed
Two real browser tabs (writer + viewer, opened via the writer's own generated `?t=` link) against the actual production Firebase project — see the full list in HANDOFF.md's own "Testing performed" for this feature, which is not duplicated here. Headline results: live score/round-advancement propagation confirmed in the viewer's rendered DOM (not just its in-memory `T`), zero console errors in either tab, the security rule's write-key enforcement confirmed by a rejected write (not just skipped client-side), and — the one that mattered most for trusting this design — **directly verified** (via a monkeypatched `localStorage.setItem`, not assumed from the code alone) that a viewer-mode tab never writes to `localStorage` even when sharing an origin with a real writer tab, so an organiser previewing their own share link in a second same-browser tab can't corrupt their real session.

One test-harness gotcha hit along the way, not an app bug: an early round of this session's own testing briefly showed a "Sync"-named tournament rendering as double-elimination in the Bracket tab when classic-elimination had been intended — traced to `loadState()` restoring a stale `cfg-schedule-logic` dropdown value left over from an earlier, unrelated test on the same `localhost` origin, before the new roster/generate calls ran. The sync mechanism itself was mirroring the real (if unintended) state correctly; this was purely a shared-test-browser-profile artifact, same category as prior sessions' documented "re-patch every touched global after a fresh navigate" gotchas.

Committed and pushed to `main` (`git commit` "Add cross-tab/cross-device live sync via Firebase Realtime Database", then `git push`); GitHub Pages rebuilt automatically. The full writer/viewer live-propagation test was then repeated a second time directly against the deployed `https://conchord.github.io/curve-tour-app/` URL (not just `localhost`), with the same result — confirms the CDN-loaded Firebase SDK works from the real hosted origin, not just the local dev server.

### Explicitly out of scope
Conflict resolution for two simultaneous writer tabs (accepted last-write-wins risk — same trust level as every other "the organiser is one person" assumption already in this app). A delete/cleanup mechanism for stale Firebase data (see the found-and-not-fixed gap above). Firebase Auth or any real per-organiser account system (the write-key scheme exists specifically so this isn't needed). Syncing the Archive tab or any Admin-only panel (Archive is local-only organiser data; viewer mode hides its nav entry entirely, same as Admin's).

---

## Admin access — real, shared-secret version (done — 2026-09-04)
The organiser pointed out, correctly, that the original "Admin password protection" (see that entry above) didn't actually restrict who could reach Admin: its hash lived in `localStorage`, which is per-browser, so any visitor with no saved password could reach Admin fully open — and could self-serve their own local password, which restricted only themselves, not anyone else. Investigating turned up a second, worse bug along the way: the app's original default landing tab was Admin itself, and the password check only ever ran from inside `switchTab()`'s click handler — meaning a fresh page load, with or without a password ever set, showed raw Admin content with **zero gate ever evaluated**. Real static hosting and the Firebase piece (see the entry above) made a genuine fix possible without needing a full account system: one shared secret, distributed by the organiser, verified server-side.

Design options were surfaced to the organiser first (a shared secret verified via Firebase rules vs. Firebase Auth with individual accounts vs. hiding Admin from the nav as a standalone fix) — they chose the shared-secret approach now, individual accounts "later on if needed," and explicitly asked to keep Admin on the visible nav (so admins can move between tabs without copy-pasting a URL) rather than hide it, plus to change the default landing tab from Scoreboard to Bracket.

### Mechanism
Extended the same Firebase Realtime Database already used for live sync, with a new `adminAuth` node and matching security rules (given to the organiser to paste into the Rules tab, same workflow as the sync feature's own rules):
```json
"adminAuth": {
  "hash": { ".read": false, ".write": "!data.exists()" },
  "verify": { ".read": false, ".write": "newData.val() === root.child('adminAuth/hash').val()" }
}
```
`hash` stores a SHA-256 hash of the shared secret (`sha256Hex()`, `js/core.js` — Web Crypto's `crypto.subtle`, safe to rely on now since the app only runs over real hosting, not `file://`), settable exactly once (`!data.exists()`) and never readable back by any client. Verifying a guess doesn't read anything — `verifyAdminSecret()` *attempts to write* the guessed hash to `adminAuth/verify`; the rule only permits that write if it matches the real stored hash, so the write's own success/failure (a normal Firebase Promise resolving or rejecting with `PERMISSION_DENIED`) **is** the answer, real or fake, without the real hash ever crossing the wire either way. Fails closed if Firebase itself is unreachable (`SYNC_DB` null → an `OFFLINE`-coded error, distinct from a genuine wrong-password rejection) rather than silently granting access when it can't check.

Replaced, not layered alongside, the old mechanism — `isAdminProtected()`, `setAdminPassword()`, `submitNewAdminPassword()`, `changeAdminPassword()`, `removeAdminPassword()`, `simpleHash()`, and `ADMIN_PW_HASH_KEY` are all gone from `js/core.js`. `ADMIN_UNLOCKED_KEY` (localStorage, per-browser) survives unchanged in spirit but downgraded in role — it's session convenience only now ("this browser already proved it knows the secret, don't reprompt every click"), not the actual security boundary, which is the Firebase rule. `switchTab('admin', ...)`'s guard simplified from `isAdminProtected() && !isAdminUnlocked()` to just `!isAdminUnlocked()`, since there's no more "protection turned off" state — Admin is always gated now. `renderAdminSecurityPanel()` rewritten to two states only (locked / unlocked-with-a-Lock-button), dropping the old "Set Password" first-run flow entirely.

**Bootstrap**: since the app itself can never write `adminAuth/hash` a second time, Claude generated a random passphrase-style secret, wrote its hash directly via a real browser session against production Firebase (confirmed a second bootstrap attempt was rejected, proving the once-only lock holds), and gave the plaintext secret to the organiser in chat to distribute. Changing it later isn't an in-app flow (see "Explicitly out of scope" below) — the organiser (or Claude, asked to) computes a new hash and pastes it into the Realtime Database console directly.

**Default tab changed from Admin to Bracket** — both the raw static HTML (`index.html`'s `.view.active`/nav-button-active classes moved from `admin` to `bracket`) and `js/sync.js`'s `initViewerMode()` (previously defaulted viewer-mode tabs to Scoreboard; changed to Bracket per the organiser's explicit request in the same message). This incidentally fixes the "raw Admin visible before any check runs" bug found above, for free — there's no longer any default state where Admin's content is what a fresh visitor sees first.

### Testing performed
Against the real production Firebase project, not a mock:
- Bootstrap succeeded once; a second attempt to overwrite `adminAuth/hash` was rejected (`PERMISSION_DENIED`), confirming the once-only lock.
- A wrong password: rejected with "Incorrect password", `T` stayed locked, prompt stayed open.
- The correct password: unlocked, closed the prompt, updated the security-panel status text, and correctly re-ran the original navigation attempt into Admin (the `switchTab()` retry-callback plumbing, unchanged from the original design, still worked correctly with the new async verification).
- `lockAdmin()`: cleared the unlock flag, returned to Bracket.
- **Fail-closed path, verified directly**: forced `SYNC_DB = null` mid-session (simulating Firebase being unreachable) and submitted the *correct* password anyway — confirmed it was refused with the distinct "can't verify right now — check your connection" message, not silently accepted and not conflated with "incorrect password."
- Default-tab change verified for both a fresh page load (lands on Bracket; Admin nav visible but locked) and a viewer-link tab (lands on Bracket; Admin nav still hidden entirely, unrelated to this change).
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario above.

### Explicitly out of scope
An in-app "change the shared secret" flow — `adminAuth/hash`'s bootstrap-once rule means the app itself can never overwrite it again by design; a safe in-app change flow needs a more careful rule (proving knowledge of the *old* secret before allowing a new one written in the same operation) that wasn't worth the added rule-correctness risk for a capability not explicitly requested this pass — deferred, likely worth revisiting alongside Firebase Auth if/when the organiser adds individual admin accounts. Rate-limiting or brute-force protection on `adminAuth/verify` guesses — Realtime Database rules alone have no mechanism for this (no server code to add backoff/throttling); accepted at the same deterrent-adjacent security level already accepted elsewhere in this app, appropriate for a community tournament tool, not a hardened target.

## Fix: misleading "coming soon" Schedule Logic dropdown entries (done — 2026-09-04)
The organiser asked whether Swiss and Group Stage were actually shipped, since the Admin setup panel's **Schedule logic** dropdown still listed "Swiss rounds (coming soon)" and "Group stage into knockout (coming soon)" as disabled options. They're not unbuilt — both are done and have been for a while (see "Swiss pooling phase" and "Group stage pooling phase" above) — they just live under a *different*, separate dropdown (**Pooling phase**), not Schedule logic. The two leftover entries in Schedule Logic were dead from the moment the phase-composability refactor split "how the field gets pooled" from "how the bracket is shaped" into two independent settings — confirmed via grep that neither `'swiss'` nor `'group-knockout'` was ever a real `SCHEDULE_LOGICS` key, just a stale, never-cleaned-up placeholder. `'kings-valley'` is the one entry in that dropdown that's genuinely still unbuilt (a different bracket structure entirely — promote/demote — not a composition of existing pieces), so it stays.

**Fix**: removed the two dead `<option>`s from `index.html`'s `#cfg-schedule-logic`. Also added a short inline hint under the **Pooling phase** field itself ("Combines with Schedule logic above — e.g. Group Stage + Classic elimination = groups feeding into a knockout bracket."), since the underlying confusion wasn't really about the dead options — it was that nothing in the UI explained these two dropdowns compose together, which is exactly what the organiser's question was really asking about.

### Testing performed
Verified the Schedule Logic dropdown now shows only the three real elimination logics plus "Kings Valley (coming soon)"; verified the new Pooling Phase hint renders correctly above its dropdown; zero console errors.

### Explicitly out of scope
Any change to Pooling Phase's own options or behavior (Swiss/Group Stage themselves are untouched, already fully working) — this was purely a dropdown-content and UI-clarity fix.

## Multiple concurrent admins (done — 2026-09-04)
The organiser said they plan to run several trusted admins simultaneously (different devices, same shared password) specifically to speed up score entry at a live event, and asked for "an elegant solution" to the collision risk that raises. Investigation (before writing any code) surfaced that the risk was bigger than a millisecond-level race: writer tabs never listened to Firebase at all, only pushed, so a writer active for even a few minutes while another writer changed something elsewhere would push its own stale full snapshot of the *entire* tournament on its next save — silently erasing the other admin's work, even in completely unrelated fields. Testing the fix then surfaced a second, deeper problem not initially scoped: there was no way for a second admin's device to become a writer for an existing tournament at all, since the per-tournament random write key that authorized pushes was only ever generated on whichever device ran Generate Schedule, and never shared anywhere. Both were surfaced to the organiser via `AskUserQuestion` before building (fix approach for the collision risk; how a second device joins as a writer) rather than assumed.

### Part 1 — Writer authorization unified with the Admin password
Replaced the per-tournament random write key entirely with the same shared secret that already gates Admin access (see "Admin access" above). New Firebase rule:
```json
"tournaments": {
  "$tid": {
    ".read": true,
    ".write": "newData.child('adminProof').val() === root.child('adminAuth/hash').val()"
  }
}
```
Actually simpler than the rule it replaced (no more `!data.exists() ||` branch — the same check now covers creating and updating a tournament identically). `verifyAdminSecret()` (`js/core.js`) now returns the computed hash on success; `submitAdminPasswordPrompt()` caches it in a new `ADMIN_PROOF_HASH_KEY` localStorage entry; `pushSyncUpdate()` (`js/sync.js`) sends it as `adminProof` on every push instead of the old `writeKey`. `lockAdmin()` clears the cached proof — without this a "locked" browser would still hold a valid credential and could keep pushing silently in the background despite the UI showing it as locked out. `generateSyncWriteKey()`/`SYNC_WRITEKEY_KEY` deleted outright as dead code, not left unused. **Known, accepted tradeoff, stated plainly rather than glossed over**: the proof hash sits in this browser's own localStorage after unlock — not the plaintext password, but a durable, directly-reusable write credential. Someone with devtools access to an already-unlocked browser could extract and reuse it. Not materially new exposure though: that same attacker already has full Admin UI access through the browser they're sitting at.

### Part 2 — The actual join flow: promotion
A second admin's device opens the organiser's shared live link (`?t=<id>`, previously permanent read-only viewer mode with Admin nav hidden entirely) and unlocks Admin with the same shared password. New `promoteViewerToWriter()` (`js/sync.js`), called from `submitAdminPasswordPrompt()`'s success handler when the tab was in viewer mode: tears down the read-only subscription, flips `SYNC_IS_VIEWER` to `false`, adopts `T.tournamentId` from the URL, reveals the Archive nav, and starts pushing/listening exactly like any other writer from that point on. Admin nav is now **always** visible, in every mode (`initViewerMode()`'s old `adminBtn.style.display='none'` line removed, and `switchTab()`'s old viewer-block `alert()` guard removed) — clicking it always just prompts for the password, uniformly. A browser that's already unlocked (promoted once before, or the organiser's own base-URL browser) skips viewer mode entirely on a later `?t=` visit: `SYNC_IS_VIEWER`'s computation now checks `isAdminUnlocked()` at load, and `index.html`'s trailing script explicitly adopts `SYNC_VIEW_TID` as `T.tournamentId` when it differs from whatever was locally restored, so following a link to a *different* tournament while already unlocked correctly joins that one rather than staying on stale local state.

### Part 3 — Concurrent edits without data loss
Every writer tab now also subscribes to Firebase (`startWriterListener()`, mirroring the viewer's own listener) and merges incoming updates via `applyRemoteWriterUpdate()`:
- **Dirty-key tracking for `T.scores`/`T.finalScores` specifically** (`syncDirtyScoreKeys`/`syncDirtyFinalScoreKeys`, populated in `scoreChanged()`/`finalScoreChanged()`): a remote value is adopted for a key only if this tab has no unpushed local edit for it. Without this, a naive whole-object merge (the viewer's existing `Object.assign` pattern) would wholesale-replace `T.scores` with the remote copy on every incoming update — silently discarding whatever THIS admin just typed but hadn't pushed yet, which would have been worse than the original problem. `pushSyncUpdate()` snapshots each dirty key's *value* (not just its name) right before `.set()`, and only clears it from the dirty set on success if the value hasn't changed since — a newer keystroke landing during the in-flight request correctly stays dirty rather than being cleared out from under a change the server never actually saw.
- Every other field in `T` merges via simple whole-field remote-wins, matching the viewer's existing semantics — a deliberate, much simpler tradeoff, since those fields change via rare, singular, deliberate actions (round advancement, reserve adds) rather than sustained per-keystroke typing.
- Two admins editing the exact *same* field at the same instant remains last-write-wins — no operational-transform/CRDT machinery here, an explicitly accepted residual risk given the actual stated need was "different admins entering different players' scores."

### Part 4 — Never destroying a focused input mid-keystroke
`renderRooms()`/`renderMultiGameFinals()` fully rebuild the score grid via `innerHTML =` on every render — naively re-rendering the instant a remote update arrives would un-focus (and lose the cursor position of) whatever an admin is actively typing, even though no *data* would actually be lost per Part 3's merge. `renderAdminRound()` (`js/render-admin.js`) is now a thin, focus-aware guard: the old function body was renamed `renderAdminRoundNow()`; the new `renderAdminRound()` checks `document.activeElement` for `.score-inp` and defers (`adminRenderPending = true`) instead of calling it. A single delegated `focusout` listener, attached once at script load (needed because the actual `<input>` nodes get destroyed and recreated on every render — a per-input listener would need re-attaching every time), flushes the deferred render one tick after focus genuinely leaves every score field, so Tab-navigating between two score inputs (blur one, focus the next) doesn't trigger a spurious mid-navigation repaint. Every existing call site of `renderAdminRound()` (there are many, across advancement.js/generation-and-roster.js/core.js) needed zero changes — the guard sits transparently behind the same function name.

**A real, pre-existing bug fixed for free by this same mechanism**: `checkGrandFinalRace()` (advancement.js) already called `renderAdminRound()` synchronously from inside a finals-score input's own `oninput` handler, while that input was still focused — a narrow focus-loss bug on every grand-final race game transition, now caught by the same guard.

### A real gap found and fixed during testing, not just argued
A promoted-from-viewer tab never runs `loadState()` (viewer mode deliberately never touches localStorage), so nothing had ever told it to show `panel-running`/hide `panel-setup` for an already-started tournament — it inherited the static HTML's default (`panel-running` hidden) even though `T.started` was `true` after the first remote merge populated real data. Caught by testing the actual promotion flow end-to-end, not just the individual pieces in isolation. Fixed by adding the same panel-visibility toggle `loadState()` already does to `applyRemoteWriterUpdate()`'s admin-tab branch, which also correctly handles a promoted admin watching `T.started` flip live if a *different* admin clicks "Confirm & Start" while they're still sitting on the schedule preview.

### Testing performed
Two real browser tabs against the actual production Firebase project (not a mock), after publishing the updated security rules:
- Rules cutover: a stale/old-style push carrying `writeKey` instead of `adminProof` correctly rejected; a properly-authorized push succeeds.
- Promotion: locked a tab, navigated it to a `?t=` link (confirmed genuine fresh viewer mode — live data received, Admin nav visible, not yet unlocked), clicked Admin, entered the correct password — confirmed promotion (`SYNC_IS_VIEWER` false, `T.tournamentId` adopted, navigated straight into Admin, Archive nav revealed) and an immediate successful push.
- "Already unlocked, adopt tournament" path: opened a second, already-unlocked tab directly at the same `?t=` link — confirmed it joined as a writer immediately, no prompt, no viewer flash.
- Concurrent different-field edits: two writer tabs edited different rooms' scores within the same debounce window; confirmed after both settled that **both** tabs' local `T.scores`, and Firebase itself, contained all four edits with none discarded.
- Focus preservation, verified deterministically rather than via fragile UI clicking (programmatic `.focus()` and simulated clicks both proved unreliable in this project's browser-automation harness — `document.activeElement` stayed `BODY` regardless): overrode `document.activeElement`'s getter to point at a real score input via `Object.defineProperty`, called `renderAdminRound()`, and confirmed it deferred (`adminRenderPending` set true, `#admin-rooms`'s `innerHTML` byte-for-byte unchanged) rather than rebuilding; then dispatched a real `focusout` event and confirmed the deferred render correctly flushed on the next tick.
- Reset teardown: confirmed `proceedReset()` clears the writer-listener reference and the next `proceedGenerateSchedule()` subscribes cleanly to the new tournament's path with no stale listener left on the old one.
- Zero console errors throughout every scenario above.

One test-harness gotcha hit along the way, not an app bug: partway through testing, several newly-added `js/render-admin.js` functions/vars (`renderAdminRoundNow`, `isScoreInputFocused`, `adminRenderPending`) intermittently reported as `undefined` in the browser despite being confirmed present in the file actually being served (checked directly via `curl`) — a stale-cache artifact surviving a tab close/reopen and even a full server restart, resolved with this project's own established workaround (`fetch(url + '?bust=' + Date.now(), {cache:'no-store'})` + `eval`). Confirmed safe to whole-file re-eval `render-admin.js` specifically (unlike `core.js`, it declares no risky top-level stateful object like `T` that a re-eval would reset) before applying it.

### Explicitly out of scope
Two admins editing the exact same field at the same instant (still last-write-wins, explicitly accepted above). Any UI indicator showing *which* admin made a given change — all writers are equally trusted, matching this app's existing single-shared-credential model. Rate-limiting or throttling how many writer tabs can be active at once — not meaningfully different from the admin-password's own already-documented lack of brute-force protection. Protecting the reserve-add field or Manage-Players rename fields from the same focus-loss risk as score inputs — nothing mutates `T` per-keystroke there (no synced value to lose if the render happens mid-typing), so the risk is narrower (a UI annoyance, not data loss); `isScoreInputFocused()` is a one-line change to broaden later if it becomes a real nuisance.

---

## Round 2: Bracket/Scoreboard/Players mockups (design exploration — 2026-09-04)
The organiser reviewed the earlier "Live Bracket Concepts" mockups (5 concepts merging Bracket/Scoreboard for viewers or bringing bracket context into Admin, plus a recommendation to keep the split and prototype the Admin side first) and said none of them fit — but backed that up with concrete, detailed feedback rather than a plain rejection, which reframed the actual problem:

- **No live events, no audience, no stream overlay** — every player plays online, alone, at their own computer.
- **Two real success criteria for a viewer**: convenience (the Google Sheets benefit — switch tabs, see your room/opponent instantly, zero clicks) and fun/engagement (seeing how the wider tournament and other rooms are doing). Scoreboard serves neither — it's admin tooling, not a viewer destination, and the organiser reaffirmed the earlier direction of integrating it further into Admin's own workflow (see "Admin: split entry + live bracket rail" in the Round 1 concepts) rather than continuing to explore it as a viewer-facing merge target.
- **Collapsing rounds is welcome**, echoing Round 1's Concepts 1 and 3, with two hard requirements: expand/collapse state must be per-browser only (never synced, never driven by the admin's or another viewer's screen), and an expanded past round must show exactly what Bracket already shows today — names, scores, ★ lucky-loser, ⚖ tie-break, 🛡 defender marks, nothing reduced.
- **No staggered reveal exists or is wanted** — confirmed by investigation, not assumed: every score reaches every connected viewer at the same instant via the same Firebase push (`js/sync.js`); the only asymmetry today is which tab a viewer is on, not any per-player timing.
- **Player search should fold into Bracket itself** — finding your own name (or anyone's) shouldn't cost a tab switch into Players.

### Investigation, before designing anything
Read-only pass over `js/render-viewer.js` confirmed precisely how Bracket/Scoreboard/Players work today: `buildBracketHtml()` renders every round fully, side by side, with no collapse mechanism at all — "current round" is marked only by a cyan header color, nothing else. `renderScoreboard()` shows the current round only, with live partial-row reveal explicitly *not* gated on room completion (unlike Bracket, which withholds a whole room's results until every player in it has a score). Players' existing search (`filterPlayers()`) is a plain show/hide substring filter over already-rendered cards — no highlight, no jump, no memory. Confirmed real precedent already exists in this codebase for per-browser-only UI state that's never synced (`adminRenderPending`, `currentArchiveEntry` — in-memory; `ADMIN_UNLOCKED_KEY`/`ADMIN_PROOF_HASH_KEY` — localStorage-backed, survives reload, never touches `T`) — a remembered "this is me" identity or an expand/collapse toggle would follow an established pattern, not invent a new one. Also found and corrected two stale HANDOFF.md claims along the way: Scoreboard's "intended for stream/public display" framing, and a Rankings-section cross-reference to a cross-device sync gap that the Firebase sync feature had already closed after that line was written — both fixed in HANDOFF.md directly (see "Design intent for viewer-facing tabs" and the Rankings section there) rather than left stale.

### What was produced
Two new full-page concepts, published as a "Round 2" addition to the same artifact as before (read back and extended, not replaced — Round 1's original 5 concepts are preserved underneath a collapsed `<details>` archive section for reference/comparison, each system's tab-switching independently scoped so the two rounds don't interfere with each other):
1. **Pinned you, collapsible history** — a sticky identity strip answers "where do I play, who do I face" with zero clicks the moment the tab opens (a name picked once, remembered per-browser — the one genuinely new mechanism this round proposes, not currently in the app). The current round stays fully expanded below it; finished rounds collapse to one line each, individually expandable to the exact detail Bracket already shows (a fully worked example at Round 5 demonstrates ★/⚖ marks preserved; a text note confirms 🛡 defender marks carry through identically for team tournaments).
2. **Continuous scroll, universal search** — the Sheet's own read most literally: one scrolling page, history collapsed to a line each, the live round open where it naturally falls. A search ribbon pinned above finds *any* player (not just yourself), jumping to and highlighting every round they appear in.

Recommendation given: don't choose between them — they answer different questions the same player asks at different moments (finding yourself vs. browsing everyone else), and both already share the same underlying mechanism (collapsed rounds, expand-to-full-detail, per-browser-only state). Ship Concept 1's pinned self-strip as Bracket's new permanent header with its collapsible history, and add Concept 2's search ribbon alongside it for looking up anyone else.

Published at the same URL as Round 1: `https://claude.ai/code/artifact/8da50b2e-9487-4b16-acf6-6e71a99ef5f0` ("Live Bracket Concepts").

### Status
Design exploration only, same as Round 1 — no code changed. HANDOFF.md gained a durable "Design intent for viewer-facing tabs" section (the convenience/fun framing, the confirmed no-staggered-reveal fact, the per-browser-state precedent) so this reframing survives independent of whether either concept above is ever built. Next step, if any, is the organiser's call.

---

## Round 3: correcting Round 2's mockups + horizontal/no-collapse directions (design exploration — 2026-09-04)
The organiser liked Round 2's "Pinned you, collapsible history" concept specifically, but caught two real execution mistakes by looking at the actual mockup rather than judging the idea in the abstract — plus asked for two directions Round 2 never tried at all:

- **"Pinned you" wrongly assumed the viewer is always the player.** A spectator or an admin can use the exact same mechanism to follow someone else, for entertainment or monitoring. Renamed "Follow a player" throughout, and the mockup's copy changed from "You're ZigZag" / "Not you?" to "Following: ZigZag" / "Follow someone else."
- **Future rounds were shown faded and collapsed by default** — the opposite of what someone following a tournament actually wants. Corrected: only *past* rounds are ever a collapse candidate; upcoming rounds render at full visual weight, same as the current round, showing whatever structure is actually knowable (format, room count) even before real seeding exists.
- **The real defect, and the one worth remembering**: Round 2's "collapsed" past rounds rendered as a one-line prose summary — e.g. "Room A: ZigZag 1st, Vortex_99 2nd, +3 more — 5 advance" — not the actual table Bracket shows. This quietly lost exactly the ★/⚖/🛡 detail the whole exploration was supposed to preserve, despite Round 2's own stated rule that expanding a round must show full detail. The rule was right; the execution silently violated it. Fixed in Round 3 by making every history row, once opened, render the identical `.room`/`table.roster` markup the current round uses — no separate "summary" template at all — and by giving at least two different past rounds (not just one) this treatment, so the pattern reads as the rule rather than a single showcased exception.
- **Two directions Round 2 never explored, requested explicitly**: a "nothing collapses, ever" vertical option, and a horizontal orientation — specifically, today's *actual* Bracket layout (round-columns, horizontal scroll) adjusted rather than reimagined.

### What was produced
Four concepts, published as a "Round 3" addition to the same artifact — Round 2 now archived alongside Round 1 (each still independently browsable in its own collapsed `<details>` section, tab-switching scoped separately per round so none of the three interfere with each other), with a visible red-flagged callout on the Round 2 archive naming the three mistakes above so the correction has a paper trail, not just a silent fix:

1. **Follow a player — corrected.** Round 2's mechanic, renamed and fixed on all three points above. Two different past rounds (Round 4 and Round 5) both fully worked out as complete two-room tables, not one example standing in for the pattern.
2. **Vertical, nothing collapses.** Every round — past, current, projected — fully expanded, always, stacked top to bottom. The follow strip still applies; collapsing and following are independent features, not a package deal.
3. **Horizontal, today's Bracket.** The actual current Bracket layout (`.h3-scroller`/`.h3-col`, deliberately modeled on `buildBracketHtml()`'s real `.bracket-scroll`/`.bracket-round-col` CSS — fixed-width columns, horizontal scroll, "current" marked only by a cyan header, exactly matching what's already built) with a follow/search bar added above the scroller. The smallest possible change of the four.
4. **Horizontal + inline score entry.** Concept 3's layout, with the current round's column becoming directly editable (reusing the `.scoreinput`/`.entrytable` styling already established in Round 1's Admin concepts) — score entry happens in the same view a player might be looking at, not a separate Admin screen. Deliberately flagged as the most speculative: a real build needs an actual access-control answer (who sees editable fields in an otherwise-shared view), which this mockup raises but does not resolve.

Recommendation given: Concept 3 (horizontal, today's Bracket, lightly adjusted) is the safest default — smallest change, closest to what's already shipped. Concept 1 (Follow, corrected) is still the strongest fit for the original convenience ask, now that its two real defects are fixed. Concepts 2 and 4 are useful reference points — 2 as the honest "just show everything" fallback, 4 as a flagged-but-unresolved future direction — not the recommendation.

Published at the same URL as Rounds 1–2: `https://claude.ai/code/artifact/8da50b2e-9487-4b16-acf6-6e71a99ef5f0` ("Live Bracket Concepts").

### Status
Design exploration only — no code changed. HANDOFF.md's "Design intent for viewer-facing tabs" gained three new rules directly traceable to this round's corrections: follow-not-identity, collapsed-means-smaller-never-thinner, and future-rounds-default-to-visible — so a future build starts from the corrected rule, not the original mistake. Next step, if any, is the organiser's call.
